# HANDOFF

Running log of what each autonomous iteration shipped. Newest entry on
top. One line per ship — describe what changed and why, not how.

The launchd prompt that drives these iterations expects this file to
exist and to be appended to as the last step of every run. It was
deleted by mistake in `be8176f` (the YsWords-remnant audit, 2026-07-23)
and silently missing until 2026-08-06. Recreated then with everything
back to v1.5.0 reconstructed from `git log`, plus a single new entry
for that day's ship. From here on, each run appends one line.

---

- **#318 phase 10 — 42 verses shipped in the asset that no widget could reach, a printed falsehood underneath them, and a crash that made every sheet on the page unopenable (v1.6.175, `2c4c1fc`).** The survey said the wheel's defect was the same one phase 8 fixed on the chronology page: **111** references printed in English to Chinese readers. That was true, and it was the smallest of three. `WheelPower` parsed neither `ref` nor `refs` nor `basis` — **24 of 42 powers carry a verse** and not one of them had a call site — on the authority of the model's own library doc comment, *"Nothing is read out of scripture, so nothing here carries a verse."* **The comment was false when it was written and it was load-bearing**, because the class was built to match it; a field list that agrees with the prose above it is not evidence, and the only detector that would have caught this reads the JSON and the class *separately*. Parsing the unread `basis` then corrected something the page was actively **printing wrong**: `_showPower` hardcoded 通行年份 · 非经文所载 for all 42, so the United Monarchy, the Northern Kingdom and Judah — all three `scripture+thiele` — told the reader their spans are *not stated in scripture* while the asset says the interval is. **The third defect was a crash nobody could have seen.** `WbType.of` uses `context.watch`, and a tap handler is not a build, so opening any of this page's four sheets tripped `provider.dart:308`. Asserts are stripped in release, so the deployed build was fine and only a developer running `flutter run` ever hit it — and the suite could not hit it either, because **the test that should have caught the localisation defect existed and passed for the same reason it could never open a sheet**: this page **at rest shows no reference at all**. Its resting state is four `RichText` nodes, three of them empty, so a span sweep over the page as pumped is a **green light for the very defect it exists to catch**. It now taps an **8×16 polar grid**, opens 56 sheets, reads 34 references, and floors on **both** numbers, so a sweep that opened nothing cannot pass. That floor was measured, not guessed — my first attempt asserted `>100` and failed at 34. **Run against `e9b19ce` in a detached worktree, the pre-fix tree failed for the wrong reason first** — 56 provider exceptions, zero sheets — and only after neutralising *that alone* did it yield the proof the phase was about: `Set:['Genesis 10:8', 'Luke 3:1', 'Matthew 27:2']`. Two harness lessons worth keeping: `rootBundle.loadString` **never completes** inside a widget test's fake-async zone, so the asset must be loaded in `setUpAll` where the service's cache then answers the page's own `initState`; and a prior session had written the page off as un-pumpable on a probe that awaited the load *inside* the test body. The harness was never broken. The new `wheel_history_disclosure_test.dart` derives the keys each model class reads by parsing `wheel_history.dart` for its `j['…']` literals, and makes every unread key **assert its own excuse** — nations' `approximate`/`basis`/`era` are constant across all 82, `region` tracks `stream` within two splits, `ongoing` must equal `end == null` — so a hand-maintained allow-list cannot drift into an alibi. All 55+82+42 references are asserted to parse *and* localise before anything renders them, and storage stays English because `parseReference` reads it back. Verified by eye on the deployed build in zh-Hans (the fresh profile boots English; the locale has to be switched in-app): 埃及第二十二王朝（利比亚人） prints `列王纪上 14:25-26  历代志下 12:2-9`, and 南国犹大 prints `列王纪下 25:8-9  列王纪下 25:21` above **`经文所载间隔 · 年份按 Thiele`**. 3,484 tests green, analyze exit 0. **The next slice is the wheel's canvas** — band names, spoke labels and the verse beside each spoke are drawn through `TextPainter`, so no `RichText` sweep can see them; the ring label was localised by hand and confirmed by eye, and nothing in the suite would notice if it regressed. #318 stays open.

- **冷知识 rendered pointed Hebrew at a size the reader could not change — #315's ninth pass, `bbba27f` + `54e71f4`.** `bible_trivia_page.dart` was the largest single item left on the ticket and **the only file that sat in all three of the ratchet's budgets at once**: 9 literals, 9 `WbMetrics` constants and 5 saturating clamps, 23 sites in one page. It had been budgeted rather than fixed on the note that it is *"a game rather than a study surface"* — **and that note is why it waited eight passes.** The page renders the pointed Hebrew of Genesis 1:1 word by word with transliteration and gloss, the 22-letter alphabet with romanised names, and acrostic verse-count charts. It is a study surface. **All five clamps saturated at or BELOW the app's own default of 20 pt**, so nothing the page sized for itself moved across the entire upper half of the slider, and the worst instance is the ticket in miniature: **pointed Hebrew frozen at 16 px, one pixel above `WbMetrics.originalFloor`**, whose own comment records that below it qamats and patach collapse into one grey smudge. A reader who dragged the slider to 40 *because of the vowels* got 16 px. **Unfreezing type makes every fixed container the new frozen number, and the two ends bite in opposite directions** — above the default the text outgrows a constant, below it the text stops at a floor while a proportional box keeps shrinking out from under it. Hence `math.max` on the alphabet cell, the ordinal column and the tag strip, while the chapter chart's `barAreaHeight + 36` is **deleted rather than scaled**: a `Column` that sizes itself cannot fall behind. Three sizes change at the default, all upward, all because they sat under the workbench's own 11 px small-print floor — the romanised letter name at 8, the chart index and the sequence caption at 10, **the only `fontSize` literals below 11 in `lib/`**. The new `bible_trivia_font_size_behaviour_test.dart` **fails 4 of its 7 tests against `d24430f`**, and the 3 that pass there say so in the file: they guard what the repair could break — rank, the vowel floor, the containers — and a frozen page cannot overflow. **Two claims withdrawn after measurement, both artifacts of a single bad probe.** "Nothing on the page moved above 20 pt" was the *harness*: 11 unstyled `Text`s here travel off the theme's scaled typography and are invisible to a probe that pumps `MaterialApp` with no `theme:` — the same probe that made `workbenchTheme`'s component themes look like a ninth mechanism when they have been on the reader's scale since v1.6.159. And "8 px is the smallest text in the app" was true only of *literals*: the **unfloored `t.scaled()` reaches 7.5 px at the default setting** in `radial_chronology_page.dart`, with 8 px twice in `originals_sheet.dart` — correctly wired, genuinely travelling, and still under the floor. Next candidate for the ticket. **Then the photograph found a regression this pass had itself introduced, which no test could have caught.** Raising the letter names from 8 px to the 11 px floor made **"Lamed" and "Samek" wrap to two lines at 12 pt while the six-letter "Daleth" did not** — the binding character is the `m`, not the character count, and a 44 px cell less 8 px of padding leaves 36 where those two need about 37. Fixed to `math.max(48.0, nameSize * 4.4)`. **No test is added because none is possible:** Flutter's test font advances every Latin glyph a full em, so in the suite all 22 names overrun and the measurement carries no information about the shipped face; the container-fit test at 12 pt stayed green throughout and was right to, since a `Wrap` cell that wraps its text does not overflow. **For Latin advance widths the screenshot is not a convenience, it is the only instrument** — which is the general lesson worth keeping from this run. Verified on the deployed build at 32 pt (vowel points legible, alphabet and names scaled, no overflow) and at 12 pt (rank holds, page compact, all 8 tag chips on one row). **One further defect found and deliberately NOT fixed:** the reader's own Aa sheet (`bible_reading_pane.dart` `_showFontSizeSheet`) hardcodes **32** in four places while `kFontSizeMax` is **40**, so the control a reader is most likely to reach stops 8 pt below the setting it edits — and its doc comment asserts the parity it breaks, *"Range matches AppSettings (12-32 pt)"*. Confirmed by clicking A+ until it greyed out. That is a frozen **control range** rather than a frozen text size, which is why none of #315's three detectors look for it, and it needs a decision about which maximum is intended before it needs a patch. Residue: **16 ceilings / 9 files + 26 literals / 5 + 0 named constants**, of which 9 literals are `app_style_preset.dart`'s preset fields — real, documented, not defects — leaving a true literal residue of **17 across 4 files**. 3,429 tests green, analyze exit 0. **Deployed as v1.6.170 and then v1.6.171**, the second build existing only to carry the wrap fix, and re-photographed at 12 pt to confirm all 22 names now sit on one line. #315 stays open.

- **#313 item 5 — the reader is a column, so it stops carrying the app's chrome (v1.6.155).** The user's photos showed three phone affordances competing with three workbench ones: the reader's own 🔍 beside the command line, its ⋮ beside the menu bar, and a floating bar laid over the text. **The obvious fix — delete the column's chrome — would have been wrong, and the BibleWorks help said so before any code was written.** bwh06 describes each of its three windows as carrying a narrow title bar whose buttons "open up menus that provide additional tools and options", so a per-column menu is *authentic*; the defect is WHICH controls it holds. The rule shipped instead — `BibleReadingPane.hostChrome` — was already written down in the codebase by the split view's second column: **a column's controls may hold what operates on the column; anything that navigates the app or opens a Resource belongs to the one menu bar.** Nothing was removed without a home, and enumerating the affordances *first* caught two silent regressions a diff would never have shown: paragraph mode hung off `showSidebarToggle`, which the workbench passes `false`, so dropping the bottom bar would have made it unreachable; and font size had that bar as its only door **anywhere**, so a removal pass had to ADD a menu entry. Chapter navigation moved to the workspace toolbar, where it greys at the ends of the canon instead of doing nothing — and that traversal was fifty lines of index arithmetic copied twice inside the reader with no test, because reaching it meant building the whole reading pane. Pulled out to `lib/utils/chapter_navigation.dart`, pure, and **one of its edge cases turned out to be a bug**: an unknown chapter number left `indexWhere` at `-1`, which compares as "not the last one" and silently jumped the reader to chapter 1. It returns `null` now, like every other reference the canon does not hold. **The claim this iteration got wrong, and the screenshot that caught it:** consolidating exposed that every search affordance routes through `_focusCommandLine`, which opens `_leftOpen` and awaits a frame — but `_buildPanes` will not lay out a command pane below 600 px at all, so the button was a silent no-op there. That was written into the commit message, the audit and the research notes as "on a phone the workbench's search had been doing nothing" — and it is **false about impact**: the 900 px verification capture showed the small-screen advisory, not the workbench, which sent me to `main.dart:796`, where `SmallScreenGate` wraps `home:` unconditionally and admits only width ≥ 992. **No user was ever affected, because `WorkbenchPage` never builds below 992 on any platform.** The larger finding is in the correction: two of `_buildPanes`' three width branches, `_commandPaneMinWidth`, the rail condition and the compact status bar are all **dead in production**, and `test/workbench_page_test.dart` pumps `WorkbenchPage` directly — *below the gate* — so its 390 px and 768 px cases assert behaviour production cannot show. Defensible as a guard on the branches; not evidence about any real viewport. The 600 px push-a-route fix stayed anyway, and the harness can now opt into the `GetMaterialApp` the app really boots with, since `pushPage` goes through GetX's contextless navigator and throws without one. Verified by driving the dev deploy, not by tests, per the ticket: at 1400 px the header carries only ⋮ and no magnifier, no floating bar, `<` greyed at Genesis 1 while `>` is live and moves to Genesis 2; 1010 px the same; 900 px is the advisory. Verdicts per affordance — **including what was NOT audited**, the Browse window and Analysis pane, assumed clean on this axis from provenance rather than measured — in `docs/PRODUCT-AUDIT.md` §8. 3,276 tests green, analyze exit 0. #313 stays open on two items from §7: routing `_showChapterSermonsSheet`, and retiring the reader's `StatsPage` push once the Aramaic sheet and lemma picker have a home.

- **Check 47 — the twenty-seven verses check 46 called editorial, read against two texts that are not our descendants.** The residue could not be adjudicated at all until there was a witness we had not fathered, and the search for one **corrected the record about a text this project has trusted since check 26**: the yahwehdehua sqlite is our *parent*, not an independent witness — it reads 雅伟 at 申命记 32:19 and 5:5, our own editorial signature. `cuvs-plus`, `official_cuv_source`, `bolls.life/CUV` and our own tagged layer were already disqualified. **Admissibility was settled by test rather than by provenance**: run a candidate against check 46's twelve *already repaired* verses and require it to read the repaired form. ebible.org `cmn-cu89s` (新标点和合本, Public Domain) reads **11 repaired, 0 defective**, the twelfth differing only by 泰尔/推罗 while still supplying the lost city; 信望愛 fhl.net `unv` is the second. The obvious excuse for all 27 — "an older recension simply reads shorter" — was **measured and refuted**: a recension difference is systematic (约但/约旦 is 200/1 in our text and 3/198 in ebible; 推罗/泰尔 63/0 against 0/64) while every one of the 27 is a singleton whose fuller form our own corpus already writes elsewhere (弟兄们 139×, 心里 435×, 称为我名下 11×). Verdicts: **5 repaired, 6 proved to be the sweep's own false positives, 16 left.** Three of the five needed no external witness at all, because the corpus witnesses itself — 耶利米书 7:14 against 耶7:10/7:11/7:30, and 诗篇 102:26 against **its own quotation** at 希伯来书 1:12. The six false positives split two ways: 申5:5, 申32:19 and 可1:24 are verse-boundary placement (our *next* verse begins with the 说 the sweep calls missing), and 创47:9, 鸿3:8, 徒28:18 are `cuvs-plus` being the corrupt side. The **16 are left deliberately, and it is the honest call rather than the lazy one**: at each, two witnesses supply a character we lack but our reading is grammatical and means the same, so check 26's rule is not met — and the witnesses' independence *from each other* is unmeasured, so repairing would risk 16 verses on one vote wearing two coats. 15 records written across the flat 简, flat 繁 and tagged layers, the tagged runs keeping their Strong's numbers; 使徒行传 26:16 gained an argued empty-`s` run, because its 我 is carried by the person of ὤφθην and had nowhere honest to land. Sweep 27 → 23. **The sharpest lesson is an instrument failure**: fhl.net answers an unrecognised `engs=` with 羅馬書 — 创世纪 9:11 came back as Romans 9:11, plausible Chinese scripture at the right chapter and verse and the wrong book, for all 27 requests, with no error anywhere. Only reading the output caught it. `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md` §47.

- **Check 46 landed — it had been finished and never committed, and the shape it was left in hid it.** The watchdog killed the run before it shipped, so the tree held a complete piece of work: **12 word-level repairs to the reading text** the app actually prints (以賽亞書 23:1 names 推羅 again, 士師記 12:7 has its 六年 back, 12:13 its 以色列, 9:57 its 归到他们), the matching tagged-layer edits with corrected Strong's (H8337 שֵׁשׁ for 六, H7179 קַשׁ for 秸 where the run had been tagged **H1 אָב, *father***), 11 code points added to the CJK subset that had been rendering as *absent text* in the Evidence Archive, regression guards naming each defect with the Hebrew behind it, and 150 lines of DATA-INTEGRITY. Verified before trusting it: both flat editions still hold **155,510 leaves**, 0 added, 0 removed, exactly 12 values changed — and all 12 read as 信望愛 (fhl.net) has them. **But it was written minified over pretty-printed files, so it arrived as 435,535 deleted lines** — nothing lost, and completely unreviewable, with `git blame` destroyed for every verse in both books. Re-serialised at indent 2, which reproduces `HEAD` byte for byte: **24 lines, the twelve verses twice.** This trap has now been paid for twice, so the repair script got a `write_like()` that reads the file's own first bytes and follows them — round-trips **9 of 9** assets byte-identically. The layout is not free but nearly: minifying saves 1.09 MB raw and **40 KB gzipped**, and Netlify serves these compressed. Two font binaries were also reverted — identical size, **identical code-point coverage**, differing only in the `head` table's `modified` timestamp. Named and *not* fixed: three other tools write Bible JSON with no layout detection. 3,243 tests green, analyze exit 0.

- **Check 39 — the Septuagint prints seven English verses inside an earlier Greek record, and the Browse column was calling them missing.** `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md`'s item 2 asked for a *count* of a class check 29c had only ever found by example: references the **English** tradition creates by dividing a verse the Greek does not. **The count is 7 of 302, and one of check 29c's own two worked examples was false.** English Psalm 116:14's Greek is **not** inside our 116:13 — the edition's own `<vs:>` markers name a Greek 115:5 between 116:13 and 116:15 and no record in the file carries it, so that reference is a gap in *our copy*, not a feature of the Septuagint. Getting there took two self-refutations, both worth recording. **(1)** The marker parser dropped every record's *implicit* leading source number — a record that does not OPEN with a marker carries its own reader key, and a marker later in the text begins a new source verse mid-record — which manufactured phantom holes in seven New-Testament verses; fixed, the NT has exactly one hole and check 34d already knew it. **(2) Continuity of the Greek's numbering does not prove a merge.** 19 absences ran straight across under the numbering and reading the Greek refuted **18**: Greek Exodus 36–40 is a shorter, *reordered* tabernacle account whose numbers are contiguous over English words that are simply not there. With numbering ruled out and no content witness available — the external LXX check 29 used is keyed by the **English** reference and shares the collapse; the tagged layers cannot be joined because the Septuagint is Greek-tagged and the Old Testament's English witnesses are Hebrew-tagged — the instrument became **length**: a merged record holds two English verses' worth of Greek. Three nets (per-book character ratio, per-chapter ratio, and records carrying an *interior* `<vs:>` marker), **55 candidates read one at a time against the KJV, 7 merges**. Isaiah 64:1 is the cleanest and the only one a marker attests: our Isaiah 64:2 opens `<vs:64:1>`, so the Greek's chapter 64 begins a verse later and English 64:1 can only be the tail of Greek 63:19 — which is where it is. It is also the only head in a **different chapter**, so its row says "printed with an earlier verse" and refuses to print a bare "19" beside a 64:1 reference. **Eight more absences have only PART of their verse next door and are deliberately not claimed** — Greek Exodus 39:14 has the ark of the covenant and its staves and not the mercy seat, so "printed with verse 34" is a promise that row cannot keep. **What the instrument cannot see is stated as a number:** 167 of the 302 have a preceding record no longer than its own English predicts and cannot be hiding anything; 80 sit in a band too small to be a second verse, and a sufficiently compressed merge there would be missed. Also measured and **reported rather than repaired**, on check 34d's precedent: **153 Greek verses at 32 sites that the edition's own markers name and we do not carry**, plus 11 source references claimed by two reader records (a Greek verse split across two English ones — the reverse of a merge, and not a defect). Psalm 115:5 could have been *written* — the LXX's 115:5 and 115:9 are the same line and we carry 115:9 at English 116:18 — and that is exactly why it was not; importing Greek into one layer of two is a decision about what the product contains. `kEditionMergedHeads` in `lib/utils/verse_text_absence.dart` is the fourth way an absence gets explained and the only one that is a **list** rather than a rule, because a rule that found these would be a rule that guessed; `test/lxx_merged_reference_test.dart` freezes the seven with the Greek clause that carries each, the eight partials, the refuted candidates and the corpus's 302. **Seen, not merely reasoned about:** a headless capture of a local release build, LXX+WH beside NASB·BSB·KJV, shows Exodus 40:31 and 40:32 reading *"Printed with verse 30"* and Isaiah 64:1 reading *"Printed with an earlier verse"* — all three said "this edition has no verse here" before. **Not deployed** — undeployed iteration 1 under the deploy-on-demand rule, nothing human-reported in it. **Still blocked on the owner:** #278 (NASB licence), #292 (source), #293 (hosting), #296 (fresh crash report), #309 (unreachable site).

- **v1.6.146** — **A journey line answers back: click a leg and it tells you what that stretch rests on (#317).** The itinerary panel could only ever be opened for a WHOLE route, so the three facts that belong to a *stretch* and to no stop row had nowhere to appear. A leg out of a provisional stop is itself provisional even when its destination is attested — and no row says so, because no row is about that line. The chord answers "why is this one leg so long", which is the question BibleWorks' own **Ruler** mode (bwh33) exists for. And an **aside** — Phoenix, which Acts 27:12 makes the entire *reason* the Fair Havens→Cauda leg exists; Syrtis, the sandbank they were afraid of — sits in the stop list where Luke put it, invisible to a reader who clicks the open sea between two harbours. Clicking a leg now haloes it, opens its route's itinerary (detail column at ≥1180 px, bottom sheet below), lights **both endpoint rows and only those** — by stop INDEX, not place id, because Lystra is stops 8 and 10 of the first journey — and prints a card carrying exactly those three, each with the verse chip that opens it. **What BibleWorks does here shaped the feature by contrast:** its map lines are *dumb drawing objects*, reachable only in **Edit mode**, where a click buys a curve-tension handle and nothing else; its only route from map back to scripture is a right-click on a **site**. Our routes store no coordinates at all — only gazetteer ids — so a leg has a verse behind it and a read-mode click on one can be honest in a way theirs cannot. **Declined, deliberately: the Ruler's travel-TIME estimate.** That needs a speed for a first-century sea passage that nothing we ship can source, and a precise unfounded number is the failure worse than a missing one; the chord is printed and named "in a straight line", and nothing else is inferred. **Selection had no free visual channel** — colour is route identity, dash is what the text claims about the manner, width is attested-and-selected — so it is drawn as a **neutral-ink halo repeating the leg's own dash**. A solid halo under a dotted provisional leg would have laid a continuous band of ink where the text supports nothing, which is the exact thing the fine-dot convention exists to prevent. Two hit-test rules, each of which otherwise yields a feature that "works" and is wrong: **places are tested before lines** (every marker sits on the ends of two legs, so a line-first test swallows the tap at every junction), and **the hit test takes the painter's lane offsets as an argument** rather than recomputing them, so it can never drift from where the ink actually is. **Verified on the deploy**, not assumed: Cauda→Malta at 1400 px shows the card with `by sea` / `874 km in a straight line` / Syrtis + Acts 27:17 + *Named, not reached* / *What puts Malta on the itinerary* + Acts 28:1, with rows **7 and 8 lit and the Syrtis row between them unlit**; the same card arrives as a sheet at 1100 px; and a zoomed capture confirms the halo stays **dashed**. 14 new tests (2,854 total), `flutter analyze` exit 0. **Conservative call, stated:** the leg card does **not** inline the verse text — there is no lightweight per-verse preview anywhere in `lib/` (`FetchVerses._loadAndParse` loads a whole edition plus its paragraph maps), so it keeps the reference chip that every stop row already uses. **Still blocked on the owner and not counted as open:** #278 (NASB licence), #292 (source), #293 (hosting), #296 (fresh crash report), #309 (unreachable site).

- **v1.6.102** — **12,099 Septuagint words answered a tap with a different word's lexicon entry, and the witness that found them was inside the file (#304, check 23).** A reader tapping the last word of Genesis 1:1 — 「γην」, *the earth*, in the verse that names it — was told the word means **"indeed, at least"**. The Septuagint half's Strong's numbers came from a lookup blind to accent, breathing and vowel length: fold **η→ε** and **ω→ο**, drop the breathings, and every wrong entry is what remains — γῆ G1093 tagged **G1065 γέ**, μή G3361 as **G3165 μέ**, ὡς G5613 as **G3739 ὅς**, εἷς G1520 as **G1519 εἰς**, νῶτος "the back" as **G3558 νότος "the south"**. The same lookup took the adjective for the adverb (καλός for καλῶς) and the verb for the noun (οἰκέω for οἰκουμένη). **Nothing caught it because every wrong number is a real number with a real entry** — check 22's lesson arriving a second time, in a different asset, by a different mechanism. **`docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md` had this asset filed under "Not checked yet" with a stated reason, and the reason was wrong:** *"the only edition in the repo with no same-language witness for the Old Testament"*. `assets/tagged/lxxwh/` is **two alignments in one file** — the Greek Old Testament and Westcott-Hort — produced by two different processes **in the same language**, so a word common to both is tagged twice and each half witnesses the other. The witness was never missing; it was behind the same directory name and so was never looked at as a separate thing. **Generalise it: before concluding an asset has no second witness, ask whether it *contains* one** — an asset spanning two corpora, two eras or two importers has an internal seam, and a seam is where two processes describe overlapping facts. **The rule is deliberately narrow and the refusals are the interesting part.** A number moves only where the New Testament half is unanimous **and** ≥3 witnesses **and** `assets/originals` (MorphGNT — other people, other source) names the same number **and** the run's own part of speech agrees. That last clause **holds 234 runs where both witnesses agree and are still wrong**: Leviticus's ἁφή "a plague-spot" is a noun **G860** spelled exactly like ἀφῇ from ἀφίημι, which the NT tags G863 unanimously. **ἰδού keeps G2400 across 1,018 runs** because the third witness never confirmed the NT's ὁράω G3708, and **εὐθύς keeps G2117** because the NT's G2112 is house style, not a correction. A second pass **filled 8,295 runs that answered nothing and did not have to** — `ειπεν` was tagged G3004 in 612 places and left **empty in 2,550 others**, same word, same edition, same parse — and takes λέγω G3004 rather than the G2036 another convention would use, because a fill states what *this edition already says*, never what the Greek means. **41,032 runs stay empty on purpose:** words the NT never uses (Σαλωμων, Μωαβ, Ιωαβ are spelled as the Septuagint spells them, not as Matthew does), so no number exists and inventing one would be a guess. **Under-attribution is recoverable; a wrong number is not, because it reads as a fact about the text.** **Three traps that each cost real time.** (1) **Key a witness on as little as will do the job** — keying on `(form, morphology)` looked more rigorous and silently lost the single largest repair, because the Septuagint half writes `V-AAI-3S` where WH writes `V-2AAI-3S`, so εἶπεν ×2,550 never matched itself; use the **part of speech only**, and assume two halves tagged by different processes have **different tagset labels even where the analysis agrees** (one calls the negative `ADV`, the other `PRT`). (2) **Always print what a guard *rejected*, ranked by volume.** The parse guard did protect ἁφή — and also refused the three **largest true repairs** (οὐκ 2,844, μή 2,263, ὡς 1,149) purely on that `ADV`/`PRT` naming difference. A guard reported only as a count is unaudited. (3) **Idempotence is a property of the pipeline, not of each pass.** The first version corrected and filled in one sweep from a witness pool built *before* any correction landed, so running it twice changed the file twice — μη looked ambiguous while its 2,263 Septuagint copies still read G3165, and only a second run filled them. Restructured to two full sweeps with a **re-survey between**, it reaches in one run the fixed point the old version needed two to find. **Also measured, and clean — a real negative result:** all **10 pairs** of the five H-number tagged layers. Per-verse Jaccard runs **70–92%**, which looks like a second corruption and is not: the discriminating measure is **containment, not overlap**, and across all ten pairs in both directions it never falls below **87%**, with the small layers **94–98% inside** the large ones (nsn-plus is 97.82% inside bsb while bsb is only 73.81% inside nsn-plus). **That asymmetry is the whole story — the smaller layers say *less* (they leave untranslated Hebrew function words alone), they do not say something *different*.** A truncation-class defect cannot hide there, because a layer saying H7 where another says H7225 collapses containment **both** ways. **0 defects in 5 layers. Jaccard answers "do these agree?", containment answers "which one is quieter?" — report both, or a coverage gap reads as a bug.** **Verification:** analyze exits **0**, **2251/2251 green**, and the repair was checked against `git show HEAD:` file by file — **0 New Testament runs touched** (that half is the witness and is already checked against `assets/originals`), **0 numbers cleared** so no run stopped answering, and verse text, run counts, morphology and implied lists **byte-identical**; all 4,672 `<vs:…>` markers still carry no number (check 21). Agreement with the unanimous half of the witness **95.77% → 99.36%**, unanswered Old Testament runs **10.34% → 8.61%**. `tools/fix_lxx_strongs.py` is idempotent — a second run writes the same md5 — and **`test/lxx_tagged_layer_test.dart` was proved to fail on the pre-repair asset in four of its seven cases** before being committed; the three that pass either way are the two asserting the repair changed *nothing* plus "every number resolves against the lexicon", which is exactly the check that proved nothing in check 22 and proves nothing here. **Keep it, never count it.** `assets/strongs/concordance.json` is built from `assets/originals/`, not from the tagged layers, so no concordance count moves. **Left for a human / next run:** the Septuagint's *verse text* has still never been checked for completeness (Nehemiah 10 is missing 15 of 39 verses in the tagged import, check 21, and that gap was never chased across the other books), and separating "no Strong's number exists" from "one exists and this edition does not know it" for the 41,032 needs an LXX-specific lexicon the repo does not have. Both are now named bullets under "Not checked yet".
- **v1.6.100** — **the Septuagint's own verse numbers were shipping as scripture (#304, check 21).** `assets/lxxwh.json` carried **4,687 markers of the form `(102:12)` in 4,541 verses** — one Greek Old Testament verse in seven. They are the edition's own chapter-and-verse for the words that follow, printed because the Greek Psalter numbers Psalm 103 as 102; rendered raw they read as text. **The tagged layer was worse**: the marker was glued onto the first word of the run, so **4,400 of them answered a lexicon tap with the entry for the word after them** — `(5:27)` in 1 Chronicles 6:1 returned G5207 υἱοί. Both layers now carry `<vs:REF>` in a run of its own with no Strong's and no grammar, typed as its own `ScriptureSpanKind` for the same reason `[雅伟]` is: a reference is not a footnote and not a supplied word. Detection was exact rather than heuristic — an unaccented Greek text writes its numerals as letters, so a strict scan and a loose scan for any parenthesised run both returned the same 4,687. Repaired by the idempotent `tools/fix_lxx_versification.py`. **This entry was written by the following iteration**: the run that shipped it hit the org usage limit after the release commit and before it could append here or commit `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md`.
- **v1.6.51** — **the gazetteer that shipped unread for months now answers "which places does this passage name", and the map is a lens over the centre pane (task #277).** `assets/bible_places.json` was in the repo, referenced by nothing — the first job was to establish what the feature *is*, and **bwh33 says something the ticket did not.** BibleWorks' map module is a full cartography suite (raster basemaps, stacked editable overlays, a polyline editor, a ruler with travel-time estimates) and this app will never compete with it; its ties to the *text* are exactly three, and **all three are manual, English-only, and leave the passage**: right-click an English word → "Lookup in BibleWorks Maps" (which requires the reader to already know the word is a place, i.e. the very knowledge a gazetteer supplies); right-click a site on the map → search the text; and **66 pre-made per-book site maps opened by hand from File | Open**. That third one is the closest match and its granularity is the **book** — Genesis' map is a map of the ancient world. **So the gap is not cartography, it is that nothing follows the verse you are on**; I corrected my own source comment mid-run once the help file showed the 66-map fact, rather than leave a claim that overstated the gap. **The base map's provenance was verified, not trusted.** `assets/bible_geo.json` was inherited from an earlier iteration killed by a usage limit, with no generator script — its "Natural Earth 50m" claim is exactly what a fabricated file would also claim, and a file of the wrong provenance here is a licensing incident, not a bug. Downloaded the three upstream layers and compared vertices at 3 dp: **coast 400/400, lakes 400/400, rivers 350/350 exact; land 3209/3220, with all 11 exceptions lying precisely on the crop box `[-15, 5, 82, 50]`** — i.e. crop corners, which is what cropping *does*. Public domain, correctly attributed, demonstrably not BibleWorks raster data. **The data needed repair before it could be indexed:** a global case-replace of the substring `eph` had run over the gazetteer at some point and damaged **eight** three-letter book codes carrying **69 references** between them, every one of which would otherwise be dropped or filed under the wrong book (`nah`→`nAlefh` and friends; the table is in the research notes). **The ticket's tab-strip worry was real but misdiagnosed.** It assumed an 11th tab kills labels and suggested grouping; reading `analysis_tabs.dart` showed the strip already wraps into balanced rows and the actual bug was that `showLabels` divided the available width by **all** items rather than by the items **on a row** — so a wrapping strip measured itself as though it did not wrap, and had been doing so since the strip started wrapping four tabs ago. Fixed by extracting the whole rule into a pure `analysisStripLayout(width, count)` returning `(perRow, showLabels)`, which is testable with no widget tree; the 12th tab therefore **cost nothing and gave the 11th its labels back**. No grouping needed. **The map is a transient lens, not a `WbCentreMode.map`** — centre modes are *persisted*, so a map mode would let the app reopen tomorrow onto a coastline instead of onto scripture, and it would have needed ~8 touch points against the lens's 3. It inherits v1.6.50's rule directly: the status bar names the mode **you can see**, so it reads "Map" while the lens is up and tapping it puts the lens down; and `_setCentreMode` now closes the lens **before** its unchanged-mode early return, or picking "Reader" while the map is up would appear to do nothing. **The second tier is what makes the tab worth opening:** most verses name no place at all, so a verse-only pane would be empty most of the time and read as broken — hence "Named in this verse" plus "Elsewhere in this chapter", with the verse's own places lifted out of the chapter list so nothing is listed twice. **Two bugs were found by writing the tests, not by using the app:** the scale bar was **invisible on first open**, because `_footer` and the map's `LayoutBuilder` are siblings in a Column and the footer builds first, so `_proj` was still null when the footer rendered and nothing rebuilt it afterwards (fixed with a post-frame `setState` on first fit, pinned by a test that opens the map and asserts a `km` readout **without touching anything**); and the dead-`_setCentreMode` case above. **Verification:** analyze exits **0**, **1637/1637 green** (+27, all against the REAL bundled gazetteer — the whole point of #277 is that the file shipped unloaded, so a fixture would test the wrong thing), and **screenshot-verified live on seeksparks-dev at v1.6.51**: the Analysis strip renders **two labelled rows of six** at a 426 px pane (the fix, visible); the Places tab on Jonah 1:2 lists **Nineveh** under "Named in this verse" and **Joppa**/**Tarshish** under "Elsewhere in this chapter" with the Eagle's View credit rendered beneath; tapping Joppa opens the lens over the centre pane with a **500 km** scale bar and the ruler reading **"Joppa → Nineveh 908 km · about 29 days on foot"** and **"Joppa → Tarshish 3778 km · about 119 days on foot"** (both within a percent of hand-computed haversine), the status field reads **"Map"**, and "Close map" returns to the reader with the pane intact; at a 356 px pane the strip degrades to **icon-only two rows of six** with no overflow. **Only assumed, not verified:** the Chinese surface — 约帕/本节地名 are pinned by widget test but were never screenshotted live. **Judgement call left open:** `_kMinLabelledTabWidth = 66` still ellipsises seven of the twelve labels ("Wor…", "Cont…", "Plac…"); that is pre-existing behaviour at 11 tabs, not a regression, and raising it would cost the labels entirely — left alone deliberately. **Traps for the next run:** CDP `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` is silently **dropped after `Input.dispatchMouseEvent`** on this build — `window.innerWidth` keeps reporting the emulated value while the page reflows to the real window and `Page.captureScreenshot` returns a 2× image, so every post-click screenshot lies about the layout it is showing; **resize the real window with `Browser.setWindowBounds` via the `/json/version` browser endpoint instead** (`/tmp/ss_win.js`) and take screenshots at native dpr 2, downscaling for reading. AppleScript window control times out (no automation permission). And a commit message containing an apostrophe inside a `$(cat <<'EOF')` heredoc breaks bash parsing — write the message to a file and use `git commit -F`.
- **v1.6.50** — **split view is a workbench MODE now, so two columns stop costing you the workbench (task #276).** The item was filed as a *styling* problem — “the reader is `Get.off(() => const HomePage())` at workbench_page.dart:299 and :854; render it in the centre pane, then restyle it to workbench_theme.dart:16 (square corners, hairlines, no shadows)”. **Read the code before believing the ticket:** line 832-835 was already `parallelMode && threePane ? _buildParallelFrame : BibleReadingPane`, so the centre pane was *already* the reader, already in workbench chrome — the radius-20/BoxShadow/floating-pill styling belongs to `HomePage`, a different route, and restyling it would have changed nothing anyone sees. What those two buttons actually did was **leave the route**, taking the command line, the search results and the Analysis pane with them; a reader who wanted two columns paid for them with everything else on screen, unwarned. **Two columns was the only capability HomePage had that the workbench lacked**, so it moves in: `WorkbenchProvider.parallelMode` (bool) becomes `centreMode` (`reader | browse | split`). **Three modes, not two, because BibleWorks keeps three and bwh11 says why** — “there is not always a one-to-one mapping between verses in different versions” (Hebrew psalm superscriptions carry a verse number English editions fold into a heading; the LXX numbers Jeremiah differently). Interleaving editions under one verse number is a quiet lie whenever they disagree; bwh38’s Parallel Versions Window uses **columns** so the reader’s eye does the aligning. **Split is not a narrower Browse — it is the mode for the case Browse cannot represent honestly.** The bool was **deleted, not kept as a derived getter**: with three modes `!parallelMode` no longer means “the chapter reader”, so every site still reading it that way would have been wrong *in split mode without failing to compile* — three compile errors beat one silent behavioural bug. **`kMinCentreWidthForSplit` is derived, not chosen**: `ResponsiveBreakpoints.minReadingPaneWidth * 2 + 16` = **976**, and a test asserts that arithmetic, because the split columns are literally the same `BibleReadingPane` at the same type size — inventing a smaller floor would assert a reading column is narrower inside the workbench than outside it. It measures the **centre pane, not the window** (the rails collapse to 44px), so “does not fit” is about the current *arrangement*; hence split does **not** require three panes, only Browse does — requiring it would make the control unreachable at exactly the moment collapsing a rail makes it usable. Consequently the View menu **greys Split with the reason “needs a wider centre”** (teaching the reader what to close) while the toolbar button **hides** (no room to explain itself) — the v1.6.17 lesson that offering a benefit and then withholding it is the failure mode. **Latent bug fixed in passing:** HomePage seeded the second column with `firstWhere(v.book == primary.currentBook, orElse: () => verses.first)`, and since v1.6.47 `Verse.book` carries the *edition’s* book name while `defaultSecondaryVersion` deliberately picks a different edition — often a different language — so the match failed and the second column **silently snapped to Genesis 1**, which is the *normal* case, not an edge one. Now matched through `bookNameToEnglish` on both sides with no `orElse`: no match leaves the column where it is. **Grep `firstWhere(..., orElse: () => list.first)` across two versions next run — every one is this bug.** The #274 warm-up learned the third mode too (split warms only its second column, not the three-edition stack) and shares `resolveSecondaryVersion` with the pane, or it would warm one Bible while the pane fetched another. `home_page.dart` **stays** — now capability-free but still the target of a dozen `pushPage` calls and the boot route; retiring it is a product decision about whether the app has one reading surface or two, recorded in its own header doc rather than folded into this slice. **The screenshot caught what 1574 green tests could not:** v1.6.49 shipped with the status bar reading **“Split” while a single fallback column was on screen** — the field named the stored preference, not `effectiveCentreMode`. The comment directly above it *claimed* the label could never lie, which was true of the tap *cycle* and irrelevant to a *persisted* preference meeting a narrower window, i.e. the only path a real reader takes. **A comment asserting an invariant is not the invariant, and it made the gap harder to see.** Chasing it found a second bug: `_nextCentreMode` called `order.indexOf(_wb.centreMode)` on a list that had already had non-fitting modes removed, so `indexOf` returned **-1**, `(-1+1) % len == 0`, and tapping restarted the cycle from the top instead of stepping off what was visible. Both fixed in v1.6.50; pinned by a **property** test rather than examples — for every mode × {0, 480, 975, 976, 1400} × {threePane, not}, `effectiveCentreMode` must be a **fixed point of itself**, which is the machine-checkable form of “the status bar can only name something the layout will actually draw”. **Verification:** analyze exits **0**, **1575/1575 green** (+25), and **screenshot-verified live on seeksparks-dev**: split renders two columns *inside* the workbench with the command line intact; `Joh 3` typed at the command line moved **both** columns; with the left column switched to CUVS the headers read **约翰福音 3** beside **John 3** with no snap to Genesis, which is the cross-language case that used to fail; at 1024 the View menu greys Split (centre = 668 < 976) and the status bar now reads **“Reader”** while `localStorage['flutter.workbench.centreMode.v1']` is still `"split"`; widening to 1440 brings both columns back without re-picking the mode. **Traps for the next run:** the columns icon in the centre-pane header is the *version-stack picker*, not split — the split control is in the **View menu**, and at 1440 with both rails open it is legitimately greyed, so a screenshot run must close a rail first; and the CDP driver needs `Input.insertText` + `Input.dispatchKeyEvent` steps to drive the command line (added to `/tmp/ss_shot.js`).
- **v1.6.48** — **the workbench’s first paint was gated on downloading Bibles it already had (task #274).** The item was filed as "panes render empty until an interaction forces a repaint… trailhead BibleReadingPane / `wb.analysisVerses`", and **both halves are wrong** — no interaction is needed (the content arrives on its own; the reporter simply clicked first), and the pane that greets a new reader is the *Browse stack*, because `workbench_page.dart:832-835` is `parallelMode && threePane ? _buildParallelFrame : BibleReadingPane` and `workbench.browseMode.v2` defaults to **true**. BibleReadingPane renders from `MainProvider.verses`, already resident; it was never the slow one. **The real shape, measured by CDP rather than inferred:** CPU is innocent (58 fps, no frame gap >150 ms, longest task 89 ms) and so is the boot corpus (settled ~1.4 s) — but then the app does *nothing* for ~2.8 s on the splash’s fixed 3 s timer, and only at **4190 ms**, with the pane already on screen and blank, does `BrowseWindow` begin fetching `genesis.json`/`bsb.json`/`kjv.json`/`hebrew.json`. **A symptom described as *rendering* was really *scheduling*** — which is exactly why 1427 tests passed with it live: a test awaits its futures, so it never experiences "not yet". **The architectural cause:** `BrowseWindow` and `ParallelVerseView` each kept a private `static Map<String, List<Verse>> _versionCache`, separate copies alongside `MainProvider`’s real LRU — so the reading version already in memory was re-downloaded and re-parsed in order to display it, and neither private map could be reclaimed by `dropCachesOnMemoryPressure()`. Both now read the one shared LRU; `preloadVersion` gained in-flight dedup (three call sites can now race for the same edition) and **lost** a guard that refused to load `currentVersion`, which was wrong precisely when boot had failed. **The fix** (`lib/services/workbench_warmup.dart`) spends the splash’s idle 3 s on the next screen — and the restraint is the point: it warms **only what the pane about to be shown will itself demand**, never speculatively, so `browseWarmupOrder` returns **empty** when `parallelMode == false`, honouring the v1.3.61 decision not to eager-preload over mobile data. The 3 s splash was deliberately left alone; shortening it is a product call belonging to #281. The planner is a pure function, so the *decision* is testable with no assets and no SharedPreferences — copy that pattern. Pref keys and the per-locale default stack moved into the service and `WorkbenchPage` now reads them from there, because if the two drift the warm-up loads editions the page never shows and the pane still starts cold; a test pins the literal key strings, since renaming either silently resets every reader’s Browse stack. **Verification:** analyze exits **0**, **1552/1552 green** (+11), and **screenshot-verified on seeksparks-dev at v1.6.48** — warm cache, saved stack bsb/nasb/kjv, reading NASB: `bsb.json` at **1559 ms** and `kjv.json` at **2025 ms** (was 4190 ms), `nasb.json` **not re-fetched at all**, with originals, Strong’s and tagged text all banked by ~3.4 s while still on the splash. The first workbench frame shows the new labelled state reading **"Preparing this chapter"** — *not* "Loading editions · BSB · KJV" — and that distinction is itself the proof, since the label names only editions still missing from the cache; a second run painted fully populated with no loading state at all. A profile with `browseMode.v2` set to false fetched **no** `bsb.json`/`kjv.json` whatsoever, confirming the conservative path live. **Not measured, and the remaining thread:** the exact duration of that "Preparing this chapter" state (per-row chapter assembly in `BrowseWindow._load()`, CPU not network) — under ~3 s in one run, absent in another. **Traps for the next run:** clicking the Browse/Reader toggle to set up a measurement is unreliable because splash length varies from 2.9 s to over 9.5 s on the same build, so a fixed wait lands on the splash and the click is swallowed — set the pref in localStorage before navigating instead (SharedPreferences on web is localStorage with a `flutter.` prefix, bools stored as the strings true/false); `Network.setCacheDisabled` re-downloads `main.dart.js` and shifts the whole timeline seconds to the right, so use the warm cache for regression comparison; ink-coverage of a screenshot cannot by itself distinguish the splash from a populated pane (both ~5 %), but the splash is *static* while content jitters ±0.3 % frame to frame; and `flutter analyze | tail` reports **tail’s** exit code, always 0.
- **v1.6.47** — **the book name must follow the reading version, not the UI locale (task #283).** The report was a Chinese UI on CUVS(简) whose KWIC list printed `Numbers 32:18`, `Deuteronomy …` ellipsised. **The cause was a missing INPUT, not a missing call.** Book names have TWO renderers — `localeAwareBookName` (formal) and `shortBookName` (3-letter abbreviation) — and only the formal one had ever been given the version, so the abbreviation followed the UI. That produced the symmetric defect nobody reported: a Chinese UI reading BSB got `民` printed beside a verse that reads "Numbers". Both now go through one rule, `bookScriptFor(locale, version)` in `book_name_mapping.dart`: a version, if present, decides (`kjv/leb/nasb/bsb/kjvs/lxxwh` → English, `-tr` → Traditional, else Simplified) and the locale is only the fallback for surfaces holding no version. **The trap that would have flipped the app:** `toLocale('Numbers','')` has always returned `民数记`, not `Numbers`, because several callers pass `mp.currentVersion` before it loads; expressing it as `bookScriptFor('', v)` quietly made an unloaded version mean English and would have changed every book name during startup — it is pinned by a test now. **Why the KWIC column takes the ABBREVIATION and the clipboard does not:** localising alone would not have fixed the reported truncation, since `帖撒罗尼迦前书 5:23` is *wider* in the 84 px column than the English it replaced; BibleWorks bwh42 documents that it abbreviates its own reference columns for exactly this reason, while a pasted reference leaves the app and keeps the formal name. The English-keyed corpora are the ones at risk — `concordance.json` is keyed `Numbers 32:18` and `ConcordanceRef.label` must STAY canonical English because `tryParse` reads it back for navigation, so the new `ConcordanceRef.display(locale, version, {short})` renders without touching the data. **The audit's own near miss, worth inheriting:** a subagent sweep flagged ~20 sites of the form `'${v.book} ${v.chapter}'` as bugs and missed `kwic_pane.dart` — the exact surface in the screenshot. `Verse.book` arrives from the version file ALREADY in that version's script (`book_name_mapping.dart:247` says why: the reading pane filters by book NAME), so the mass edit would have double-translated every one of them. Rule: **check whether a string is English-keyed data or version-loaded text before localising it.** Fixed for real: kwic_pane (list + clipboard), word_forms_section, and `version` threaded into word_analysis/morph_search/related_verses/phrase_match panes plus copy_center, dashboard, the reading pane's floating header and bible_trivia. **Deliberately NOT changed:** `stats_page.dart:2290` prints a raw English reference only as the fallback for a missing `ui_strings` key — localising it would hide the missing key. **Verification:** analyze exits **0**, **1541/1541 green**, 21 new tests in `test/book_script_test.dart` including "no Latin letters survive under a Chinese version" and a corpus test that all 66 books abbreviate to ≤3 characters in all three scripts (which is what keeps the last-character fallback unreachable). **And the browser screenshot path from v1.6.44/46 is UNBLOCKED** — a ~90-line CDP driver on Node 24's global `WebSocket` (no installs) drives the deployed dev build; two traps: filter `/json/list` by URL because Chrome's update tab steals index 0, and sleep ~1.8 s before `Page.captureScreenshot` or you photograph a stale Flutter frame. **Screenshot-verified on seeksparks-dev at v1.6.47, both directions, same word G3956 and same tab:** CUV+S(简) → `太 1:17 / 太 2:3 / 太 2:16`, BSB → `Mat 1:17 / Mat 2:3 / Mat 2:4`. **Observed but not acted on:** the workbench centre pane still shows a spinner for several seconds on first paint — that is queue item #274, untouched.
- **v1.6.46** — **the divine name was being printed as a translator's insertion (task #282).** The report was cosmetic — `主 [雅伟]` on some KWIC lines, `主 [ 雅伟]` with a stray gap on others — and the spacing turned out to be the least of it. **Start with what the bracket MEANS, counted rather than guessed:** every square bracket in `cuvs-yhwh.json` is one of exactly two tokens, `[雅伟]` × 212 and `[基督]` × 17 (`cuvs-yhwh-tr` mirrors it with `[雅偉]`), so the convention is closed and enumerable. Reading the contexts settles what it is: `X[雅伟]` is a **referent gloss** — the word X printed here refers to Yahweh — marking κύριος where an OT quotation had YHWH, and in a handful of OT verses marking a PRONOUN (`我[雅伟]`, `他[雅伟]`) whose antecedent is the Name. `[基督]` is the identical device pointing at the Messiah, and **Matthew 22:44 carries both four characters apart**, which is why they cannot be told apart by styling one as an insertion. **The direction is the whole point:** a supplied word means the target has MORE than the source (`darkness [was] over`), a gloss means the source had something the target LOST. `scripture_markup.dart` had ONE bracket rule — `_suppliedRe` → `supplied` → italic — so the app set 雅伟 in the convention that has meant "the translator added this" since the Geneva Bible, i.e. the reverse of the claim the edition exists to make. **Three consequences, one root.** (1) Semantic, as above. (2) **The clipboard, the one with effects outside the app:** `copyVerseText` resolved the bracket under `keepSuppliedBrackets`, an option that asks "mark the translator's insertions?" — so a reader answering *no* pasted Matthew 22:44 as `主雅伟对我主基督说`, **a divine title Matthew never wrote**, with nothing on screen to show it was never there. Those brackets are now unconditional. The same flattening lived in **five copy-pasted result-list preview pipelines** (search_page ×2, command_pane, originals_sheet, strongs_entry_page) — now one `versePreviewText`, which is precisely why all five carried the same bug. (3) **The tagged assets, and the worst of the three:** `tools/import_yahweh_modules.py` walked the text word by word and split the gloss across runs in **193 verses** — `{"w":"有主 [","s":"G2962"}, {"w":"雅伟] 的使者","s":"G32"}` — so the closing half was TAGGED, and **hovering the divine name in Matthew 2:13 answered "angel"** (G32 = ἄγγελος), a wrong lexicon answer nobody had reported. KWIC joins runs with a space, so the keyword column for G2962 held an orphan `主 [` while `雅伟]` opened the right context: **that** is the `主 [ 雅伟]` in the screenshot, and the un-split cases (the importer also spaced the bracket off from the Chinese, which the base JSON does not) explain the other form. **One cause, both symptoms.** `TaggedTextService.reuniteGlossRuns` puts the gloss back on the run that opened it, once at load, so KWIC / Browse / hover all get repaired runs without knowing. **The near miss worth inheriting: bracket SHAPE is not a safe key.** Measured across all six tagged sets — bsb 18,744 bracketed runs 0 split, cuvs-yhwh 193 splits, lxxwh 184, nsn-plus 42 — and the LXX/WH and NASB splits are **legitimate**: `[το αυτο]`, `[ And Philip said … of God."]` are textual-critical brackets around disputed *words and whole verses* where every word inside is a real word with a real number. Reuniting those would be vandalism, so the repair is keyed to the two tokens and a test asserts bsb/kjvs/lxxwh/cuvs-plus/nsn-plus come out unchanged. Transformation was prototyped in Python against the real assets before any Dart: 198 verses changed, zero runs dropped. **A test earned its keep by being wrong:** the first asset assertion was "the run carrying `[雅伟]` must be tagged G2962" and the data refuted it in twelve places — δεσπότης G1203 at Jude 1:4 / Luke 2:29 / Rev 6:10, θεός G2316 at Acts 16:32 where the manuscripts read θεοῦ, κατέναντι G2713 at Romans 4:17 where a preposition phrase swallowed the 主 — all correct, because **the gloss attaches to whatever word precedes it, not to κύριος**. The shipped invariant is structural instead: *no run may OPEN with a gloss*, because then its number belongs to a word printed after it. **Rendering:** brackets kept, body upright at full text colour, brackets muted — kept because unbracketed `主雅伟` misquotes the verse and weight alone cannot carry that boundary at workbench sizes in CJK; upright because italic is the insertion convention; and the two kinds print **identically** because the edition sets them identically and giving the Name extra weight *here* would make the 212 glossed occurrences louder than the thousands where 雅伟 simply stands in the text unbracketed. **Deliberately NOT done, and both are judgement calls a human may overturn:** `主*` (Matthew 9:28, Luke 24:34, plus traditional mirrors) is left verbatim — both are κύριος addressed to Jesus so it is *probably* `主[基督]` with the gloss dropped, but that is an inference from two data points with no edition key available, and inventing a rendering for an unknown editorial mark is the class of guess this project must not make about scripture; note the brief cited Matthew 7:22 / 8:2 / 8:6 for this form and **those three verses contain no asterisk in any bundled version**. And the **classic reader was already innocent** — `build_verse_content_spans.dart:285` has since 2026-06-30 rendered the body in the theme accent with a thin underline, notation rather than insertion — so it was left alone; it drops the brackets the workbench now keeps, a one-character-pair divergence that is a product decision, not a defect. **Verification, honestly separated:** analyze exits **0**, **1520/1520 green**, the new suite has three asset-level tests that run against the shipped JSON (the bug survived 1,475 green tests because every one used strings a human wrote and the defect was in a file an importer wrote), and a "premise" test asserts the raw asset really does still split the gloss so the repair cannot quietly become a no-op. Deployed from a clean worktree at the pushed SHA and **string-probed on the immutable deploy URL**: `YHVH`, a token introduced by this commit and present nowhere at `70bd2ae`, appears exactly once in the served `main.dart.js`. **NOT verified: no browser screenshot.** The headless-Chrome path is still blocked by the loading page (see v1.6.44); what stands in for it is a widget test that pumps the real render path and asserts the painted plain text is `有主[雅伟]的使者` with no italic span and muted brackets — a render-contract check, not a picture.

- **v1.6.44** — **the iPad command-line wipe (task #273), fixed as a defect class rather than as three lines.** Tapping an operator chip on iPad put the token in, and then the reader's *next keystroke emptied the field*. **The name of the bug hides the mechanism: `controller.text = x` is not a plain setter.** Flutter implements it as `value = value.copyWith(text: …, selection: TextSelection.collapsed(offset: -1), composing: TextRange.empty)` — so the two-statement idiom (`.text =` then `.selection =`) publishes **two** `TextEditingValue`s, and **the first one carries a selection that describes no position in the text**. On desktop and web the second lands before anything observes the first, which is exactly why this was invisible here and why **1400+ green tests said nothing**. iOS drives text input *asynchronously*: the engine can latch the caret-less state and the platform's echo can arrive *after* the framework has already set the real caret, leaving the field holding a selection that does not describe its text — and the next keystroke is applied against that range, which is why the symptom is "everything disappears" rather than "the caret is in the wrong place". One assignment leaves no window for the two to disagree. **`lib/utils/atomic_text_edit.dart` → `setTextAtomic(text, {caret})`, with two deliberate properties, not incidental ones:** the caret is **clamped into range**, because *an out-of-range selection being unrepresentable* is the invariant the helper exists to hold; and `composing` is **cleared**, because a composing range is measured against the *old* text and can point past the end of the replacement — which matters more here than in a generic app, since `bwh17b_CommandLineFarEast.htm` shows BibleWorks built its command line an entire dedicated three-mode IME (toggled with `<insert>`, a coloured flashing box showing the mode), i.e. **a Bible workbench's command line is an IME surface as a matter of course**, and this app's readers type Chinese into it. **The brief named three sites; the class had eight.** Five more were reachable on iPad and none were in the report: the **recent-search tap on the search page** (the same text-then-selection race), and four that assign `.text` with *no second statement at all* and so simply publish `-1` and leave it — the **settings paste-key field**, the **Gemini key card** (which can fire *while the field is focused*, from a cloud sync), the **feedback prefill**, and the **`+Verse` fallback branch** in the reading pane, whose *other* branch was already atomic. **Verification, honestly separated:** analyze exits **0** (checked by exit code — the first run failed on an info-level `unnecessary_import` that a grep for "error" would have shipped), **1475/1475 green**, and the new tests are proven to *bite*: reverting `_insertToken` to the old two-step fails them with `Expected: <1> Actual: <2>`. The mechanical grip is to **attach a listener and assert on the published value sequence** — exactly one publication per interaction, never a `baseOffset < 0` — since asserting on the final text alone cannot see this bug at all. A "premise" test pins the Flutter behaviour the fix depends on (`c.text = x` really does leave `selection.baseOffset == -1`), so if that ever changes, the file says so instead of quietly becoming cargo cult. **NOT verified, and this must not be read as fixed on device: there is no iPad here, and the symptom cannot be reproduced on any other platform by construction** — what is confirmed is that the cause is gone. **Dead end worth inheriting:** `debugDefaultTargetPlatformOverride = TargetPlatform.iOS` does *not* help, because `flutter_test`'s fake text input applies editing state synchronously, so the race that *defines* the bug cannot exist in the harness. **Also not verified:** headless Chrome renders the deployed v1.6.44 splash but never reaches the workbench — the loading page arms its auto-advance only once verse data arrives, and `--virtual-time-budget` starves those fetches — so the dev deploy is confirmed live and running Dart, but no rendered workbench screenshot was obtained.
- **v1.6.43** — **the Context tab: what this passage is made of.** BibleWorks `bwh10h` lists the words of the pericope, chapter and book around the focused verse, "to identify likely themes". **A naive reading — "a word list with a scope selector" — misses the three things that make it worth building, and a published review names the failure directly: in BibleWorks itself you "have to scroll past insignificant high frequency words like articles, pronouns, and prepositions".** (a) **The unit is the PERICOPE**, and `assets/section_titles.json` had only ever been read as a heading to print above a verse — `lib/utils/pericope.dart` reads it as *ranges*, which is the one scope the already-shipped Word List Manager (`bwh26`) structurally cannot express, and which is why this is not that feature again. A pericope crosses chapter boundaries (so the caller must supply chapter lengths) and a verse can precede the book's first heading; both are handled, not assumed away. (b) **Function words are filtered by default**, which requires a part of speech — and `assets/originals/` carries **two incompatible tagging schemes**, Greek CCAT (`N-----DSF-`) and Hebrew/Aramaic OSHB (`HR/Ncfsa`), **in which `R` and `P` mean OPPOSITE things** (Greek `P` = preposition, Semitic `P` = pronoun). `lib/utils/word_pos.dart` picks the scheme off the Strong's *prefix*, never the code, and resolves the Semitic head morpheme, which sits at **neither a fixed start nor a fixed end**: `HTd/Ncmpa` (Hebrew article prefix) heads *second*, `ANcmsd/Td` (Aramaic determined state) heads *first*, and `HR/Ncmsc/Sp3ms` heads in the middle — so "take the first segment" and "take the last segment" are both wrong and both pass a hand-written test. The bare adjective tag `Aamsa` must **not** be read as Aramaic, so the language marker is stripped only when char[1] is uppercase. (c) **The default ordering is keyness (log-likelihood G²), not raw count**, measured against **the containing book MINUS the scope** rather than the whole Bible — authorial vocabulary varies so much between books that scoring John 1 against Leviticus mostly rediscovers that John wrote John, and excluding the scope from its own baseline stops a locally-exclusive word inflating its own expected value. **The payoff, asserted end-to-end against the real assets rather than mocks:** John 1:1–18 goes from a list headed `ὁ ×34, καί ×16, αὐτοῦ ×15` to one headed `χάρις, γίνομαι, φῶς, θεός, μονογενής, σκηνόω` — the prologue's own vocabulary — and Genesis 1 to `אֱלֹהִים, רָקִיעַ, מִין, אוֹר, בדל`. **The eleventh tab exposed a real layout defect and the test suite caught it:** eleven bare icons no longer fit across a 320 px Analysis pane and `AnalysisTabStrip` answered by *clipping* them; it now wraps to a **balanced** second row (6+5, short row padded so every tab keeps the same width) rather than scrolling, because a tab hidden behind a scroll affordance on a 320 px pane is a tab nobody finds — and the selected tab is now the *last* one, which is exactly the case scrolling would have hidden. The two strip tests that hard-coded `width: 700` now **derive** the threshold from `AnalysisTab.values.length`, so the next tab does not break them. **Conservative calls, all reported:** whole-Bible keyness was **not** attempted — it needs either 66 book loads or licensed NT-only statistics — so the book scope honestly reports *no baseline* and shows no keyness at all rather than a number that means nothing; `sectionTitleSetByVersion` was **not** extended to cover `bsb`/`kjvs`/`lxxwh`/`cuvs-plus` (that would change reading-pane headings for five versions, well outside this feature), so the Context tab uses a **local** locale fallback, which is safe only because all three heading sets were verified to sit on identical verse boundaries (1443 headings each, same keys) — the choice changes the title text, never the range. **Known limitation, not a bug:** on a very small scope against a large baseline (Psalm 23) keyness degenerates toward pure rarity, which is the statistic behaving correctly. **Verification, honestly stated:** analyze clean and **1466/1466 green**, including two widget tests that render the *real* `ContextPane` against the *real* asset bundle at both ends of the Analysis pane's width (320 and 560 px) and assert no overflow, the pericope label `1:1-18 · The Word Became Flesh`, the counts `18 verses · 252 words · 83 distinct`, the presence of χάρις and μονογενής **and the absence of ὁ** — the whole thesis of the feature, checked on real data. **A real browser drive was NOT done: `flutter devices` exposes no Chrome/desktop device in this unattended launchd session and there is no click-automation binary, so "looks right when clicked" is asserted from rendered-widget tests, not observed.** There is also **no golden/font infrastructure** in this repo and no bundled Greek or Hebrew font, so a pixel golden would have rendered the very text that matters as boxes — which is why the check above asserts on rendered *text*, not pixels. **Ship bookkeeping, and the rule kept this time:** the tree was shared — v1.6.42 plus two Eagle's View data commits landed under this run — so paths were staged **by name, never `git add -A`**, every diff was checked for deletions that would have reverted the other writer, and the dev build ran in a **detached worktree** at the pushed SHA (`7e58aab`), with the version bump then re-applied to the shared tree **by editing, never by `cp`**. **Pre-existing data defect noticed, deliberately not fixed:** the Strong's gloss for `φῶς` renders as "compare G5316 (φαίνω), G5346 (φημί))" — a truncated lexicon entry in the bundled data, visible in any pane that prints glosses, not caused by this change.

- **v1.6.40** — **the Copy Center: what leaves the app, and under whose licence.** BibleWorks `bwh28` (Copying Results) + `bwh29` (Output Format Options). **A naive reading of "add a copy button" misses four things, and all four are why this is a dialog:** (a) a reference list with **no text** is a *separate artifact* — what a footnote or index is made of — not a degenerate case of copying text, and `bwh29` gives it its own separator set; (b) merging consecutive verses is real logic, not string joining — `Gen 1:1–2; 2:1` has to know where a chapter and a book end; (c) the separators are **configurable because the world is not the USA** (`Gen 1,1` is how a German reader writes it, so hard-coding `:` is a parochialism, not a default); (d) multi-version copy has **two shapes** — grouped by verse, or by version — and neither is a superset of the other. **Two things BibleWorks does not need but we do.** The apparatus (`[supplied]` words, `<note:>` footnotes) has to be a *choice*: brackets are damage in a handout and a scholarly claim in a paper. And copying puts publisher text on someone else's page, which is **the** moment the licence has to travel with it — so the rights line is reused **verbatim from the About page** via `attributionKeyFor`, not redrafted at the clipboard, meaning what ships is wording already reviewed, in the reader's own locale. **That obligation is also the feature's ceiling, and this is the part worth keeping:** the Lockman Foundation's published quotation provision for the NASB — the permission the About page *claims* — is **500 verses**, and every other permission held here is narrower ("non-commercial study only"). A Copy Center with no limit is a bulk text exporter with extra steps, so a copy including any licensed edition stops at 500 and the dialog says it was **the licence, not the app**, that stopped it; public-domain editions (`kjv`, `bsb`, and the three Eagle's View rows, whose *text* is public domain even though the electronic edition is not) are uncapped. **Architecture: a pure core** — `lib/utils/copy_format.dart`, no Flutter and no IO, with book names, verse text and rights all arriving as injected callbacks, so the whole engine is testable without a widget or an asset (**44 tests**, including `bwh29`'s own worked example verbatim). `ffThreshold` defaults to **0 = never abbreviate**: the app knows the exact verse list, `1:1-4` is unambiguous and `1:1ff` is not, and both SBL and Chicago discourage it — the setting exists because `bwh29` has it, the default disagrees with BibleWorks on purpose. **Entry points:** File ▸ Copy…, a toolbar button, and **Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+C — Shift deliberately**, because plain Ctrl+C is the browser's own copy and is the right tool for a selection the reader already made with the mouse; taking that key would break it page-wide for one dialog. **The narrow-layout widget test caught a real crash** the desktop test could not: `_options()` returned a `ListView` that the ≤900 px branch nested inside another `ListView`, i.e. an unbounded-height viewport — now a `Column`, scrolled by its parent. **Deliberately NOT touched:** the mobile reader's three-mode quick copy (`AppSettings.copyFormat`, devotional/withRef/plain). Those modes were tuned by explicit user feedback ("灵修模式不是一节一行而是全部都一起的") and rewriting them on this engine would change output nobody asked to change — **but that path has no rights line at all today, which is the single highest-value follow-up here.** Conservative calls, all reported: plain text only (no `text/html` clipboard flavour — it needs JS interop around the crash-hardened `ClipboardHelper`, which exists precisely because iOS Safari rejects the async API); curly `“ ”` hard-coded (wrong for zh-Hant, which wants 「」 — a real imperfection, smaller than a quote-character setting nobody would find). **Ship bookkeeping — I broke my own rule and it is worth recording:** `tools/release_web.sh` was run **in the shared working tree**, and the other writer's `assets/ot_synopsis.json` was written to disk *mid-build* (18:49, ~2 min after the build began) and was swept into the bundle, so the v1.6.40 dev deploy is **a mix of my `2b4b02e` and their in-flight OT-synopsis work**. Benign in outcome — that asset is reference ranges plus group titles, no verse text, committed by the owner in `00bce47` carrying its own "Used by permission" attribution, and they shipped it themselves minutes later — but the lesson stands: build from a detached worktree. They also **swept my v1.6.40 `pubspec.yaml` bump into their `d4e5aa4`**, so there was nothing left for me to commit; the `1.6.41` sitting in the tree at the end of this run is **theirs, in flight — do not commit it.** **Verification, honestly stated:** analyze clean and **1429/1429 green** (44 pure-core + 10 licence/localisation + 7 widget tests that tap through the real dialog at 1280 and 760 px and in zh-Hans); the release web build compiled clean under dart2js; and the deployed bundle was confirmed to contain the feature by string-probing `main.dart.js` on the immutable deploy URL (`Copy Center`, `Sample output`, `publisher quotation limit`, and the Chinese strings as `\u`-escapes). **A real browser drive was NOT done — launching Chrome was denied in this unattended session — so "works when clicked in a browser" is asserted from widget tests, not observed.**
- **v1.6.38** — **the Forms readout: how certain is the parse on screen?** BibleWorks `bwh10q` describes two lookups running in opposite directions, and **the one the name suggests is the less interesting one.** Top section = a paradigm (λόγος → λόγον 130×, λόγος 63×, λόγῳ 45×) — useful, and roughly what you would guess. Bottom section = a claim about **certainty**, and that is the reason to build it: the Analysis pane prints ONE parsing in a box with nothing qualifying it, so a reader had no way to know that the אָדָם in front of them is tagged H120 "man" in 18 places and H121 "Adam" in 2 and that **the tagger picked one**. Guessing this feature from its name would have shipped a list of inflections and missed the point entirely. **The corpus number that drove every decision: 4,756 of 130,950 surface forms are ambiguous — 3.6%.** So (a) the form→parses index stores *only* those 4,756, and a miss decodes to `FormAmbiguity.unambiguous` rather than to an error or an empty state — absence **is** the answer, and 130,950 keys collapse to 319 KB; (b) the ambiguity block renders only when ambiguous, because a badge reading "unambiguous" on 96.4% of words trains the reader to stop seeing the block that matters. **Placement — a section under the parsing box, not an eleventh tab — for a reason worth keeping:** at the Analysis pane's 288 px minimum a `_TabButton` is 17 px icon + 4 px inner + 2 px outer ≈ 29 px, so the existing **ten tabs already come to ~290 px and overflow**; and more decisively, half of `bwh10q` is a *qualifier on the parsing line*, and a qualifier one tab away from the claim it qualifies will not be read. Note for the next reader of the tab strip: `AnalysisTab.morphology` is *labelled* "Forms" but renders `MorphSearchPane` (the GSE) — the label is misapplied and this feature is not that pane. **Sizing was measured, not estimated:** refs-per-triple cost 0→5.62 MB, 1→7.63, 2→8.27, 3→8.61; interning the 66 book slugs to integer indices (`"39 1:1"`) recovered ~1.1 MB; final **7.50 MB over 151 shards, 49.6 KB mean**, sharded by Strong's *hundred* because the caller holds exactly one number at a time — the word under the cursor — and in running text the next word is usually a neighbour, so one small fetch serves many hovers. `count` is the honest total and is never capped; only the three example refs are. **Trap that wasted real time: `slugToBook` maps *abbreviations* (`gen`, `1co`, `3jn`), NOT the full slugs (`1_corinthians`) that name the files under `assets/originals/`** — a crude regex "proved" 18 books were unmapped when none were. Fixed by inverting the canonical names through the same slug rule rather than adding a second 66-name table that could drift, with a test asserting all 66 filenames resolve. **A correction to the research notes, and it is the exact trap the brief warns about:** they claim "bwh36 RWP = Robertson's Word Pictures"; the real `<title>` of `bwh36_RWP.htm` is **"TSK and Nave's"**.
