The claim was inferred from the *filename*. **Ship bookkeeping, third confirmation of the same lesson:** the shared working tree was red (1 failure, `nasb-ev should be Simplified`) purely from the other writer's uncommitted Eagle's View rows — a detached worktree at the same commit gave **1357/1357 and a clean analyze**; report the worktree numbers. `pubspec.yaml` could not be staged by hunk (the `assets/forms/` addition shares a hunk with the local-only `- assets/tagged/nsn-plus/` line), so the blob was **constructed explicitly** — `git show HEAD:pubspec.yaml`, apply only the intended addition, `git hash-object -w`, `git update-index --cacheinfo` — and then verified with `git show :pubspec.yaml`, i.e. against the *staged blob*, not against a diff. **Two collisions with the concurrent writer, both instructive:** it pushed my commits for me (a `git push` from the shared repo carries whatever is committed there), and it bumped the version while my build was running, so my first dev deploy went out labelled v1.6.37 from a commit that was *not* the v1.6.37 release commit — corrected by re-deploying from the true tip as **v1.6.38**, verified live (`version.json` = 1.6.38, `assets/forms/index.json` = 200, 438,821 tokens). Also **do not `cp` a file into the shared tree without diffing it first**: doing so silently reverted a file the other writer had just committed. It separately discarded the local-only Eagle's View rows from the working tree; they were never committed, and `git show HEAD:lib/constants/bible_versions.dart | grep -c nasb-ev` = 0 at every point in this run.
- **v1.6.34** — **Phrasing: the structure of the sentence made visible, without a canvas.** SeekSparks' answer to the Diagramming Module (bwh25), now reachable from Tools. **Start here: the model, store and page (1,405 lines) were written by the PREVIOUS iteration and left UNTRACKED when its run died on the org usage limit — `run.log` shows eight consecutive `exit 1` iterations after 16:12.** This run finished them rather than starting something new. The lesson generalises and costs nothing to apply: **run `git status` for untracked `lib/` files before picking a backlog item**, because a cut-off run leaves a complete feature on disk that no commit, no HANDOFF line and no research note mentions, and the next run's instinct is to start a fresh item beside it. **Why an indented text structure and not bwh25's vector canvas**, verified against the help topic rather than inferred: bwh25 is a CAD surface (~60 Reed-Kellogg symbols, drag-to-connect, snap-to-grid, page setup), and its own overview says what shipped with it is "a fully diagrammed Greek New Testament (by Randy Leedy)" — **the half readers actually valued is licensed content we must not copy, so building the editor ships the half nobody praised**, while the editor is the half every review of Logos' equivalent calls clunky. It is also the wrong shape for *this* app specifically: the workbench is gated to tablets where there is no precise pointer, and half the corpus is RTL, which a symbol library must mirror wholesale — indentation is a flow direction, not a symbol, so it is RTL-safe for free. Three things worth carrying forward. **(1) The joints are proposed from the morphology already bundled**, which neither BibleWorks nor Biblearc does. Four levels, each a strict superset of the last, so changing level mid-study can only ever *add* a line — that monotonicity is what makes the level safe to touch. Verse starts break at *every* level because only 37.3% of tagged Greek verses begin at a clause joint, so omitting them leaves two thirds of verses running into the line above with nothing on screen distinguishing "the versification cuts this sentence" from "the tool is broken". **(2) Two invariants make the pane trustworthy, and both are counter-intuitive.** Breaks are stored as a *diff against the proposal* (`added`/`removed`), not as a line list, so a manual break survives a level change and a deleted break stays deleted when a finer level re-proposes it. Depths are stored *raw* and clamped to `previous + 1` only at layout, so outdenting a parent pulls its children up to follow without consuming the depths they chose — put the parent back and they spring back. Storing the clamped value would have flattened them permanently with no way for the reader to know a gesture aimed at one line had eaten another. **(3) A Semitic word is a stack and which morpheme you ask matters:** the conjunction and preposition are PREFIXES (first morpheme), the participle/infinitive is the HEAD — of 7,489 infinitives only 837 stand first, so a first-morpheme test misses 89% of them. **Trap that cost seven red tests: Greek morph codes are exactly ten characters.** `_isGreek` tests `c.length == 10`, so a hand-written `'C-------'` does not fail loudly — it falls through and parses as *Semitic garbage*, matching nothing. Every fixture in `phrasing_test.dart` is now attested in `assets/originals/*.json` with its corpus count in the comment. **Ship bookkeeping, twice-confirmed now:** the working tree was red (1 failure, `nasb-ev should be Simplified`) purely from the other writer's *uncommitted* Eagle's View rows; a detached worktree at the same commit gave **1325/1325 and a clean analyze**. Report the worktree numbers. The deploy was likewise made from that worktree, and the result was *verified* rather than assumed — `assets/nasb-ev.json` and `assets/nsn-plus.json` on dev return the 20 KB SPA fallback as `text/html`, while `bsb.json` returns 6.1 MB of `application/json`. Minor pre-existing quirk noticed but deliberately not fixed mid-run: `bump_version.sh` still greps for `const String kAppVersion = String.fromEnvironment(`, which `app_version.dart` no longer matches, so **only `kAppReleaseTime` gets stamped** and `kAppVersion`'s fallback sits at a stale `'1.3.113'`. Harmless today because every build passes `--dart-define=APP_VERSION` and `version.json` comes from pubspec, but it is a live trap for any build that forgets the define.
- **v1.6.33** — the version gutter sized to the longest label that exists, and one distinct opaque colour per version. *(Shipped and deployed by the preceding run; recorded here by the next one.)*
- **v1.6.32** — **the same original word, lit in every translation at once.** The word under study now draws every *other* printed occurrence of its lexical Strong's number in a green fill, across the Chinese line, the English line and the originals line simultaneously — so the reader can see that 万物 / "All things" / πάντα are one word, which is the whole reason a parallel view exists. **BibleWorks has no such feature, and the help proves the divergence is deliberate rather than an oversight on my part.** `bwh11_BrowseWindow` lists every highlight the Browse window can draw — Toggle Difference Highlighting (same-language versions only), Clear Hit Highlighting, manual phrase selection, the Diagrammer echo — and *none* of them is lexical. BibleWorks answers this question in a **pane**, not in the text: `bwh10_AnalysisWindow` says "The Use Tab shows a list of all occurrences of a word in the local context as the mouse is moved over words in the Browse Window." A list tells you *how many*; colour in the text tells you **which word over there is this word**, which is the only form of the answer that survives four parallel rows. Decisively, `bwh10l_AWUse` states "The Use Tab is currently not supported for double byte languages like Chinese" — the one reader this app exists for is the reader BibleWorks explicitly could not serve here. Four things worth carrying forward. **(1) The lit set is derived from the pane's subject, not re-decided.** `siblingStrongs()` takes the single value `_analysisWord` already resolves ("the pinned word if there is one, otherwise the last one hovered"), so the green words and the Analysis pane are *incapable* of disagreeing — there is one answer and both read it — and the echoes therefore follow the pin for free, staying lit while the reader walks the pointer off the text to read the pane. That is the entire point: a highlight that died on mouse-out would reintroduce the exact defect v1.6.28 fixed. **(2) The chapter gate needs its trailing separator.** A pin outlives navigation, so the lit set is gated on the `book|chapter` printed *now*; but `John|1` is a prefix of `John|11`, so testing the bare prefix leaks chapter 1's pin into chapters 10–19 — lighting words with no pinned word visible anywhere to explain the colour. Test it against `John|11`, not just against another book. **(3) Hue alone is not a third state.** `siblingBg` green and `selectionBg` blue measure **1.04 / 1.05 / 1.08:1** in luminance across the three palettes — for a red-green colour-blind reader those two fills are the same fill, which would have collapsed hover and echo into one. The fix is a second, non-colour channel: `wordMarkUnderline()` underlines only the reader's OWN subject (hover, pinned) and never an echo, so the three states are green-fill / blue-fill-plus-underline / blue-fill-plus-underline-plus-gold-border. It is a free function rather than three lines in the widget purely so it can be tested, since the widget cannot be. **(4) Threading the raw pointer position would have flickered, and only reasoning caught it** — no test and no screenshot would have. The Browse window threads the *latched* subject down as `hoverKey` and each word overrides it with `withHover()` only while the pointer is physically inside that word. Thread the live pointer instead and, in the 5px `Wrap` gap between two words, the latched subject matches its own `litStrongs`, falls through to `WordMark.sibling`, and every word the reader sweeps past blinks green on the way out. The rejected design is preserved as a named test so it cannot be "simplified" back in. **Data note, measured not assumed:** 2,432,524 word slots surveyed — the format is uniformly `[GH]\d+` with **no zero-padding anywhere**, so `==` is a safe comparison; 62,795 slots are empty, which is why "empty must never match empty" is its own test; and 86.6% of cuvs-plus numbers in a verse also appear in BSB's same verse, so the cross-row echo actually fires most of the time rather than being a demo-only feature. **Process:** verified by driving headless Chrome over CDP at 1280x800 and counting exact-RGB pixels rather than eyeballing screenshots — the Read tool downscales a 1280px capture to ~320px, which is far too coarse to see a 1.5px border, whereas a pixel census answers "did the echo light in N distinct row bands" objectively. One trap: a Flutter `Tooltip` overlay can *occlude* part of the browse column, so a before/after pixel delta will show phantom "new" highlights that are really just un-occlusion — diff the coordinates and look at them before believing the number.
- **v1.6.28** — **hover proposes, a click commits.** The Analysis pane followed the mouse, which meant it could not be used: hovering a tagged word filled the pane, and moving the pointer *toward* that pane crossed every word in between, so the content the reader was reaching for was gone before they arrived — unscrollable, unselectable, no click-through to a lexicon entry. The feature destroyed itself in the act of being used. BibleWorks has the identical defect and its own help admits it; `bwh10a` documents Shift-to-freeze "while you move the mouse cursor to the Word Analysis Window to use the scroll bar", and `bwh10l` adds a Freeze checkbox *and* a right-click "update only when shift key depressed" inversion. **Three escape hatches is evidence the default is wrong, not a feature set to copy.** Shift-freeze is also the wrong shape for this app: it needs a keyboard, on a build deliberately gated below 1072px so it can run on tablets *where there is no Shift key and no hover at all*; it must be held while the same hand moves the pointer; and it freezes the PANE rather than naming a WORD, so nothing on screen says what you froze — which is why BibleWorks draws no mark for it. So a click **pins**, and a pin is an *identity*, not a *mode*: it names one printed occurrence, it is drawn in the text, and while it stands the pointer may go anywhere without changing the subject. Shift-freeze stays, because it costs one boolean and a BibleWorks reader will try it out of habit. Four things worth carrying forward. **(1) The occurrence key must contain book and chapter, not just verse and index** — a pin outlives navigation, so without them pinning word 2 of verse 3 in John 1 lights up word 2 of verse 3 in John 2 the moment the reader turns the page: a gold marker on a word nobody chose, in a verse they have never seen. `versionCode` is in the key too, so the same Greek word on the originals row and on a tagged translation row are two distinct marks, which is correct. **(2) `pinMark` had to be its own palette colour.** The app's single gold `#C9A227` measures **1.54:1** against the paper theme's tan `selectionBg` — on 护眼纸质 the marker simply would not have been there. The three palettes now measure 3.98 / 6.67 / 4.13:1 and the test *computes* the ratio from sRGB luminance rather than hard-coding it, so a future palette edit that dims the marker fails the suite instead of shipping. Relatedly, every mark reserves the same 1.5px border and only the colour changes: a border that appeared on click would widen the word and reflow the line under the reader's own pointer, so the click would move the thing it had just committed to. **(3) Clicking a DIFFERENT word moves the pin rather than swallowing the tap.** This is the single most dangerous failure mode, because on a touch screen a tap is the only way to look at anything, and a pin that refused to move would strand a tablet reader on the first word they ever touched with the text apparently dead. Three exits, all verified: click the same word again, Esc, or the worded Unpin button. Nothing is pinned on first load; the empty state is untouched. **(4) Esc needs `HardwareKeyboard`, not `Shortcuts`/`Actions`,** which never fires — Flutter routes key events from the *focused* node upward and nothing is focused in a mouse-driven reading pane. The handler returns `false` so it observes without consuming, and is gated on `ModalRoute.isCurrent` so a standing pin cannot eat the Esc that should close a dialog above it. A latent bug fell out of this: `onWordTap` was passing the *workspace cursor* verse instead of the tapped word's verse, so crossRefs / stats / related / phrases / verseLists — everything routed through `_analysisVerse` — was filed under the wrong verse whenever the two differed; harmless while click was a minor gesture, load-bearing the moment click became *the* gesture. The callback now carries the whole `BrowseHover`. **Two process notes.** The state machine is a Flutter-free `AnalysisFocus` because it *had* to be: `browse_window_tagged_test` already documents that `FetchVerses.loadVerseList` never resolves under `flutter test`, so the Browse window sits on its spinner, no word ever renders, and **no widget test can reach this behaviour at all** — the wiring was verified by driving headless Chrome over the DevTools Protocol at 1280x800 (Node 24 has a built-in `WebSocket`, so zero installs). Two traps in that, both of which cost real time and produced convincing false "the fix doesn't work" results: `targets.find(t => t.type === 'page')` is *not* your app once Chrome opens an About tab, so filter targets by URL; and `Page.captureScreenshot` can return a **stale frame**, so wait ~2s after an interaction and re-shoot with no input before believing a bad result. Also: the pin opening the right pane reflows the browse column, so re-measure pixel coordinates from the current screenshot before every click instead of reusing them across a sequence.

- **v1.6.27** — **morphological search**, BibleWorks' Graphical Search Engine (bwh17), which the last research pass named the single biggest remaining gap. The reader could already see what a word *is*; it could not ask what else in the book looks like it. Now a ninth Analysis tab builds a query by clicking parts of speech and features, scoped to chapter / book / testament, seeded from the word under the pointer, over the MorphGNT (CC BY-SA 3.0) and OSHB/WLC (CC BY 4.0) tagging that was already bundled and licence-clear — no BibleWorks data anywhere near it. Three things are worth carrying forward. **(1) A Semitic word is a stack of morphemes, and it matches only if ONE morpheme satisfies EVERY constraint at once.** Asking each constraint of the word as a whole — the obvious implementation — returns 7016 "feminine verbs" in the WLC where there are 6129: 887 false positives, typically `HC/Vqv2mp/Sp3fs`, a *masculine* imperative wearing a feminine object suffix. That number is measured against the real text and pinned in a test, because the wrong answer looks entirely plausible on screen. **(2) Every offered value carries its live count, and a value that would return nothing is not offered at all** — the counts come out of the same single pass as the hits (a word lands in slot S's tally when some morpheme fails *at most* S), and the "what if I switched this one facet" promise was verified by actually re-running the switched query over Genesis and comparing totals, not asserted. **(3) The tab is APPENDED, never inserted:** `workbench.analysisTab` persists by INDEX, so reordering the enum silently moves every reader who had a tab open to a different one. Two data defects surfaced from surveying the whole corpus rather than reading the spec: 1068 Aramaic verbs were being given *Hebrew* binyan names, and OSHB's `x` "unknown or unnecessary" placeholder was being read as a value on 2684 words. The POS→slot map is likewise measured, not copied from the documentation — MorphGNT leaves personal-pronoun person blank, so `RP` genuinely has no person slot, and offering one would have produced a dead chip on every pronoun query. Performance needed no cleverness: 4037 distinct codes across 438,821 words parse in 18 ms, so memoising the parse makes every repeat query free (~100–300 ms warm across a whole testament). The pane keys on the *book*, not the verse — a query is the reader's work and must survive the pointer crossing a word — and splits into two scroll regions (facets capped at 45%, hits taking the rest) because a long facet list otherwise pushes every result below the fold, which is how the widget test found it. **Two process notes for whoever runs next.** The pure core (`morph_query.dart`, `morph_search_service.dart`, the `morphology.dart` refactor and three test files) is *in* `b21d17b`, labelled "chore(release): v1.6.26" and described by nothing — swept in by another writer's `git add -A` while it was still in progress. That is the second time; the rule in this repo is **stage exact paths by name**, and it is not optional. And this build was made from a scratch `git worktree` at the committed tree, not from the working copy: the working copy registers `assets/nasb-ev.json`, `assets/nsn-plus.json` and `assets/tagged/nsn-plus/` under its own comment saying never to deploy them, so `release_web.sh` run in place would have shipped restricted licensed texts to dev. Same reason the version bump here is staged as a single hunk of `pubspec.yaml` rather than the whole file.
- **v1.6.25** — the small-screen bar moved from two panes to three, on the owner's instruction (「手机不能打开三个栅栏，而我们的App是有三个Column，所以…不能打开三个的，全部都把它显示让他用我的另外一个软件」). Two columns was the wrong bar: search beside the text with no word analysis is not a smaller workbench, it is a reader with a search box, which is what YsWords already is and does better on a phone. The gate is now the three-pane minimum (1072 px) in either orientation, and the copy drops the two-pane figure entirely rather than naming a threshold the app no longer honours. Consequence recorded in the test rather than left to be discovered: **the classic 1024×768 iPad no longer qualifies** — 48 px short — so it goes to YsWords in both orientations, while iPad mini is portrait-blocked but fine in landscape (1133). Closing that 48 px means trimming the pane minimums (240 search / 480 reading / 320 analysis), which is a separate decision about how narrow a column may get, and was deliberately not bundled. *(Ship bookkeeping note: the code landed in `46bc7e5` and was deployed to dev as 1.6.25, but the version bump and this line were never committed — the run ended between `release_web.sh` and its "next: git commit + push". Recorded by the following run.)*
- **v1.6.24** — CJK font fallback on that language switch: 简 and 繁 were rendering as tofu. The labels used a bare `TextStyle`, which resolves to Roboto, and Roboto carries no CJK glyphs — so the two controls that exist *specifically* for Chinese readers were the only unreadable things on a screen they cannot otherwise get past. Fixed with `fontFamilyFallback: kCjkFontFallback`, the same chain the rest of the app uses for exactly this reason. Worth recording for every future run, because it generalises: **the tests could not have caught this.** They assert `find.text('简')` finds the widget, and it did — the string was present and correct, only its glyph was missing. Font coverage is invisible to the widget tree and shows up only in a rendered screenshot.
- **v1.6.23** — a language switch on the small-screen wall. Making the gate a hard block turned the interface language from a preference into a trap: Settings sits *behind* the wall, so a reader whose device is set to English but who reads Chinese would be stopped by a screen they cannot read, with no way to change it. The switch is the only exit, so it belongs on the wall. EN / 简 / 繁 as three flat buttons rather than a dropdown — someone who cannot read the wall cannot be asked to open a menu labelled in the language they do not read — and each label is written in its own script, so every reader can find their own. System language already worked (`AppSettings` seeds from `_detectSystemLocale()` on first launch and only a persisted choice overrides it); this adds the manual override the block made necessary. One paint-order bug the new tests caught: with the switcher first in the `Stack`, the centred content painted over it and ate every tap — visible, positioned correctly, and completely dead, with nothing about the layout looking wrong. It is last in the stack now.
- **v1.6.21–v1.6.22** — the small-screen gate stopped arguing with itself, then became a real gate. v1.6.17 gated on `min(width, height) >= 480` — "a phone is a phone in either orientation" — while the copy offered rotation as the one-tap fix. Those are incompatible: a phone's short edge does not change when it rotates, so no rotation could ever satisfy the gate the copy was promising to satisfy. A reader who followed the advice landed on "this screen does not reach two columns in EITHER direction … two need about 736. This screen is 844 x 390", which contradicts itself using its own printed numbers. User: 「把手机横过来并没有改变」. What decides whether the workbench works is whether the columns fit *right now*, so that is now the test — `paneCountFor(width) >= 2` means no advisory, and rotation is offered only when the other dimension genuinely crosses 736, which on a 360x640 phone it does not, and offering it there would be the same broken promise in a smaller size. Then, on the owner's instruction (「不要有仍然继续，就一直是block住」), `Continue anyway` was removed. I had argued the other way when this was specified, and that reasoning holds for a *reminder* — but this is not one: a one-column workbench is not a product the owner wants shipped, and every reader let past forms their opinion of SeekSparks on the worst version of it. The hard block is defensible rather than hostile precisely because there is a real destination behind it (YsWords, phone-first, linked directly) rather than a dead end. A dismissal persisted by v1.6.20 or earlier is deliberately no longer honoured — those readers are exactly the ones who met the self-contradicting advisory and tapped through it, so a stale bit would hold the gate open for the worst-served group. `onContinue` stays on the widget's API: it is what a future setting or a QA build would wire up, and removing it would make restoring the choice a larger change than the decision warrants.
- **v1.6.20** — the command line learned its **verbs** (BibleWorks bwh44, Command Line Shortcuts). v1.6.19 taught it to *search*; it could already navigate; but it still could not change what you were looking at or narrow where you were looking — which is most of what a BibleWorks reader does between searches, and all of it without touching the mouse. Now `d kjv` / `d -kjv` / `d c` / `d english` edit the Browse stack, `p kjv bsb` restacks in the order given, `l gen` / `l matt 5-7` / `l nt` scope the search and bare `l` lifts it, and a bare number is a verse in the chapter you already have open (`3:16` is chapter and verse in the book you are in). `l` is the one that closes a genuine functional hole rather than a fluency gap: `workbench_provider` carried a comment saying the workbench command line "always scans the whole Bible — book scoping lives in the standalone SearchPage", so until now the workbench literally could not search only the Gospels. Four things a naive reading of the help file misses, and they are where the work was: `d` is not one command but a **set editor with four arities** (add, remove, clear, add-a-whole-language); the **search version can never leave the display**, because BibleWorks defines `d c` as "clears all versions except search version", which makes that an invariant of the display and not a default anyone may override; `p` is not `d` three times — it carries an ORDER, which is the one thing the existing version-checkbox dialog cannot express; and a bare number is a **verse**, not a chapter, which is the opposite of what every other Bible app's jump box does. Routing the verbs *first* in `_submit` is safe for the same structural reason v1.6.19's grammar was: every verb is a single ASCII letter followed by whitespace or end-of-line, or a line made only of digits and a colon, and no bundled edition code, book name or query control character has either shape — pinned by a 19-input "not a verb" test group rather than argued, since the failure mode is silent theft of a search someone typed. Every verb echoes what it did, above the results rather than instead of them: a verb's whole effect lands in a *different pane*, which below the three-pane breakpoint is not even on screen, and `d bsb` must not throw away a hit list that took three commands to build. Refusals name the token (`d niv` → "No edition called \"niv\"" plus the list of ones we do have) because falling through to a text search for "d niv" would find nothing and teach a first-day BibleWorks migrant that the verb does not exist. Deliberately not implemented, each recorded with its reason: `d fav`/`o fav` (no favourites UI to name, and their absence removes the `d c` ambiguity), `f c`/`f u`/`f a` (no result checkboxes exist here), `t #` (the help's "current context tab" has no equivalent surface — mapping it onto the Analysis tabs would invent a meaning, and those numbers have already shifted eight times), and the command line's "never forgets" behaviour where ↑ also restores the search version of the recalled line. `l` deliberately stops at chapter granularity: verse granularity is the Verse List Manager's existing job, and its limits are already a corpus snapshot, so the versification caveat is unchanged rather than newly introduced. `parallelMode`/`parallelVersions` moved out of `workbench_page`'s State into `WorkbenchProvider` — a verb typed in the left pane has to reach state the centre pane owns, and a compiler-checked migration beats a published mirror that drifts. Logic in `utils/command_verb.dart`, Flutter-free; 70 core + 23 widget tests against a stand-in edition registry so adding a Bible cannot break the grammar; en/zh-Hans/zh-Hant throughout. One bug caught while writing the tests: the help strings shipped BibleWorks' own examples (`d nas`, `d 太`) and neither resolves here — `nasb` is the code and `太` is a *book*, not a version, so the on-screen help was teaching two commands that fail.
- **v1.6.19** — the command line finally speaks the BibleWorks query grammar (bwh16). The workbench pane has been *called* a command line since v1.0.2 while only ever doing a substring scan, so a reader arriving with BibleWorks muscle memory who typed `.love god` searched for that literal string and was told there were no results — and plain `god world` failed the same way for a different reason, because the scan strips spaces, so a two-word search could never match anything and never said why. Now: `.` AND, `/` OR, `'` lexical phrase, `;` linear phrase, `!` negation, `*`/`?` wildcards, `*N` word gaps, `;N` verse context. Three things a naive reading of the help file misses, and they are where the work was: `!` has two arities (a verse exclusion in AND/OR, but a one-word *position* inside a phrase, which is the documented `'!your *5 house` trap); `*` has two meanings (a character wildcard when glued to a word, a word gap when standing alone); and `;N` is a **verse** context, which quietly makes the window rather than the verse the unit of search. Three deliberate divergences from BibleWorks, each taken as the conservative option: a windowed hit reports *every* participating verse, not just the first, so no verse in the list lacks a search word; a NOT term excludes on the verse rather than the window, so widening a context never silently subtracts results; and the pane echoes the parse back in words ("All of: god, world"), which no competitor does — a grammar whose operators are punctuation is only safe if the reader can see what the punctuation was taken to mean, otherwise a wrong query is indistinguishable from a wrong Bible. The grammar is routed *first* in `runSearch`, which is safe precisely because control characters are first-position only, so no reference, version abbreviation or Strong's expression can be stolen from the branches below it — pinned by a test rather than argued. Highlighting had to learn the grammar too or every command result would have listed verses with nothing marked in them. Two real bugs found by testing against the actual 31,102-verse KJV rather than fixtures: `.?` matched the entire Bible (a bare metacharacter became an any-token slot; now gated on CJK presence, since English wildcards arrive as their own whitespace-delimited piece), and a pointed Hebrew query folded at parse time was compared against unfolded search keys, losing every hit before tokenization. Performance verified on real data, not assumed: `.melchizedek` tokenizes 2 of 31,102 verses; the worst realistic query `.lord god israel*;10` runs in 121ms, which is why the engine is synchronous rather than paying for an isolate hop. Also worth recording: the KJV spells the NT occurrences "Melchisedec", so that search returns 2 verses and not 9 — every corpus figure in the tests was measured, none invented. Logic in `utils/command_query.dart`, Flutter-free; 56 core + 13 real-corpus + 19 widget tests, en/zh-Hans/zh-Hant throughout.
- **v1.6.18** — Eagle's View import: three public-domain Strong's-tagged editions (kjvs KJV+Strong's/TVM, lxxwh Septuagint+Westcott-Hort in a new `grc` language group, cuvs-plus 和合本+Strong's). Decoder in tools/import_eaglesview.py. NASB/nsn+/RSV/NET deliberately excluded — redistribution not permitted. OPEN: ask the pastor about the AOSurvey conversion rights + the Modern Concordance index before importing it.
- **v1.6.17** — the small-screen advisory. SeekSparks is a fork of YsWords, the phone Bible reader; v1.6.9 routed phones into the Workbench for visual consistency and thereby made the real problem worse, because below 600px the workbench collapses to its centre pane and a phone got a menu bar over one column of verses — YsWords with extra steps, and a worse copy of its own parent. Silently degrading into the thing you are not is worse than saying "not here". So phones now get one screen explaining what the layout needs, and `Continue anyway` writes a flag that is never unwritten — an advisory, not a lockout, shown at most once ever. Gated on the SHORT edge, never the width: a phone in landscape is 844×390, wide enough to pass a width test but ~320px of text after the 68px of chrome, about eight lines. Rotation is only offered when it is actually true — a 360×640 phone rotated is 640 wide, which clears the app's 600px side-pane gate so two panes *do* appear, with the reading column at 384, under `minReadingPaneWidth` 480; the rule is therefore `portrait && paneCountFor(longEdge) >= 2`, and `paneCountFor` is the same helper the on-screen copy quotes, so rule and message cannot drift apart. Two ambiguities taken conservatively rather than guessed: Flutter web cannot reliably tell a phone from a short desktop window (iPadOS Safari reports as Mac), so the copy states the layout's requirement and the *measured* viewport instead of diagnosing the reader's device — actionable whether the fix is to rotate, resize a window, or move to a tablet; and the YsWords link uses the intl site for all three locales rather than routing zh-Hans to the China build, which would guess where a reader is from what they read. No error icon, no scolding — nothing has gone wrong. New in research this run, from bwh06's "Optimizing the BibleWorks Workspace": BibleWorks hit the same wall on "pad computers with limited space" and answered it the *opposite* way — hide the chrome (column control strips, toolbar, main menu, status bar) and keep all three columns, because chrome is affordances and columns are the product. That makes a hide-chrome toggle the highest-value follow-up (68px back ≈ 25% more text on a landscape phone); it was deliberately not bundled here. Logic in `utils/workbench_fit.dart`, Flutter-free, with the pane constants defined as sums of the real minimums so the figures shown on screen are the layout's own; 16 core + 15 widget tests, en/zh-Hans/zh-Hant throughout.
- **v1.6.16** — Vocabulary Flashcards (BibleWorks bwh40) as the eighth Analysis tab. Found ~2,700 lines of it already sitting uncommitted in the tree — the earlier 2026-08-07 run built it well and died before shipping, leaving the suite red because it had appended an eighth tab while `phrase_match_pane_test` still asserted `AnalysisTab.values.last == phrases`. That was the wrong invariant: lastness breaks on the next legitimate append, which is the one operation the test exists to bless, so it now pins the index instead. Finished and shipped that work rather than restarting it. The point of bwh40 is not flipping cards — it is that the deck is scoped to the passage you are about to read, so scope (chapter/book/corpus) is the primary control and the frequency floor is a published syllabus (Mounce 50×, Metzger 10×, Pratico & Van Pelt 70×/50×) rather than an invented ladder. The third mode `Read` is the Example Verse Finder, which twenty years of reviewers never once mentioned and which is the best idea in the module; its two knobs count differently on purpose — the minimum counts distinct lemmas, the maximum counts tokens. The drill does within-sitting repetition only; cross-day SM-2 needs dates and a scheduler and is deliberately a separate future feature, not a half-built one. Nothing licensed is copied: Strong's keys, public-domain glosses, frequencies measured from the bundled text rather than quoted. Added the 16 widget/service tests the dead run never wrote, against real 3 John Greek — note for future runs that `testWidgets` bodies run in a fake-async zone where real asset I/O never completes, so the caches must be warmed in `setUpAll`. Verified the code's own forum citation instead of trusting it: thread #3431 is real, but it has **12** archived posts rather than the "thirteen" the comment claimed and is ~5 users rather than an exodus — corrected before shipping. Research written up in `research-notes.md`, including the highest-value follow-up: STEPBible-Data (CC BY 4.0) would supply the lemma→part-of-speech mapping whose absence forced dropping that filter.
- **v1.6.15** — Phrase Matching (BibleWorks bwh51) as the seventh Analysis tab. Related Verses shipped two days earlier asks which verses share the most *words*; this asks which repeat the same *sequence*, because a bag of words cannot tell "thus says the LORD of hosts" from a verse that merely contains those five words scattered about — and finding who *quotes* a verse is a question only word order can answer. The gap is the feature rather than a tolerance: 1..G words may be inserted between phrase words, which is what lets Matthew's "and they shall call" still read as Isaiah's "and shall call", and G bounds each step rather than the whole phrase so a 3-word phrase cannot spread across half a verse. A dropped word is allowed too, but only an interior one — dropping an end word would silently turn a length-3 phrase into a length-2 one. Chopping a verse into 3-word windows leaves mostly filler and BibleWorks offers no answer to that, so phrases in more than 1% of what was scanned start unchecked with their verse count on the chip; Accordance's ">50% common words" rule was rejected because it discards "a virgin shall", which is the whole point of Isaiah 7:14. Verified against the real 31,102-verse KJV, not just fixtures: Jer 31:33 → Heb 8:10 then Heb 10:16, Ps 22:1 → Matt 27:46 and Mark 15:34, Isa 7:14 → Matt 1:23, each top of the list; worst case Esther 8:9 at 319ms. Logic in `utils/phrase_match.dart`, Flutter-free; 64 core + 6 real-corpus + 14 widget tests.
- **v1.6.14** — Verse List Manager (BibleWorks bwh27) as the sixth Analysis tab, plus the search-limit plumbing that is the point of it: every tool here already produces or consumes a set of verses, and there was nowhere to keep one. Two lists with an active-list radio, selection modelled as a composable *layer* (select-common + delete = difference, select-unique + delete = intersection) rather than a filter, and import that appends verbatim while `Sort list` is what dedupes. The filter toggle makes the active list the limit for subsequent searches (bwh29/bwh44 `l test.vls`), with a tappable banner in the command pane so results are never silently filtered. Versification remapping is deliberately absent — it needs licensed `.VMF` maps, so version is stored as provenance only and kept out of `VerseRef` identity. `Import ▸ From Document` scans prose for embedded references, which exposed and fixed a real pre-existing `reference_parser` defect where `Jude 14-15` yielded only Jude 1:14. Logic in `utils/verse_list.dart`, Flutter-free; 60 core tests + 11 widget tests.
- **v1.6.13** — Related Verses (BibleWorks bwh50) reaches the Analysis pane. The app already shipped curated cross-references, which is precisely why the *computed* version is new: it takes the words of the verse you are on, lets you uncheck the ones that carry no sense or weight one ×3, and counts how many each of the other 31,101 verses shares — so it finds the parallels no editor listed. Chinese gets overlapping character bigrams instead of a bundled segmenter. All the logic sits in `utils/related_verses.dart` with no Flutter import; 49 new tests, including a cross-check that the fast scanner agrees with the readable reference tokenizer.
- **v1.6.12** — removed the U+25A1 missing-glyph markers from 士师记 13:7 and 18:10 (and the mirrored traditional verses in 士師記). The two □ glyphs were a corruption of the reverence space CUV prints before 神 — every other occurrence in the file is normalised away. Added a regression test that verifies both CUVS-YHWH editions have exactly 31,102 verses with no U+25A1, and spot-checks the two fixed verses carry the canonical text.
- **v1.6.11** — 护眼纸质 reaches the workbench. The paper theme used to stop at BibleReadingPane's content subtree; chrome, the parallel Browse window, and every `WbColors.of` call site stayed on the neutral desktop palette. Added `WbColors.paper` + a `paper:` flag on `workbenchTheme`, watched `AppSettings.readingPaperTheme` in `WorkbenchPage.build`. Also added value equality to `WbColors`.
- **v1.6.10** — search hits are now marked in the Browse text itself, not only in the verse list. A hit list without marked hits is a table of contents.
- **v1.6.9** — the chrome (menu/toolbar/status bars) is present at every width. Phones read as the same tool with side panes collapsed, not as a different app.
- **v1.6.8** — finished the `WbType` migration: 82 hardcoded size call sites reduced to 5. Settings sliders (Font Size, Menu Size, Line Spacing) now drive the workbench.
- **v1.6.7** — Settings actually reach the workbench: 2 of 10 user settings honoured before, now all of them through `WbType.of(context)`.
- **v1.6.6** — stripped the NASB pilcrow (¶) that was being printed as scripture, without touching the actual verse text.
- **v1.6.5** — stopped printing publisher markup (StripePosition tags etc.) as if it were scripture.
- **v1.6.4** — Word List Manager + search-hit distribution. Two BibleWorks analysis tabs backed by real data.
- **v1.6.3** — Key Word In Context (KWIC), straight from the BibleWorks feature set.
- **v1.6.2 (BSB)** — added the Berean Standard Bible, the only English translation here with Strong's tagging. Closes the gap that left the English parallel row untagged.
- **v1.6.1** — release plumbing fix.
- **v1.5.2** — fix: Compare-two-versions actually compared two versions instead of duplicating one.
- **v1.5.1** — release plumbing fix.
- **v1.5.0** — per-word Strong's on the Chinese line (和合本雅伟版 + BDB/Thayer).
- **v1.6.52** — **the product audit, and three pages nobody can open (task #281).** The brief framed this as *redundancy*; it is mostly **reachability** and **modality**. **The audit's test comes from the help file, not from taste**, because the obvious frame — *YsWords/devotional = cruft, BibleWorks = real* — is provably wrong: BibleWorks 10 ships a Timeline (bwh39), Maps (bwh33), flashcards (bwh40) **and Bagster's Daily Light (bwh52), a daily devotional**. What its modules share is that each **round-trips to the text** — the Timeline *"jump[s] directly from an event … to a Bible reference"*, Daily Light *"exports to the Search Window the list of verses"*, and bwh03 says the whole program is *"heavily cross-linked"*. So: **does this screen take you to the text, or bring you back holding something?** That test earns its keep by disagreeing with the naive one twice, both corrected by reading code rather than filenames: **`stats_page` is not engagement metrics** (`grep -c "streak\|daysRead"` = **0**) but top-lemma tables, per-book originals distribution and hapax legomena off `concordance.json` — bwh23/bwh26 territory wearing the name most likely to get it cut; and **`bible_trivia_page` is not a quiz** (no score, no timer) but 68 curated entries on acrostics, the Tetragrammaton and numeric structure, each jumping to its reference. **The headline finding: `DashboardPage` is referenced by nothing outside itself.** `main.dart` made `SmallScreenGate(WorkbenchPage)` the root deliberately — *"SeekSparks is a Bible STUDY tool, not a devotional"* — but nothing was pointed away from the Dashboard afterwards, so it and the two pages reachable only through it, `FamilyTreePage` (2,620) and `FeedbackPage` (468), went dark together: **4,508 lines**, plus a live Settings section that reorders a page nobody can reach — ~6,000 lines hanging off one question, *does SeekSparks have a home screen?* **The identity decision was already made and then not finished.** Found by BFS over the **import graph** from `main.dart` (255 files in `lib/`, 221 reached); now enforced by **`test/page_reachability_test.dart`**, whose allow-list *is* the review list, executable. **1,628 tests passed with all of it unreachable** — same lesson as v1.6.48: every test that touches those pages constructs them directly, so no unit test can notice that nothing else does; an invariant about the graph *between* units must be asserted about the source. **Traps if you touch that test:** conditional imports (`import 'x_stub.dart' if (dart.library.io) 'x_io.dart';`) span lines with the real target inside the `if` arm — miss them and every platform-split service reads as an orphan; and stop scanning at the first `class `, or doc comments quoting `.dart` become phantom edges. **Deleted ~1,714 lines, all grep-proven, nothing user-visible:** stats_page's three dead tabs (they have carried *"safe to remove in a future cleanup pass"* since Round 56), `parallel_verse_view.dart` (superseded by `browse_window`), `bible_stats_service.dart`, 28 `ui_strings` keys for reading plans removed in v1.2.69, `AppSettings.pickVerseAfterChapter` end to end — *a preference whose only effect was to be remembered* — `greeting.dart`, and `path_provider`+`crypto`, which the pubspec itself said to drop if a second consumer never came (fifteen months, none). **Near-miss worth carrying: nearly deleted a fix.** `version_colors.dart` had **zero** references and was created by 3cf2f0a to fix the version-pill colour drift; first read was *"a shipped fix was never wired"*. Wrong — it was written for `parallel_verse_view` and died with it, and its guarantee **survives** in `kVersionTagColors`/`wbVersionColor()`, which are fully opaque and keyed on the version **code**. **When a file is unreferenced, ask whether it is redundant or whether it was never connected — grep cannot tell those apart and they mean opposite things** (bears directly on #285's warning that `kVersionTagColors` is keyed on labels). Likewise `liquid_glass.dart` looks orphaned but is imported by the orphaned `feedback_page`, and deleting it broke `analyze`: **transitive dead code is not independently deletable.** **Sharper diagnosis than "redundant": modality** — surfaces that should be simultaneous are full-screen pushes, the same defect #276 fixed for the Reader and #284 will fix for 原文逐字. **`search_page` (2,877) is a second search UI** with AI mode, recent searches and a scope banner the Workbench pane lacks, so **#280 is blocked behind merging them** — a scope model built twice will drift, and it already has. Then `stats_page` → Analysis tabs, then the `map_viewer_page` vs Places (#277) duplication. **Deliverables:** `docs/PRODUCT-AUDIT.md` and `docs/DELETION-REVIEW.md`, the latter holding back everything the policy reserves for a human — the Dashboard cluster, the two search implementations, the map duplication, and **#278 bundled NASB, flagged and untouched**. **Verification:** analyze exits **0**, **1630/1630 green** (+2), deployed **v1.6.52** and probed on the immutable deploy URL — `pickVerseAfterChapter`, `planResetProgressConfirm`, `ParallelVerseView`, `BibleStatsService`, `planJumpToToday` all **absent** from `main.dart.js` while live symbols remain, and a 1440px screenshot shows the workbench booting three-pane on Jonah 1 (NASB), which is the real check on the edited `AppSettings` load path. **Not verified:** stats_page's surviving tabs were confirmed by compiler + analyze only, not screenshotted — reaching them needs Reader mode and several clicks.
- **v1.6.53–v1.6.54** — **the chrome pass reaches Settings, and gets a ratchet so it cannot un-happen (task #279).** v1.6.53's own entry was never written — that run was killed by the watchdog after committing and before deploying — so this covers both. **The finding that changes the shape of the whole 13-page pass belongs to v1.6.53 (commit 49242dd): the pills were never in the pages, they were in the theme.** `workbenchTheme()` builds from a fresh `ThemeData.light/dark` and returns `base.copyWith(...)`, discarding `main.dart`'s per-widget themes, and it only ever named **three** components — card, input, tooltip. Everything else rendered on stock **Material 3 defaults, which are stadium-shaped and elevated**. *No page ever asked for a pill; it asked for a `FilterChip`.* Squaring the components in the theme (all five button families, chips, sheets, dialogs, menus, snackbars, AppBar, tabs, list and expansion tiles, the progress bar) fixed a large share of #279's 67 sites everywhere at once, and cannot drift. Switch, Slider and the checkbox keep their radius — those are affordances, not chrome. **So re-count before estimating the rest: #279's per-page counts were taken *before* the theme was fixed.** Measured on `settings_page` after it: of the **54** sites the ticket lists, **19 were `Card(` and are now square for free**; what was left were **16 inline sites the theme cannot reach** — plain `BoxDecoration`s, `ToggleButtons.borderRadius`, an `OutlinedButton.styleFrom(shape:)`, two `ClipRRect`s and a `CircleAvatar`. The remaining twelve pages are likely far cheaper than the ticket implies. **Three calls the theme's own carve-out did not decide, each taken on a reason rather than a feel:** the two progress bars were `ClipRRect(radius: 2)` wrapped around a `LinearProgressIndicator` — not affordances, and the theme *already* sets `progressIndicatorTheme.borderRadius: BorderRadius.zero`, so they were **overriding the rule they should have been following**; the **Google sign-in button** is square now, because Google's branding guidelines fix the wordmark, the logo and the white/`#DADCE0` pair and **corner radius is not among them**, so it keeps everything actually specified (the instinct to treat a third-party button as untouchable *in whole* is what needed correcting); and the **colour swatches were `CircleAvatar`s** — a swatch is a *sample*, not a control, its round shape carries no meaning, and **bwh29's own "Program Colors" / "Morphology Colors" sections use square ones**. Selection there had been signalled only by an overlaid check, so it now also gets a 2px border, since hairline-vs-2px is how the rest of the workbench says *this one*. **The new mechanism is a ratchet, not a checklist: `test/page_chrome_pass_test.dart`.** The failure mode of a thirteen-page pass is not a missed page, it is a **converted page quietly growing a rounded card back a week later** — and no widget test can catch that, because `BorderRadius.circular(10)` renders perfectly and passes every assertion about *behaviour*; it is wrong only against a rule that lives in prose. Same species as v1.6.52's `page_reachability_test` — **an invariant about the source that no unit test can see.** Its `_passed` list is the pass's progress bar: a page joins it in the iteration that converts it and can never silently leave, with a sibling test blocking the cheapest way to make it pass (emptying the list). Comments are stripped before scanning so prose about the rule cannot fail the rule. **Negative-tested by injecting an offender**, which is the only way to know a source-scanning test is doing anything. Density untouched throughout, per the brief: it is the *chrome* that must match, not the line height. **Deliberately not attempted, and it is the bigger half of the Settings problem:** bwh29 shows BibleWorks' Options Window is **one window with a section list on the left**, not a long scroll — *"To move to a different section in the options, click on the item in the list on the left."* SeekSparks' settings is a single 4,286-line scroll with anchor keys that scroll-to-position. That left-list shape is the structural answer to "4,286 lines for 12 tiles", and it is **not a chrome change** — it belongs to #281's size question. **Correction to the brief, for whoever takes #280:** it says to first verify whether `l 创 限定检索范围` is a phantom because grepping for `searchScope`/`scopeBooks` found nothing. **That grep is stale.** v1.6.20 shipped the `l` verb, and `workbench_provider.dart:70` carries `Set<String>? searchLimit` with a `searchLimitLabel` beside it — **verified in code, not taken from the handoff.** So #280 is **surfacing and extending, not building from zero**. And bwh29 adds a requirement the brief omits: limits can be *"define[d] … by name for later recall"* — **named, saved scopes are part of what the feature is**, so build the model with a name field rather than retrofitting one. **Verification:** analyze exits **0** (by exit code), **1665/1665 green**, deployed **v1.6.54** and **screenshot-verified live at 1440×900** — the Profiles "Local only" badge renders **square with a hairline** (magnified 8× to check, because at 1:1 a 12px radius and a 0px radius are one pixel apart), the seven Primary Colour swatches are **square**, and the Paper/Carbon style-preset rows are **square**. Before/after pairs taken on the same surface. **Trap that cost real minutes and will cost them again:** the **first load of the main dev URL after a deploy serves the previous bundle from Flutter's service worker** — `version.json` already said 1.6.54 while `main.dart.js` was still 1.6.53, so the after-screenshot showed the *unchanged* pill and looked like the fix had not shipped. `Network.setCacheDisabled` does not touch a service worker. **Navigate twice.** Also: the immutable deploy URL is a **different origin**, so it has no saved settings and boots into the small-screen gate rather than the workbench — fine for string-probing `main.dart.js`, useless for screenshotting a page you have to navigate to. And a stale `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` from an **earlier session** survived on the target and pinned `innerWidth` to 320 while `Browser.setWindowBounds` reported 1440; `Emulation.clearDeviceMetricsOverride` on a *new* socket did not clear it — re-asserting the override with an explicit `setDeviceMetricsOverride` did, and it then held across clicks, contrary to the v1.6.51 note. **Check `innerWidth` with an `eval` step before trusting any screenshot's width.**
- **v1.6.55–v1.6.56** — **the chrome pass reaches the Bible Evidence archive, and bwh10 argues *against* flattening it (tasks #279 + #283).** Target chosen by re-deriving priority rather than trusting the ticket's counts: family_tree (21) and dashboard (15) are heavier but **frozen behind #281's unanswered "does SeekSparks have a home screen?"**, and search_page/map_viewer are merge-and-duplicate candidates, which leaves `evidence_page` (11) + `evidence_detail_page` (8) as the heaviest **actionable** pair — and the audit's verdict on them is *Keep*. **The design question was whether the archive should stop being a gallery**, since `stats_page` had just been converted on the principle that *a frequency table is a table*. **bwh10 settles it the other way, and this is the first time in the pass that the help file blocked the flattening instinct.** The Mss tab is a verse-linked image surface whose *entire* help topic is image **processing** — unsharp mask, sharpening, despeckle, grayscale, reverse, histogram stretch — every verb existing so the user can see the artefact **better**. BibleWorks does not shrink a manuscript image into a row; it treats it as a **specimen you examine and hands you a darkroom for it.** So the grid and the photographs stay at size and only the framing goes — which kills the hero's 12px `ClipRRect` on a stronger ground than "the theme says square": **rounding an artefact photograph crops the artefact.** The test for #279 is therefore not *is this decorative* but ***what is this surface FOR***. **Second confirmation of v1.6.53's lesson, now a rule:** the two search fields' `OutlineInputBorder(borderRadius: 10)` were **overrides fighting a theme border that is already square** — same shape as the settings progress bars — so when a page looks unconverted, **grep for overrides of what `workbenchTheme()` already sets before writing any new styling; the theme is usually right and the page is arguing with it.** **#283 was bundled in deliberately, because restyling a header that prints the wrong text is not worth shipping:** six surfaces printed the archive's raw English keys to a reader on a Chinese version. **Two findings generalise beyond this page.** (1) **A localisation bug can be structurally impossible to fix at the call site** — `uiStrings['evidenceForBook']` was `'经文实证 — '` with **no `{book}` placeholder**, so the page concatenated a raw name because the template gave it nowhere to put a localised one; when auditing for #283, **grep the strings table for entries that look like prefixes, not just the call sites.** (2) **A parameter can be accepted and never used for months**: `_ReferenceChip` — the single control on the detail page that round-trips you back to the text — has taken a `locale` argument since the day it was written and never passed it anywhere, and **`dart analyze` cannot see this**, because the field *is* used, by the constructor. `test/evidence_localization_test.dart` asserts on **rendered text, not on the mapper**, since `localeAwareBookName`/`localizedReferenceLabel` were always correct and only the *call* was missing; **mutation-checked** by reverting the `_ReferenceChip` line, which fails 2 of its 5 tests. It also pins the rule a naive fix would break — `bookScriptFor` says the **reading version** picks the spelling, **not the UI locale**, so a Chinese UI on KJV must still print `Genesis`; a fix keyed off `locale` passes the Simplified and Traditional cases and fails only that one, so that case is its own test. **The 320px screenshot the brief mandates earned its keep and produced v1.6.56:** the detail page's metadata chips sit in a `Wrap`, and **a `Wrap` does not shrink a child that is already wider than the line**, so `Shrine of the Book, Israel Museum, Jerusalem` overflowed at 320 **and at 390, which is a real phone**. The bug predates the pass; **what changed is visibility — a soft tinted pill just faded off the edge, where a hairline box that crosses the edge reads as broken.** Generalise it: **squaring a surface and giving it a 1px border will surface pre-existing overflow that a borderless chip hid** — budget for it on the remaining pages, and take the 320px shot before calling one done. `EvidenceDetailPage` now sits in `responsive_all_pages_smoke_test.dart`, which had `EvidencePage` but **not the page you reach from it**, and its fixture carries the long location **on purpose**, because with a short one the test passes against a page that still overflows in production — **a responsive test is only as good as the width of its longest string.** **Found and deliberately NOT fixed:** `assets/bible_evidence.json` carries **188 double-encoded sequences across 96 of its 225 entries** (`â\x80\x93` where an en dash belongs — `'1946â€“1956'`), UTF-8 read as Latin-1 and re-encoded, almost certainly from the Vite/React migration the archive was ported out of; it renders as visible garbage in the timeline and discovery-date chips. It is a data change to a shipped asset touching 96 entries — its own slice with its own verification — and folding it into a chrome pass would have made both harder to review. **Verification:** analyze exits **0 by exit code**, **1674/1674 green** (+9), deployed **v1.6.56** and **screenshot-verified live at 1440 and at 320 on the shipped bundle** — cards and hero images flush in hairline frames with no radius or shadow, `WbPanel` header strips on the detail sections, the reference chip square, and at 320 the location chip now ellipsises inside the column. **The #283 fix was verified in the running app, not only by test:** with the reading version switched to `cuvs-yhwh` **while the UI stayed in English**, the card and the chip both print **以赛亚书 40:8**, which is precisely the reading-version-wins rule. **Two new screenshot traps.** **Chrome clamps a real window to a 500 CSS px minimum** — a `Browser.setWindowBounds` of 320 reports `innerWidth` 500 — so emulation is unavoidable for the narrow shot; but **`Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` at a width much larger than the real window makes Flutter lay out against the emulated dpr**, rendering the whole UI at a different CSS scale (the 21px menu bar measured ~9) while `innerWidth` reads the requested value the entire time, so **the usual `innerWidth` check does not catch it and every click coordinate taken from an earlier screenshot misses.** Working recipe for a two-width run: **resize the real window and drive all navigation with the override cleared, then apply the override only for the narrow capture, where no clicking is needed** — and `Emulation.clearDeviceMetricsOverride` *does* work when sent on the same socket that set it (v1.6.54's "did not clear" was a cross-session case). Also: **SeekSparks gates below 1024px**, so an emulated-320 navigation lands on the small-screen advisory, not the workbench — the narrow shot of any inner page can only be reached by navigating wide first and narrowing after. **Dead end, do not repeat:** there is no BibleWorks help topic for an evidence/archaeology archive — SeekSparks' has **no analogue at all**, which `docs/PRODUCT-AUDIT.md` already correctly reads as an argument *for* it.
- **v1.6.57** — **there is no home screen, and a Resource has to bring you back (tasks #281 + #279).** The item was the user's Decision A on `docs/DELETION-REVIEW.md` §1: delete the Dashboard cluster, **keep the Family Tree** and move it under **RESOURCES**. The deletion is the easy half; **the half that was not in the brief is that every "open in Reader" in the app had silently stopped returning to the workbench**, and it was the *same* principle that found it. **bwh07 splits Tools from Resources on one question — does the item OPERATE on the current text, or is it a reference database you CONSULT?** Tools = Word List Manager, KWIC, Phrase Matching, Related Verses, Synopsis, Diagrammer; Resources = Commentaries, Grammars, **Maps**, **Pictures/Bible Views**, X-Refs, Dictionaries, Lexicons. `assets/family_tree.json` — 277 people with dates, relations and citations, **none of it derived from the open chapter** — is unambiguously the second kind, so it goes in Resources. The same test settles **Decision C** in advance: one map surface under Resources, while verse-linked "which places" stays in the Analysis pane, **because that one does operate on the current text**. **SeekSparks' menus violate the taxonomy today and I deliberately did not re-sort them** — Evidence, Timeline and Trivia sit under Tools but are databases; "Notes & highlights" and "Go to book…" sit under Resources but are user data and navigation. No defect behind it, so it is a recommendation, not a change. **The defect: `MaterialApp.home:` gives its widget `Navigator.defaultRouteName` (`'/'`).** #276 made the Workbench the root (`home: _RootRouter(...)` → `SmallScreenGate(WorkbenchPage())`), but `navigateToReader()`'s predicate only knew `'/HomePage'` and `'/Workbench'`, so the `popUntil` walk **fell through to the root, stopped on `route.isFirst` without setting `foundExistingReader`, and pushed the CLASSIC single-pane `HomePage` ON TOP of the Workbench** — command line, search results and Analysis pane gone, the reader **ejected out of the workspace #276 had just folded it into**. `isFirst` is now **part of the answer, not a fallback**: the bottom of this stack IS a reader — a claim about `main.dart` strong enough to need pinning, because the day someone adds an onboarding wall the predicate starts **lying** and every "open in reader" becomes a **silent no-op instead of an eject**, so a test reads `lib/main.dart` and asserts the root still resolves to `WorkbenchPage()`. **The defect class is the point: behavioural correctness hiding a structural loss.** A `HomePage` renders the requested verse **perfectly** — right chapter, right highlight, right scroll — so every assertion anyone would write (*"does tapping this citation show me Genesis 12"*) passes. **What was lost is not the verse, it is the workspace**, and no unit test has an opinion about which of two correct-looking screens you are on. Third instance of the same species after `page_reachability_test` and `page_chrome_pass_test`: **an invariant that lives between units, asserted about the source because the units are individually fine.** **Second-order lesson, a real dead end: the reachability test over-approximated and HID this** — all seven offenders import `home_page.dart`, so the reader read as reachable from everywhere. **Reachability says "an edge exists"; it cannot say "the edge is the wrong edge",** and a graph invariant that only answers *can you get there* will not notice that the way you get there **destroys the thing you came from**. **Seven pages had each independently grown the same body** — prepare the jump, then `pushPage(const HomePage())` — highlights, evidence, evidence_detail, trivia, timeline, person_detail_sheet, strongs_entry; the helper existed the whole time. **That duplication is why one wrong predicate broke the round-trip in seven places at once**, so `test/reader_round_trip_test.dart` bans the pattern at source, carving out only `navigate_to_reader.dart` (owns the one legitimate push) and `open_reader.dart` (deep-link entry, **runs before any navigator stack exists, so it has nothing to pop back to**). **Trap, hit for the second time: the ratchet fired on `home_page.dart`'s own doc comment**, which described the old pattern in prose — both wrong, so the comment was rewritten to say what is now true *and* the scanner strips comment lines; **a ratchet that fires on its own explanation gets deleted rather than fixed.** Assume every new source ratchet needs it. Incidental proof the centralisation was total: repointing the seven sites left **12 unused imports** (`home_page.dart`, `app_nav.dart`, `get.dart`) — **nothing still needed to know the reader's class name**, a stronger statement than the test makes. **Deleted ~3,700 lines:** `dashboard_page` (1420), `feedback_page` (468), `feedback_service` (171), `liquid_glass` (764), `dashboard_section`, both `browser_info_*` platform stubs, settings' 235-line `_DashboardSectionsCard`, 18 `ui_strings` keys, and the dashboard order/visibility fields on `AppSettings`; **`_knownOrphans` in `page_reachability_test` is now EMPTY** — the allow-list *was* the review list and the review is done. **Kept on purpose: the SharedPreferences purge in `resetSettings()`, written out as literal strings** (precedent: `'offlineMode'`), because **deleting a preference's code does not delete the preference from users' devices** and a reset that leaves `dashboard_section_order` + seven `dashboard_section_visible_*` keys behind is a reset that does not reset. **Verification:** analyze exits **0 by exit code**, **1671/1671 green** (+5, −3 deleted-page smoke entries), deployed **v1.6.57** and **screenshot-verified live at 1460 CSS px through the whole round-trip** — Resources dropdown → Family Tree → 277 people, groups expand → Abraham's sheet lists its verse references → tapping **Genesis 12** lands back on the **three-pane workbench** with the centre pane on 创世纪 12, menu bar and search and Analysis panes intact. **That last frame is the whole iteration: before the fix it would have been a single-pane HomePage.** **Not attempted, now unblocked by the same decision:** #279's chrome pass on `family_tree_page` (21 sites, was frozen behind the home-screen question), and Decisions **B** (one search), **C** (maps into Resources) and **E** (Chinese version labels — remember `kVersionTagColors` is keyed on LABELS, not codes).
- **v1.6.58** — **the chrome pass reaches the family tree, sheet included (tasks #279 + #283).** *(Entry written one run late — v1.6.58 shipped and deployed but its run ended before documenting itself. The code is in `13bfd09`.)* The heaviest page left on #279's list and the one #281's Decision A unblocked by keeping the family tree and moving it under **Resources**. **The rule the page produced:** era colour survives on **ink** — a coloured title, a 2px left rule, a tag — and dies as **decoration**: gradient header fills, alpha washes, tinted badges. That is the same test `evidence_page` passed for photographs; the workbench tolerates a saturated thing when it carries information. `WbPanel` gained `onTap`/`accent` and `WbTag` gained `onTap`/`icon`, which retired five bespoke pill widgets (`_RolePill`, `_SpouseChip`, `_RefBadge`, `_BridgeChip`, `_NextEraTag`). **Two traps worth inheriting.** `era_palette`'s new `eraColorFor` keys on `WbColors.isDark`, **not** `Theme.brightness`, because under the paper palette those two disagree and a brightness-keyed lift washes every era out on a cream ground. And the `Theme` has to wrap the **State**, not `build`: `_showDetail` opens the person sheet from `State.context`, so a `Theme` applied inside `build` sits *below* it and the sheet comes up in the app palette behind a converted page — which is exactly why `page_chrome_pass_test` now lists the sheet alongside the page. **Two bugs found on the way:** `_BridgeLeafRow.isRecentlyJumped` was declared and never read, so the jumped highlight never appeared on bridge leaves; and `person_detail_sheet._localizedRef` omitted the reading version (#283), so a reader on a Traditional edition tapped a Simplified book name and landed on a Traditional page — one book, two spellings, inside a single round-trip. It delegates to `localizedReferenceLabel` now, which also stops dropping everything after the first semicolon in a multi-book reference.

- **v1.6.61** — **the phone splash hang, and the widget the brief blamed was innocent.** Prod v1.6.56 sat on the splash forever on an iPhone. The named suspect was `small_screen_advisory.dart:71`, the `_dismissed == null` blank `Scaffold`; but that screen is empty and surface-coloured and the reader was looking at the *splash* — logo, 寻光, a verse — which is **upstream of the gate entirely**. `SmallScreenGate` wrapped `WorkbenchPage` **inside** the post-splash router, so no fix within it could reach a phone that never got past `LoadingPage`. **The root cause is one sentence: whether a viewport can carry the workbench depends on nothing the boot produces, yet the answer was sequenced behind all of it.** The gate now wraps `home:` and is answered on frame one. **Three defects fell out of that.** (a) A phone parsed every bundled Bible before being told to use YsWords — 6-15 s of splash under a 12x CPU throttle, unbounded on cellular. (b) The gate blocked on a `SharedPreferences` read whose value **could not change the answer** — `Continue anyway` was removed in v1.6.20 and the gate has passed `dismissed: false` unconditionally ever since, so the whole round-trip was dead async work whose only observable effect was a blank frame; deleted, along with `kSmallScreenDismissedKey`. (c) **The genuine forever-hang, and it is not phone-specific:** `LoadingPage._scheduleAdvanceIfReady` arms the 3 s hand-off and may only arm it once verses exist, but v1.2.28's build-time safety net set the one-shot flag **before** calling it, so a build arriving while `verses` was still empty spent the shot on a no-op and nothing re-armed. That is the *normal* ordering whenever main.dart's 4 s watchdog beats the active version's fetch — a cold cache or a slow connection — and the v1.2.28 comment block describes this exact bug as the thing it fixes, reintroduced one line above itself. The flag now belongs to the callee and means "the Timer is armed". **`_autoHardReload` could not save it:** its `stillStuck` test asks `bootInFlight || _retrying || loadError != null || verses.isEmpty`, and in the stuck state the boot had finished perfectly — a liveness check written against the failure you imagined will not catch the failure you got. **Why 1715 green tests missed it:** on a warm desktop the verses are in hand before `LoadingPage` mounts, so the racing build never happens, and every gate test pumped the widget directly rather than through the router, making its *position in the tree* — the actual defect — untestable by construction. `test/splash_advance_test.dart` drives the real ordering and was **confirmed to fail against the old code** before being accepted. **Reproduced and verified by screenshot, not by suite:** CDP `Emulation.setCPUThrottlingRate: 12` on an emulated 375x812 (network throttling does *not* work — a cold re-download delays the 4 s timer's practical start too, closing the race) showed v1.6.56 on the splash at t≈6 s and v1.6.61 on the advisory at the same instant; the advisory paints by t≈1 s unthrottled. 1024x768 re-checked for the big-screen path — three panes, unchanged. **Shipped to dev AND prod** (pre-authorised for this bug); prod jumps 1.6.56 -> 1.6.61 and so also carries v1.6.57-1.6.60.
- **v1.6.62** — **one worldwide build, and the largest Google dependency was not in the Dart.** SeekSparks shipped two bundles differing by a compile-time flag whose entire job was to skip the parts of boot that talk to Google, because those hang behind the GFW. **The flag was the workaround; this removes what it worked around.** Deleted: `firebase_core` / `_auth` / `cloud_firestore` / `firebase_database` / `google_sign_in`, `CloudAuthService` (657 lines), `RealtimeDbSyncService` (908), the never-initialised Firestore `CloudSyncService`, the web plugin registrants, `CloudSetupDiagnostic`, `SetupInstructionsCard`, `GoogleGLogo`, `build_flags.dart` and every `kChinaMode` branch, plus 78 orphaned ui-strings and the sign-in UI in Settings / reading pane / About / onboarding. **The change that matters is one removed `await`:** `main()` used to await `Firebase.initializeApp()` + `getRedirectResult()` behind an 8 s cap, so a reader in China paid that timeout before a single verse was parsed. **Safe to delete because every local write was `prefs.setString(...)` FOLLOWED BY `requestUpload()`** — persistence never depended on the sync layer, so no data moves; the only sync→local path was the BYOK Gemini key, which only ever filled an empty value. `google_fonts` went too: it downloaded 14 of the picker's options from fonts.gstatic.com, i.e. the picker offered a dozen choices that were dead for exactly the readers this release is for. **An unknown font key does not throw — it resolves to nothing and renders the default**, so `migrateLegacyFontKey` retires removed keys in two passes (the legacy CSS table pointed `Georgia`→`Lora`, and Lora is now gone too), and six style presets that hard-coded `Inter`/`EB Garamond`/`Merriweather` were repointed; `test/font_catalog_migration_test.dart` locks both and the preset check was **confirmed to fail against the old values**. **The finding that reframed the item:** Flutter's bootstrap fetches CanvasKit (~1.5 MB, nothing paints without it) from `www.gstatic.com` — `flutter build web` already emits the same files locally and simply does not use them. `--no-web-resources-cdn` in `release_web.sh` + `build_web.py` fixes it; **without that flag the rest of this work would have been cosmetic.** **Verified by measurement, not assumption:** CDP network capture of a cold boot on dev = 21 requests, **all same-origin, zero Google hosts**; Chinese (创世记 12) and Greek (Ἐν ἀρχῇ ἦν ὁ Λόγος, polytonic diacritics intact) both screenshotted rendering correctly. `flutter analyze` clean **by exit code**; 1703 + 6 new tests green. **One dependency remains and it is measured, not guessed:** opening a Hebrew word study pulls four fonts from `fonts.gstatic.com` (incl. `notosanshebrew`) via Flutter's engine-level `fontFallbackBaseUrl` — pre-existing, unrelated to this change, and it means **Hebrew would render BLANK in China**. Bundling an OFL Hebrew face as an asset, exactly as `NotoSansSC-Sub` already does for CJK, is the next item. Also unresolved by choice: the catalogue has **no serif left**, so `reverent` / `reader` / `paper` are carried by spacing and material alone. Dev only; prod not authorised.
- **v1.6.64 / v1.6.65** — **a version code outlives the version, and nothing was checking (item -3, the prod crash).** `https://seeksparks.netlify.app/#/genesis/1?v=cuv-yhwd` died on `FormatException: Unexpected token '<', "<!DOCTYPE "...`, and the message named none of the chain: `cuv-yhwd` was removed from the catalog with its asset in **180ba8b**, boot restored the code from SharedPreferences without validating it, `FetchVerses` asked for `assets/cuv-yhwd.json`, and **Netlify answered an unknown path with the SPA fallback — `index.html`, status 200** — which `rootBundle.loadString` returned quite happily for `json.decode` to choke on. **On the web a missing asset does not 404 into an exception**, so the symptom reads as a *corrupt* Bible rather than an *absent* one; it also means any "does this asset exist?" check by HTTP status is worthless on this host (`curl` the retired asset and you get 200 and ~20 KB of app shell — the same 20,266 bytes that proves `nasb-ev.json` did *not* ship). **Live since the removal, not a v1.6.62 regression**, and prod had been rolled back to v1.6.61 on that wrong hypothesis, surrendering the 6.1 MB bundle win for nothing — **check the date of the removal against the date of the rollback before believing a regression story.** **Wider than one code:** `cuv`, `cuv-tr`, `cnv`, `cnv-tr`, `cuv-yhwd`, `biblexg`, `biblexg-tr` (pinned from `git log --diff-filter=D`, cross-checked against `bible_versions_language_test`). A version code is written to prefs on every chapter change, into every share link as `?v=`, and into the persisted Browse stack, so **retiring an edition strands bookmarks, synced devices and anyone who simply had it open**; five entry points could carry a stale code, **only two were guarded, and one of those keyed on `bibleVersions` rather than `availableVersions`** — a version hidden via `disabledVersions` would have *passed* the check and then failed to load. **The brief named `?v=` as the suspect and it was already guarded**; the genuinely unguarded path was `MainProvider.restoreState`, which assigned `savedVersion` straight to `currentVersion` behind two hardcoded special cases — so the real-world mechanism is that the reader opened `?v=cuv-yhwd` while it still shipped, the code was persisted, and **every boot since crashed before the first verse painted**, a bug that worsens with time and that a fresh browser cannot reproduce. **The brief did not mention the fifth entry point:** the persisted Browse stack (`workbench.parallelVersions`), restored in two places and consumed in four more. `retiredVersionSuccessors` maps each retired code to the surviving edition **preserving language AND script** — `cuv-yhwd` → `cuvs-yhwh` is the identical text sharing all 31,102 references, so that reader loses nothing, and handing a 繁體 reader 简体 is **not a smaller version of the right answer, it is an unreadable one** (pinned by test). An unlisted code falls through to the fallback, so a future omission **degrades quietly instead of crashing**. `resolveReadingVersion` takes the fallback as a **parameter, because only the caller knows it**: boot wants the locale default, a deep link wants the edition already on screen — **a stale link is a reason to ignore the link and never a reason to move someone off the Bible they were reading.** **Guarded at the point each list lives, not at each restore site:** `WorkbenchProvider.parallelVersions` became a getter/setter that runs `loadableVersions` on assignment, so the page, the command line and the version picker are covered by one rule — **filtering per call site is exactly how this came to be validated on some paths and not others** (it also sidestepped an edit conflict with another writer live in the tree; the better design and the available one coincided). `FetchVerses` now refuses an HTML payload with an exception **naming the asset** and **skips the retry ladder** for it — a missing file will not appear on the third attempt, and the escalating 20/40/60 s timeouts spent a minute of the reader's boot to reach the same answer.
