A substitution is not an error but not nothing either, so `RetiredVersionNotice` tells the reader once, **above the router**, catching both the boot path and the deliberately-unawaited `UrlSyncService` path. **Verified live, not assumed:** analyze **0 by exit code**, **1772/1772 green** (+29), and both entry points reproduced in headless Chrome against the real deploys by seeding `localStorage['flutter.version']` — **prod v1.6.65 boots Genesis 1, rewrites the stored code to `cuvs-yhwh`, and shows the notice, with zero JS errors** captured via a `preload` hook on `error`/`unhandledrejection`; the Parallel view renders with retired codes seeded and the garbage entry dropped. **Item -2 discharged in the same push** (pre-authorised, gate run from a clean tree at the pushed SHA): prod `version.json` = 1.6.65, **`main.dart.js` 4,836,473 bytes — down from 10,965,398, the 6.1 MB the rollback had undone** — `nasb-ev.json` and `nsn-plus.json` both return the 20,266-byte SPA fallback, and Settings → Export produces a real payload stamped 1.6.65. **Trap worth inheriting:** CDP clip coordinates are **CSS pixels** but screenshots come back downscaled; reading menu coordinates off a 600px-wide image of a 1440px viewport clicks at 2.4× the wrong place. **Cost named:** `retiredVersionSuccessors` is hand-maintained, so retiring an edition still means remembering a row — two tests reduce that to a failing suite rather than a crash (every successor must be loadable; no retired code may also be live), and a forgotten row still degrades to the fallback.
- **v1.6.66** — **one scope model for search *and* analysis results, and the fix was a denominator rather than a label (task #280).** The scope already existed and already narrowed the left search pane; what nothing did was carry it into the **result lists** — KWIC and the Analysis stats pane ran over the whole corpus no matter what the banner said, so `G2962 · 64 处` was ambiguous about which of the two numbers it was, and a reader with a limit set could read a count that silently contradicted his own filter. **bwh23 answers the ambiguity better than a label does: BibleWorks prints the scoped statistic *beside* the whole-version figure**, so the count is now `inScope / total` (`11 / 46`) with an `N in all — limited to X` sub-line, which is strictly more informative than naming the scope and removes the need to trust the banner. Not copied verbatim: BW writes `*64 (1278)`, and **the asterisk is a footnote marker with no footnote** — it means "limited" only to someone already taught, and it does not travel to a Chinese UI. **The line for *which* lists obey the scope is bwh10l's, not an oversight:** lists that enumerate *hits in the corpus* (KWIC, word stats) honour the limit; lists that *describe* a corpus (Context word lists, Vocabulary) and reference databases (cross-refs, places) deliberately keep their own range — BibleWorks' Use tab ignores verse limits by design and carries a range of its own, because "how often does this word occur in Mark" and "what words does Mark use" are different questions and only the first has a scope. **A latent #283-class bug fell out of the wiring:** `LimitSpec.label` is canonical **English** and no display site re-localised it, so `l 创` set a scope that printed *"Genesis"* into a Chinese banner. The provider now keeps the **spec** as the scope's identity and one `scopeDisplayName(spec, locale, version)` renders it everywhere — book names follow the **reading version**, not the UI locale, so every display site is fixed at once instead of three of them being fixed by hand. **Terminology per the brief — 希伯来圣经 / 希腊圣经, never 旧约 / 新约** (same reasoning as the 雅伟 restoration); `oldTestament` / `newTestament` survive only as **label keys**, and `l ot`, `l nt`, `l 旧约`, `l 舊約`, `l 希伯来圣经` all still parse, because **you do not take away the word someone types in order to change the word he reads.** A test asserts no string on the scope path contains 旧约/新约/舊約/新約. **The core is pure and the sheet has a pinned contract:** `lib/utils/search_scope.dart` (groups, canonical order, books↔spec, `scopedCountLabel`) with 21 tests, plus 6 that pin the picker's three-state group chip and — the distinction that would otherwise rot — that **dismissing returns `null` (cancel) while "Clear all" + Apply returns a non-null EMPTY set (clear the scope)**; `wholeBooksOfSpec` returns null when a range names chapters, so `l matt 5-7` is shown but not silently rounded up to Matthew. **Two traps worth inheriting.** `setEquals` lives in `package:flutter/foundation.dart` and **`material.dart` does not export it**. And `cond ? uiStrings['k']?[locale] ?? 'fallback' : (…)` **does not parse** — the null-aware index inside a conditional produced five errors (`non_bool_condition`, `dead_null_aware_expression`, `argument_type_not_assignable`, `missing_identifier`, `expected_token`), **not one of which names the real cause**; an `if/else` assigning a `final String` is the fix, and the analyzer's error text is worth distrusting whenever `?[` and `?:` meet. **Verified on the deployed build by screenshot, not by suite:** the banner `Limited to Pentateuch (5852)`, the status-bar field `Limits: Pentateuch`, the stats pane reading `1 / 3`, `2 / 20`, `495 / 2127` under *"In scope / in all, rarest first — limited to Pentateuch"*, and the KWIC header `H1254 · 11 / 46 hits` over `46 in all — limited to Pentateuch`; a narrow pass at `innerWidth 1044` with the sheet open showed no overflow. analyze **0 by exit code**, **1799 green** (+27). **Three things deliberately not done, so nobody re-derives them:** the scope is **session-scoped and does not survive an app restart** — the brief asked for persistence *across searches in a session*, and a limit that outlives the window is exactly the "you may have forgotten you have search limits set" failure bwh09 warns about; the 旧约/新约 inside long historical prose in `ui_strings.dart` (~1017–1131, 2275–2417, e.g. "旧约的七十士译本") was **not** find-replaced, because rewriting quoted history is an editorial pass and not cleanup; and bwh29's **named saved limit selections** plus bwh16's **"Make Search Results New Search Limits"** — the loop that turns a result set into the next scope — remain unbuilt and are the natural next items. Dev only; prod not authorised.
- **v1.6.67** — **the order of the columns is part of the comparison, and BibleWorks answers "which versions" and "in what order" in two different windows (task #288).** The ticket read as *the parallel-version picker is a checkbox list*; the defect is larger than its styling. **A column of checkboxes cannot express an order**, so the picker re-sorted the reader's stack into registry order every time it was opened — `p bsb kjvs lxxwh` on the command line set an order that the only pointer-driven editor then silently destroyed, and nothing asserted which of the two was right because the order had no test. **The help file is why the sheet has two sections rather than one.** bwh09's *Choose Display Version(s)* is a checkbox tree and answers selection only; **bwh29's *Version Display Order* is a whole separate window** of Move Up / Move Down / Move to Top / Move to Bottom whose result saves to a named `.vdo` file — and the tell is its **Show Active Only** checkbox, which is evidence that ordering is only tractable over the subset you have already chosen. bwh38's Parallel Versions Window folds both into Available → Add → up/down → OK. Hence a draggable **"In display order"** list stacked over an **"Available"** catalogue, not one list wearing both jobs. **Staged with Apply/Cancel, not live-apply:** a drag is far easier to do by accident than a checkbox, applying parses an edition's JSON, and both BibleWorks equivalents end in OK — whereas the old sheet applied on **dismiss** and had no other way to apply, so there was no way to back out of an edit at all. That is neither model, and it is the actual bug. `null` means CANCEL, following `search_scope_sheet.dart` from v1.6.66. **The reading version is drawn but inert** — no grip, no remove — because the invariant is inherited rather than invented: `applyDisplayVerb` seeds every stack with the search version, the command line refuses `d -kjv` (`cannotRemoveSearchVersion`), and `command_pane` strips it before storing; the count is of **columns**, so it includes it. The model is a Flutter-free `lib/utils/version_stack.dart` (normalise / display / toggle / move) so the contract is testable with no widget tree, and every entry point goes through `normaliseComparisons` — which is also what keeps a stale `cuv-yhwd` in SharedPreferences from a pre-retirement build out of the stack, i.e. the **v1.6.64 crash class**, where a missing asset comes back as Netlify's `<!DOCTYPE` SPA fallback. **`ReorderableListView.onReorder` is deprecated in favour of `onReorderItem`**, which adjusts `newIndex` for the lifted row itself, so `moveComparison` takes its indices at face value and the classic off-by-one — invisible on an upward drag, wrong on every downward one — now lives in exactly one place, the framework's. The widget test drives `onReorderItem` directly rather than simulating the gesture (the gesture is Flutter's to get right; the index convention was ours) and reads the resulting order off the rows' **screen positions**, so it asserts what is painted rather than what is in the draft. Fixed in passing: the old sheet enumerated `bibleVersions` rather than `availableVersions`, so it would have offered `disabledVersions` — that set is empty today, so nothing user-visible changed. **Verification:** analyze exits **0**, **1827/1827 green** (+28), and **screenshot-verified live on seeksparks-dev at v1.6.67** at 1440×860 and again at **1000×700, the narrowest layout there is** (below ~992 px the small-screen gate takes the screen, so there is no 320 px case to photograph): locked reading row with lock icon and the word "reading", three draggable rows with grips and remove buttons, the Available catalogue grouped by language, and a pinned hairline action row reading "4 versions displayed" / Remove all / Apply — square corners, hairlines, no shadows throughout. End-to-end at the minimum size: adding LEB appended it to the **end** of the display order and to the pane's last column rather than dropping it into registry order, reopening the sheet showed the persisted order intact, and removing it restored the stack. **Only assumed, not verified live:** an actual pointer **drag** — the reorder is pinned by test through `onReorderItem` but was never performed with a mouse in the browser — and the Chinese surface strings. **Deliberately not built:** named `.vdo` order files, `<o name>` recall, and bwh38's Parallel Versions *Favorites*; note for whoever picks that up that the same "named, recallable configuration" gap now exists in **two** places, #280's saved search limits and #288's version favourites, which argues for one manager rather than two. **Trap for the next run, and it cost an hour:** `Page.navigate` to a URL differing from the current one only in the **hash fragment** is a *same-document* navigation, so the document never reloads and the screenshot shows the **old bundle after a successful deploy**. The service worker was innocent (`getRegistrations()` and `caches.keys()` both returned 0) and so was the server (`curl` of the deployed `main.dart.js` found 11 occurrences of the new version) — navigate to a cache-busting **query** instead, `/?cb=1667#/john/1:1`. And contra v1.6.51's note, `Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` did survive `Input.dispatchMouseEvent` on this build, provided it is re-issued in the same step list.
- **v1.6.68 / v1.6.69** — **"What is the NEAR5? I clicked on it and nothing happens" was three defects, and only the third one is the sentence the reader wrote (task #294).** The brief's first instruction was to establish whether the buttons are BROKEN or merely OPAQUE, because the previous run's harness never reached the Flutter field and its reproduction proved nothing. **They are both, and the broken part was invisible to 1849 green tests.** (1) **Two chips emitted queries our own parser rejects:** `G25` + `✶` produced `G25 * ` → `parseStrongsBoolean` returns **null** → silent fallthrough to a text search for the literal string, and `!` produced `G25 !G26` → also null. The `✶` case is the sharpest — **`G25✶` is the form the shipped help documents and it was the one query the `✶` button could not make.** One glue rule now gets all three documented meanings right (`G25`+✶→`G25*` prefix wildcard, `.faith`+✶→`.faith*` character wildcard, `'信心 `+✶→`'信心 * ` word gap), and the parser splits a glued `!` back off so the same keystrokes mean NOT in both grammars. (2) **v1.6.63's "one search" merge regressed discoverability:** the deleted `search_page.dart` hid the combining bar until a Strong's number was present and gave every chip a localised tooltip; `command_pane.dart` showed them unconditionally with **none**, the `?` card documented **only the text grammar** (press `?` to ask what NEAR5 is, get ten lines about `.love god`), and a shipping string still promised the old behaviour ("they appear once you type a number") — that key and 7 siblings were deleted after grep proved them unreachable. The strip now **dims rather than hides** the combining operators (a button that vanishes as you type cannot be learned from) and answers a tap on a dim one with a hint row instead of silence. (3) **The actual "nothing happens" — the browser's select-all echo, found only by driving the deployed build over CDP.** Tapping a chip blurs Flutter web's hidden `<input>`; putting focus back makes the browser **select ALL of it**, and that select-all is echoed into the framework as the new editing state: after typing `G25` the DOM reads `sel (3,3)`, after tapping `NEAR5` it reads **`"G25 NEAR5 "` `sel (0,10)`** — so the next character REPLACES the query the button just helped build. `G25` → tap `NEAR5` → type `G26` → **the line reads `G26`**, and Enter runs a plain lexicon lookup. It applied to **every chip**, not just NEAR. Every write to the line now goes through one `_writeLine` that arms a guard undoing exactly that signature — same text just written, wholly selected, once — so a select-all the reader made himself is untouched; **rejected a post-frame re-assert** because the echo arrives from a DOM event on its own schedule and can land after the frame. **`flutter test` never has a DOM input, which is why the suite was green for the whole life of the bug**; the pin that works replays the echo through the platform channel (`testTextInput.updateEditingValue` with `TextSelection(0, 10)`) and was **confirmed to fail with the listener removed**. Generalise it: **any "clicking X does nothing" report about a control that writes to a text field should be checked for caret state over CDP before the insertion code is touched** — the insertion code here was never broken, and a run that trusted the report's wording would have rewritten the one part that worked. **The parity decision, taken conservatively and stated rather than implied:** bwh16 writes proximity as **`*n` BETWEEN two words inside a phrase** (`'faith *3 christ`) — which SeekSparks already ships (`cmdSyntaxGap`), so the question was only ever about the *Strong's* grammar — and **`*n` was NOT adopted there**, for three verified reasons: `*` is already the prefix wildcard so `G25 *5 G26` sits one space from `G25* 5 G26`; **their `n` counts INTERVENING words while our `NEARn` counts word-position DISTANCE, so NEAR5 ≡ BibleWorks `*4`** and an alias would silently shift every window carried over; and **theirs is DIRECTIONAL, ours is not**. The `?` card now states that difference. **Ordered proximity remains a real, named parity gap** — deliberately not closed, because inventing a second operator name would add confusion to the one task whose purpose is to remove it. Also shipped: the distance is **editable** (the parser always took any `NEAR<n>`; only the button was pinned to 5), with `−`/`+` rewriting the token in place, preserving the reader's own spelling of `NEAR`/`WITHIN`, clamped to the parser's own 1–50 — and the handler takes a **delta read off the live line**, because one that closes over the build-time distance steps once for two quick taps (that was a genuine bug the test caught). Pure core in `lib/utils/command_draft.dart` (`analyseCommandDraft` / `withNearDistance` / `describeCommandDraft`), whose one subtlety is that **only a line made of NOTHING BUT operators is read as a button press** — uppercasing every token and looking for `AND` turns `.love and god`, an ordinary English search, into a nag about Strong's numbers. **Verified live on seeksparks-dev at v1.6.69, not in the suite:** the strip dimmed on an empty line and bright after `G25`; tapping `NEAR5` giving `G25 NEAR5 ` with `sel (10,10)` and the hint "needs a second Strong's number after it, e.g. G26"; typing `G26` now **keeping** the first number; `+` twice giving `G25 NEAR7 G26` with button label and hint agreeing; and **Enter returning 7 verses** (约翰福音 15:9, 17:26) — the whole chain end to end. `flutter analyze` **0 by exit code**, **1851 green** (+24). Dev only; prod not authorised.
- **v1.6.70 / v1.6.71** — **a total says how often; it does not say where — the word-distribution chart (task #290).** The ticket's load-bearing instruction was wrong, and the whole feature turns on catching that. It said the chart must be **Greek-only** and **absent for Hebrew**, because the Stats tab's existing source, the Eagle's View Greek profile, returns null for every Hebrew number; it also called `analysisStatsHint`'s *"Whole-Bible occurrences"* a defect for 27-book data and asked for it fixed in three locales. One inspection of `assets/strongs/concordance.json` disposes of both: **14,039 entries, 8,640 H + 5,399 G**, each entry's per-book map `b` **summing exactly to `n`** — uncapped, complete, both testaments — while the reference list `r` beside it stops at 500 and counts distinct VERSES. `analysis_tabs.dart` was **already calling that service once per word and throwing `byBook` away**, so the Hebrew chart cost one field, not one service; and the hint string is *correct*, since the figures it describes are the concordance's, which are whole-Bible — **left unchanged on purpose**. The one thing the ticket got right it got right for the right reason, and the data proves it: `G3056` is **330 occurrences across 317 verses**, so the footer says *"Counted by occurrence, not by verse — one verse may carry the word more than once"* rather than borrowing bwh23's *"# vss in bk with hit"*. **The same numbers are drawn twice, and the show-zeros question is answered oppositely in each — because it is a question about width, not taste.** bwh23 defaults "Show Zeros" off and offers a **New Window** command conceding that a 66-category chart *"allows you to display more of the graph at once"*; so the labelled chart in the centre pane drops empty books and states their absence as *"In 24 of 66 books"*, while the **label-less strip in the 256–560 px Analysis pane must keep them** — with no book names, a bar's POSITION is the only thing that names its book. `G5357` (φιάλη, 12, Revelation only) settles it on screen: one bar at the far right of an empty ruler reads as "Revelation" with no text at all. The strip is a `CustomPainter`, not 66 boxes in a `Row`, because fixed-width children at the narrow end of a resizable pane is precisely where RenderFlex overflow hides. Rows run down, bars grow rightwards — bwh23's default, since its "Swap Axis" is what turns names horizontal — with canonical / most-first order from its "Sort Books", the reader's current book outlined, and rank-in-book printed only where it exists (Greek) rather than zeroed for Hebrew. **The defect the screenshots caught and 1,862 green tests did not:** at 1440 the header read `λόγος G3056 330`; at **992** it read `φιάλη… 12` — word and number shared one `Flexible`, so the ellipsis ate **the number**, which is the header's entire job given the chart outlives the tap that opened it. v1.6.71 moves the number outside the `Flexible`: the word truncates, the number cannot. **Deliberately not built**, each because the honest version needs data the repo does not have: bwh23's normalised `%: hits / words in the book` (no whole-Bible per-book word count exists — an invented denominator is a plausible wrong chart), row→verse navigation (`refs` caps at 500, so it would half-work silently on exactly the common words), and a scoped chart (it is whole-Bible and **says so** under an active limit, which is bwh23's parenthesised-asterisk admission in fewer pixels). **Found in-area and left for its own ticket:** `analysis_tabs.dart` ~L492 computes `inScope` from `result.refs` — distinct verses, capped at 500 — and hands it to `scopedCountLabel` beside a `total` that is whole-Bible occurrences, so one ratio prints **two different units** and silently truncates for any word in more than 500 verses. Incidental fix carried along: `search_stats_strip.dart` had a hardcoded `"OT … · NT …"`, English in a Chinese UI (#283's class) naming the corpora in the terms #280 settled against — now `希伯来圣经 / 希腊圣经` via `uiStrings`.
- **v1.6.72** — **the sermons had no author on any page that showed them (task #291).** The ticket said the credit was "partly present" and missing from the detail view; it was worse than that. `sermonsTagline` ("Pastor Eric Chang's sermon library") was in `ui_strings.dart` and **rendered by nothing** — the only grep hit was its own definition; the library header said just 讲道; the detail page showed id, date, passage and topic chips and never said whose preaching it was; and `_copySermonBody` put forty paragraphs on the clipboard under a "From SeekSparks" footer with **no byline**, so quoted sermon text left the app unattributed. The name rendered in exactly one place — a Settings offline-pack row that spelled it a fourth way. Three unrelated `ui_strings` entries, three spellings: the drift the user reported was the symptom, not the disease. **BW's answer is bwh10i, the Version Info tab: provenance follows the focused resource, and the copyright lives inside the version's own `.bww` rather than being retyped in the UI.** So provenance belongs beside the text and gets **one** definition. `assets/sermons/index.json` has no author field and adding one means rewriting 289 records for a constant, so **`lib/constants/sermon_credit.dart` plays the part of the `.bww`** — the name is written three times total, once per locale (following `sermon_topics.dart`'s precedent) — and the library header, the detail byline, the clipboard payload, the About row and the Settings row all compose from it via `preacherName()` / `sermonLibraryCredit()` / `withPreacher()`, with `{name}` templating keeping the two `ui_strings` entries that need it from spelling it again. **The ratchet:** a test fails the build if any `uiStrings` value contains 张熙和 / 張熙和 / "Eric", and a companion test fails a template that carries `{name}` in one locale but not all three — the quiet version, where English gets the byline and the Chinese locales silently lose it. Restraint is deliberate: **one line per surface, never a list row** — crediting all 289 rows would be advertising, not attribution. **Corpus arithmetic, measured not trusted:** the row said "587 sermons"; the index holds **289 records** whose `parts` sum to **589**, in up to 3 languages for **867 files**. 587 was reaching for the part count and stating it as sermons, and the code comment at `offline_pack_service.dart:28` was not even self-consistent with itself (587 × 3 ≠ 867). **NOT changed, and it needs a human:** About credits the sermons "© 梁家铿", who is the **translator of the biblexg edition** (`aboutLicenseLjk` = 圣经释经事工), not the preacher — unexplained since the initial commit `6f13365`. He may be the publisher who granted permission, which would make the line correct standing beside the new byline; **deleting a rights claim on a guess is worse than carrying an unverified one**, so it stands, flagged in a code comment. **Trap for the next chrome-adjacent change:** `main.dart:608/699` paints the AppBar `lightScheme.primary` / `darkScheme.primaryContainer`, so an AppBar subtitle written as `scheme.onSurface` is nearly invisible — use `appBarTheme.foregroundColor`. Caught by reading the theme, not by the analyzer and not by 1,871 green tests. Verified by screenshot in all three locales (CanvasKit renders a missing glyph as **absent text**, so 張/師 had to be seen, not assumed).
- **v1.6.73** — **"bundle an OFL Hebrew face" was 869 uncovered code points, and Hebrew was 79 of them (task #289).** The measurement had to come before the fix: unioning the cmaps of the only two fonts the app bundled (Roboto **922** code points, NotoSansSC-Sub 6,017) and subtracting them from every character in `assets/**` + `lib/**` gave **CJK 384, Greek Extended 209, Hebrew 79, emoji 70, Hangul 23, transliteration diacritics ~50**. **Roboto's Greek is MONOTONIC**, so all 209 assigned code points of U+1F00–1FFF — every breathing and accent in the Westcott-Hort NT and the LXX, i.e. the app's own subject matter — were being drawn by a font downloaded from **fonts.gstatic.com**, and the inherited v1.6.62 research note asserting "Roboto has Greek but not Hebrew, so Hebrew is the uncovered case" is **wrong**. Hebrew was only the *reported* symptom because a word study is where a reader meets a bare lemma. The download is not app Dart and not `google_fonts` (gone in v1.6.62): it is the **Flutter engine**, which fetches a Noto face from `fontFallbackBaseUrl` for any code point covered by no *registered* font — which is also why v1.6.62's boot audit came back clean while a word-study click did not, the fetch being lazy and glyph-triggered. **The 384 missing CJK characters are the sharper finding: `tools/build_cjk_font_subset.sh` hardcoded `/Users/pliu0036/Documents/yswords`, the fork PARENT**, so the subset had been built against the wrong repository for months and the gap was precisely the text that exists only here — `thayer_zh.json`, `bdb_zh.json`, the zh-TW sermons, i.e. the Chinese lexicon glosses that are this app's differentiator. Nothing caught it because **CanvasKit renders an unresolvable font as ABSENT TEXT, not tofu**: the failure is silent and total, and a reader behind the Firewall saw a gloss with words missing rather than a row of boxes. A prior run had already met this and documented the wrong conclusion — `workbench_theme.dart` carried "fontFamily deliberately NOT pinned … none of those carry Hebrew. Leaving it unset lets the engine reach its own broader fallback", which is a description of *depending on the download*; the comment is fixed alongside the cause. `tools/build_font_subsets.py` replaces the shell script and builds all four subsets in one pass, **unioning each with the cmap already on disk so a rerun can only add coverage** — the property whose absence let the fork-parent bug survive a regeneration. **Hebrew and Greek are subset BY RANGE, not by scan, because they are also INPUT** — a reader can type ἀγάπη or אהב into the command line and a scan-derived subset blanks on the first form no asset happens to contain; CJK stays scan-derived only because the full face is 16 MB. **The bundling line is "everything a reader reads":** scripture, lexicon, transliteration, and the search syntax the command line prints in its own help — but **not decorative emoji**, since a monochrome symbol face would displace the colour glyphs iOS and macOS already draw, a native regression bought with a web fix, and the discriminator is visible in the data (the decorative glyphs in `bible_evidence.json` carry U+FE0F, the load-bearing ones do not). Five symbols cleared that bar into their own 1 KB file, including the **□ that stands in for a character lost from Judges 13:7** in `cuvs-plus.json` — when it vanishes the verse silently reads as though nothing were missing. **Chosen so native cannot change:** the three families sit AFTER the OS font names in `kCjkFontFallback` and in both of `main.dart`'s theme chains, so the system faces still win every glyph they have and only CanvasKit — where those CSS names are unresolvable — ever reaches the bundles; `NotoSansExt-Sub` is additionally built as `EXT − Roboto's cmap`, a pure gap-filler, so chain order cannot alter a glyph that renders today. **Conservative call, stated rather than implied:** this reproduces today's *mixed* rendering offline (Roboto for unaccented letters, Noto Sans for accented) rather than improving it — making original-language typography *consistent* means pinning a family on original-language spans, which changes how Greek looks for every existing reader, and that is a typographic decision, not a side effect of a networking fix. Fixed in passing because it was load-bearing: the stats page's **Aramaic row carried a SYRIAC badge** (`ܐܒܓ`) contradicting its own comment — biblical Aramaic is written in the Hebrew square script — and it was the last glyph in the UI still able to trigger an engine font download. `test/bundled_font_coverage_test.dart` parses the sfnt cmap subtables (formats 4/6/12) in pure Dart and asserts real coverage plus declaration-and-chain membership, with **two guards against passing vacuously** (Roboto alone must parse to exactly 922 and must NOT contain א or ἀ; the asset scan must see >5,000 distinct characters) — necessary because **a widget test never loads a font**, which is why 1,879 green tests could not see any of this. **Verified live on seeksparks-dev at v1.6.73 by screenshot, twice over:** with `fonts.gstatic.com` blocked, Genesis 1:1 renders fully pointed (בְּרֵאשִׁית בָּרָא אֱלֹהִים … with niqqud and cantillation) and John 1:1 renders full polytonic (Ἐν ἀρχῇ ἦν ὁ Λόγος … breathings, circumflex, iota subscript, grave) alongside Traditional and Simplified Chinese, and the Hebrew word study — the ticket's exact scenario — opens with transliteration `(rê'shîyth)` intact. **Then again with the requests HANGING rather than blocked**, via `Fetch.enable` with no `continueRequest`, because the Firewall drops packets and a blocked request fails *fast* while a dropped one stalls until timeout — identical render, so nothing is on a blocking path. **Remaining and deliberately not closed:** a word-study click still fires **3 speculative `notosanssc` shard requests**, and downloading those exact shards and diffing their cmaps proves **every code point in all three is already bundled** — the engine asks for fonts it does not need. `fontFallbackBaseUrl` would end them, but pointing it at a dead same-origin path would also **kill web emoji for everyone** (88 distinct emoji live in `bible_evidence.json` / `maps_index.json` and reach the screen only via that download), so the trade needs its own ticket rather than a guess. **Also found while measuring, not fixed:** `assets/bible_evidence.json` ships **154 mojibake sequences** (132 `â€“`, 3 `â€”`, 19 `Ã©`) — visibly corrupt text in prod from a UTF-8-read-as-Latin-1 round trip — and the command line's help prints `G25✶` while the parser accepts `G25*`, so a reader copying the help types a character it rejects. `flutter analyze` **0 by exit code**, **1879 green**. Dev only; prod not authorised.

- **v1.6.74** — both thrones on one axis (task #292). Reading 1-2 Kings, the question is never who reigned but who reigned *at the same time*: the text dates every accession by the other kingdom's regnal year, so two lists side by side cannot answer it. Judah and Israel now share one vertical axis, selecting a king lights every king who stood on the other throne during his reign, and each king carries the accession synchronism plus both the Kings and the Chronicles account. **On Thiele throughout** — named in the header and carried in a `system` field so another chronology could be added without a migration — with every figure taken from a citable source recorded in the asset rather than derived arithmetically from the regnal totals, because accession-year convention and co-regencies are exactly what Thiele's system resolves. `scripts/build_hebrew_kings.py` asserts the invariants that make the table trustworthy: the throne never vacant between 931 and the two falls, reigns handing over at a shared year, no two sole reigns overlapping. **Co-regencies are modelled rather than flattened** — a king carries a *list* of spans, so Thiele's six Judean co-regencies render as the overlaps they are and Tibni, who never reigned alone, is representable at all. Reign lengths span four orders of magnitude, so bands stay true to scale and names live in a separate lane pushed apart by a pure, tested layout function. Deployed to dev; the release bump itself was left uncommitted by that run and is committed here.
- **v1.6.75** — **the prod "Null check operator used on a null value" was ours, and the error reporter had been eating every one of them.** Two reports on v1.6.65 the same day, iPhone/iOS-Safari and Mac/Chrome, identical message. **Fix the reporter first or you diagnose noise:** `runZonedGuarded`'s handler is typed `(Object, StackTrace)`, dart2js elides that in release, a zone error with a **null stack** got through, and `ErrorReporter.report` called `.toString()` on JS null — throwing *inside* the reporter, outside `_send`'s try/catch, so the real error was discarded and replaced by the reporter's own. Every zone error with a null stack had been arriving as this one string for months. `report()` now takes `Object?`/`StackTrace?` and survives an object whose own `toString()` throws. **The actual crash, deobfuscated without a rebuild:** the source map 200s as `<!DOCTYPE` (Netlify's SPA fallback again — the same trap as v1.6.64), so read the deployed bundle's line directly; `main.dart.js:49661` is a bare `n.toString`, dart2js's null-assert, sitting between an `await …go(-o)` and `uG(n.h(0,"state"),"flutter",…)` — i.e. `currentState! as Map` in `MultiEntriesBrowserHistory.tearDown`, engine `history.dart:225`. **Two conditions, both ours to break:** (a) `serialCount` lives in `history.state`, which is per-tab session history that iOS Safari keeps for months, so a count inherited from a *previous document* sends teardown's rewind into entries this document's engine never tagged; (b) `_writeStateToUrl` pushed `pushState(null, '', newHash)` on every chapter change, manufacturing exactly those untagged landing spots. Reproduced live (three null-state entries + `replaceState({serialCount:2})` + reload) — the reader's exact message, same line. **Not fatal, and that is the worst part:** the app keeps painting, but `_browserHistory` is left a *disposed* MultiEntries instance with its popstate listener gone, after which `serialCount` climbs on every navigation — the bug feeds itself across reloads (`{"serialCount":3,"state":null}` versus a healthy `{"flutter":true}`). So: `main()` zeroes an inherited count before the first frame, which is the one step that makes `if (backCount > 0)` false and skips the rewind; and our own entries carry `{'seeksparks': true}`. **Zeroed, not deleted** — `_hasSerialCount` tests the value not the key, so removing it reintroduces the crash by another door. **The marker is an *unrecognised* key on purpose,** not a copy of `{flutter: true}`: the engine's `onPopState` branches only on `origin == true` and `flutter == true`, so `null` and ours fall to the same `else` and back-button behaviour is unchanged by construction — then confirmed by running the same navigation script against both builds and getting identical hash, `history.length`, back, back and forward. **Deliberately not done:** migrating to `SystemNavigator.routeInformationUpdated`, because in single-entry mode `setRouteName` always *replaces* and Back would stop stepping chapters for everyone. **Still unproven:** what first put a count > 0 into that reader's tab (a tab opened on an older `Router` build, backgrounded and reloaded much later, fits; the phone path never writes a URL at all, so the small-screen advisory is not it). **`url_sync_service_web.dart` is behind a `dart.library.js_interop` import, so 1,879 green tests ran alongside this for months** — the decisions are now a pure function in `lib/utils/history_state_repair.dart` that a VM test drives, the untestable line is held by a source ratchet with an anti-vacuous length guard, and the *stub* is ratcheted too (a method missing there only fails on a native build, which CI never runs). Also fixed: `retired_version_notice.dart` used `firstWhere` with no `orElse` on a widget whose entire premise is that version codes outlive the versions they name — a retired fallback would have turned the notice into a `StateError` on frame one. **Found while measuring, NOT fixed: browser back/forward does not step through chapters on either build,** though `url_sync_service.dart`'s header says it does — the engine's single-entry `onPopState` `else` branch calls `go(-1)`, *removing* any entry it did not create, so it pops ours and snaps back. Long-standing, identical on prod; the honest fix is either URL-as-share-link-only or adopting a `Router` to earn multi-entry mode legitimately.
- **v1.6.76** — **three search defects a live audit found and 1,955 tests could not (#295).** `flutter test` has no DOM text field and no rendered results header, so all three shipped green; driving the deployed dev build over CDP — typing into the real command line, reading the real header — found them in an afternoon. **(1) The divine name reported its own frequency wrong.** `H3068` printed 共 500 節 because the header counted the refs it *had*, and the build pipeline truncates every entry's verse list at 500 — while the uncapped total sat loaded and discarded. 123 of 14,039 entries sit on that cap, hiding 160,564 occurrences; יהוה alone is 6,521. The header now reports **verses AND occurrences**, the two numbers BibleWorks' status line gives, which genuinely differ (G25 is 143 hits in 110 verses), and says outright when the list stopped early — suppressed under a search limit, where a Bible-wide total would read as the scope's own. **(2) The wildcard chip's own example did nothing.** Its tooltip advertises `faith✶`, tapping it after `faith` produces exactly that, and submitting searched for a *literal asterisk* — guaranteed empty, since no Bible text contains one. A bare `*` query now runs as `.query`. `?` is deliberately left alone: Scripture is full of question marks and `who is this?` is a substring search someone may have meant. **(3) `d nas` was refused** though the matcher's own doc comment opens with `nas` as its first example — it compared exact codes only. An unambiguous 3+ character prefix now resolves, exact still winning, so `kjv` stays KJV and never drifts to KJV+S and `l` stays the scope verb. **The third fix then had to be made twice**, which is the durable lesson: the display verbs resolved editions through `VerbContext.resolveVersion` in `command_verb.dart`, an independent exact-code-or-label comparison, so the command pane was fixed and the defect stayed on screen. One rule, two copies. `VerbContext` delegates to `matchVersionAbbreviation` now. **Both of these ran green the whole time** — when a claim is "the search box accepts X", the suite is not evidence.
- **v1.6.77** — **the Word Study tab stops being a bottom sheet parked in a pane (task #284).** The tab has **two bodies**: hovering a word gives `WordAnalysisPane`, hovering a verse or making a selection gives `OriginalsSheet` — and the second was still the phone's modal, so moving the pointer one grade coarser swapped the whole tab between two visual languages. **The header was worse than cosmetic:** `workbench_page.dart` skipped `WbPaneTitle` for this tab *precisely because* the sheet drew its own, so the **pane's** title and collapse chevron appeared and vanished as the pointer moved between a word and a verse. `WbPaneTitle` is unconditional now and the sheet draws no header; only the copy button survives, because it copies the word table this widget itself built (bwh10q puts the Forms tab's Options button in the body for the same reason). **The diff is far smaller than it looks, and knowing why is the reusable part:** `workbenchTheme` already remaps the *semantic* `ColorScheme` roles (`surface`, `onSurface`, `onSurfaceVariant`, `outline`, `primary`, `surfaceContainerHighest`), so **prose colour was never the problem**. What does not survive that remap is only three things — geometry, type scale, and the *tonal* roles (`primaryContainer`, `secondary`, `tertiaryContainer`) that `ColorScheme.fromSeed` fills with Material pastels the workbench palette has no equivalent for. Those three, and only those, are what `lib/constants/word_study_style.dart` resolves. **Both columns live in one pure data class rather than twenty-six inline `embedded ?` ternaries**, which is what makes "the phone modal did not move" an assertion instead of a hope — and a source ratchet can then demand that no literal `BorderRadius.circular(` survives in the widget (one exemption, marked `modal-only:`, for the distribution sheet, which is never docked). Docked: every radius zero, every border a hairline, Strong's numbers **green numerals instead of tinted pills**, and the type finally scaling with the workbench font-size setting — this was the one pane in the workbench that ignored it. **Two decisions worth inheriting.** The selected chip's ring is **`pinMark`, not a new colour**: tapping a chip here is the same act as pinning a word in Browse, and `pinMark` is the palette's one marker already contrast-checked (WCAG 3:1 non-text) against `selectionBg` in all three themes — including 護眼紙質, where the app's gold accent measures **1.54:1** and would simply vanish. And `chipMaxWidth` is **112** so two chips plus the gap and the list padding clear the 256px pane floor: 2×112 + 6 + 2×8 = 246. `listPadding` and the lemma size are copied from `WordAnalysisPane` deliberately — the tab's two bodies must not shift the left margin or resize the headword as the pointer crosses between them. **Verified by screenshot on dev, not assumed:** interlinear grid, tapped entry card, related-word chips, the CBOL attribution CC-BY-NC-SA 4.0 requires (now guarded by its own test, because losing it in a restyle is a licence breach), light + dark + 護眼紙質, and the pane title holding still across both bodies. Substance unchanged throughout.
- **v1.6.78** — **#279 reaches Bible Trivia, and the chrome was the smallest thing wrong with it (#279).** The brief said "restyle"; the page had **three defects the restyle would have preserved**. **(1) A curated entry pointed at a verse that does not exist:** `reference: 'Philemon 16'`, and because Philemon has one chapter `parseReference` read **16 as the chapter**, so that entry's jump landed nowhere — the one class of bug a 68-entry hand-written catalogue is guaranteed to have and no widget test was looking for. `trivia_catalogue_test.dart` now resolves **every** reference against `assets/kjv.json` (the asset stores `chapter`/`verse` as **strings** — `int.parse` them) and separately requires the five single-chapter books to carry an explicit `:`, because that failure mode is silent rather than throwing. **(2) The reference badge printed raw English** (`Genesis 1:1` under Chinese prose) — the #283 class on a surface #283 never listed; one call to `localizedReferenceLabel`, plus a test asserting the zh labels contain no `[A-Za-z]`. **(3) The testament toggle still said 旧约/新约**, and chasing it found the last two survivors in `stats_page.dart`; `statsBooksOT`/`statsBooksNT` are deleted, not merely unused. **The generalisable finding is about the test, not the page:** a page-level overflow smoke test under-tests everything behind a disclosure — the word-diagrams only build when a tile is *expanded*, so at 320 px they had never been rendered by anything; the new test drives the page's own search box and taps the tile rather than reaching for private widgets, which is also the only way to reach them from outside. Chrome itself was mostly **deleting page-level `shape:`/`backgroundColor:` overrides** — `workbench_theme` already squares `bottomSheetTheme` with `showDragHandle: false`, so both sheets were arguing with the theme; the hand-drawn rounded pill handle is **gone rather than squared**. **Conservative call, recorded as such:** bwh10k's Verse Tab is trivia's real BibleWorks analogue and would make this an auto-following Analysis tab, but the strip is at 10–11 tabs and #277/#284 already settled that another tab is not the answer — it stays a Resource. `ChapterVerseCountsDiagram` has **zero** instances in the catalogue and has therefore never rendered; left in place. `bible_trivia_page.dart` is on the `_passed` ratchet, one entry covering the page and both its sheets.
- **v1.6.80** — **#279 reaches the workbench's own centre pane, and the pass had been measuring the wrong population (#279).** The brief sized it as "13 of 25 pages in `lib/pages/`, 67 sites", so on that arithmetic it was half done — counting all of `lib/` finds **42 files / 130 sites**, and the **single heaviest offender in the app was `bible_reading_pane.dart` at 30 sites**, never on the list because it is not a page. That is the finding, not a detail: **by the governing principle the workbench IS the app, and the workbench is built of widgets** — `home_page.dart` has 2 sites and no route reaches it; the centre pane had 30 and is never not on screen. A **v1.6.50 handoff line was already wrong** because of this, recording that the rounded card and shadowed floating pill "belong to HomePage, a different route"; screenshots of dev show both sitting permanently above verse 1 and at the foot of the reading pane. **`_GlassSurface` — the auto-hide header, the bottom bar and the selection bar — gave three findings, only one of them chrome. (1) The blur never ran:** it took an `opaque` flag whose `false` branch built a `BackdropFilter`, and all three call sites passed `true`, so "glass" was a name for an opaque fill; the branch is deleted rather than kept as an option nothing selects. **(2) Squaring the corners is what makes the correct border legal** — `Border.paint` throws *"A borderRadius can only be given on borders with uniform colors"*, so the rounded version **could not** have a top-only hairline and had settled for a 4-sided outline whose bottom edge doubled the pane divider beneath it; it now takes `Border(top:)` or `Border(bottom:)` per anchor edge. The rule did not merely permit the better border, it was the precondition for it. **(3) Removing a shadow can force a colour change:** the progress pill's `surface` at 0.86 was legible only because the shadow lifted it off the scrolling verses, and one hairline cannot also hide bleed-through — it is opaque now. Generalises: **translucency and shadow are usually load-bearing together.** Also squared: `_BookIntroCard` (the literal card the spec forbids, the first thing above verse 1), `_SectionHeading`, `_FloatingHeader`, `_RefChip`, and every sheet the reader can open from the pane. **The ratchet gains the other half of its job:** `_passed` is a progress bar and cannot say how much is LEFT, which is exactly how "67 sites" was allowed to rot — `_remaining` now declares a per-file ceiling for every unconverted file in `lib/` and fails three ways (a clean file grows its first rounded corner, a dirty file grows more, a file reaches zero and is not promoted). Numbers may only go down, and lowering one makes the test demand the new figure. **Deliberately not done, and recorded as such:** bwh11 docks navigation in the Browse Window header and never overlays chrome on verse text, so the maximal reading of parity is to **dock these bars** — that is layout, layout is density, and density is out of #279's scope by the brief's own words; only corners, borders and shadows changed. **Two things the ratchet structurally cannot see.** Material gives `IconButton.filled`/`filledTonal` a `CircleBorder` by *default*, so the A−/A+ circles in the font-size sheet have no source line to grep — found only by screenshot, and the fix is one `iconButtonTheme` entry that lands on every surface at once, so it is its own iteration. And **a stale `main.dart.js` will lie convincingly**: the first capture of the local release build showed the rounded card still there against source that provably had none. Disable cache, re-navigate — and note that **navigating clears the CDP device-metrics override**, so re-issue the size step or every later clip lands in the wrong place. **Verified by screenshot at 1400 and 1012 px, light and dark:** header, intro panel, bottom bar, the wrapped two-row selection bar, the font-size sheet, and no overflow revealed by squaring. 護眼紙質 is reasoned, not shot — the `paperTheme` branch is a two-token swap inside the same square `DecoratedBox` verified in the other two themes.
- **v1.6.81** — **#279 reaches the book → chapter → verse picker, and the conversion was decided by hosting, not taste (#279).** After the centre pane, `book_chapter_picker.dart` was the heaviest file left (18 sites) and it is the app's **primary navigation surface** — the workbench Resources menu, the status bar's reference field and the reading pane all push it. **It has TWO hosts and only one is a page:** `BooksPage`, and `SidebarPanel` via `home_page.dart:325`. A widget with two hosts cannot rely on a host to theme it, so the picker now wraps **its own subtree** in `workbenchTheme` and `BooksPage` re-applies it for the AppBar and the ground the centred column sits on (idempotent — brightness comes from the parent, the paper flag from settings). **The failure this rules out is silent rather than loud:** `WbColors.of` falls back to `.light` when the extension is missing, so an unthemed host does not crash, it renders a light palette inside a dark app; two of the new tests exist only to pin that, mounting the picker in a deliberately unthemed host and asserting the palette resolved *inside* it. **Collapsing three implementations of "a number in a box" into one `_NumberTile` was not tidying:** `_chapterTile` drew a `Card`, `_gridChapterTile` a `Material` and `_VersePickerChip` a third thing, and the chapter grid was **the only one of the three missing `FontFeature.tabularFigures()`** — a column of 11/12/13 never lined up. `_bookTile` was declaring its fill and border **twice**, drawing a hairline over an already-bordered box. A duplicated widget hides a defect in two of its three copies. Deleting `BooksGlassSurface` also retires a **per-frame `BackdropFilter` over a scrolling list**, and its 0.78-alpha fill had to become opaque — the same v1.6.80 lesson, that translucency and shadow are load-bearing together. **The picker had no test at all** — only a four-width overflow smoke test that pumps it and asserts nothing — which is how a refactor that renders perfectly can navigate wrong: `_selectChapter` does **not** fire the caller's callback, it opens a verse grid; picking a verse sets `pendingJump` **before** firing so `books_page.dart:113` suppresses `jumpToTop`; "Top" fires with none, which is what makes jumpToTop legal again; and `didUpdateWidget` keeps the verse step across a same-book chapter change and drops it across books. **Two test traps worth inheriting, each of which cost a debugging round:** `AppSettings.booksViewMode` defaults to **`'grid'`**, so the surface an untouched reader sees prints the **abbreviation** (`Gen`), and a finder for `Genesis` reads as "the tile is not rendering"; and `AppSettings._locale` defaults to **`'zh-Hans'`**, so the first verse tile reads **本章开头**, not `Top`. The fix is not to hardcode the Chinese either — the test resolves `uiStrings['versePickerTop']![settings.locale]!` from the live settings object so it cannot drift. **Look user-visible strings up through the same table the widget uses.** **Conservative call, recorded as such:** bwh11 documents navigation **List Boxes** in the Browse Window header — version, book, chapter, verse as dropdowns, to "lookup various Bible references without doing any typing" — and SeekSparks already has them in the Browse frame; replacing this full-page picker with them is **layout**, which #279 scopes out. **Verified by screenshot on a local release build at 1440 and at 1000 px (the 992 px gate floor), light and dark:** book grid, chapter grid, verse grid, the expanding list view, and a `scale: 4` clip on the "Top" tile settling the corner at pixel level — square, one hairline, no shadow. The 800 px column cap finally reads as a panel because its gutters are hairlined instead of being two unexplained empty strips. 護眼紙質 and the `SidebarPanel` host are **reasoned, not shot** — the same one-line idiom as the pages already converted, and the two palette tests respectively.
- **v1.6.83** — **the 1,192-plate picture archive finally has a door, and the ratchet that was supposed to be guarding the chrome pass had a blind spot (#279, bwh07).** This run's feature was **already written**: the 15:55 watchdog kill took the previous iteration during *verification*, not mid-write, and the tree it left came back `analyze` exit 0 and **2065/2065** untouched — so the rule is now **test a killed run's dirty tree before discarding it**, because HANDOFF is written last and a killed run leaves no other record. What shipped: `assets/maps_index.json` has held 1,192 illustrations for months — 55 bundled maps and 1,137 public-domain Doré/Tissot/Schnorr plates, each with trilingual title and caption and a book/chapter filing across all 66 books — reachable **only** by already reading a chapter that happened to match, with `mapsBrowseLibrary` ("Browse all illustrations") sitting unused in `ui_strings` the whole time: the door was designed and never hung. bwh07 files a picture database under Resources, so that is where it went, beside the Atlas and the Family Tree, on the same consult-vs-operates test. **Where it beats the thing it is chasing:** Bible Views is photographs browsed *by place* with no idea which passage a picture belongs to; every plate here is chapter-scoped, so the index is ordered **canonically** and takes the #280 book scope — narrowed to Acts it stops being a bag of pictures and becomes Acts, in order, which is why `illustrationAnchor` is scope-aware rather than a fixed sort. Two defects came out with it: the viewer's filmstrip used to append **the entire library** under a header reading "Related illustrations", and `page_chrome_pass_test` matched `BorderRadius.circular(` only — blind to `BorderRadius.vertical(top: Radius.circular(16))`, the form *every bottom sheet uses*, which is how `bible_reading_pane` was recorded PASSED in v1.6.80 while carrying ten of them; ceilings that moved the day the matcher widened are annotated as **re-measurements, not regressions**. Thumbnails were correctness, not polish: 55 survey plates up to 1920×3288 are 95 MP, a grid decoding ~48 MP **never produced a frame**, and `cacheWidth` does not prevent the decode — 420 px copies are 2.4 MB total, regenerated by `tools/gen_map_thumbs.sh`, falling back to the full plate when absent. **Verified on dev by screenshot** at 1460 px in zh-Hant: the 插畫 entry in 資源, the grid in canonical Genesis order, kind chips summing to 1192, square corners and hairlines; the thumbnail asset was confirmed fetched in the network log, and the CDN plates confirmed **20/20 loaded** via `img.naturalWidth` — a stronger check than a frame grab, and necessary because full-page `Page.captureScreenshot` **hangs** on this grid while `eval` keeps answering (a clipped capture succeeds; the driver exits 0 printing nothing, which reads exactly like a broken step file). Book references follow the **reading version** per #283, so "Genesis 3" under a KJV reader in a Chinese UI is the rule, not the defect. **v1.6.82 has no entry above** — same killed run.
- **v1.6.87** — **the Analysis strip was measuring its labels in the wrong script, and the toggle the reader asked for (#297).** The reported symptom was 「…」: in a Chinese UI the labelled strip wrapped to two rows and then **cut every long label** — 原文…, 相关…, 经文…. The cause is one constant. `_kMinLabelledTabWidth = 66` decided whether a tab could carry a name, and its own comment described it as *"about six characters of 11.5 px text"* — **a Latin measurement applied to full-width glyphs**. 原文研读 is four of them, ~46 px of text before the icon, the 5 px gap and the padding, so the rule answered "the labels fit" and Flutter then ellipsised them. **English never showed it, Chinese always did**, and the suite cannot see it at all: `find.text` matches an ellipsised label by its full text, so a green run proves nothing here. The fix is to stop guessing — the width a labelled tab needs is now **measured with a `TextPainter` from the strings the strip will actually draw**, at the **selected tab's w700**, because measuring the lighter weight would leave the one tab the reader is looking at as the only one that gets cut. `analysisStripLayout` takes it as a parameter and stays pure and unit-tested; the label list is public for the same reason the measurement exists, so nothing can measure a copy that has drifted from what is drawn. **Two quieter faults came out with it.** The widget and the measurement now share one font size and one CJK fallback list, since a measurement of a different style is not a measurement; and **the three existing tests that asserted "labels come back when there is room" hard-coded that same 66** — under the test font, whose glyphs are squares, English needs ~147, so those tests were pumping a strip too narrow for its own labels and asserting about the wrong width. They derive it now. **The toggle** goes beside the pane's own collapse chevron — 「可不可以有toggle可以切换在这个menu旁边」 — with **two states, not three**: the strip already chooses well, so the only question worth a control is "decide for me" vs "I want the names", one tap either way. Automatic stays the default and still refuses more than two rows, because past two the strip eats the pane it labels. **Asked directly, it spends rows rather than letters** — down to one tab a row, which at any width this app can reach (256 px and up) is wider than any label, so the request is *always* honoured and the toggle can never look like the dead buttons of #294. **Verified by screenshot on dev in 繁體 at a ~264 px Analysis pane** — the wider of the two scripts: automatic draws two balanced rows of six icons; asked for names it draws four rows of three with **all twelve labels whole**, 原文研讀 / 相關經文 / 經文列表 included, no ellipsis anywhere; the button shows a pressed-in active state and the choice **survives a reload**. 简体 is covered by the widget tests (all twelve Simplified labels found whole at 420 px) — seeding `flutter.locale` did not switch the deployed app's UI language, which is a harness gap, not a product one. Also carried out on this deploy: **v1.6.86's #298 reader fix, which the watchdog killed mid-release** — dev had been sitting one commit behind since.
- **v1.6.88** — **the word list's romanisation described a different word than the row it sat on, and the same mistake was mirrored in the Word Study header (#303).** A word list row is a **lemma** — every inflection of ἔρχομαι counts into one row — but the row printed the **most frequent** surface form beside the **first occurrence's** transliteration, two different words on one line: John's G2064 row read `ἔρχεται · ēlthen`. Measured across all 66 books before the fix: **8,030 of 58,447 rows (13.7%)** printed a romanisation of a form other than the one shown, worst in Leviticus 24.6%, Exodus 23.1%, 1 Kings 20.9%, John 19.1%. The row is a lemma, so **all** of its romanisation now comes from the lexicon keyed by Strong's, and both defects vanish by construction. **The corpus only carries `t` for the Greek NT — 138,013 tokens have one, 300,808 Old Testament tokens have `t: null`** — so the Hebrew Bible was not showing a *wrong* romanisation, it was showing **none**; every OT row gains one for the first time. **There are two romanisation systems in this repo and they do not agree:** `assets/originals` uses plain macrons (`ēlthen`, `iēsou`), the Strong's lexicon uses the classic diacritics (`érchomai`, `Iēsoûs`, `ʼĕlôhîym`), and they are not convertible without inventing data — hence the rule now enforced by test: **a value describing a LEMMA comes from the lexicon, a value describing one OCCURRENCE comes from the corpus, and the two never share a column.** The **76 extended MorphGNT numbers** (G6000–G6095 with gaps, plus G6897/G6986/G7013/G7530/G9577/G9992) have no Strong's entry, and they get **no lemma and no romanisation at all** rather than the occurrence's — falling back there would restate the bug on exactly the rows nobody can check. **The audit found the same fault mirrored in `word_analysis_pane`**, which printed the occurrence form in red with the **lemma's** transliteration bracketed beside it (`ἦλθεν (érchomai)`); it now prints `lemma · translit` on its own line and **omits the lemma when it equals the form**, so `ὁ` reads just `ho`. Also fixed: the word-by-word **clipboard export** put `entry.translit ?? w.translit` in one column, mixing the two systems in a table a reader sorts in a spreadsheet; `context_words.dart` carried a first-occurrence value in a field **no widget ever read**, deleted rather than fixed. `StrongsService.lookupAll` exists because the precedent (`context_pane.dart`) awaited `lookup()` per row **and capped at 400 rows**, which would have silently dropped every lemma past the four-hundredth. **The trap that nearly hid a call site: a literal U+0000 byte in `lib/widgets/analysis_tabs.dart`** (a cache-key separator) made `grep -rn` print *"Binary file … matches"* with no lines and made **ripgrep skip the file silently** — a clean "no matches" for a file with four real hits, in an audit whose entire method was grep. It is written as a `\u` escape now; suspect this whenever a repo-wide grep comes back emptier than expected, and note that the same byte makes a shell heredoc fail outright — including the one that tried to write this sentence. The briefing's second figure (91.5% of 58,365) counts the ~39,500 rows with **no** occurrence romanisation as mismatches; of the 18,872 rows that had one, **13,924 (73.8%)** differed from the lemma's even after stripping diacritics — same data, different denominator, so **quote the denominator with the number**. **Verified by screenshot on dev in 繁體:** the Word List over John 1 showing `ἦν → G1510 · εἰμί · eimí`, `ἐρχόμενον → G2064 · ἔρχομαι · érchomai` (the reported row) and the collapse rule on `ὁ → G3588 · ho`; and the Word Study header over the LXX+WH line showing `ἀρχῇ` above `ἀρχή · archē` and `ὁ` above a bare `ho`. `flutter analyze` exit 0, **2,103 tests** exit 0, ten of them new and four of those loading the shipped `john.json` so the assertion is about the data that ships.

- **v1.6.89** — **the systematic data-integrity audit (#304), and the three editions that filed verses under labels nothing could resolve.** #303's premise was that 8,030 wrong rows surfaced only because one reader looked at one row; the question this run answers is **how many more has nobody looked at**. Method: cross-check assets that describe the same fact from different directions, so a disagreement is a bug in one of them. **Ten checks, 155k+ tagged keys, 438,821 tagged words, 294,820 verses, 279,597 cross-references, 9,856 place links** — full table in the new `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md`, counts reported **even where zero**, because "checked 31,102 references, found 0 gaps" is a different claim from "we believe it is fine". **Three defects found and repaired, all of them a LABEL, none a word of scripture.** (1) **`cuvs-plus` numbered the whole of 1 Chronicles 22 one verse low** — what every other edition calls 22:1 (「大卫说：这就是耶和华神的殿」) was filed as **21:31**, so ch21 held 31 verses and ch22 held 18. The nasty part: `assets/tagged/cuvs-plus/` carried the *same* shift, so the text and its Strong's tags agreed with each other and nothing looked odd — while `assets/originals` is canonical, so **the Word Study pane showed a 和简+ reader the Hebrew of the NEXT verse for the entire chapter**. Verified before touching it by normalising away the 雅伟/耶和华 restoration and the 藉/借 and 么/吗 orthography: cuvs-plus 21:31 is character-identical to cuvs-yhwh 22:1 and 22:N is 22:N+1 for all eighteen. (2) **`leb` labelled Micah "Mic" and Nahum "Nah"** (152 verses) — `Verse.id` is `bookNameToEnglish[book] ?? book`, so an LEB highlight in Micah was keyed `Mic-1-1` and never matched the same verse in any other edition, which is precisely the cross-version persistence that id exists to provide; ids also began `000`, book zero. (3) **`cuvs-yhwh-tr` labelled 2 Timothy 提摩太后書** (83 verses), simplified 后 in an otherwise traditional name, matching neither key in the table. **All three failed the new guards before the repair — checked by stashing the repaired assets and watching the test name all three, not by assuming.** Repairs are reproducible via `tools/repair_reference_defects.py`, and it preserves each file's own layout (leb/cuvs-yhwh-tr are indent-2, cuvs-plus and the tagged files are minified) — guessing wrong buried a 19-verse correction in a 217,000-line reflow on the first attempt. **THE WORST FINDING IS NOT FIXED, DELIBERATELY, AND IS THE NEXT SLICE: `assets/originals` is numbered in HEBREW versification and every display surface asks it in ENGLISH.** Found by asking fix (1)'s question of the whole corpus — all five tagged layers disagree with originals *identically* (Joel 21, Numbers 16, 1 Chronicles 15, 1 Kings 14, Job 8), and that uniformity is the tell: one systematic difference, not five import bugs. `OriginalsService` joins them by the **reader's** `'$chapter:$verse'` (`originals_service.dart:49`). Joel end to end: originals has four chapters (20/27/5/21, the Hebrew book), every edition has three (20/32/21); English **2:28** asks `originals['2:28']`, Hebrew ch2 ends at 27, pane is **empty**; English **3:1** asks `originals['3:1']` and is handed וְהָיָה אַחֲרֵי כֵן אֶשְׁפּוֹךְ — **the Hebrew of 2:28**. Not repaired because the fix is a sourced Hebrew↔English mapping and deriving it arithmetically is guessing at scripture. Also documented, not fixed: **`leb` is missing Judges (618 verses) and Obadiah (21) outright** — an import gap, and scripture is never invented to close one; 2,203 words carry no morphology code (the brief's prediction that codes were *undecodable* is **false** — all 4,037 distinct codes decode; the blanks are absent codes); and no date in `bible_timeline`/`hebrew_kings`/`family_tree` carries a per-record source. **Clean and worth saying so:** concordance.json's 14,039 entries all sum correctly **and agree exactly with `assets/originals`** (0 numbers in one and not the other, 0 totals disagreeing, 0 book-level cells disagreeing); all 9,856 `bible_places` links resolve with 0 unmapped abbreviations (Bethlehem is present); 0 Strong's numbers in the wrong testament; the 76 lexicon-less numbers are all extended-MorphGNT covering 86 words, enumerated in the test so a 77th cannot appear unnoticed. `flutter analyze` exit 0, **2,108 tests** exit 0, five of them new. **Verified on the deployed dev build by fetching the assets back**: `leb.json` serves Micah/Nahum and no longer serves Mic/Nah; `cuvs-plus.json` serves 1 Chronicles 21 with 30 verses and 22 with 19, and 22:1 is 「大卫说：这就是耶和华神的殿」.

- **v1.6.90** — **the reader's chapter-and-verse is not the original's, and for 1,823 references the Word Study pane answered with a different verse's Hebrew (#304, check 9).** `assets/originals` is numbered in the Masoretic tradition for the Hebrew Bible and the critical text for the Greek; every edition we ship is numbered in the English one. `OriginalsService` joined them on the reader's own `"chapter:verse"`, so where the traditions diverge the pane showed authoritative-looking Hebrew **about a verse the reader was not reading** — the worst kind of defect for a tool whose Greek nobody can check against anything except us. **The 152/204 the previous run reported was an undercount by an order of magnitude:** comparing key sets sees only verses missing from one side, while walking every reference found **1,823 resolving to another verse and 152 to none, across 36 books** — Psalms 979, 1 Chronicles 103, Deuteronomy 65, Daniel 62, Nehemiah 60, Exodus 58. **The table is derived, not asserted, because arithmetic on verse counts would be guessing at scripture.** `tools/derive_versification.py` reads the mapping out of the text instead: per book, a **banded monotone alignment** of the original's Strong's-number stream against three independent tagged translations (KJV+S, 和合本雅伟版, BSB), beads (1,1)/(1,2)/(2,1)/(1,3)/(1,0)/(0,1) scored by Dice over each verse's Strong's set, keeping a row **only where all three agree** — 110 rows failed unanimity and were dropped rather than guessed. Per-word voting was tried first and left 10% noise; it has no order constraint, which is the whole reason the monotone model works. **Three independent confirmations that it is TRUE and not merely self-consistent.** (1) It **reproduces the documented Masoretic table it was never given**: Gen 31:55→32:1, Lev 6:1→5:20, Num 16:36→17:1, 1 Kgs 4:21→5:1, 1 Chr 6:1→5:27, Job 41:1→40:25, Joel 2:28→3:1, Mal 4:1→3:19, 2 Cor 13:14→13:13, 3 John 14→14+15, and every psalm superscription including the four two-line ones (51, 52, 54, 60). (2) The **`absent` list fell out of the data** as exactly the **sixteen Received-Text verses** — Mt 17:21/18:11/23:14, Mk 7:16/9:44/9:46/11:26/15:28, Lk 17:36/23:17, Jn 5:4, Ac 8:37/15:34/24:7/28:29, Rom 16:24 — and no others; nothing in the pipeline knows that list, it emerges from KJV-vs-BSB disagreement because KJV follows the Received Text and BSB the critical one, so **their disagreements are evidence, not noise**. The Hebrew Bible adds exactly one, also unprompted: **Nehemiah 7:68**, the horses and mules, absent from BHS — a reader there was being shown the Hebrew for camels. (3) **A text-support gate on every changed row**: the new target must resemble the reader's own tagged verse at least as well as the identity it replaces. **1,971 improved, 19 equal, 0 degraded.** A deletion cannot be scored that way ("we now show nothing" scores 0 against everything, so the ordinary gate would always revert it and restore the wrong Hebrew) and is gated on the **rival claim** instead. **Fixed at the one join point, so all fifteen callers are corrected together**, and two directions are kept deliberately distinct: `originalKeys` is **coverage** (a reference returns every original verse it renders, so a merged psalm heading displays in full) while `rekeyBook` is a **partition** (each original verse lands under exactly one reference, so statistics and search cannot double-count) — **438,821 original words before the re-key and 438,821 after**. The pane now also distinguishes **"we have no data" from "there is nothing to have"**, a new localised string for the verses the critical edition omits. **Residue stated rather than papered over: Revelation 12:18 stays at identity** because KJV renders it inside 13:1 (Dice 0.973 vs 0.914) and BSB inside 12:17 (1.000 vs 0.927), so no single table serves both editions — under-coverage, not a false statement, which is the trade this document prefers. Ten more verses stayed at identity for want of unanimity (Ps 135:1-2, Zeph 2:1-2, Jn 7:53-8:1, Rev 12:17/13:1, Lk 1:1-2) and identity was checked to be right in every case; the disagreements are gaps in the Chinese tagging, not numbering differences. `flutter analyze` exit 0, **2,123 tests** exit 0, and the new suite **re-proves the map from the shipped assets** rather than pinning constants, so a regression in the data fails the run. **Verified by screenshot on deployed dev v1.6.90 in 繁體, both directions of the defect:** 約珥書 **2:28**, which returned nothing at all before, now shows H1961 וְהָיָה · H310 אַחֲרֵי · H8210 אֶשְׁפּוֹךְ and its WTT row in Browse — a row that simply did not exist when the lookup returned null — while 約珥書 **3:1**, which was handed 2:28's "I will pour out my spirit", now shows H3588 כִּי · H2009 הִנֵּה · H3117 בַּיָּמִים · H1992 הָהֵמָּה, which is Masoretic 4:1.

- **v1.6.91** — **the LEB shipped as a bad scrape: two books missing, and eight books ending with a word no Bible contains (#304).** `assets/leb.json` arrived at `6f13365 Initial commit` from the YsWords fork and had never been checked; the previous run documented its missing Judges and Obadiah as unfixable because **scripture is never invented to close a gap**. It is fixable with a *second copy of the same edition*, and chasing the two books turned up **five** defect classes with one signature — a scrape that lost page boundaries. **R1** Judges (618) and Obadiah (21) absent entirely, **639 verses restored**; **R2** two verses merged into one record so the second reference did not exist, **11 split**; **R3** a clause of the verse simply gone, **12 restored**; **R4** the **next book's title glued onto a book's last verse**, **8 trimmed**; **R5** three records filed under the book label `"The"`. **R2 and R4 are the ones that matter, because they read as scripture and are not**: `Hebrews 1:10` did not exist — 1:9 held both verses — so every cross-reference to it resolved to nothing or to the wrong verse, and `Romans 16:24` ended "…Amen. **Corinthians**" while `1 Peter 5:14` ended "…in Christ. **Peter**", so a reader quoting the verse quoted a word no edition of the Bible contains. **Nothing was invented, and the witness was measured before it was trusted for a single word**: over the 30,431 verses both copies hold they agree on **30,276 (99.491%)** whitespace-blind, which establishes edition identity, and converting the witness's HTML conventions into this repo's house style reproduces our own text **exactly in 30,151 (99.080%)** — that second figure is the **honest error bar on the imported material** and the residue is 125 Psalms structural differences plus ~155 verses where *our* copy is itself inconsistent about the space before a note marker. **Every repair is a containment check, so the script can only add text it has proven missing:** R3 asserts our text is a **prefix** of the witness's and appends only the tail; R4 asserts the trailing token equals the next book's name **and** that what remains ends in sentence-final punctuation (without that second clause it cuts into a sentence); R2 asserts our record **opens with** the first half and that what we hold is a **suffix** of the second. R2's guard began as strict equality and failed on all 11 — the diagnosis showed **one uniform cause** (our copy had also dropped the word introducing the quotation: `saying,` ×9, `and`, `And,`), and that uniformity is what licensed weakening it. **A hypothesis I killed by calibrating rather than assuming:** I believed the witness truncated block quotations and would corrupt poetic Judges 5, and built a word-count-ratio detector against BSB to catch it — the suspect verses scored 0.89–1.25 against a **30,169-verse median of 1.062**, and Judges/Obadiah a median 1.050, so **the witness is the correct copy and ours was the defective side**. The whole model inverted. **Also resolved the other way:** Romans 16:25-27, 2 Cor 13:14 and Acts 19:41 are **not** defects — the LEB carries the Romans doxology *inside* `<note: Some manuscripts include vv. 25-27, …>` on 16:24, so those verses genuinely do not exist in this edition. **After the repair LEB has 21 gaps and all 21 are known critical-text omissions** (Mt 17:21/18:11/23:14, Mk 7:16/9:44/9:46/11:26/15:28, Lk 17:36/23:17, Jn 5:4, Ac 8:37/15:34/19:41/24:7/28:29, Rom 16:25-27, 2 Cor 13:14, Neh 7:68) — enumerated one by one, nothing left over, which is a far stronger claim than "21 gaps remain". Repaired reproducibly by **`tools/repair_leb.py`** (idempotent; run with no flags to verify, `--write` to apply), and **matching the file's existing indent-2 shape is load-bearing** — a compact rewrite of the same data made the diff **213,865 deleted lines** instead of 4,581 added / 52 deleted, the second time that trap has cost a cycle. **Two standing guards generalise the classes across all 11 editions:** every edition must carry every book of the canon it covers (NT-only editions distinguished by whether they contain Genesis, not a hardcoded list — **this one would have caught the missing books on day one**), and no book may end with the name of the book that follows it (language-agnostic: strip a leading numeral from the next book's own label). Both pass everywhere now, plus a five-point regression pinning the repair. `flutter analyze` exit 0, **2,126 tests** exit 0, three suites new. **Known residue, stated rather than papered over: the witness drops the LEB's `{…}` idiom braces entirely** (0 of 31,102 records) whereas our 64 original books carry them in 8,041 verses (26%), so the 660 imported verses ship **without that notation** — under-coverage of a marker, not a false statement, the same trade as Rev 12:18. **Verified by fetching the asset back off deployed dev v1.6.91**: 31,199 records, 66 books, Judges 618, Obadiah 21, no `"The"`, `Hebrews 1:10` opens `And, "You, Lord`, `1 Peter 5:14` ends `who are in Christ.` **The wider lesson for the next reader: the code in this repo has been reviewed, the data it was seeded with never was.** Anything inherited from the fork should be assumed unchecked until a cross-check says otherwise.

- **v1.6.92** — **the Qere lives in a footnote, so 1,235 words of the Hebrew Bible had no parse at all (#304, check 2a).** 2,203 of 438,821 tagged words showed a blank parsing line in Word Study; the previous run recorded it as an upstream gap needing its own slice. **It was not upstream.** OSHB encodes the **Ketiv** as the verse's own `<w>` and the word actually **read aloud** one level down in a following sibling — `<note type="variant"><rdg type="x-qere"><w>` — so anything that iterates the verse's `<w>` children never sees the Qere. Two further shapes needed handling and are only reachable by walking `list(v)` in document order rather than by tag: **Qere without Ketiv** (read but not written, 10 cases, existing *only* inside the note) and **Ketiv without Qere** (a `<rdg>` with zero `<w>` children, 6 cases). Reading the Qere as authoritative and letting the Ketiv gap-fill takes the **Hebrew Bible from 1,252 untagged words to 1**. **That the old merge left those slots blank rather than borrowing the Ketiv's code is the only reason this was a coverage bug and not a wrongness bug:** measured, **653 of the 1,244 K/Q slots carry a DIFFERENT parse on the Qere than on the Ketiv**, so the obvious "fix" of borrowing would have printed a false parsing on over half of them with nothing looking odd. Blank was the honest failure, and the check that says so is worth more than the fix.
