Greek adds ~85 more via a **movable-nu** match (ἀπέχουσιν/ἀπέχουσι is one word with one parse, and SBLGNT may carry either), scoped to the environments where the nu is actually movable — `-σι`, `-ε`, `-τι` — and for a non-verb only where MorphGNT's own slots say **dative plural** (10-char code, case at index 6 = `D`, number at index 7 = `P`); ἐστί is why `-τι` is in that list and my first environment set was wrong without it. **A wider fallback was measured and REJECTED, and the rejection is the most important thing in this entry:** "if the untagged form appears elsewhere in the same verse with exactly one code, reuse it" closes **179** more Greek words and is **wrong** — SBLGNT's only τοῦ at Matthew 3:16 is the **neuter** of ἀπὸ τοῦ ὕδατος while the received text's second τοῦ is the **masculine** of τοῦ Θεοῦ, so the rule is plausible, prints a confident wrong gender, and no test in this repo could tell. It is written into `tools/merge_morphology.py`'s docstring so it is not rediscovered as a good idea. Caught only because I dumped both word sequences side by side **before** implementing. **Total 2,203 → 869 of 438,821 words (0.50% → 0.20%), 1,334 newly parsed. Rewriting all 66 asset files on the strength of "the number went down" is not evidence**, so the regeneration was fingerprinted before and after against what must NOT move: **0 words of text changed, 0 Strong's numbers changed, 0 transliterations changed, 0 parses lost**. It also surfaced the **2 parses that did change, both corrections** — 1 Chronicles 9:4 `בָּנִי` (H1137, the proper name Bani) had been parsed as a common-noun construct and is now `HNp`, and Proverbs 8:35 recovered a dropped 1cs suffix — neither of which is visible in a 440k-word diff without the fingerprint. **The 869 that remain are a fact about the editions, not a TODO**: the shipped Greek originals are a **received text** and SBLGNT is a **critical** one, so 868 readings have nothing to take a parse from, 222 of them being John 7:53–8:11 and Romans 16:25–27 entire — that exact set is now pinned by a test, so if it grows something regressed. The single Hebrew survivor is at **2 Samuel 18:20**. **Word Study now SAYS so instead of rendering nothing**: a missing parse prints a bordered line naming the corpus it was measured against (SBLGNT for Greek, Open Scriptures/WLC for Hebrew), because a blank that explains itself is information and a blank that does not is a bug report. **Corpus counts in tests are measurements and they move** — 1,334 new parses took `morph_search_service_test.dart` from 6,129 hits over 299,556 scanned to 6,192 over 300,807; rather than bumping the one failing number I re-derived **every** count in that file, confirmed the other three (Genesis qal-perfect 698, Matthew aorist-active-imperative 602, Aramaic Aphel 24) genuinely unchanged, and wrote the *reason* for the movement into the test. `flutter analyze` exit 0, **2,128 tests** exit 0, two new. **NEW DEFECT FOUND AND DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED, with the reasoning recorded:** four verses print the Ketiv **and** the Qere as two consecutive words — 2 Samuel 18:20 (`כי על על כן`, a doubled word to any reader), Jeremiah 51:3, Ezekiel 48:16, Proverbs 8:35. Measured the neighbouring phenomenon so the two are not conflated: the **1,257 unpointed Hebrew words** corpus-wide are the ordinary Ketiv convention and are correct. Deleting a word from shipped scripture is a text-editorial decision and the right repair is almost certainly a reader-side K/Q marker, so it went to `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md`'s queue rather than into this diff. **Verified on deployed dev v1.6.92, not assumed**: six `assets/originals` files fetched back off the deploy are byte-identical to the repo's, John serves 228 untagged of 15,635 words and Romans 89 of 7,111 while Genesis, 1 Chronicles and Proverbs serve **0**, 2 Samuel serves exactly **1** (the doubled `עַל` at 18:20), John 8:1 is wholly unparsed, and 1 Chronicles 9:4 Bani serves `HNp`. **Screenshot** of the new box on dev: clicking Ἰησοῦς in the BGT line of 約翰福音 8:1 pins a Word Study that now reads 「無詞形分析。希臘文詞形取自 SBLGNT，本處經文的部分讀法不見於該底本，故無可依據的分析。」 where it previously rendered nothing. The Hebrew wording of that box was **not** screenshotted — its only instance corpus-wide is 2 Samuel 18:20 and the driver could not reach that reference — so it was verified instead by finding all six new localised strings (zh-Hans/zh-Hant/en, both variants) in the deployed `main.dart.js`; the report says which claim rests on which. **The lesson for the next reader: when data is missing, check whether the plausible substitute is actually EQUAL before either congratulating the old code or replacing it** — here it was equal only 48% of the time.

- **v1.6.93** — **233 references printed the edition's typesetting instruction where the reader expected scripture (#304, check 14).** 和合本 combines verses under one number and prints 「見上節」 in the number column of the ones it swallowed; the Septuagint file stores the literal string `OMIT` for verses the critical text lacks. Both are *instructions to a typesetter*, and the app was rendering them as the verse's text — so 詩篇 8:7 read「见上节」in the reader, `copyVerseText` pasted it into a sermon as though it were the psalm, and a search for 上節 returned **227 hits that are not scripture**. **The previous audit saw this and deferred it as "presentation, not accuracy"; that reasoning was wrong and the doc now says so.** A string the reader will quote is a claim about the text regardless of why it is in the file. **The census is the deliverable, not the fix** — 295,527 records walked, every non-scripture reference classified and counted: **213 merged** (71 each in cuvs-yhwh / cuvs-yhwh-tr / cuvs-plus, four stored spellings including the long form 「合和譯本並入上一節」), **3 forward-merge** 「见下节」, **16 `OMIT`** in lxxwh, **1 blank** in biblexg-v2-tr. **The head is not `verse - 1`, and the data proves it rather than the comment asserting it:** merges CHAIN — 詩篇 8:7 **and** 8:8 are both 「见上节」 and both belong to verse **6**, so the obvious resolver would have sent the reader from one placeholder to another. `mergedVerseHeads` walks the chapter forward and remembers the last reference that had words; a test pins the Psalm 8 shape verbatim, and asserts that an `OMIT` or a blank can never *become* a head. **Whole-match, never `contains`** — 「除酵节，又名逾越节，近了。」 contains 上节 and is real scripture, so a substring rule would have blanked the verse it was meant to protect; the recogniser matches the trimmed string exactly (plus the `<note:…>` wrapper the editions use), with a 24-character length gate so it never scans a real verse. **The note is localised to the UI locale, not the edition's script** — a 繁體 reader on a 简体 Bible gets 「與第 29 節合併印行」, because the sentence is the app speaking about the edition, not the edition speaking. **Where the logic lives is the point:** the whole rule is a pure file, `lib/utils/verse_text_absence.dart`, with no Flutter import — the reader, Browse, the clipboard and the search-key builders all ask it the same question, which is what made the missing surface findable. **One was missing:** `verse_popup_sheet.dart`, reached from sermon-citation chips, both rendered and *copied* the raw marker; grepping every caller of `parseScripture|sanitizeVerseText` is how it turned up, and it is fixed in the same commit. KWIC and the concordance are covered because they read the tagged layer, which placeholders have no entry in — that is **reasoning, not measurement**, and the doc labels it as such. **The corpus test is deliberately wider than the fix, because a census alone is circular:** if an edition adopted a new phrasing tomorrow the count would just be updated. A second test greps every short verse mentioning the verse above or below (`上[节節]|下[节節]|并入|並入|OMIT`) and fails on **any** that the recogniser does not classify — 0 today, so drift surfaces in CI instead of on the reader's screen. A third asserts all 213 merged references resolve to a head that actually has words. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,148 tests** exit 0 (+20). **Verified by screenshot on deployed dev v1.6.93, not assumed:** 創世紀 24:30 in the 雅繁+ row now reads **「與第 29 節合併印行」** in muted italic — the correct head, the correct script for the 繁體 UI, and no 見上節 anywhere — while the BSB and LXX+WH rows for the same reference show their own real text, which is the per-edition behaviour the fix requires. **Not verified:** the `OMIT` note and the English strings were exercised only by unit test. **The lesson: a placeholder is data about the book, and the app must never let it pass for the book.**

- **v1.6.95** — **the search-statistics chart drew the 500-verse cap and called it the word (#308).** The report was a question about a label — 「the 27 in John, is it the number of actual word frequency in John or the number of 'verses'?」 — and the label was indeed missing: `Most in: John 27` never said what 27 counted, while the same word occurs 37 times in John, so a reader arriving from software that plots occurrences was wrong by ten. bwh23 makes it an explicit menu choice ("Number of verses in the book containing a hit" vs "Number of hits in the book") for exactly this reason. **Measuring it found something worse under it.** `concordance.json` describes each word twice — an uncapped per-book map of OCCURRENCES (`b`, sums exactly to the total) and a VERSE list (`r`) that the build pipeline stops at **500 entries in canonical order** — and the strip tallied the second one. **123 of 14,039 entries reach that cap, and 58 of them then name the wrong heaviest book.** H3068 (יהוה) drew as **3 books peaking in Exodus 341**; the truth is **36 books peaking in Jeremiah 712**. The header directly above already said 「僅列出前 500 節」, so the app admitted the list was cut and then charted the cut as if it were the shape of the divine name. **The fix is not a label, it is a choice of source:** the unit is now a declared property (`HitUnit`) that every builder must name and every surface prints, and `strongsDistribution` draws **whichever description is a CENSUS** — verses while the list is whole, the uncapped occurrence map once it is not, and, where neither is complete, **no chart at all** plus a sentence saying why. Two cases hit that last branch: a composed expression (`G3588 AND G2532` is set algebra over two prefixes that both end inside Matthew — the result is 335 verses and every one of them is in Matthew), and a scoped result over a capped entry, where the whole-Bible map describes a different corpus than the listed verses do and so can be neither the primary count nor the secondary one. Where both counts are known the strip prints **both** (`約翰福音 27 (37×)`), because the pair settles the distinction permanently; only the leading book carries it, since three pairs will not fit a 240 px pane, and the tooltip cannot be relied on to carry it instead — **a tablet has no hover** (#299). **It also closes the unit mix #290 documented and deferred:** the Stats tab printed `inScope / total` as capped VERSES over uncapped OCCURRENCES. A whole-book limit is now answered by summing the occurrence map over those books (same unit, no cap); a chapter-level limit can only be answered from the verse list, so it says so in its own hint string, drops the denominator rather than inventing a comparable one, and prints **`≥N`** when the list it read was cut — a floor is true where a count would not be. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,174 tests** exit 0 (+13, including a regression that freezes both H3068 shapes so the Exodus/3-books answer can never come back). **Verified by screenshot on deployed dev v1.6.95, not assumed** — all four cases in the 繁體 UI at 1400 and at 1000 (just above the 992 gate): H3068 → 「最多出現於（按出現次數）：耶利米書 712 · 詩篇 687 · 申命記 548 · 36 卷書」; G25 → 「共 110 節 · 143 處」 with 「最多出現於（按節）：約翰福音 27 (37×) · 約翰一書 17 · 路加福音 9 · 22 卷書」, every figure matching the asset exactly; the composed expression → no bars and the truncation sentence; and no overflow or ellipsis at the narrow width. **Not verified by screenshot:** the Stats-tab scoped hint and the `≥N` floor, which were exercised only by unit test and by reading the data. **Found and NOT fixed, deliberately** (recorded in `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md`): a *scoped search* over a capped entry silently returns wrong RESULTS, not just a wrong chart — `l jer` + `H3068` filters the 500-ref prefix, which ends in Leviticus, and yields **zero** hits where Jeremiah holds 712 occurrences. That needs `concordance.json` regenerated from `assets/originals/` against an asset-size budget, which is its own item. **The lesson: when a surface and the data disagree about what was counted, do not label the surface — find out which of the two sources is a census, and refuse to draw when neither is.**

- **v1.6.96** — **a scoped search read a 500-verse prefix as the whole word, and answered zero (#304, check 15).** `concordance.json`'s `r` stopped at 500 refs per Strong's number, and `r` is in **canonical order** — so a capped entry was never a 500-verse sample, it was Genesis-through-wherever-500-landed (H3068's ended at Leviticus 2:14). Anything that *filters* or *intersects* the list inherited that silently, because a filter over a prefix returns a well-formed empty answer: `l jer` then `H3068` returned **zero verses for the divine name in Jeremiah**, where it occurs 712 times, with no qualification. 123 of 14,039 entries reached the cap. #308 found this the day before and recorded it as "blocked on an asset-size decision" — but the decision was only ever blocked on a **measurement**: uncapping costs 1.75 MB raw, **307 KB gzipped**, and there was no trade-off, just an unmeasured number standing in for one. A compact ordinal/delta re-encoding would have been cheaper and was rejected as the *less* conservative option, since it needs a canon-ordinal table between the generator and every consumer and this repo has already shipped one versification defect from that class (check 9). The regenerated asset was proved a **pure superset before shipping** — 13,916 entries identical, 123 strict extensions, 0 refs dropped or moved, `n` and `b` untouched — so the fix cannot have changed an answer that was already right. **The trap worth carrying forward: removing a limitation obliges you to audit everything built to apologise for it.** #308's truncation apparatus inferred "cut" from a list that *reached* the cap, so left in place it would have printed 「首 500 節」 above a complete 5,522-verse list — the fix manufacturing its own untruth on exactly the words it had just repaired, with all 2,180 tests green, because the tests pinned the machinery rather than the claim. The signal is repointed at the one real remaining incompleteness (a **wildcard** expansion stopped at its 300-number limit; measured: 13 prefixes exceed it), and the Stats tab's `≥` prefix is gone. Five tests freeze it, two naming no number and both failing on the old data: **the books in `r` must equal the keys of `b`** (121 entries fail under the cap) and **no long list length may be shared by more than 20 entries** (a cap is invisible per-record, obvious in the distribution — 123 words all "happening" to occur exactly 500 times). `ConcordanceService.lookup` is now memoised; it regex-parses every ref and KWIC re-looks-up per page, which at 14× the list length is 6,977 matches per "load more". Verified on dev, not assumed: `l jer` + `H3068` now answers **共 614 節**, and unscoped **共 5,522 節 · 6,521 處** with the chart drawn across the whole canon (最多出現於（按節）：Psalms 627 (687×) · Jeremiah 614) — the two paths agreeing on Jeremiah's 614 independently.

- **v1.6.97** — **three sermons stopped a third of the way through in Traditional Chinese, and ten more are summaries wearing the clothes of a translation (#310).** `assets/sermons/` — ~45 MB of preaching the app presents exactly the way it presents scripture — had never been audited by #304. Two defects, found as two ratios. **First:** Han characters in each zh-TW body over its zh-CN source is **1.000 in the median and never below 0.9949**, except 100 (**0.353**), 369 (**0.425**) and 370 (**0.736**) — no gradient in between, so not a style. They do not read as broken: each tracks the Simplified for two paragraphs and then condenses to the end, so sermon 100 finishes while still setting up the parable it is named for. The defect is **upstream, not in this repo's ingest** — in the source corpus every `<id>.zh-TW.txt` is byte-identical to `<id>.zh-proofread.txt`, and 370 proves it at part level (part a abridged 8,427 Han vs 22,507, part b intact 30,807 vs 30,695, which is exactly why it kept 73%). **The conversion was derived, not assumed**, because the wrong OpenCC config would have quietly rewritten three files into a variant system the other 286 do not use: the shipped Traditional corpus uses 爲 **23,701** times against 為 635, 裏 10,262 against 裡 311, 着 6,915 against 著 375 — all the `s2t` forms, all the ones `s2tw`/`s2twp` flip — and `s2t(zh-CN)` reproduces **192 of 286** healthy files byte-for-byte and **99.866%** of characters overall, while the reverse holds for only 100, which is what fixes the direction. An earlier positional character diff produced a plausible "second conversion stage" (爲→為 ×328, "→「 ×297); it was an alignment artefact and **was overturned by measurement before anything was written** — re-run through `difflib.SequenceMatcher` the table dissolved into human proofreading, including real content edits (`'access'`→「進入的途徑」) that a regeneration must not invent. Index titles left alone: `titles['zh-TW']` is s2t of `titles['zh-CN']` in 285 of 289, the four exceptions are benign (「vs.」 set as 「對比」), and the body H1 agrees with the index in only 153 — so re-deriving titles would have churned correct data on worse evidence. **Second, and larger, found only by asking the other axis:** Chinese characters per English word is **1.403** in the median, and **ten files sit at 0.097–0.194 with nothing at all between them and 0.933**. Those ten zh-CN bodies are not translations, they are competent summaries — a tenth of the length, ordinary paragraphs, a proper closing line, sermon 126 opening 「讓我們先回顧這個診斷性比喻的核心信息」, i.e. prose written *about* the sermon. Nine of the ten are one topic batch ("The Parables of Jesus"); their Traditional bodies are faithful conversions **of the summary**, so both Chinese locales are affected. **This one cannot be repaired here and pretending otherwise would be the defect** — no full Chinese exists upstream, and writing ~85,000 words is translation, not a fix. So the conservative option, named as such: `index.json` marks them `"condensed"`, the page prints a hairline notice above the body naming the text a summary and pointing at the language that holds the whole sermon, and **the notice is prepended to the clipboard copy**, because a summary pasted into someone's notes without it is indistinguishable from the preaching and will be quoted as the preaching. Both thresholds sit in empty bands rather than in a gradient, and **both tests were proven by restoring the defective data and watching them fail** (85% of the Simplified; and the marked set must equal the re-measured set, so an unearned mark fails as loudly as an unmarked summary). `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,190 tests** exit 0 (+9). Verified by screenshot on deployed dev v1.6.97, not assumed: the Traditional body of 126 shows 「本篇為內容摘要，並非講道全文。完整講道見英文。」, the English body of the same sermon shows **nothing** (it is the complete one), and the Simplified body under an **English** UI shows "This text is a condensed summary, not the full sermon. The full text is available in English." — the sentence following the interface language rather than the body's, on one line, no overflow. Deployed assets re-fetched over HTTP and byte-matched to local, with all ten markers live. **Carried forward for the owner, not an engineering task:** the ten sermons need a real Chinese translation; until then the marking is the honest state. **The lesson: when a repair would mean generating the content, mark and disclose instead — and when a brief names one defect, measure the other axis before believing it is the only one.**
- **v1.6.99** — **the Phrasing pane read Greek and Hebrew only, so a reader who does not know them opened it and found nothing to phrase (#307).** BibleWorks' own diagrammer loads "any Bible version in BibleWorks as a source of text to be diagrammed" (`bwh25_Diagrammer.htm`), so this was a parity gap, not a design choice. Phrasing now has a **Translation / Original** source switch and defaults to the edition already open in the workbench. A translation carries no morph code, but a **tagged** one carries a Strong's number per run — and a conjunction is the one part of speech whose Strong's number IS its identity, so the **clause level survives translation**; the preposition (43 numbers, 99.4% of `P-`) and relative-pronoun (4 numbers, 99.94% of `RR`) lists were **counted out of `assets/originals`** with a majority test, which is what keeps ὡς G5613 (4 `P-` against 504 otherwise) from cutting a line before every "as" in the NT. **Verbals cannot survive** — mood is not recoverable from a number — so unsupported levels are **disabled with a reason** rather than left as controls that propose nothing, and the reason distinguishes a tagged edition from an untagged one, which proposes only verse breaks. Two silent-corruption risks closed: breaks/depths/relations are word **indices**, so translations get their own store key (`<version>.txt`) and analyses saved against the originals still load against the words they were made from; and the RTL test now reads the **script of the text**, not `strongs.startsWith('H')`, which would have mirrored the whole diagram for anyone phrasing Genesis in 雅简+. Chinese arrives one character at a time (`phrasingTokens`, punctuation carried so `join()` reproduces the verse) and is drawn without the inter-word gap Greek needs. Also the user's second ask: **line one indents**, clamped to a stop of its own (8) since it has no predecessor. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,224 tests** exit 0 (+21). Verified by screenshot on deployed dev: KJV John 1:1 phrases in English with the untagged notice, 雅简+ phrases in Chinese with Clauses live and + Verbals greyed, and line one's indent survived a full reload. **Deferred slice** (from the user's Word screenshot, not attempted): conjunction on its own line, (a)/(b) parallel-member labels, underline emphasis.
- **v1.6.101** — **80.6% of the BSB's word taps answered with the wrong lexicon entry, and the check that finds it is the second witness (#304, check 22).** The brief pointed at markup: two tagged layers were printing their source's alignment codes as scripture. Both are fixed — but repairing the second one uncovered something two orders of magnitude larger, which is the reason this entry exists. **22a, cuvs-yhwh:** the importer's `TAG_RE` knew only the well-formed code `<W([HG])(\d+)(x?)>`, and the hand-edited MySword module it reads has **133 damaged ones of 533,914** — a dropped bracket, a doubled `W`, a character typed into the middle. Unrecognised, they became TEXT, which drew them on screen (「他若`<H518>`行恶」) *and*, because a code that is text never splits the run, left 他若 carrying **H4672, the number of a word further on**. The first count in the doc (133 codes / 126 verses / 27 books) was **wrong and was corrected by re-measuring**: a paired-token regex cannot see `<WH3808怜恤` or `以色列>人`, so the honest figure is **329 stray characters in 169 verses across 29 books**. Repaired by recognising every damaged shape and then deciding, per shape, whether the *number* survives: **kept** where pure normalisation recovers it, **dropped** where the digits are in doubt (`3WH808`, `WH85x3`, `WG358x8`) or the prefix is not W/H/G — under-attribution is recoverable and a wrong Strong's is not. Five codes with a Chinese character inside were decided one at a time against the printed edition (`<你们WH935>` — 你们 *is* scripture at Nu 15:18; `<WH的8687>` — the 的 is not), and **two characters are dropped on purpose**, both checked as absent from the printed text. The `s` suffix was **not guessed**: a corpus-witness script asked how the same word is tagged everywhere the tags are well formed (厚待/H3190, H3318 出来, H7925 清早) and it reads as a plain lexical tag. Stray angle characters **329 → 0**, Latin letters **→ 0** (a test that earns its place because Jer 34:8's code had lost *both* brackets — `的仆人WH853x<WH5650>` — so a scan for `<…>` could never have seen it), agreement with the printed edition **27,448 → 27,592 verses**, the two Chinese lexicons regenerated **byte-identical**. **22b, bsb:** 726 HTML tags and **25,357 runs that are not words** (20,508 `. . .`, 4,849 `vvv`, the tables' two ways of writing "folded into a neighbouring phrase") are gone, and with them **24,586 lexicon taps on a spaced ellipsis**. **22c, and this is the finding:** re-reading the tables showed **311,267 of 386,063 runs — 80.6% — carried a Strong's number that was the first digit of the right one.** H7225 בְּרֵאשִׁית "in the beginning" shipped as **H7**; H6870 Zeruiah as **H6**; H8352 Seth as **H8**. One helper doing two jobs: `cell()` groups thousands, *correctly*, because the tables store a printed count like 74,600 as a number in some rows and text in others — fed the Strong's column it made `"7,225"` and the digit scan stopped at the comma. The same helper fed the column that orders the **original** words, where the grouped string failed `int()` and fell back to `0`, so **970,285 rows shared one sort key** and the implied-word walk — the whole point of that function's design — was running on nothing. Both now read through a `code()` accessor that never groups; the sort key is a **float**, because 72 rows carry a fractional one to place a split word between two whole-numbered originals. **Why nothing caught it, which is the part worth keeping: a truncated Strong's number is still a real Strong's number.** All **432,859** resolved against `assets/strongs/` before the fix as well as after — H7 has an entry and it looks plausible. Every test expressible about the *shape* of the data passed. What catches it is the **second witness**: `assets/tagged/cuvs-yhwh/` is an independent alignment of the same originals made from the other side of the language, and the two agreed on **13.36%** of each verse's numbers. They now agree on **92.79%** across the 30,746 verses both cover, and **that ratio is the regression test** — it needs no knowledge of the bug's shape. Verified by measurement, not assumed: **0** runs lost or gained a number, so the repair only ever corrected a value; distinct lexicon entries the layer can reach **1,861 → 13,115**; **30,838 of 31,086** verses reconstruct the printed text (was 8,269), the 248-verse residue bounded by test; and **all five other tagged layers were measured for the same signature** and sit in a 0.08–0.44% single-digit band with 12,795–14,193 distinct numbers, i.e. clean. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,244 tests** exit 0 (+12). Verified by screenshot on deployed dev v1.6.101, not assumed: BSB Genesis 1:1 draws H7225 · H430 · H1254 · H8064 · H776, tapping "In the beginning" opens the Word Study on **H7225 רֵאשִׁית *rêʾshîyth*** (it would have opened H7), and 雅简+ 列王纪上 21:8 — the verse whose damaged code used to swallow the whole relative clause — renders 「送给那些与拿伯同城居住的长老贵胄」 intact with every word carrying its own number and no code on screen. Deployed assets re-fetched over HTTP and byte-matched to local. **The lesson: a data defect that produces well-formed, resolvable, plausible output is invisible to every check written from inside the data — only an independent rendering of the same thing can see it, and this repo now has enough second witnesses to make that a cheap standing test.**
- **v1.6.103** — **15 Greek words told the reader a different word's meaning, and the corpus had never once been shown an outside witness (#304, check 24).** Every earlier check on `assets/originals` was **internal**: check 1 proved a Strong's number *resolves*, check 2b proved a morph code *decodes*, check 3b proved the concordance agrees with the text it indexes. All green, and **not one of them could catch a row that is well-formed and wrong** — a real number sitting on a word it does not describe. This is the largest asset the app ships (438,821 words) and the column set nobody had checked for **truth**. The witnesses were already in the repo, cached beside the merge tool that built these assets: **MorphGNT/SBLGNT** (CC BY-SA 3.0), which carries a lemma and **no Strong's numbers at all**, and the **Open Scriptures Hebrew Bible** (CC BY 4.0), which carries them directly. **The morphology is exact — 436,630 words against two independent editions, 0 disagreements**, and that result is what makes the rest findable: a morph code proven to reproduce MorphGNT is **independent of the number beside it**, so a row where the two contradict is a row where one is wrong. Hebrew Strong's: **0 in 299,567 compared** (the denominator is printed, because "0 disagreements" was also true before the check compared anything). Greek Strong's: **15 wrong**, several reversing a well-known verse — Luke 4:17, Luke 23:53 and Rom 9:26 said **"not" where the Greek says "where"** (Luke 23:53 holds both words in one clause, οὗ οὐκ ἦν, and tagged them identically); Acts 5:28 the same confusion pointing the other way; 2 Cor 11:1 said **"kill" for "bear with me"**; Acts 8:2 buried **"a crown" instead of Stephen**; Matt 5:32 and Luke 16:18 called a **divorced** woman "left behind"; Jas 1:25 gave a **verb's** entry to a word **its own morph column calls a noun**. **Not changed, and the reasons are in the repair script**: John 6:17 where the witness is wrong and we are right (ἤρχοντο is "they were *going* across the sea"), the feminine δούλη under G1401 which this corpus does every time, Eph 2:13 where two editors disagree about a category, Rom 8:24 where **both our columns agree with the witness and only our accent follows another edition** — a base-text question a tag edit cannot settle — and **3,779 lemma differences (2.76%)** that are Strong's 1890 spelling against a modern critical text (ἔπω/λέγω, Δαβίδ/Δαυίδ). **Two instrument errors are recorded because they nearly became findings**: comparing Hebrew **by position** invented **883 Strong's + 1,082 morph disagreements out of nothing** (the editions differ on Ketiv/Qere and maqqef, so 5,471 verses have different word counts and everything after the first difference compares against its neighbour — re-run through `difflib`, **1,965 → 0**, and an earlier draft of the doc reported a 1 Chr 9:4 disagreement **that does not exist**); and a Hebrew POS extractor that took the pronominal-suffix segment instead of the head produced **586 false positives**. Both had the same tell — findings **clustered inside single verses** — which is why every number here carries its denominator (**99.31%** of Greek and **99.59%** of Hebrew aligned; the 951 + 1,240 that did not are listed as unexamined). **A second, older defect surfaced from rebuilding the derived indexes rather than from the audit**: `assets/forms/` changed in **749** entries, which 15 words cannot explain — and rebuilding from the **unrepaired** originals changed **729**, so the shipped index had been stale since check 2a filled in missing morphology, and **1,243 word-forms had been showing no parse at all in the Word List** while the parse sat in `assets/originals` the whole time (**1,243 gained a code, 0 lost one**). `tools/audit_originals_witness.py` is kept rather than thrown away and **deliberately does not reuse `merge_morphology`'s Hebrew parser** — a witness that shares the accused's code cannot see the accused's errors, and rewriting it is what caught an off-by-one through Qere `<note>` elements. It takes `--originals`, so aimed at the previous revision it reports **6 open findings and exits 1**, and against this commit **0 and exits 0**. Both heuristics were adopted only after **measuring their whole-corpus false-positive count across a threshold grid** — the self-contradiction check returns **4 slots in 137,062 words**, and loosening it below 20% loses Luke 4:17 οὗ, a real defect standing at 3 of 19; ten apparent "conflicts" were **Greek using one lexeme in two syntactic functions** (τρίτον "a third time", ὑπὲρ ἐγώ "I more so") and are exempted as **named pairs**, never as a whole block, since John 6:23 ἀλλά "but" tagged G243 ἄλλος "other" only survives a narrow exemption. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,255 tests** exit 0 (+5); **all four new guards were shown to fail on the pre-fix data before being kept**, and the Dart transcription independently reproduces the Python instrument's exact findings (`ephesians 2:13 οἵ`, `τις|RI----NSM-`), which is the only reason to trust a transcription. Concordance moves as predicted: G4162 **0 → 1** (ποίησις occurs once in the NT and Jas 1:25 is it), G1991 4 → 3, G4736 6 → 7, token total unchanged at 438,821. Also committed: `tools/fix_lxx_untagged.py`, which produced v1.6.102's shipped data and had never been committed, leaving shipped data whose derivation existed on one machine. **Not verified by screenshot** — this run changed data and its indexes, not a rendering surface, and the claim is about what a tap reports, which the tests assert on the bytes. **The lesson: a green results table is a list of questions someone thought to ask. Check 24 was not on the "next" list at all — it came from noticing that check 2b proved codes *decode* and never that they are *true*. And repairing a source obliges you to diff the rebuilt derived assets against what was committed, because the excess is where the older defect is hiding.**
- **v1.6.105** — **the Settings type sliders advertised travel the workbench did not have: 11 of Font Size's 29 stops and 2 of Menu Size's 9 were inert (#311).** Font Size offers 12–40 pt and Menu Size 0.7–1.5×, but `WbType.resolve` clamped the derived scales to **0.75–1.6** and **0.8–1.4** — *narrower than the sliders themselves* — so dragging Font Size from 32 to 40 changed the label and moved nothing, and across the whole drag the workbench travelled **2.13×** while the control said **3.33×**. That is what 「跳动很小」 was describing, and it is a control lying about itself. **Why it was invisible:** the slider ends were literals in `settings_page.dart` and the clamp was *different* literals in `workbench_theme.dart`, with nothing connecting them — so the fix is not better numbers but a single source: `app_settings.dart` now exports `kFontSizeMin/Max/Default` and `kMenuScaleMin/Max`, both sites read them, and the clamp is written as `kFontSizeMin / kFontSizeDefault`, which makes the two structurally incapable of drifting apart again. **The clamp is kept, deliberately, as a guard rather than a design bound** — `setFontSize` does not bound its argument, `restoreState` does `(raw − 12).round() + 12` with no bound, and the settings-import path accepts any `num`, so a value that never came from the slider can still arrive — and the comment now says which of the two it is, so the next reader does not re-narrow it. **The wider ends were measured before being adopted, not assumed:** ten screenshots of a local release build, 12/20/40 pt × 0.7/1.0/1.5×, at 1456 px and at the 1000 px three-pane minimum, in 繁體, light and dark. Nothing overflows, overlaps or loses a pane at either corner; two things degrade at the far corner and both do so gracefully — the search pane's operator strip wraps to a second row, and the Browse title ellipsises to `Gene… NA…`, which is the correct answer to "40 pt in a 200 px pane" (shrinking it back would be the app overruling the setting again, i.e. this very defect). The version gutter looked like a blocker and was not: hard-coded at 92 px until 2026-08-08 it *would* have clipped `CUV+S(雅伟)` at 1.4×, but it is now derived from the type size by a pure character model — its doc comment still quoted the old 0.8–1.4 range as fact and was corrected, **a comment that quotes a constant being a second copy of that constant**. `WbType.fallback` is referenced nowhere but its own declaration and all 552 call sites go through `WbType.of(context)` with `context.watch`, so no surface was frozen off the setting; the style presets (16–22 pt, 0.85–1.0×) were already inside both ranges, so no preset changes meaning. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,283 tests** exit 0 (+4). Verified by screenshot on deployed dev v1.6.105, not assumed: 40 pt / 1.5× in the parallel view draws visibly larger chrome *and* body text with NASB / 雅简+ / BSB / WTT tags all fitting their column, 12 pt / 0.7× draws the same workbench dense and tiny, and the Settings font slider sits at its minimum when 12 pt is the stored value. **The lesson, and it is about the tests: `test/wb_type_test.dart` already had five tests on this exact function and every one of them passed throughout — because each asked "does SOME change move it" and none asked "does EVERY stop the reader is offered move it". A monotonicity spot-check cannot see a dead zone at the end of a range; when a widget is driven by a control with discrete stops, iterate the control's stops.** Next: **#312**, whose brief names this defect as the root of its worst symptom.
- **v1.6.108** — **#313, first slice: in the workbench a selection acts on the docked panes, and a modal is for a DECISION, not for CONTENT.** The owner's complaint was 「這個閱讀的板塊裡面的功能都是單獨的，好像跟右邊的那個不是聯動的」, and its literal reading is wrong — checking it first is what kept this run from rebuilding something that works. Selection → pane has been wired since #276 (`MainProvider.selectedVerses` → `WorkbenchProvider.analysisVerses`), and a screenshot confirms it: tapping 創世紀 1:1 repaints Word Study with that verse's Hebrew before any button is pressed. What was genuinely unlinked is the **other** direction: the reader could name a subject — 原文, 互參 — and the only surface it knew how to name it on was a modal bottom sheet, so on a three-pane screen asking about a verse **covered the verse**, while a docked pane holding exactly that content sat idle 700 px to the right. That is YsWords inheritance: on a phone a sheet is the only place content can go, and the workbench inherited the habit along with the widget. BibleWorks says the same thing structurally (bwh10: the Analysis Window's "primary function is to display an analysis of the Biblical text in the Browse Window") — the analysis surface is docked and persistent, never summoned over the text. **The layering is the design:** `BibleReadingPane` is mounted both standalone and as the workbench centre, so it must not know about panes or widths. It names a subject (`ReaderAnalysisRequest`) and the host answers a `bool` — "I took it"; nobody takes it and the sheet opens exactly as before, which makes phone behaviour unchanged *by construction* rather than by a flag. (The reader's 🔍 already handed search back to the command line this way; the four content actions were simply the ones nobody had converted.) Two defects were anticipated in code rather than found on screen: taking a request clears `_pinnedKey`/`_analysisWord`/`_analysisFrozen`, or a Browse hover from ten minutes ago outranks the selection in `_buildAnalysisBody` and wins the pane the reader just addressed; and the originating button fills and takes the accent, because the pane change lands at the far edge of a 1400 px screen and #294's lesson is that a control with no local feedback reads as dead. Tests split by what is actually pure: the subject→tab map is asserted directly **including its inverse** — add a forward mapping without the round-trip and routing still works while the button never lights, a defect visible only to someone looking at the right pixels — while the width decision belongs to the host and is driven through the real tree at two widths. **A gate discovery: below 992 px there is no reader on the web to fall back to.** `SmallScreenGate` returns `none` only when `paneCountFor(width) >= 3`, so the small-screen gate and the three-pane gate are the same number; 900 px gets the advisory, not a reader with a sheet, and the brief's "screenshot the sheet at 900" is unachievable on web — the fallback is pinned by a widget test at 768 and reachable in the app only via the classic `HomePage` route and native mobile. Screenshot trap added to the notes: at 1010 px the bar takes its `maxWidth < 560` two-row branch, and a clip sized for the one-row layout catches only the count row, which reads exactly like "the actions were dropped at this width". `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,305 tests** exit 0 (+7). Verified on deployed dev, not assumed: at 1332 px 互參 repaints the pane with 希伯來書 11:3 / 約翰福音 1:1-3 / 詩篇 33:6, the hub icon fills, no `BottomSheet`, the verse stays on screen; 原文 then swaps the pane back to the interlinear and the icons trade states — same at exactly 1010 px. **Left deliberately undone, with reasons recorded in the enum's doc comments and pinned by a test that says it is the one to edit on purpose:** 相關講道 should become a tab (BibleWorks docks it in Resource Summary) but its body is a private widget inside an 8,000-line file and a 13th tab must be re-measured against #297's strip arithmetic; **AI 解經 stays a sheet by design**, since a docked pane promises to follow the selection and doing so would spend a network call per verse and give generated prose the same frame and weight as the corpus. Not attempted: the stats_page/verse_list_pane duplicates, folding the reader's own 🔍/⋮/floating bar into the workbench chrome (#269 owns it), and the full `showModalBottomSheet`/`showDialog`/`pushPage` sweep into `docs/PRODUCT-AUDIT.md` — that last is where this rule gets applied at scale and is the natural next slice.

- **v1.6.109** — **the modal sweep, and the build version printed once (#313 item 6, #314).** The sweep the last slice named as its successor is now `docs/PRODUCT-AUDIT.md` §7: all **46** `showModalBottomSheet`/`showDialog` sites and **43** `pushPage` sites classified against the rule v1.6.108 wrote into `reader_analysis_request.dart` — *a modal is for a DECISION; content that has a pane goes to the pane.* **It contradicts the brief twice, and both corrections matter more than the code in this release.** `verse_list_pane`'s three dialogs are not duplicates of the Analysis 經文列表 tab — they **are** that tab (`workbench_page.dart:2139` mounts the widget as `AnalysisTab.verseLists`) and all three are decisions with typed returns. `stats_page`'s nine sheets are the wrong unit: four are pickers, and rewriting the other five would be wasted work because **the page itself is the duplicate** — `StatsPage` has exactly **one** construction site in the whole app, `bible_reading_pane.dart:6970`, the reader's overflow menu, while the workbench answers the same question in `AnalysisTab.stats`. Retiring that entry point is deferred deliberately: 4,529 lines still hold the only home for the Aramaic sheet and the lemma picker. Net result — **one routing fix outstanding** (`_showChapterSermonsSheet`, the chapter-scoped sibling of a request that already exists), one entry point to retire, and **32 modals confirmed correct**, which is the point: the inherited-phone-app problem is real but concentrated in two files, not spread across the app. **Not shipped on purpose:** routing chapter-sermons through `ReaderAnalysisRequest.sermons` today is a **no-op** — `analysisTabForRequest` returns null for it, so no host claims it and the sheet opens anyway; it lands with the tab, not before it. #314: the menu bar's `SeekSparks <version> · <time>` above 1200 px is gone, and the `width >= 1200` branch with it. The status bar's copy survives because it is **tappable** (it opens About) where this was inert text. Verified on deployed dev at 1400 px: the menu bar's trailing slot is the language globe alone, and the status bar reads `v1.6.109 · 2026-08-11 21:00 UTC+10`. `test/workbench_version_display_test.dart` is a source-level ratchet pinning it at two renders — the status bar and the Help menu — because mounting `WorkbenchPage` needs the whole provider stack and the defect was "a second call site exists", not a wrong pixel.

- **v1.6.110** — *(backfilled 2026-08-12; the run that shipped it left no entry)* — **the map plates say where they came from, and 40 of them had to (#300).** `BibleMap.source` only ever answered where an image's *bytes* load (asset / cdn / legacy_url); nothing answered where the image **came from**, and a licence turns on the second question. Querying Wikimedia for the 1,192 plates found an actual breach rather than a metadata gap: **40 Sweet Publishing illustrations are CC BY-SA 3.0 with `AttributionRequired = true`** (artist Jim Padgett) and the app named nobody — "free to use" and "no credit needed" are different things and only the first had ever been checked. The other two findings are recorded rather than hidden: **996** plates are public domain because their artists died over a century ago (Tissot 620, Schnorr 223, Doré 144, Rembrandt 5, and 9 on the entries' own unverified description, stored as the weaker basis it is), and **151** have no recoverable origin — their CDN bytes carry no EXIF, XMP or IPTC at all — written as `"license": "unknown"` **explicitly**, because an absent licence reads as "not checked" and this one was checked. Shipped: `assets/maps_provenance.json` (nine collection records, generated by `tools/annotate_map_provenance.py`, every plate naming one); the viewer prints the credit **under the picture**, which is where BibleWorks puts it (bwh47: the per-resource display, not the manual's collected chapter, "is the place to look for definitive copyright information"), and it draws for the unknown plates too, since printing nothing there would make them look exactly like a public-domain plate that needs no credit; and the About page's one row reading "Public domain / Creative Commons archives" becomes three, because one reassuring sentence was covering three different legal situations. Rights were never in question — the owner cleared these plates. What was missing is provenance, and **the Sweet obligation was invisible because of it.**
- **v1.6.111** — *(backfilled 2026-08-12; the run that shipped it left no entry)* — **a phrasing says three things and the app could say one of them (#307).** Indentation carried subordination; the two marks the request's own Word document uses had no expression at all. **Parallel members**: (a) (b) (c) down the side, **derived** from a set of marked line starts and never stored — lettering restarts when an unmarked sibling at the same depth ends the run, and a nested series letters separately without disturbing the run it sits inside; storing the letters would let them drift the moment a line is split, joined or outdented. **Emphasis**: an underline on the words of a line, never on the gloss. Both are sets of word indices — the identity the rest of the model already uses — so they survive a level change like every other edit, and their JSON parser is **deliberately looser** than the one for added/removed: word 0 is filtered out of a break set because it can never be toggled, but it **is** a line start, so reusing that filter would have silently dropped a mark the reader can see on screen. **And the artifact is the point.** This team works in Word because they teach from the result, print it and hand it out, so the copy now puts `text/html` on the clipboard alongside the plain text — indent in **points** (24 pt a level, the tab stop Word ships with), the relation greyed, the member letter, real `<u>`, superscript verse numbers, direction on the wrapper. The rich write is built on the copy **event** rather than `navigator.clipboard.write`: synchronous and needing no permission, which is exactly the combination that avoids the iOS Safari `copy_fail` crash class this app already paid for once. Where it cannot be done the copy still succeeds as plain text and **says so**, because a phrasing pasted without its indentation has lost most of what the reader built and calling that "Copied" is a lie they find in the document. Two pre-existing export bugs fell out and are fixed: the export rendered the **whole chapter** under a heading naming the verse window, and it joined Chinese with spaces, printing s p a c e d  o u t scripture — the gap is now derived inside the exporter from the words themselves, so no caller can get it wrong. This also removes the last direct `Clipboard.setData` call in the page, which `clipboard_helper.dart`'s own doc names as the crash class.
- **v1.6.112** — **the queue has days left in it, so the thing that replaces it is now a tracked document (#302).** `docs/PARITY-BACKLOG.md`, 607 lines: what SeekSparks still owes each of its four sources — BibleWorks 10 **by module**, Eagle's View, Yahwehdehua, YsWords — and what it has decided **not** to owe them. `REJECTED` is a first-class verdict alongside HAVE/PARTIAL/ABSENT/BLOCKED, because saying once that the Graphical Search Engine is not worth rebuilding (building it first would be **a steering wheel for an engine that does not turn** — the query language underneath is the missing part) beats re-litigating it every run; the model for how those entries read is `command_verb.dart:55-79`, which already records what was deliberately not implemented and why. §7 is the **fifth axis the owner named and no feature inventory contains**: UI/UX and detail fine-checks, a standing section rather than a list that empties — 1400 / 992 / the pane minimum, EN + 简 + **繁** (traditional runs wider, so 繁 is the one that breaks), light and dark, 12/20/40 pt after #311, **no hover on a tablet**, and RTL. The loop prompt's "when the queue is empty" clause now points here, with `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md` taking precedence because accuracy outranks everything. **Writing it was an audit, and four things the queue held as fact did not survive it.** **#301 (Yahwehdehua) is effectively CLOSED** having been budgeted at "several iterations": four of its five layers already ship — the BDB/Thayer Chinese lexicon was imported **2026-08-11** into `assets/strongs/bdb_zh.json` (**8,853** H entries) and `thayer_zh.json`, wired at `chinese_lexicon_service.dart:101` and reached from `word_analysis_pane.dart:157`; the Chinese TVM grammar codes are on every tagged layer but `bsb` (**46** distinct codes in Genesis alone); the LEB translator notes are **inline** in `leb.json` (**11,365** occurrences of "Literally"); WH is duplicated in `lxxwh.json`. The fifth, LC, is a WLC-family Hebrew text whose morphology is **entirely** the null placeholder `H9999`, so its only remaining value is as an independent witness. **Two corrections worth more than the closure:** that lexicon is **not scholarly BDB** — median Hebrew entry **111 characters** — and labelling it "BDB" in the UI without the qualification would be the app stating something untrue about its own sources; and, **negative result, measured, so nobody checks twice**, the yahwehdehua export is **not** the missing witness `DATA-INTEGRITY` item 4 wants for the LEB `{…}` idiom braces (**0** verses in it carry one, and its Gen 1:6 lacks what ours has). **An automated pass reported the gitignored Eagle's View NASB as a live wiring bug**; it is excluded **by design** (`.gitignore:60,69-71`, `git ls-files` confirms), and an audit that cannot see `.gitignore` reports every deliberate exclusion as a defect — what misled it is a stale `nsn-plus` mention at `tagged_text_service.dart:164`. Also recorded: **Strong's keys in `assets/strongs/` are unpadded** (`H430` present, `H0430` missing), a trap for any future importer. §8 ranks what to pick next — DATA-INTEGRITY items first, then a **docked User Notes tab** (the largest Analysis-pane gap, and the one #313's own rule already argues for), version difference highlighting, Nave's Topical Bible, list comparison, compound parenthesised search, transliterated original-language input — and lists the BLOCKED entries that must never be picked (#278, #293, #296, #309). `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,365 tests** exit 0. **Not verified by screenshot, and deliberately so: this release changes no rendering surface** — it adds a document and two backfilled log entries, and the claim it makes is about the code, which was checked by reading the code. **The lesson: two of four subagents returned inaccurate verdicts this run** — the `.gitignore` one, and one that could not find the YsWords source and silently substituted this repo's own initial commit. Every load-bearing claim in the document was therefore re-verified by hand, and §6 opens with an explicit "method limitation, stated up front" instead of a confident paragraph about a repo nobody read. **Delegate the search; never delegate the verdict.**

- **v1.6.113** — *(backfilled 2026-08-12; the run that shipped it left no entry)* — **check 25 asks a question the first twenty-four did not: do the references the app WRITES itself resolve?** 5,933 of them across seven assets — synopsis tables, 4,329 section headings, family tree, timeline, gallery, king list. Six of the seven are clean; **all 14 failures are in `ot_synopsis.json`**, where an importer that could not express a range crossing a chapter truncated 12 of them: seven name a verse that cannot exist, and the other five *resolve and are simply wrong* — 2Ch 29:1–31:21, three chapters of Hezekiah's reform, became 31 verses. The same end-discarding was in `parseReference`, so John 18:28–19:16 (the trial before Pilate) indexed as one verse. **And nobody could have seen any of it: none of the 139 OT groups had ever been reachable** — the menu gated on `isGospel`, `hasSynopsis` had no callers, and a book-keyed map silently dropped 37 more passages by de-duplicating them.
- **v1.6.114** — **206 characters of 和合本 render perfectly and cannot be read (#304, check 26)** — the first comparison of this repository's *Chinese scripture* against a witness obtained outside it. 詩篇 146:6 shipped as 雅伟造天丶地丶海: `丶` U+4E36 is the **dot stroke**, the character used to name a piece of a glyph, standing where the enumeration comma 、 belongs, at 150 of the 206 sites; the rest are ordinary ideographs in the wrong place (恉 for 腮, 逿 for 趟, 承巡菇业 for 承受产业). **Check 10 could not have found one of them** — every character is an ordinary CJK ideograph in the same block as its neighbours, so nothing is missing, nothing malformed, no key broken. **Four witnesses were needed because each is corrupt where another is clean**, and a majority vote across them would have entrenched three of the six classes; hence the load-bearing rule, that *a witness corrupt at the same site abstains rather than vetoes* — it is not voting on whether the site is broken, the unreadable character settles that, it is supplying the reading. 383 further disagreements with the external witness are left alone, because "a witness spells it differently" is a weaker claim than "this cannot be read". Also repairs 1,764 verse ids that collided 562 times (latent — `Verse.fromJson` never reads that field).

- **v1.6.118** — **check 29 gave 426 references the same sentence; asking WHY found two of them were not absent at all but WRONG (#304, check 30).** The critical text prints 2 Corinthians 13 in **thirteen** verses, joining what the English tradition numbers 12 and 13 — so the grace benediction the English tradition calls 13:14 is its verse 13. `leb`, `biblexg-v2` and `biblexg-v2-tr` follow that numbering; the app keys every edition by the **English** reference. All three therefore answered **2 Corinthians 13:13 with the grace benediction**, sitting beside a KJV column reading "All the saints salute you", with nothing on screen to say the two are different verses — plausible, wrong, and unfalsifiable by a reader, which is the class that outranks every feature in the queue. Second: both 梁家鏗譯本 files cut **使徒行傳 8:40** at a comma — 「腓利卻出現在亞鎖城，」 — and filed the rest under **8:41**, a reference no versification tradition gives; `_knownBeyondCanon` in `data_integrity_test.dart` had been **excusing it since #304 on a note the chapter does not contain** (there is no NA28 note anywhere in Acts 8; the only note in v40 is the geographical gloss 「即向北沿海，」). A hand-written exception had grown an explanation nobody checked, and *a reference beyond the canon is compared against nothing*, so a defect can sit in one wearing a plausible reason. **The line `tools/repair_verse_numbering.py` draws**, because most numbering differences must be left exactly as they are: a reference holding a **superset or prefix** of the canon's text is the edition's own versification and is honest — the reader sees all the words, in order — and is untouched; a reference holding a **different verse** is a defect. So 13:12, which holds canonical 12 AND 13, was deliberately not repaired, and LEB's Acts 19:40 and the 3 John 14/15 splits were left alone. Every change is guarded on the text it expects, refuses rather than guesses, invents no character (the join added none — the comma was already the clause separator) and is idempotent. **The classification itself is DERIVED, never tabulated**, because an asserted classification is a second copy of a fact and a second copy rots: the publisher's own `verseLabel` range (**42** — 路加福音 1:1 is `1-4` and holds the whole prologue, the largest single class in the corpus and the only one where the edition itself is the witness), `versification.json`'s `absent` set (**67** — BSB 16 of 16, NASB 13 of 13, four editions and a table derived in v1.6.90 for the Word Study pane agreeing on a set nobody designed for), and **the same table's `map` read for OVERLAP**, which is new (**4**): where two reader keys resolve to one original key the original numbers them as one verse, so an edition following it prints them together — safe because an unmapped key resolves to itself, so two unmapped references can never appear to share. **113 of the corpus's 424 absence rows now say why they are empty and 46 name the verse the words are in**, via a fifth `VerseAbsence` kind, `notInCriticalText`, which claims only what the table proves (the original has no words here) and NOT what `omitted` claims (the publisher left it out on purpose). `lxxwh`'s 302 are excluded on purpose — a different canon in a different base text, answered by check 29 against an independent LXX. **Residue: 9, every one a named loss** — Philippians 1:2 in both 梁家鏗譯本 (1:1 ends on a dangling 「：」 and the greeting is nowhere; the only thing this check found that was not already known, and not ours to repair since the words are not in the file to move), 馬可福音 6:8-11 (簡 only, needs a 繁→简 conversion this repo will not invent), and LEB Romans 16:25-27, **left unexplained on purpose** — the doxology is in the edition inside the note at 16:24, but no derivation reaches it and inventing one for three references would be the hand table this check exists to avoid. Python (`audit_data_integrity.py` check 30) and Dart reach the same counts by different routes, and the Dart test computes with the **shipped** `rangeLabelHeads`/`sharedOriginalHeads`/`Versification` rather than a copy, so it fails if the display rule drifts from the measurement. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,459 tests** exit 0.

- **v1.6.119** — **116 psalm titles were in `assets/leb.json` from the day it was imported and no reader had ever seen one (check 31).** The LEB numbers a superscription `title` rather than an integer, and `FetchVerses` filtered every non-numeric verse out at load — so the words were dropped between the asset and the model, which is why every test stayed green and why nothing looked broken: a psalm with no title looks exactly like a psalm. They are the **only** non-integer verse numbers in **357,738** records across **13** editions, so the filter that discarded them protected against nothing else. The completeness witness came from outside the repository: the **34** psalms left untitled are precisely the traditional list of psalms carrying no Hebrew superscription, which is a check the import cannot fake — a dropped title shows up there as an extra number rather than as a silence. `foldSuperscriptions` lifts each title onto **verse 1 of its own chapter** as a typed `Verse.superscription` and refuses to guess: a title whose chapter has no verse 1 is counted as an orphan, never attached across a chapter or a book boundary, and an edition with no titles gets its own list back **identically** so the loader's filter still sees what the fold declined to claim. **Three placements were rejected and the reasons are the design.** Verse **0** would make Browse assert that `kjvs`, `bsb` and `lxxwh` *lack* a title they in fact carry merged into verse 1 — an empty cell is a claim. Prepending to `text` destroys the distinction the renderer needs, and the title is not the verse: in the Hebrew it **is** a different verse, which is why it sits outside the selection `Stack` and the tint stops above it. On the **clipboard** the flattening is correct and is done deliberately, because plain text has no type system and a pasted psalm that silently loses its title is worse than one that runs the two together. All 116 render through `buildAnnotatedSpans`, extracted from `buildVerseContentSpans` for the purpose, because every LEB title ends in a versification note and one ships a `{music}` gloss — routing them around the markup path would have printed the braces. **The same edition carried a second defect, and it was visible on screen the whole time:** `leb.json` was the only one of the eleven whose verses were padded with trailing whitespace, and not a few of them — **all 31,199**, against **0** in every other edition. The reader never showed it, because every sanitiser in `text_patterns.dart` ends in `.trim()`; **Browse does not**, handing the raw string to `parseScripture`, so every LEB row in the compare pane stood a blank line taller than the columns beside it. `test/kjv_integrity_test.dart` had asserted exactly this rule since check 27 — over **three of eleven** editions, because check 27 was a KJV investigation. **A guard scoped to the file that motivated it is how a defect survives a test that describes it**; the new `test/edition_text_integrity_test.dart` keeps the rule and replaces the scope. Running it corpus-wide surfaced two more space-before-punctuation sites that are **named rather than repaired**: LEB Judges 14:6's padded parenthesis, because no external LEB witness exists in this repository to check a repair against, and NASB Jeremiah 29:28's four nested closing quotes, which are U+2009 thin spaces and correct typesetting. **31a is a clean check reported because it is clean**: `kjvs.json` departs from three independent external KJV witnesses in **0 of 30,708** unanimous verses, where `kjv.json` departs in **699** — the two disagree with each other in **894**, of which **757** are orthography across 185 shapes (show/shew ×67, cherubim/cherubims ×41, honour/honor ×34), **117** are psalm superscriptions and **20** are the Psalm 119 acrostic letters. That is enough to say the file labelled KJV+S *is* the KJV and the one labelled KJV is a revision, and the run **deliberately stopped there** — relabelling an edition is the owner's call (#285), and restoring the superscriptions and acrostics to `kjv.json` would be this repository inventing text for an edition it cannot witness. Searching the titles was also declined on purpose: `MainProvider.searchKeys` indexes what a result row can display, and a hit whose words the row cannot show reads as a bug. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,503 tests** exit 0, and verified by screenshot on the deployed dev build in both surfaces — the reader (Psalm 51's Nathan title and Psalm 90's *A prayer of Moses*, italic above verse 1, gloss and versification marker intact, selection tint covering verse 1 and stopping above the title) and Browse (the title in the LEB cell, and the blank line gone).

- **v1.6.120** — **thirteen kings of Judah were shown to the reader as having been born in the year they took the throne (check 32).** `assets/family_tree.json` held a Thiele accession or coregency year in a field named `birthYear`, and `displayYears` printed it in the same slot, in the same words, as Abraham's birth: a reader was told **Asa was born in 911 BC**, and 911 is the year he came to the throne. **This is a defect class none of the previous thirty-one checks could see** — the number is well-formed, in range, internally consistent, and *wrong in kind*. Nothing about a schema, a range check, or a cross-edition witness detects a value that is correct and means something other than its field name claims; only asking *what does this number rest on* does. Five of the thirteen are **sole-reign** starts, so they did not even match the reign the app's own Kings of Judah & Israel chart draws from `hebrew_kings.json` — two shipped surfaces disagreeing about the same king. **Check 8 ("no date shown to a reader carries a per-record source") is now CLOSED**, but the provenance was the means, not the finding: every one of **277** people and **98** timeline events now carries a `dating.kind` (`birth` **27** / `reign` **14** / `approximate` **236**) and a `basis` (`scripture`, `scripture+thiele`, `thiele`, `conventional`), and the kinds are **DERIVED by `tools/audit_dates.py`, never tabulated** — a `birth` is claimed only where a stated age in the text reproduces the year, and every one cites the verses it came from. **32a is clean and is reported because it is clean**: the Anno Mundi chain, re-derived from the Masoretic Genesis 5 and 11 ages, matches the asset in **19 of 19** records, **0** disagreements — the oldest and least verifiable part of the file turned out to be the soundest. **32b, 11 stated intervals, 1 disagreement**: creation→flood is **1652** years in the timeline where Genesis's own chain gives **1656**, because −4000 is a *rounding* of Ussher's 4004 while −2348 is his flood taken as printed — two incompatible treatments of one chronology, so both events are now `conventional`/approximate. **32d needed no external authority at all**: **121 of 242** BC records — exactly half — share a year with another record and **152** are round to the decade, and the **44** sitting on −1690 are Genesis 46's men, the grandsons who went down into Egypt with Jacob, while **the same app's timeline dates that descent to −1876**. **32e: the legend named a chronology the file does not follow** — it credited **Ussher** for figures that are not his (Ussher's Abraham is **1996 BC**, verified externally; the file says **2166**, which is what the 1 Kings 6:1 chain gives off Thiele's Solomon), and the timeline declared **97** events over 98 while naming no chronology at all. **The tool refuses to classify anything until it has proved it can read**: 20 figures the text states are probed against the verse that cites them and the run aborts if one is missing — **9 of the 20 failed on the first run**, because the probes were written from remembered KJV spellings and the Berean prints 175 as a numeral in Genesis 25:7 and as "a hundred and twenty" in Deuteronomy 34:7. **A probe that hard-codes one spelling tests the typography, not the figure**, so `numpat` now generates an alternation over every written form of a number. Two parser bugs it then caught are worth naming: the anaphora resolver returned **"Jerusalem"** as Rehoboam's name from "established himself in Jerusalem and reigned" (a name governed by a preposition is a place or a father, never the subject), and the AM chain **missed Genesis 11:10** because the verse lowercases "when" mid-sentence — which mattered, since 11:10 names Shem alone and is exact where 5:32 names three sons at once, and the 2-year gap it created was the tool's, not the asset's. **The largest decision was reversed on purpose.** The first implementation marked all 240 underivable years `undated` and rendered **nothing** — and blanking **87%** of a shipped dataset is a product decision, not an accuracy one, which is not a call an unattended run gets to make. **"Withdraw the claim" and "delete the data" are not the same conservative option, and only the first is actually conservative**: `approximate` renders "c. 1000 BC" and withdraws the false precision, while every original number stays in the file, so the owner's decision is still open. `displayYears` is the **single join point** — all 8 year-rendering call sites funnel through it, so the rule is stated once and tested directly rather than at 8 widgets. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,517 tests** exit 0 (14 new: the formatter in EN and both Chinese scripts, and the asset invariants — every reign span equals the one `hebrew_kings.json` cites, no birth year sits on a reign start, and `_meta`'s counts are recomputed rather than trusted). **Verified by screenshot on the deployed dev build**: Asa now reads **"reigned 911 BC – 870 BC"**, with Solomon, Abijah, Jehoshaphat, Jehoram and Ahaziah likewise, while Rehoboam — one of the **three** genuine births among those sixteen, since 2 Chronicles 12:13 states his accession age — correctly still reads as a bare birth–death range, and the Lukan lineage reads "c. 1000 BC". **Still open, and named rather than fixed**: the −1690 cluster and the 152 decade-rounded years are now *labelled* approximate but not *corrected*, and correcting them means choosing a chronology, which is the owner's call.

- **v1.6.121** — **the app could not find words it was showing you (check 33).** Every check before this asked whether a word we print is the *right* word; this one asked the question nobody had put — **of the words the app already prints, how many can it not find?** — and a search returning nothing is the app stating *"this Bible does not contain that phrase"*, which is a claim about the text made in the way most readers will ever query it. **33a:** check 31b made the LEB's **116** psalm superscriptions visible in the reader, in Browse and on the clipboard in v1.6.119, and stopped at the corpus — `MainProvider.searchKeys` and `wordKeys` were built from `Verse.text`, which by construction is the psalm *without* its title. The size of that is not 116 verses but every word those lines are the only home of: **eleven** words occur in an LEB title and nowhere in its 31,083 verses (`director`, `ascents`, `maskil`, `miktam`, `gittith`, `shiggaion`, `muth-labben`, `shushan`, `eduth`, `leannoth`, and Psalm 34's `crazy`). **The witness is unusually strong because three editions carry the same titles by different routes** — `kjvs` and `bsb` merge them into verse 1 and have always been searchable, the LEB types them apart — so the two arrangements *had* to disagree, and the fix is proved by agreement rather than assertion: Nathan the prophet **15 → 16**, matching 16 and 16; Jeduthun 11 → **14**/14/14; Gittith, Shiggaion, `maskil` **0 → 13** against BSB's 13 and the KJV+S's **Maschil** 13; `miktam` 0 → **6** against **Michtam** 6; `ascents` 0 → **15** against *songs of degrees* 15; `music director` 0 → **55** against *chief Musician* 55. The spelling differences are the point — `kjvs` returns **0** for `maskil`, so the agreement cannot be an artefact of one text being derived from another. **33b is the finding, and it came from asking why one of my own predicted queries still failed.** `music director` returned 0 after the titles were in the corpus, because **`[supplied]` words render WITHOUT their brackets** (`bracketSpanKind`: only divineName and gloss keep them) while `sanitizeForSearch` left the brackets in the key — so LEB Genesis 1:2 prints *"darkness was over the face of the deep"* and its key held `darkness[was]over`. **16,975 of that edition's 31,083 verses carry a bracket — 54.6% — and 17,932 across the six shipped editions**, against the 116 titles that started it: two orders of magnitude, and the standing rule *"also ask how many more of these are there and MEASURE it"* paid for itself again. The reusable lesson is sharper than the rule: **the flagship query for defect A is the cheapest probe for defect B**, because a query you have predicted an exact answer for is an assertion about the whole pipeline and not only the part you changed. **The fix is two definitions and no new behaviour.** `Verse.scriptureText` is the flattening stated **once** — four call sites had each written `superscription + ' ' + text` themselves (the corpus, the verse-number copy handler, the Copy Centre, the command pane's preview and its copy-all), and **a boundary that flattens differently from the corpus is exactly how an app searches one string and shows another**. `sanitizeForSearchKey` is a **new** function rather than a change to `sanitizeForSearch`, which also feeds result previews and copy paths where the brackets still tell a reader which words the translator supplied — a corpus key is the one place with no reader to inform, and removing a delimiter cannot invent a hit because every character left is a character on screen. Browse's `_SuperscriptionLine` now takes the same `SearchHighlight` the verse body does, since a hit whose words the row cannot show reads as a bug. **What was checked and deliberately not changed:** KWIC and the concordance are Strong's-driven through the tagged layer, which holds no superscription, so a hit there can never be in one — the queue item assumed they needed the same work and they do not; the Strong's result preview keeps `.text` for that reason; the reading pane does not highlight search hits **at all** (`highlightsForQuery` reaches Browse only), so making it do so is a feature and not this defect; `related_verses_pane` and `phrase_match_pane` render the corpus string itself and were already self-consistent. **My own first measurement was wrong and that is what found 33b** — the Python probe stripped `[` `]` on the assumption that the production sanitiser does, and reading `bracePattern` rather than trusting the name caught it: **a probe that reimplements the production sanitiser tests the probe**, so the frozen test now drives the loader's real path end to end (`foldSuperscriptions` → drop non-numeric → `Verse.fromJson` → `setVerses` → `SearchService.scanText`). `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,537 tests** exit 0 (20 new). **Verified by screenshot on the deployed dev build**, both halves: the search returns **16 results** for *Nathan the prophet* under the LEB with **Psalms 51:1** among them and its preview legibly showing the title, and Browse marks *Nathan the prophet* inside the superscription line exactly as it marks the verse body. **Named rather than fixed:** Browse splits only `ScriptureSpanKind.plain` on the terms, so `music director` now *finds* the 55 psalms and highlights only `director` — pre-existing (the old key held `the[music]director`, so a bare `music` query already matched and already went unmarked) and closing it would change verse rendering for all 17,932 bracket-bearing verses, a wider blast radius than a cosmetic gap earns in the same pass as a correctness fix.

- **v1.6.122** — **the small number in brackets, which three checks handled and none read (check 34).** `assets/lxxwh.json` carries the Septuagint's OWN chapter-and-verse inline as `<vs:c:v>` wherever it differs from the English reference the record is keyed on, and the app **renders** it — a muted "(9:2a)" in front of the Greek — so it is a printed claim, not metadata. Check 23 typed it, check 29 counted it, check 31 sanitised it; **none of the three asked whether it is TRUE**, and 4,687 of them had shipped unread. Put to an outside witness (`api.getbible.net/v2/lxx`, the same one check 29 used): **4,200 of the 4,247 it could resolve are verbatim right**, and three groups are not. **34a — 144 markers in Proverbs 25:1–29:27 name a chapter exactly SEVEN too high**, so the app printed "(32:1)" for a book whose Septuagint has **31** chapters: the claim is impossible whatever the words say, and the words then settled where they belong — all **138 records are verbatim the witness AT THEIR OWN ENGLISH NUMBER, 138 of 138**. **The repair is a subtraction, not a deletion, and that distinction saved six markers**: seven comes off the chapter, 138 then name their own reference and are dropped as saying nothing, and **25:10a, 26:11a and four more survive as the lettered sub-verses the rest of Proverbs already has 42 of** — deleting the 144 outright would have been simpler and would have thrown them away. Where the seven came from is **not recoverable from the file**; Rahlfs prints Proverbs' chapters out of order (24:22 → 30:1-14 → 24:23-34 → 30:15-33 → 31:1-9 → 25-29), so a count of printed *blocks* rather than of chapter numbers is where an error of this shape would come from, and that is **written down as a guess**. **34b — 129 sub-verse letters are in the wrong alphabet**: α β χ δ ε φ γ η σ is **Adobe Symbol's encoding of a b c d e f g h s** read as though it were Unicode, so the page said "9:2c" and we printed "(9:2χ)". **No outside witness exists for this** — the witness numbers verses, not sub-verses — so it is proved **internally, twice**: 1 Kings 16:28 carries eight markers in one record running α β χ δ ε φ **γ** η, with **γ SEVENTH**, where Latin `g` belongs and Greek γ (third) does not; and independently the frequencies descend cleanly through a b c d e f g h (**65 α, 23 β, 17 χ, 12 δ, 6 ε, 3 φ, 1 γ, 1 η**), which no Greek ordering produces. **34c — 6 markers repeat their own record's number mid-verse**, so a reader of Matthew 26:61 saw *"Later two came forward (26:61) and said"*; **a marker records a DIFFERENCE, and one that repeats the key is empty by definition**. They are **named rather than detected**, because two identity markers are doing real work — Lamentations 1:1 runs `1:0` then `1:1`, Revelation 13:1 runs `12:18` then `13:1` — and a rule that ate them would be the repair inventing a defect. **34d is a negative result that disproved its own hypothesis**: Matthew 12:47 is in the witness and in **neither** of our layers, and the witness brackets it — Westcott and Hort's own mark of a doubted reading — but **we do carry the only other verse it brackets (Matthew 21:44)**, so "this edition drops bracketed verses" is false and the absence is ours. Importing it is **declined and left open as a product call**: the only available source inlines UBS4 variants as duplicated words, the tagged layer would not have it, and the app already makes the weakest true claim there. **Two instrument limits are declared rather than passed off as findings.** The `westcotthort` witness is **English-keyed**, so it cannot test the NT marker column at all — found by testing our text at our OWN key, where **12 of 19 apparent disagreements matched exactly**; and 132 OT markers fall where the witness has no verse. **And two of the measurement's own bugs are worth more than either**: `norm()` had to drop spaces before six "disagreements" like γαβηρωθχαμααμ vs γαβηρωθ χαμααμ stopped being word division (13 → 1), and the bracketed-verse sweep keyed **English** book names against a witness whose NT names are **Greek** (`Κατά Ματθαίον`) and confidently reported both verses missing — the bug that, once fixed, produced 34d. **Joshua 19:47/19:48 was nearly filed as a transposition by pattern-matching check 29**; the witness says both markers are correct. **The two ratchets that need no witness** are now in the suite: a sub-verse suffix must be Latin, and **a marker may not name its own reference unless a different one precedes it in the same record** — plus no Proverbs marker above chapter 31.
