Counts: flat **4,687 → 4,543** markers in **4,405** verses, tagged **4,672 → 4,528**, and the gap between the layers is **still exactly 15** (Nehemiah 10's known coverage gap), which is how the repair proves it touched both identically. **No Greek moved**: with every `<vs:…>` stripped, all **30,800** records are byte-identical before and after. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,537 tests** exit 0. **Verified by screenshot on the deployed dev build**: Proverbs 25:1 now opens on `αυται` with **no (32:1)**, Joshua 8:30 reads **(9:2a)** with a Latin `a`, and Proverbs 25:10 still carries **(25:10a)** — the sub-verse letter the subtraction preserved and a deletion would have destroyed. **Still open, observed and deliberately not measured in this pass**: tagged Matthew 26:61 holds a run `{"w":" ","s":"G3004"}` — a whitespace word carrying a Strong's number — recorded as its own future check rather than folded into this one.

- **v1.6.123** — **seven titles printed over passages that contradicted them (check 35).** `assets/ot_synopsis.json` holds 139 groups of parallel Old Testament passages imported from Eagle's View and reachable from the reader since v1.6.113, and every group makes **two** claims at once: *these passages are parallel*, and *this title names them*. Check 25 proved the 313 references resolve to verses that exist; **it asked neither question** — a reference can be perfectly well-formed and point at the wrong chapter, and a title can be printed over a passage that refutes it. `tools/audit_ot_synopsis.py` asks both. **The alignment instrument had to be built before it could be trusted, and raw overlap measures nothing** — every OT passage shares H853, H3605, H1961 and H559 with every other — so a passage reduces to the multiset of Strong's numbers behind it, each weighted by inverse verse frequency, compared by cosine, and run over **both** tagged layers (`kjvs` and `cuvs-yhwh` tag an English and a Chinese translation with the same Hebrew numbers over the same 23,145 verses), so a pair judged dissimilar by both is dissimilar in the **Hebrew** and not in one translator's word choice. **A score means nothing without a null**: 3,994 random contiguous same-book pairs drawn matched on the real length distribution give kjvs p98 = 0.157, cuvs-yhwh p98 = 0.188, and results are reported as percentiles rather than as numbers nobody can size. **The scoring rule was wrong on the first run and the fix came from a case whose answer was already known**: scoring a group by its *worst* pair put group 41 "David's Children" at 0.000 in English, because 1 Chronicles 3:1-9 / 2 Samuel 3:2-5 are the sons born in Hebron and 2 Samuel 5:14-15 / 1 Chronicles 14:4-7 the sons born in Jerusalem — two disjoint name lists, one subject, and the group is correct. A group of four may legitimately hold two passages unlike each other; what cannot happen is a passage resembling **nothing else in its group**, so each passage scores by its best sibling and the group by its **loneliest** passage. Flags fell 15 → 11 and group 41 left the list. **35a: 11 of 139 flagged, every one read, 10 correct.** Eagle's View deliberately includes one-verse synchronisms beside long narratives — Isaiah 6:1 "In the year that king Uzziah died" against two 2-verse death notices, Isaiah 1:1, Isaiah 20:1, Jeremiah 1:1-3, 1 Kings 2:46, Psalm 132:8-10 quoted at the close of Solomon's prayer — and a single verse carries almost no vocabulary, so it scores low **because it is short**. That is an instrument limit, recorded as one. **The eleventh is reported and deliberately NOT repaired**: group 4 "Hebrew Servants" is Leviticus 25:8-38 | Deuteronomy 15:1-11, and the Hebrew-servant law begins one verse *after* both ranges end while Exodus 21:1-11, the primary passage, is absent — both ranges are the sabbatical-year and jubilee release, a real parallel under a different title. Whether the editor meant the release or the servant law is a question about a source this repository does not carry, and re-scoping the group would be inventing an intent. **35b is the finding: 7 titles misspell a word, and the passage each labels is the witness.** A title word of five letters or more **absent** from its own passages while a word **in** those passages sits within two edits of it is the exact shape of the defect, and the corpus answers it with no dictionary: `Jepheth` → **Japheth** (1 Chronicles 1:4; CUV 雅弗), `Johoash` → **Jehoash** (2 Kings 14:15-16, the group's own two verses, twice; CUV 约阿施), `Manesseh` → **Manasseh** (2 Chronicles 33:1, 2 Kings 21:1; CUV 玛拿西), `Oholiah` → **Oholiab** (Exodus 31:6 and 35:34 read "Aholiab"; CUV 亚何利亚伯 transliterates a final *b*, and "Oholiah" is not a name in any tradition), plus `Assasination`, `Illneess` and `Settler's in Jerusalem` — a possessive apostrophe followed by a preposition. **Four of the seven are proper names and each is proved by TWO independent translations of the group's own verses; nothing outside this repository was consulted and nothing was guessed.** **`Bezalel` is deliberately not repaired**: the KJV spells him `Bezaleel` and the audit flags the one-edit difference, but Bezalel is the spelling every modern version uses — a translation convention, not an error, and repairing it would be the instrument overruling the editor. **35c answers "how many more of these are there" and the answer is none**: the same instrument over **1,443** `section_titles.json` headings and **70** gospel-synopsis titles produced **0** real defects from 23 raw hits, all 23 explained — modern spellings against the KJV (Melchizedek/Melchisedec, Pergamum/Pergamos, Malta/Melita), British inflections `/usr/share/dict/words` lacks (Baptised, Sympathises), and possessive tokens (`Jesus'`, `Cyrus'`). The defect is **confined to the Eagle's View import**. **35d — the Chinese half, where the obvious method was discarded rather than tuned.** Chinese has no spaces to segment a name on and over a long passage every 2-gram of a title sits one character from something, so the naive test returned **334 findings of which none was an error** (会幕 vs 帐幕 is word choice, 麻风 vs 麻疯 orthography). The **Strong's numbers are the way across**: a name the English title gets right resolves in the group's own verses to a Hebrew number, the same number in the Chinese layer over the same verses gives the CUV's own spelling, and the Chinese title is asked for that exact string — **50 names carried through and the Chinese title uses all 50, 0 disagreements**. **A check that finds nothing must publish how hard it looked**: corrupting one character at a time inside the resolved names catches **137 of 143, 95.8%**, over the 42 of 139 titles carrying a resolvable name — but across *all* 904 title characters the rate is **15.2%**, which is the honest answer to "would this catch any error in a Chinese title" and the answer is no. It catches errors in names, and there are no errors in names. **The repair is recorded where a reader of the data can see it**: each of the seven is appended to the asset's own `corrections` block with `field: "en"` and a reason — the same mechanism the two schema-2 reference corrections already used — and `TITLE_CORRECTIONS` is duplicated into `tools/import_eaglesview_ot_synopsis.py` so a re-import from the Eagle's View source cannot silently undo it. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,540 tests** exit 0 (3 new: the seven titles by exact value, the `corrections` block must record exactly those seven, and the standing rule of 35b — which needs no dictionary and no external witness, and against the pre-repair asset names exactly Oholiah, Jepheth, Johoash and Manesseh). **Verified by screenshot on the deployed dev build**: 2 Chronicles 33 → ⋮ → Parallel Passages now prints **"Manasseh's Reign"** over 2Ch 33:1-20 | 2Ki 21:1-18. **Still open, named rather than fixed**: group 4 above; **97 of 139 Chinese titles carry no name the instrument can resolve** and are unchecked, since the source ships no Chinese witness; the alignments are checked for **vocabulary only**, so a group whose passages share vocabulary but are not the same event would pass; and check 34's deferred blank-run item is now measured and closed out as a note — **18 shipped runs** carry a Strong's number with no word, and Matthew 26:61's `{"w": " ", "s": "G3004"}` is a **double count** in the concordance (the import split one word around a `<vs:26:61>` marker into two runs both carrying G3004), not a loss of text.

- **v1.6.124** — **the family tree's 338 relationship claims, and three names no Bible we ship contains (check 36).** `assets/family_tree.json` prints a name for **277 people** and cites the verses each one appears in; check 25 proved those **665 references resolve** but never asked whether the passage they resolve to *contains the name printed over it*, and for three people it does not, in **any of the five English editions the app ships** (bsb, kjv, kjv+s, nasb, leb): `Melki` → **Melchi** (Luke 3:24, the record's only reference, reads Melchi in all five and Melki in none — and the file already called the other man `Melchi (II)`, so an English reader could not see that the two entries were the same name while a Chinese reader always could, both being 麦基), `Josek` → **Josech** (Luke 3:26 reads Josech in bsb/nasb/leb and Joseph in kjv/kjv+s; Josek in none), and `Joshua / Jose` → **Joshua** — **not a name at all but two editions' spellings joined by a slash, sitting in a field the app prints verbatim.** **Which spelling survives was measured, not chosen**: of the **39 people in the Lukan chain, 24 agree in both traditions, 11 carry a name only bsb/nasb/leb print and exactly 1 carries a name only the kjv prints** — the file's English is the modern critical text, **11 to 1**. That same measurement is why **`Janna` is deliberately NOT touched**: the kjv does read Janna at Luke 3:24, so the name is witnessed by a Bible we ship and changing it would be a consistency preference, not an accuracy repair. **36a, the shape of the graph: 277 people, 312 parent-child edges, 26 marriages, 0 structural issues** — no dangling id, no asymmetric spouse, no non-reciprocal parent/child, no self-parent, no cycle. **36b, the dates: 8 findings in 290 comparable edges** (19 skipped as `dating.kind == "reign"` accession years, 3 crossing the am/bc seam) — 3 of Terah's cross the seam, 4 are impossible gaps (Eliphaz→Amalek **0 years**, Heli→Mary 5, Hagar→Ishmael 10, Nathan→Mattatha 10) and 1 is a parent dead 166 years before the child (Levi d=−1716 → Jochebed b=−1550, contradicting Numbers 26:59 which her own record cites); **none repaired — every one is a chronology judgement, not a typo.** **36c1: after the repair 276 of 277 English names appear as a whole word in some shipped edition of some verse the record itself cites**, with no spelling aliases, no near-miss allowance and no second chance from a verse the record does not point at; the sole exemption is **Jude**, whom Matthew 13:55 and Mark 6:3 spell Judas/Juda while every English Bible calls the epistle's author Jude. **The Chinese half was checked too, because it is the half our readers use**, joined to the English on the numeric verse `id` (**31,086 of 31,086 BSB verses join**) and by **exact containment only — an edit-distance test over two- and three-character CJK names is the same instrument that produced 334 false findings in check 35** — giving **267 of 277 present in the CUV at their own verses, an upper bound** (米拿 matches inside 亚米拿达). **The 10 absences are recorded and frozen by id rather than repaired**: eight transliterate the modern critical name in a chain where the CUV follows the Textus Receptus and reads a different name altogether (Luke 3:26 约瑟克 vs 约瑟), which is editorial policy and not error, and telling them from the two that look like slips is a translation judgement. **The sharpest of those two is the single highest-value human minute in this check**: the tree spells Reuben 吕便 6 times and 流便 twice, the CUV reads 流便 **85 times and 吕便 never**, and `hezron_reuben` is *named* 希斯仑（流便之子） while its own summary reads 吕便的儿子 — **one record spelling one name both ways.** **36c2 reviews the 338 prose claims against the passages cited**: tier 1 (a cited verse states it) rose 298→**302**, tier 2 **31**, tier 3 (no cited verse states it) fell 9→**5** — and **the 4 that moved were exactly the 4 predicted before the repair as Melki/Josek artifacts**, which is the check probing itself. **The instrument's limit is stated rather than hidden**: it only surfaces claims no passage states *within the window*, so the 5 inferred links are a lower bound and the 31 tier-2 claims are unreviewed. **Frozen by 6 tests** in `test/family_tree_names_test.dart` (~1s), which resolve every reference with the app's own `parseReference` rather than a reimplementation: the three names by exact value, the `corrections` block must record exactly those three, **no displayed name may contain a slash**, every record's refs must name at least one verse, the strict English rule, and the 10-id Chinese set as a ratchet that may shrink but not grow or move. Reverting two names fails 3 of the 6, with the general rule naming `Melki` and `Joshua / Jose` without being told to. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,546 tests** exit 0. **Verified by screenshot on the deployed dev build**: Resources → Family Tree → search *Melchi* returns **2 of 277** and prints **Melchi** and **Melchi (II)** together, and *Jos* prints **Josech** and **Joshua** with no slash. **Still open, named rather than fixed**: the 8 chronology findings, the 10 Chinese names (escalated to "Next, in order" item 6), the 5 inferred links and the 31 tier-2 claims.

- **v1.6.125** — **five claims the book-intro card printed ABOVE the text, that the text denies (check 37).** `assets/book_introductions.json` was the last curated asset this repo listed as unchecked, and its position is the whole problem: the card renders *before the first verse*, in the app's own voice, and most of its sentences are not devotional but **countable**. **37a–37c, the references: 0 findings in 66 records** — every `keyPassage` resolves, names its own book, and overruns no chapter; the single flag is Lamentations 3:22-23, which `lxxwh` lacks (its chapter 3 carries 62 of 66, missing 22, 23, 24 and 29) and which is one of the **302** Septuagint absences check 29 catalogued and v1.6.116 already renders as *"this edition has no verse here"*. **37d, the quotations: 137 scored, 0 misattributed.** 59 records print a quotation inside `keyPassageDescription` and name the `keyPassage` as its source, so each asserts *these words are in that passage*; the test cannot be string equality (the quotations elide with … and are the author's own rendering) so it is a **ranking** — IDF-weighted cosine over tokens and bigrams, one token per Han character, against **five** English editions or **three** CUV-family ones, so a low score means *no edition we ship says this* rather than *this edition words it differently*. **The rule that had to be corrected before any of it was believed: a quotation is NOT suspect merely because some other verse scores higher** — Matthew 21:5 *is* Zechariah 9:9, and Romans 1:17 and Hebrews 10:38 both quote Habakkuk 2:4 — so only the **named** passage failing in absolute terms is evidence, and the best-scoring verse elsewhere is context for a human, never the trigger. The weakest reading, 2 Samuel's *Son of David* at **0.000**, is an epithet the card names and not a sentence it quotes. **37e is the finding: 5 countable claims were false, all fixed in three locales each.** **Lamentations** *"Five Acrostic Dirges"* — chapter 5's 22 verses open with only **11 distinct letters** against chapters 1–4's full 22, and **the app already contradicted itself**, `bible_trivia_page.dart` shipping *"four are alphabetic acrostics"* with `brokenChapters: [5]`. **Philippians** *"the word 'joy' appears 14 times"* — **no edition we ship prints it more than 7** (bsb 5, kjv 6, kjv+s 6, leb 5, nasb 7; Chinese 喜乐 12), and 14 is reachable only in the Greek and only by counting χαρά (5) and χαίρω (9) while silently dropping συγχαίρω (2); the whole family is **16**, which a reader can confirm in the app's own concordance, so the card now says so and says it is the Greek. **Obadiah** *"The Bible's Shortest Book"* — false on every metric (**3 John 218 original-language words to Obadiah's 285**, 2 John 13 verses to Obadiah's 21); it is the shortest book of the **Hebrew Bible**. **3 John** *"The shortest book in the Bible"* — **directly contradicted the Obadiah card**, so one of the two was wrong on any reading; this one is true but only by original-language word count, which it now states. **Isaiah** *"the longest and most quoted prophetic book"* — Jeremiah is longer on all three measures (**1,364 verses to 1,292**, 39,741 BSB words to 34,089, **21,580 Hebrew words to 16,672**); Isaiah leads only on chapters, and *"most quoted"* was checked separately and **kept**, `cross_references.json` pointing NT verses at Isaiah **2,601** times against Jeremiah's 1,041. **The claim that survived is recorded because the next reader will doubt it too**: Esther's *"the only book that never explicitly names God"* was reported FALSE by a subagent and is **true** — by Strong's number over `assets/originals`, **Esther is 0**, Song of Songs is exactly **1** (the יָה inside שַׁלְהֶבֶתְיָה at 8:6) and the next book up is Obadiah at **8**. Left unchanged. **The regression trap was found on the way out**: mirroring the five fixes into `scripts/build_book_introductions.py` and then comparing the two structurally showed the generator had **already drifted from the shipped asset in 36 fields across 21 books** — **34** occurrences of 耶和华 where the asset ships 雅伟, plus a partial 祂→他 pass in 4 fields — so **running the declared source of truth would have silently reverted every house-policy correction**. It was brought back verbatim, **preserving the asset's own choices rather than imposing new ones** (the Colossians summary keeps 祂 in one clause and not the other), and now reproduces all 66 records exactly. **Frozen by 5 guards** in `test/data_integrity_test.dart` that **derive their numbers from the corpus rather than restating the strings** — the acrostic count from the Hebrew initials, the joy count from the tagged Greek by Strong's, the shortest book from word totals over `assets/originals`, Jeremiah's length over Isaiah's, and the generator's fidelity — and **all five fail against the pre-repair asset**, checked by mutation. **Five instrument defects were fixed before any result was believed, every one having produced confident false findings**: a missing 兩 in the Chinese numeral table produced 7; a careless `(\d+)\s+verses?` produced 3 false Lamentations findings; an English possessive of a name ending in *s* (`Jesus'`) opened a quotation span that closed at the next real opening quote, handing the scorer 38 words of the author's own prose to score against Acts 1:8 — **and two more surfaced only by re-running the tool against the CORRECTED asset**, which is the only way they could have: the word-frequency check counted a claim the card **scopes to the Greek** against the Chinese editions, where 「喜乐、欢喜」 is a gloss no Chinese edition contains as written, and the acrostic check flagged any book containing a non-acrostic chapter, so it could not tell *"Five Acrostic Dirges"* from *"Four of Them Acrostics"*. Both now read the number claimed and compare it with the number the corpus has. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,551 tests** exit 0 (5 new). **Verified against the deployed dev build** by fetching `/assets/assets/book_introductions.json` over HTTP: all 66 intros present and all five corrections live. **Still open, named rather than fixed**: the locale number-parity sub-check raises **47** flags that are nearly all English number-*words* with no Chinese digit and is declared an instrument limit rather than 47 findings; 1 Peter's *sojourners* appears in no shipped edition of 1 Peter but the KJV's *sojourning* at 1:17; Mark's *favorite word is 'immediately'* ranges 13–40 by edition; Jeremiah's *forty years* against its own 627–582 BCE date field; and **the un-countable half of every summary is unchecked** — *"the most detailed prophetic portrait of the suffering Servant"* is not a sentence any instrument in this repository can weigh.

- **v1.6.126** — **the Greek search box could not find a single accented word the app prints (#321).** Reported from **outside this project** — a reader in Hong Kong searched for `ὁ θεός`, the phrase the app had just shown her, and was told the Bible does not contain it. **It occurs in 1,486 verses.** **The cause is a two-sided comparison folded on one side only, and the asymmetry is structural rather than accidental:** `assets/lxxwh.json`, the app's only Greek edition, is set **without accents** (Genesis 1:1 is literally `εν αρχη εποιησεν ο θεος τον ουρανον και την γην`), while every Greek word the app **shows** her — Word Study's parsed forms, the Strong's lexicon's lemmas — carries them. The app therefore *invites* a spelling its own corpus does not use, and three separate normalisers disagreed about what to do: `MainProvider.searchKeys` kept every accent, an old `stripHebrewPoints` in `command_query.dart` folded Hebrew points on the query only, and `strongs_service._normaliseForLemma` dropped combining marks. **The measurement that sized it is the number worth keeping: of the 19,555 distinct accented Greek forms this app displays, ZERO were findable as typed** — a **100% false-negative rate on its own displayed vocabulary**, shipped. 19,430 are findable now: `ὁ θεός` 0 → **1,486**, `ἀγάπη` 0 → **248**, `Ἰησοῦς` 0 → **602**, `ἐν ἀρχῇ` 0 → **29**. **The 125-form residue is reported and deliberately NOT chased**: **14** carry the elision koronis U+1FBD (`ἀλλ᾽`, `ἐπ᾽`, `ὑφ᾽`), which is not a letter wearing an accent, so the generated table correctly omits it and a hand-added exception beside a generated table is exactly the drift hazard the generator exists to prevent; the other **111** are genuinely absent from lxxwh — `συνκρίνοντες` in the tagged layer against the edition's `συγκρίνοντες` — **a real difference between two witnesses that a search must not paper over.** **The fold is confined to the comparison layer and is deliberately NOT in `sanitizeForSearchKey`**, because that function also builds `wordKeys`, and the Phrases and Related panes **print** `wordKeys`; unaccented Greek and unpointed Hebrew must never reach the screen or the clipboard, so the new `searchCorpusKey` folds only the one corpus in the app that has no reader. **BibleWorks confirms both halves of that design and corrects a third**: its help (`bwh17`, `bwh12`, `bwh29`, `bwh26`, read NUL-safe — **479 of 583 files contain NUL bytes**, the known trap) shows accent- and vowel-point-insensitive is its **default**, opt-in per language via a status-bar toggle, and for Hebrew *text* versions not even offered — *"Hebrew text searches are **always** accent insensitive"*; its word list keeps the accents **"regardless of this setting"**, which is precisely the folded-`searchKeys`/displayed-`wordKeys` split built here; **but for Western European versions it defaults to accent-SENSITIVE**, so the honest rule is *fold the original languages, respect the accents in modern European ones* — today theoretical for us and recorded rather than implemented, since we ship no such edition. **Greek and Hebrew fold differently and that forced a second function**: Greek polytonic is **precomposed**, so folding is 1:1 and length-preserving, while Hebrew points are **combining**, so folding is length-**reducing** and a hit lands at an offset the displayed string does not have — hence `foldDiacriticsAligned`, which returns a `sourceIndex` map so `splitOnTerms` finds the match in folded coordinates and slices it out of the **original**, and the reader is shown `ἀγάπη` with its accents, marked. **A comment that had never been true was found on the way through**: `_normaliseForLemma` claimed `agape` / `Agápē` / `ἀγάπη` all reach the same entry, but its body dropped only combining marks and **not one character in that lexicon is a combining mark** — all **8,273 accented Greek and 13,835 accented Latin** characters in it are **precomposed**, so the normaliser had never once fired and all **5,517 accented Greek lemmas** were unreachable by their own spelling. **The table is generated, not hand-listed** (`scripts/build_diacritics_table.py`, **738 pairs** over four Unicode ranges, **14 codepoints excluded** because NFD leaves them a non-letter — U+0385 → ¨, U+037E → ;, U+0387 → ·), because Dart has no NFD and a hand-maintained fold drifts from the corpus it claims to describe; it **re-runs to a byte-identical `lib/utils/diacritics.dart`**, checked. **Safety is pinned rather than asserted: the fold changes 0 verses in bsb, kjv and cuvs-yhwh** (5 in leb, all embedded transliterations), so no existing English or Chinese result can move. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,581 tests** exit 0 (**30 new** in `test/diacritics_test.dart` plus a rewritten `command_query_test.dart` group), including the ticket's own requirement — an accented and an unaccented query return the **identical verse set** — measured against the real 30,800-verse edition on disk, and a test that the fold **cannot invent a hit**. **Verified on the deployed dev build by reproducing the reader's query**: LXX+WH set as the reading version, `ὁ θεός` typed accents and all, and the panel returns **`ὁ θεός — 1486 verses`** with the accented query echoed back unchanged. **Still open, named rather than fixed**: the 14 koronis forms and the 111 orthographic divergences above; the Western-European accent-sensitivity divergence, which needs a settings toggle BibleWorks has and we do not; **modifier letters** ʼ (U+02BC, 1,720×), ʻ (U+02BB, 1,544×) and ᵉ (U+1D49, 1,417×) in the Strong's Hebrew transliterations, which are category Lm not Mn, are untouched by NFD, and are reachable by substring anyway (`abad` finds `ʼâbad`) — left, and recorded in a test so the next reader knows it was a decision; and **the second half of the same report is not a bug** — 「the true God」 returning 3 verses is a discoverability failure, handed to **#299**.

- **v1.6.127** — **the Atlas filter narrowed the list and the map drew all 1,271 places anyway (#319).** Scoped to Obadiah the sidebar correctly read `12 / 1271 places` while the map went on drawing Capernaum, Chorazin, Dalmanutha and **1,256 more**; clicking one returned `0 / 1 references`, the line "Not named inside the current scope", and then a blank rectangle — so the code **knew** the place was out of scope, drew it anyway, and then refused to say where the verse it had just counted actually was. **Two views of one filter that disagree is the defect class of #280 and #308, and this is its third appearance.** The root cause was a comment that had never been true: the map's `muted` layer was documented as "the geography around it", and it is nothing of the kind — it is the **exact complement of the index's own filter**, because `atlasIndex` caps nothing, so under a scope that layer is precisely what the reader just excluded, drawn in the same ink as the answer and one pixel smaller. The map now draws the scope and nothing else, behind **one layer control that prints its own denominator** ("Show 1259 others") so it can be read against the index's `12 / 1271` and *seen* to be the same filter. **Hidden means hidden from the eye, the finger AND the footer** — `showContext` is routed through the located set, the unlocated count, the fit bounds and the painter together, because a layer that is invisible but still hit-tested is a dot answering a tap nobody can see they are making. **The detail panel stops trimming and starts splitting**: `partitionRefsByScope` keeps both halves of a place's reference list instead of discarding one, because the reader has already picked the place, and dropping the verses in Jeremiah that name Teman does not narrow an answer, it withholds one — leaving them unable to tell "not in Obadiah" from "nowhere in scripture", the worse of the two things they could believe. Both halves are counted and on screen, the out-of-scope ones subdued but still tappable, under a header that names the scope and offers **"Clear the filter"** as a way out of it. **bwh33 is the same model from both ends, verified against the help topic rather than recalled**: every BibleWorks overlay is a check-box, its **66 per-book views** draw "the sites mentioned in each book" rather than the whole database dimmed, and **Auto-dim** exists so labels stay readable over what is left — the context is dimmed or toggled, never silently deleted. Also, below the 1180 px detail column a map tap now opens the sheet, so a dot that lights up always has somewhere to put its answer. **Verified on the deployed dev build, not assumed**: Obadiah scope → `12 / 1271 places`, twelve pins drawn, "Show 1259 others"; Teman → `1 / 6 references`, "5 more outside Obadiah", "Clear the filter", and all five listed.

- **v1.6.128** — **23 of the 59 books the gazetteer covers opened with a place they are barely about (#319, #308).** Found while verifying v1.6.127 on the deployed build. `AtlasSort.references` promised in its own doc comment to be "the only order that answers a question: scoped to Acts it prints the itinerary of Acts" — and the comparator ranked on `refs.length`, the **whole-Bible** count, even under a scope. It did not fail to implement that sentence, **it falsified it**: the ranking really answered "the most famous places, among those this book happens to name", which is a question nobody asked. A book about crossing the Jordan opened with a city it names **eight** times; the book set in Susa opened with a city it names **once**. **Joshua** Jerusalem 8 → **Jordan 58**; **Esther** Jerusalem 1 → **Susa 17**; **Jeremiah** Jerusalem 97 → **Babylon 150**; **Psalms** Jerusalem 17 → **Zion 34**; **Judges** Jerusalem 4 → **Gibeah 23**; **1 Samuel** Jerusalem 1 → **Ramah 13**; **Ezekiel** Jerusalem 26 → **Egypt 44**; **Daniel** Jerusalem 9 → **Babylon 15**; **Jonah** Tarshish 2 → **Nineveh 8**; **Nahum** Egypt 1 → **Nineveh 3**. The first row of a sorted list is the one a reader trusts most, so this was a wrong answer in the most-read position on the surface. **The printed number was the same defect stated numerically**: each row showed a bare `755` beneath a header that had just said `12 / 1271 places` — two counts, two denominators, one of them unstated — so scoped to Esther the row claimed **755 for a city the book names once**, overstating it by 754. Rows now use `scopedCountLabel`, the same house convention as that header and as the detail panel, and read `1 / 6`; with no scope it returns the bare figure and the unfiltered list is byte-identical to before. Ties on the scoped count fall back to the whole-Bible one, so a book naming Zion and Zoar once each still leads with Zion, and the count is lifted into the sort key rather than recomputed in the comparator, which sees each place O(log n) times. **The commit message on `bc0497a` is wrong in two numbers and one book name, and `0f0b18e` corrects it rather than rewriting it.** It claimed "all 62 books", "12 more decided by a tie-break", and "Nahum led with Tarshish over Nineveh". The measurement behind it was a **python probe reading `assets/bible_places.json` directly**, and the raw asset's code for Jonah is **`nah`** — which reads like Nahum, and is the exact confusion `place_geo.dart:112` already warns about **in writing** ("it reads like Nahum and it is **Jonah**", the surviving damage of an old global case-replace of `eph` that truncated eight book codes). The probe also did not know that `parseGazetteer` **merges possessives**, so its twelve "alphabetical ties" were Jerusalem and `Jerusalem's` counted twice. **Re-measured through `parseGazetteer` and `atlasIndex` themselves: 1,271 places, 59 books, 23 tops strictly wrong, ZERO ties.** The count survived; the denominator and one example did not — Nahum *was* wrong, differently, leading with Egypt over Nineveh. **The measurement is now a test against the shipped asset rather than a sentence in a comment**: no book may open with a place that is not among the most-referenced inside it, and four headline books are pinned by name. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,599 tests** exit 0. **Verified on the deployed dev build**: Joshua scope → `462 / 1271 places` opening with **Jordan `58 / 179`** and then `Egypt 14 / 558`, with the map's own control reading "Show 809 others" — 462 + 809 = 1271, the two surfaces agreeing arithmetically. **Still open, named rather than fixed**: **#317** (journey routes) is next and its research is seeded — BibleWorks draws routes as **just another overlay** ("colored lines showing the journeys of Abraham") and reports travel time as a **min–max range** built from min/max speed and hours per day, where our Atlas footer prints a single confident "about 2 days on foot"; the Atlas has **no hash route**, reachable only by clicking through Resources → Bible Atlas, which makes both deep-linking and screenshot automation awkward; and the gazetteer's references touch **59 of the 66 books**, the absent seven being **1–3 John, 2 Thessalonians, James, Philemon and Proverbs** — every raw code in the asset resolves and none is dropped as unrecognised, so this reads as the text rather than a data loss, but it is **observed, not audited verse by verse**.

- **v1.6.129 / v1.6.130** — **place records now show the illustrations we already have, and the measurement is the feature (#320).** The ticket attached a condition to itself — *measure the join rate before promising it, because a name match is a guess, not a join, and a wrong picture on a place is worse than no picture, since a reader will believe it* — so the numbers are in `lib/utils/place_illustrations.dart` beside the rule they chose. **`assets/maps_index.json` has no join key**: not one of its 1,192 plates names a gazetteer id and no field could be made into one, so every candidate join is inferred and the only question is which inference is safe. **Chapter coverage alone** reaches all **1,271 places and 17,177 pairs** — a 100% "join rate" that means nothing, because it answers "was this plate drawn from a chapter that happens to name this place": it puts four *Valley of Hinnom* plates on **Ziph**, *The Creation of Eve* on the **Tigris** and *Peter's Vision at Joppa* on **Caesarea**; restricting it to single-chapter plates (695 places, 6,594 pairs) narrows the blast radius without changing the kind of error. **Matching the Chinese names** would add 13 places and 80 pairs and cannot be made safe, because Han text has no word boundary: 撒冷 is a substring of 耶路撒冷 and 埃及 is a substring of 出埃及记, which is simply the Chinese name of Exodus — both would fire on every plate in those books. **Adjectival forms** would catch "a Samaritan woman" and would also turn `Cana` into `Canaan`. **What shipped is the English name as a whole word AND the plate's declared chapter range covering a verse that names the place.** Both halves are load-bearing: the name alone puts *Simon of Cyrene* plates on Libyan Cyrene, and the chapter alone is the rejected join above. That reaches **79 places, 218 pairs, 149 distinct plates — 6.2% of the gazetteer**, which is the honest number and the reason the strip is **absent from 94% of place records rather than apologising on them**. All 218 pairs were read by hand. **The word boundary must be Unicode-aware and not `[A-Za-z]`**: the place **Dor** is a whole word inside **Doré** by the ASCII rule, and that one slip alone attached **145** plates to one small town. **`kAmbiguousPlaceNames` exists because removing the chapter gate showed what it was silently carrying** — the gazetteer's **On**, the Egyptian city of Genesis 41, is a whole word in **51** captions because `on` is an English preposition; **Adam** is a town in Joshua 3:16 and a man in 10 captions; **Ham**, **Esau**, **Laban**, **Rahab**, **Tamar** and **Abel** are all towns and all people. The gate rejects every one of those *today*, which means the feature was correct **by luck rather than by rule**, and one new plate covering Genesis 41 with the word "on" in its caption would have ended that; 21 names are now refused outright, at a cost of **zero** of the 218. Deriving that set from the people data was rejected as automatic-and-wrong: Midian, Ephraim, Canaan and Sheba are each a person as well as a place and all four are in the verified 218 with plates that plainly mean the place — the distinction is which sense a *caption* uses, and no dataset in the repo records that. **Credit rides along with #300 by tap-through**, verified on the deployed build: the first Jerusalem plate opens as *Jebus — Ancient Jerusalem* with **"Image source · Source not recorded"** under it and the strip's own title carried into the viewer as **"36 illustrations naming it · 1/36"**. **A second, accuracy defect was withdrawn on the way**: two doc comments claimed in passing that the gazetteer's ordinal separates two distinct cities. It does not. Of its **80** ordinal groups, **66 (131 entries) carry byte-identical reference lists** and the other **14 all overlap** — **not one is a clean partition** — and `Antioch 1` and `Antioch 2` share the same **18** references, so nothing in the repo can tell them apart. The data is untouched (withdraw the claim, don't delete the data); the measurement is frozen in `test/atlas_index_test.dart` and written up as **`docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md` check 38**. **v1.6.130 fixes a defect in v1.6.129, found by screenshotting it.** The strip rendered nothing when the join was empty — right for the 1,192 places with no plate — but the Atlas's **book scope empties it too**, and there the same silence says "no picture of this place exists" about a place that has several. Measured before fixing: **292 (place, book) pairs across 56 of the 79 joined places** have a scope under which every plate falls away — `Ai` scoped to Genesis, Ezra, Nehemiah or Jeremiah showed nothing while **three** plates existed; `Asia` scoped to Romans hid four. So more than two thirds of the feature's surface carried a filter that could make it lie by omission. The panel now prints the header alone reading **`0 / 3`** and drops only the thumbnails — the #319 rule that `place_illustrations.dart`'s own doc comment had already committed to and the widget then discarded. **Verified on dev**: `Ai 1` scoped to Genesis reads **`0 / 3 illustrations naming it`** above **`2 / 31 references`** and **`29 more outside Genesis`**, three headers agreeing in one unit; clearing the filter restores **`3 illustrations naming it`** and the three plates. **Two instrument errors recorded rather than quietly corrected** (check 38): a python probe over the raw asset reported **151** distinct plates where the Dart parsers say **149**, and said 9 of the 14 differing ordinal groups overlap where the Dart says **all 14** — both wrong in the direction that understated the finding, the third check to record that rules live in the parser and not in the asset; and the 292 pairs were first reported as touching **48** places, which counts distinct **names** where the 79 counts **ids** — 56 by id, the same ordinal collision this check is about, since `Ai 1` and `Ai 2` are two entries and one name. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,618 tests** exit 0. **A screenshot-path fact that cost a wrong conclusion here**: the web build is now **skwasm**, which composites images into the canvas, so `document.querySelectorAll('img').length` is **0** on a page visibly showing loaded plates — the "prove images loaded by reading the DOM" trick from v1.6.83 is dead, and a zero there now means nothing. **Still open, named rather than fixed**: the join's **recall is unmeasured** — 6.2% is what the rule *finds*, and how many plates genuinely depict a gazetteer place but caption a person ("The Woman at the Well") is not something any instrument here can count; **Chinese captions are not searched at all**, so a reader in 简体 sees a strip built entirely from English text, correct but asymmetric; and **which Antioch is which** still needs a source the repo does not have. **#317** (journey routes) remains the next human-reported item, its research seeded in the v1.6.128 entry above.

- **v1.6.131 / v1.6.132 / v1.6.133** — **a tablet that works when you turn it was told, in Chinese, to go buy a tablet (#316).** The reader's device is a Mi Pad at **949 × 1375**: portrait it is 43 px short of the 992 px three-column gate, landscape it clears it by 383. `WorkbenchFit.adviceFor` had this right all along — `rotate` is returned **only** when `paneCountFor(height) >= 3`, so on that branch the whole workbench is one gesture away. The **copy** did not. Commit `46bc7e5` raised the bar from two columns to three on 2026-08-07 and rewrote **only the English**; both Chinese variants of `fitRotate` kept the two-column sentence, which ended in 「SeekSparks 需要平板或电脑」 — advice to go get a different device, printed **on a device that already qualifies**, directly above numbers that said so. The structural cause is that `uiStrings` is a `Map<String, Map<String,String>>` and **nothing in it forces the three variants to say the same thing**; a locale can be left behind silently and only a reader will notice. So the ratchet is the fix that lasts: for each of `en` / `zh-Hans` / `zh-Hant`, `fitRotate` may not name a tablet, a laptop or YsWords, and must promise **three** columns rather than two — checked against the three OLD strings first, which it rejects, because a ratchet nobody has seen fail is a comment. `fitRotateNeeds` now also prints the long edge (「横过来就有 1375 px」), the YsWords line is demoted from a `FilledButton` to `fitYsWordsAside` on a quiet `TextButton` — on this branch the reader's device is not the problem and sending them away answers a question they did not ask — and the two stale doc comments on `WorkbenchAdvice` that still described the two-column rule were corrected in the same commit, since the next person to touch that enum would have read them. **v1.6.132 fixes a defect in v1.6.131, found by screenshotting it.** The pane diagram at the top of the wall drew as **three grey hairlines**: each bar is a childless `DecoratedBox` inside `Expanded` inside a `Row`, and `Row`'s default `CrossAxisAlignment.center` passes a **loose** cross-axis constraint, so all three sized themselves to **zero height** and only their 1 px borders painted. The enclosing `SizedBox` measured its full 56 px throughout, which is why the suite was green: **no widget-tree assertion could see it**, and it also meant v1.6.131's change from `highlight: 2` to `highlight: 3` was notional — highlighting three bars says nothing when all three are 0 px tall. `CrossAxisAlignment.stretch`, and three tests that measure the **bars** rather than the box. **v1.6.133 is the one that outranks the ticket it was found under.** The 简体 screenshot of v1.6.132 showed the title, the rotate instruction and the YsWords button as **□□□** while the two paragraphs between them read perfectly. The first diagnosis — font weight, since the tofu strings were all `w600` and `pubspec.yaml` registers `NotoSansSC-Sub` with a **single** asset — was **wrong**, and the theme's own `titleSmall` disproves it by being bold and fine. It is **which style**: `workbenchTheme` rebuilds the text theme from `ThemeData.light()` and then restates the parent's `fontFamilyFallback` on **five** styles, so the other **ten** — `displayLarge/Medium/Small`, `headlineLarge/Medium/Small`, `titleLarge`, `titleMedium`, `labelLarge`, `labelMedium` — kept Roboto and **no fallback chain at all**. Roboto has no CJK, the engine's last resort is a download from fonts.gstatic.com, and `--no-web-resources-cdn` deliberately removes it. The advisory's title was `headlineSmall`, its instruction `titleMedium`, its button `labelLarge`; the healthy paragraphs were `bodyMedium` and `bodySmall`. **Seven strings, seven correct predictions, and the ratchet names exactly those ten styles when run against the pre-fix code** — including `labelLarge` resolving to a **null** fallback inside a real widget tree, which is the style no `textTheme.labelLarge` grep finds because Material buttons pull it from the theme themselves (**~90 button call sites**, plus 22 AppBar titles on `titleLarge` and 14 AlertDialog titles on `headlineSmall`). This is also **why `lib/` carries `fontFamilyFallback: kCjkFontFallback` roughly a hundred times**: each is a local patch of this one omission, applied wherever someone happened to look. One `.apply()` now carries the chain across all fifteen, which cannot change anything that already rendered — a fallback is only ever consulted for code points the primary face lacks. **Verified on the deployed dev build at the reader's own size**: 949 × 1375 portrait in **EN, 简 and 繁**, all three reading "turn it" with no tofu and no advice to leave (「把设备横过来，完整的三栏工作台就会打开。」 / 「把裝置橫過來，完整的三欄工作檯就會打開。」), the diagram drawing three filled full-height bars, and the version legible in the footer as `v1.6.133 · 2026-08-17 02:06 UTC+10`; then **1375 × 949 landscape**, where the gate dissolves with no relaunch and the **full three-column workbench** renders in 繁體 with the same version in its status bar. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,639 tests** exit 0. **Dark mode was NOT screenshotted, and the reason is recorded rather than glossed**: Chrome's `Emulation.setEmulatedMedia` flips `matchMedia('(prefers-color-scheme: dark)')` to `true` and the app goes on painting light — `themeMode` is absent from localStorage, so the setting really is `system` and this is the **instrument's** limit, not a finding. Dark is asserted instead, on `RichText` rather than `Text` so the style is the fully-resolved one the reader sees — a `Text.style` assertion would have passed straight through the tofu — with a 3:1 floor (not 4.5: the aside, the inactive language buttons and the version footer are meant to recede) and a `judged >= 8` guard, because a walk that finds nothing otherwise reports success having read nothing. **Observed in passing, not audited**: the landscape 繁體 workbench prints relative times in **简体** — 「6 小时前」 and 「4 天前」 under a 檔案/檢視/搜尋 menu bar, where 繁體 wants 小時 — which is the same class of per-locale drift as #316 itself, in a different file. **#317** (journey routes) remains the next human-reported item, its research seeded in the v1.6.128 entry above.

- **v1.6.134** — **the journeys, drawn only as far as the text takes them (#317).** Paul's **first** and **second** journeys ship as bwh33's overlay model — stackable sheets the reader checks on, each in its own colour, composing on one map. Two routes, not four: #317 asks to start narrow, and these two already exercise every hard case the rest will need (a route walked out and back, a route retracing another, stops the text does not support, and both Antiochs). **The design question was which channel carries what.** The brief asked for distinct colour AND dash per toggle; that was **departed from deliberately** and the departure is the substance of the feature. Dash was spent on **what the text claims**, not on which route it is: **solid** = the text says they went by land, **long dash** = by sea, **short dash** = it names the move but not the manner (Athens, 「manner not given」), **fine dots** = it does not put them there at all. Had dash also encoded route identity, "provisional" and "route 2" would compete for one channel and the more important would lose. Route identity is **colour plus a numbered ordinal badge** — colour-blind-safe, and it additionally says the order, so it is strictly richer than the channel given up. **Lystra is `8,10` on one badge**, which is the doubling back said out loud rather than two markers overprinting into a smudge. **No coordinate is ever invented, and the schema makes it impossible**: a stop names a gazetteer id and nothing else, and `journey_asset_test.dart` asserts no stop object carries `lat`/`lon`/`ll`/`coords`. **An unresolved stop BREAKS the line rather than bridging it** — skipping a stop and joining its neighbours draws a leg nobody claimed, and draws it *solid*, which is the same lie as inventing a coordinate with better manners; the build fails if any shipped stop fails to resolve. **`corridorLanes` is why the return leg is visible at all**: the first journey walks Galatia and walks the whole of it back, and on a shared chord the return overprints the outbound exactly, so a there-and-back reads as one-way; the second journey retraces the first, so it happens between routes too. **The sign convention is the trap and a failing test caught it with the wrong assertion** — the lane offset is along the perpendicular of the segment's OWN direction of travel, which reverses on the way home, so an out-and-back pair must be handed the **SAME** number and the reversal is what separates them; handing them opposite numbers, the obvious move, cancels against the reversal and restacks them. The code was right, the test was wrong, and the fix was to assert the resulting **geometry** and to rewrite the doc comment to state the convention. **Dashing is clipped (Liang–Barsky) before it is dashed**, because at a city-block zoom a leg is millions of pixels long; the shortcut of drawing long legs solid was rejected since solid *means* "by land" and would lie at exactly the zoom the reader looks closest at. **An accuracy defect was found and fixed on the way, outranking the ticket**: six files claimed the two Antiochs are **~800 km** apart. Measured with the repo's own `haversineKm` on the repo's own coordinates it is **500.0 km** — corrected in `places_pane.dart`, `atlas_page.dart` (×2), `atlas_index.dart`, `place_geo.dart`, `place_geo_test.dart`, `atlas_page_test.dart`, and frozen by `closeTo(500, 5)` so it cannot drift back. **This also answers, for these two entries only, the question v1.6.129's check 38 left open.** Check 38 is right that the reference lists cannot separate the ordinal groups — `Antioch 1` and `Antioch 2` carry the same 18 refs — but the **coordinates** can and do: `[36.2, 36.16]` is Antakya on the Orontes and `[38.31, 31.17]` is Yalvaç in Pisidia. The itinerary rests on the coordinate, not on the refs, and stop 6's note says so in prose. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,678 tests** exit 0. **Verified by screenshot on the deployed dev build** at 1400 px in English: both routes drawn and composing, out-and-back legs visibly **parallel** rather than overprinted, arrowheads on the direction of travel, `6,12` / `7,11` / `8,10` / `9` badges, map labels carrying `Antioch 1` and `Antioch 2` distinctly, the itinerary panel printing the caution, the straight-line total (「1948 km … not the distance travelled」), 「Where this itinerary comes from」, and a verse chip on **every** stop; Iconium at stop 4 carries a **`Provisional`** tag with 「16:2 reports Timothy's standing in Lystra and Iconium; it does not say they travelled there」. At **1100 px** the detail column correctly drops, the grammar key prints under the switches, and the itinerary arrives as a **bottom sheet**. **Dark was not screenshotted** — same instrument limit as v1.6.133 — but the palette is asserted light-vs-dark per slot in `journey_route_test.dart`; **繁體 was not screenshotted either**, and is covered instead by the asset test requiring name/range/basis/notes in all three locales. **A harness fact that cost most of the round and is now in the notes**: the dev deploy sat on the splash forever through a fresh profile, a cleared service worker and a cleared `localStorage`, which reads exactly like a boot regression and is **not one** — the persistent Chrome on 9333 reports `visibilityState: "hidden"`, `Page.bringToFront` does not clear it, a hidden tab runs **no frames**, and the splash only advances from a post-frame callback, while `Page.captureScreenshot` still returns a painted frame and hides the cause. **Run a second Chrome with `--headless=new` on another port** and it boots in ~20 s. Also: measure click targets from a `clip` (whose rect is honest CSS) and never from a full-page shot — reading the journey rows off one put them at y≈80 when they are at y≈159, and the miss looked exactly like an unwired tap handler. **Still open, named rather than fixed**: the **third** journey and the voyage to Rome are not written; selecting a **segment** does not yet drive the Analysis pane per #313's rule (only whole routes are readable); and the panel prints stop names **without** the gazetteer ordinal, leaning on each stop's note to separate the two Antiochs where the map badge does it structurally.

- **v1.6.135 / v1.6.136** — **the Related Sermons tab, and the 87.7% of its rows that were making a claim the data does not support (#313).** The last routing fix #313 left open, deferred by v1.6.108 and v1.6.109 with the note that it *"lands with the tab, not before it."* It landed, and the tab turned out to be the smaller half. `sermonsForVerse` matched at **chapter** level on purpose — a sermon expounding Genesis 1:1-5 is worth having when the reader is on Genesis 1:7 — but returned **one flat list**, and its exactness survived only as **sort order**, which is not a label. **Measured across the shipped corpus before writing any widget: of the 9,857 rows that list can produce across its 946 verse-level keys, 1,211 — 12.3% — cite the verse the reader actually selected.** On Romans 8:1 it hands back **49** sermons and says nothing about which of them are on 8:1. That is the **#308** defect class (a list whose unit is unstated), so this stopped being "add a 13th tab" and became an accuracy fix. The split lives in **`lib/utils/passage_sermons.dart`**, pure and free of `rootBundle`, so the claim each row makes is asserted directly rather than through a pumped tree. **The colon in the key prefix is load-bearing**: `Psalms 119:1` starts with `Psalms 1`, so a prefix match without it folds all of Psalm 119 into every result for Psalm 1 — a test names that trap. Rows now print **which** verses each sermon cites with consecutive runs folded (`cites v5-6`, `cites v9, 24`), measured first at **max 4 verses per sermon per chapter** so the folder never has to be clever. **Two departures from BibleWorks, declared in the source**: one row per **sermon** rather than bwh10's per-**reference** Resource Summary entry (per-reference would mostly repeat titles at that density), and the two tier headings, which are ours — BibleWorks leans on ordering where we would rather say it in words. **The tab is APPENDED**, since `workbench.analysisTab` persists by **index**; `morph_search_pane_test.dart` is the index ledger and it **failed on this change**, which is exactly its job — a reader with Places open keeps Places open across the upgrade. #297's strip arithmetic does not move: `Sermons` / 講道 are both narrower than the widest label already carried. With the tab existing, `ReaderAnalysisRequest.sermons` finally has a destination, so **`aiExplain` is now the only request that maps to null**, and the test that asserts it now says that is a **product decision** (a docked pane follows the selection, and following it there would spend a network call per verse and give generated prose the same frame as the corpus) rather than a deferral. **v1.6.136 fixes a defect in v1.6.135, found by screenshotting it**, on the very case the feature was designed for: the count line read **"49 sermons cite Romans 8 — 1 of them cite Romans 8:1."** One template was doing duty for **two independent verb agreements** and the singular half is the common one. `sermonCountKey` is pure and picks among four shapes; the **zh entries are deliberately identical sentences** because Chinese inflects for neither number, and a test walks every reachable `(total, onVerse)` pair asserting the key it names exists in **all three locales** — a missing key falls through to the English default **silently, in Chinese**, which is where nobody would notice. `total == 1 && onVerse == 0` is **unreachable, not unhandled**: the with-verse line is only drawn when a sermon is on the verse and the total counts it. `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,698 tests** exit 0. **Verified by screenshot on the deployed dev build at 1400 px**, English and 繁體: Romans 8:1 reads 「49 sermons cite Romans 8 — 1 of them **cites** Romans 8:1.」 above **Preaching this verse** (one row, `cites v1`) and **Elsewhere in this chapter** (`cites v5`, `cites v5-6`, `cites v9, 24`, …); the reader's ⋮ → **Related sermons** pulls the Sermons tab forward **with the verse still on screen and no BottomSheet**, proven by first parking the pane on Word Study and watching it switch; 繁體 gives 「Romans 8 共 49 篇講道引用，其中 1 篇引用 Romans 8:1。」 / 講這一節 / 引用第 1 節 / 本章其他經節 with no tofu, and the 張䁱和牧師講道集 credit (#291) at the foot. **The chapter-only tier is reachable in production, not dead code** — measured: **351 of the corpus's 1,297 keys are chapter-level**, and Romans 8's sort last reading 只引用整章. **Book name and date were checked and left alone**: `displayBook` follows the **edition** via `localeAwareBookName`, so a 繁體 UI on NASB correctly reads `Romans 8` exactly as the centre pane's own header does, and the date uses the model's shared `displayDate`. **Still open, named rather than fixed**: opening a sermon **at the citation** inside its body — BibleWorks does this, and it needs body offsets the corpus does not carry, so it was **deferred rather than guessed**; and #313's remaining items are retiring `StatsPage` as a reader entry point and folding the reader's 🔍/⋮/floating bar into workbench chrome per #269. **#315** (269 hardcoded `fontSize` literals) and **#318** are unblocked by this landing.

- **v1.6.137** — **the eight screens where the Font Size slider moved nothing at all (#315).** #311 fixed the *arithmetic* — every stop of the slider moves `WbType`. It did not fix the **reach**: a `fontSize:` literal is by construction a size that never changes, whatever the reader sets. The report was two photographs of 詞彙表 and 語法分行 at 12 pt with 「这些字很难看清楚还有很多界面都是」, and the **last four words are the whole ticket**: it was not one screen. **Re-measured before touching anything, and the brief's own number was stale**: 162 literals in 23 files today, not 269/34 — earlier rounds had already converted the two surfaces actually photographed, so what was left was *the rest of the app*. That splits in two, and **only one half is the reported defect**. Most files scale their body text and write literals only around it (disproportionate, still readable). On **eight** the setting was **inert end to end** — not the body, not the labels, nothing — which is a different and worse thing, and it is what 「还有很多界面都是」 means. Those eight, **58 sites**, now go through the scale: `about_page`, `bible_timeline_page`, `family_tree_page`, `floating_toast`, `collapsible_english_ref`, `copy_center_sheet`, `highlights_sheet`, `person_detail_sheet`. **About is not on the list for its size** — it carries the MorphGNT / OSHB / Eagle's View attributions, where legibility is a **licence obligation** rather than a comfort. **What bounds the risk of a 58-site sweep is that the conversion is provably appearance-neutral at defaults**: at `fontSize` 20 and `menuScale` 1.0 both scales are exactly 1.0, so `t.scaled(12)` **is** 12 — which also makes a before/after screenshot a strict comparison rather than an impression. **Two things deliberately NOT converted, and the brief was wrong about one of them.** `app_style_preset.dart`'s nine `fontSize:` are **fields of a preset** — the value handed to `setFontSize()` when the reader picks "Compact" — so routing them through the scale would make the presets **scale themselves**, a loop; item (3) of the ticket asked for exactly that. And `WbPanel` subtitles and `WbTag` counts answer **Menu Size**, not Font Size: that split is #311's design showing up correctly on screen, not an omission, which is why the Family Tree's group blurbs stay small while its titles grow. **The ratchet is the part that lasts, and it exists because no widget test can see this defect**: a `TextStyle` assertion passes whether the number came from the setting or from thin air, and the number **is** real — it simply never moves. Only the source can say which. So the eight move from `budget` to `finished`, where the allowance is **zero** and a literal reappearing is the reported bug coming back rather than new debt; **104 literals in 15 files remain, every one booked**, with `bible_reading_pane`'s 41 left to #279's chrome pass on purpose (splitting one file between two tasks is how a merge conflict becomes a silent style regression). `flutter analyze` exit **0**, **2,698 tests** exit 0. **Verified by screenshot on a LOCAL release build at 12 / 20 / 40 pt** — About, Family Tree (collapsed groups *and* an expanded lineage), the person detail sheet, the Timeline and the Copy Center, all of which grow end to end with correct wrapping and no overflow — **and then on the deployed dev build in 繁體 at 40 pt**, where About renders 【本應用是非商業的個人／教會研經工具】 at full size with **no tofu** (v1.6.133's fallback chain holding) and the version legible as `v1.6.137`. **Named rather than glossed, three things.** (1) `highlights_sheet`'s only caller carries `// ignore: unused_element` — the floating header opens `HighlightsPage` instead — so it is converted but **unreachable and NOT screenshotted**, and the ratchet's comment now says so instead of implying every finished surface is one tap away. (2) **Text that inherits a THEME style still does not scale**; the About page's contact link is the visible case, and that is a larger surface than this ticket, which is about literals. (3) **A harness fact that cost the round its first hour and is now in the notes**: seeding `localStorage['flutter.fontSize']` **does not take** — the key survives the reload and the app goes on rendering at 20, and the two before/after captures came back **byte-identical**, which reads exactly like "the fix did not ship". Drive the **Settings slider** instead (track spans CSS x 444→955 at 1400 px), and **re-measure its y every time**, because the Settings page scales with the setting it is setting. Also `/tmp/ss3.js` picks its target with a hardcoded URL regex that does not match `127.0.0.1`; it now honours `SS_MATCH`, without which every "local build" screenshot is silently of the dev deploy.

- **#323 — 1,607 wrong Traditional characters, and the blanket fix that would have added more.** The owner reported 以賽亞書 2:16 printing 他施的船**只** for 船**隻**. It was one symptom of a conversion that resolved each ambiguous character once instead of per word, and the proof is that the correct character appeared **zero** times in 31,102 verses — 隻 0 against 只 1,219, 餘 0 against 余 230, 淨 0 against 凈 519. Repaired: **只→隻 548** occurrences in 309 verses, **余→餘 230** in 227, **幹→乾 199** in 185, **幹→干 111** in 104, **凈→淨 519** in 412, with **9 幹 kept** (才幹, 枝幹, the lampstand's shaft, Job's trunk). **幹 is three-way, not two** — Simplified 干 covers 乾 (dry), 干 (to offend, plus twelve transliterated names: 亞干 is Achan, 王干大基 is Candace) and 幹 (a trunk, an ability) — so the ticket's own "幹 → 乾" would have swapped a third of that class for a different wrong answer, printing 乾犯 and 亞乾. **opencc was not run and must not be**: `s2t` repairs most of this and *invents* a defect at 以賽亞書 29:17, turning 「不是只有一點點時候嗎？」 into 隻有, which is why every rule is a named context and an unrecognised character is left untouched and reported. 只 turns on a numeral or determiner immediately before it, which splits 1,219 occurrences into 548 classifier and 671 adverbial contexts with no overlap in either direction. **`biblexg-v2-tr.json` is clean and was not touched** (隻 50, 餘 38, 淨 78; all 31 of its 幹 genuine) — the ticket's guess that one pipeline damaged both files is false, and a test now says so, so a later pass does not go looking there and break something that was never wrong. `tools/repair_cuvs_yhwh_tr.py` is idempotent and byte-for-byte preserves the shipped serialisation.
- **#312 — phrasing opens on the sentence, and names whose punctuation drew it.** Phrasing argues about a unit of thought and the unit is a sentence; the pane opened on the cursor verse, which is the wrong unit for **21.5% of BSB, 23.1% of LEB, 26.0% of 和合本雅伟版 and 27.9% of KJV** verses. The bounds are an **edition's** claim and the pane says so: LEB makes Ephesians 1:3-14 one sentence, the KJV three, the BSB seven, and `assets/originals` carries **0 sentence marks in 438,821 words**, so a reader phrasing the Hebrew gets bounds borrowed from the edition they were reading, named in the note. A semicolon and a colon are deliberately not terminators — the KJV ends Eph 1:7 on one and counting it cuts 1:7-12 into three sentences that are not there. The 12-verse cap is measured (sentences run past it for 0.50%-0.70% of verses and every one is an enumeration — Joshua 15's town list, Ezra 2, Luke 3's genealogy) and set at 12 rather than 10 because LEB's Ephesians 1:3-14 is exactly 12. A capped sentence is still named and one tap away.
- **v1.6.139 — #322, the Browse pane had THREE reference layouts on one screen, and the untagged one was the row that would not line up.** An untagged edition (NASB, KJV) built its whole line as a single `Text.rich` with the reference as the FIRST SPAN, so the reference took part in line-breaking: the verse began after it and every **wrapped** line began *underneath* it at the far left. The two tagged paths each built their own `Row` with an **intrinsic-width** reference cell, so their prose began wherever that row's own verse number ended — `1:1` and `1:12` starting the text at different x. All three now go through one `BrowseVerseRow` whose column is a **minimum**, not a fixed width: the width comes from a character model, and a model can be beaten by a font wider than the one it was calibrated on, so the failure mode is a slightly ragged column rather than a **clipped reference, which would misname the verse**. The column is sized to the references **actually on screen**, so a reader in Obadiah does not pay for `Song of Solomon` existing, and it follows the font-size slider. References get their own per-class em model because the version gutter's flat 0.78 em over-estimates a sixteen-character mixed-case reference by **45%** — about 70px of a 480px reading pane spent on nothing; a test checks the model never UNDER-estimates any of the **66** English book names against advance widths read out of the shipped Roboto at `wght=600`. **The word-gap asymmetry is deliberate and the first draft's reason for it was wrong.** The originals `Wrap` keeps `spacing: 5` and the tagged one keeps none, which reads like the very inconsistency this pass was cleaning up. The comment justified it as "a tagged run carries its own trailing space" — **false for two of the five editions.** Measured whole-corpus, share of runs carrying a leading *or* trailing space: `bsb` **91.57%** (trailing), `kjvs` **68.53%** (**leading**, `", Sheth"`), `lxxwh` **94.28%** (mixed), `cuvs-plus` **0.00%** and `cuvs-yhwh` **0.05%** — because **Chinese does not put spaces between words at all**. Against that, `assets/originals` carries whitespace on **0 of 438,821** words, so the gap is required there and forbidden everywhere else: the same 5px would double-space the BSB, mis-space the KJV and open holes inside 亚当生塞特. Three tests freeze those numbers, because the assets are regenerated offline and a convention change upstream would jam the pane with the whole suite still green. RTL is untouched **on purpose** — the reference stays OUTSIDE the `Directionality` scope, or a Hebrew line throws it to the right-hand end and the column stops running straight down the page. **Verified:** analyze **0**, suite **2,734 green** plus 12 in the new file; screenshot on dev v1.6.139 at 1400px of 2 Chronicles 1 with NASB · BSB · KJV · WTT stacked shows one straight reference column, NASB's **wrapped** second line hanging in the text column instead of under the reference, and the Hebrew still right-aligned with its reference on the left. **Note for the next run:** the `#322` brief asked for the left offsets to be read with `javascript_tool`; **that is not possible on this build** — it is skwasm and draws text into the canvas, so there are no text nodes to measure. The equal-x claim is carried by a widget test asserting `getTopLeft().dx` across all three render paths, which is stricter than reading the DOM would have been.
- **v1.6.140 — #318, an interactive Bible chronology, derived from the text rather than typed in.** Genesis 5 and 11 as bars on one Anno Mundi axis, both traditions, reachable at Resources → Bible Chronology. **The numbers are read out of the shipped KJV and LXX by `scripts/build_chronology.py`, which hardcodes verse ADDRESSES and never values** — 20 patriarchs, 18 internal `begat + after == lifespan` sums, and independent agreement with `family_tree.json` on **14 of 14** shared figures. **The second witness earned its keep on the first run and the self-check did not.** Genesis 7:11 gives the flood year as an ORDINAL, and unaccented Greek cannot separate ἕν "one" from ἐν "in", so the parser read 「εν τω εξακοσιοστω ετει」 as **1** and put the flood at **AM 1643**; every a+b=c sum still passed, because a wrong number that is internally consistent is still consistent. Anchor moved to **7:6**, a cardinal in both texts, and the totals now land where the reference works put them — **MT 1656, LXX 2242**. **Both texts ship because the divergence is a fact about manuscripts we already carry**, not a reconstruction: Adam fathers Seth at **130** in the Hebrew and **230** in the Greek with the same 930-year total, nine such gaps compound, and the axis visibly rescales on the switch (endAm 2187 vs 3449). Two findings are surfaced rather than smoothed: **the best-known claim from these genealogies — Adam alive when Lamech was born — is Masoretic-only**, false on the Greek by 524 years, and **the LXX has Methuselah dying 14 years AFTER the flood**, which the generator emits as a note *conditionally, only while the arithmetic still shows it*. Every year is **AM and never BC**: Ussher's 4004 BC is named in the asset precisely to say it is **not** adopted, and the Samaritan Pentateuch is absent and says so, because we do not ship that text. **Bars, not the source chart's radial fan** — comparing durations is the whole exercise and bars at different angles cannot be compared by eye; the grammar kept (duration as a bar, colour by descent) is centuries old and unowned, and no artwork, data table, index or wording was taken from the copyrighted sheet. **Two defects found by measuring and by looking, neither of which any "no exception" test would have caught.** (1) `_nameLane`/`_rowHeight`/`_detailPanelWidth` were fixed pixels holding text the font slider scales **0.6×–2×**; the lane is the one label in the app deliberately **not** ellipsised (#297), so at 40 pt it did not clip and did not throw — it painted the name across the bars. All four now derive from `t.text`/`t.scaledChrome`, the 900px side-by-side threshold is now the **sum** of the widths it protects rather than a literal, and a test measures `getMaxIntrinsicWidth` against the granted width at 12/20/40 pt. **The test font gives Latin a full em, ~2× a real face, so the English names are not asserted in absolute pixels** — what covers them is that the lane is a fixed multiple of its text, plus the screenshot. (2) The selected man's life-band was drawn in **each row's own** descent hue, so one span read as two, blue over Seth's line and brown over Shem's; it now takes the selected man's colour, per the #317 rule that a contested channel encodes the **claim**, not the identity. **Verified:** analyze exit **0**, suite **2,768 green** (25 asset/arithmetic + 6 widget). Screenshots on a LOCAL release build at 1400px, English: the chart with Methuselah's bar ending exactly on the flood line at 1656; the panel showing 187/782/969 against Genesis 5:25-27 with 10 contemporaries longest-first; and the Septuagint view with the axis rescaled and Methuselah's bar past the flood. **Deployed to DEV only** — #318 was a human request for a featured module, which is the "owner asked" trigger rather than the 3-iteration count; **prod untouched and still needs explicit approval**. **Harness note:** `{"emu":[1400,900]}` against an already-1400px headless window **halved the layout scale** (the app drew into 700×460 of a 1400×900 viewport) while `innerWidth` kept reporting 1400 — the documented "override WIDER than the window rescales" trap fires at **equal** sizes too. Drop `emu` entirely when the headless window is already the size you want; `unemu` restored dpr 1 and 1:1 shots. Resources → Bible Chronology is at CSS **(215, 10)** then **(245, 160)**.

- **#318 phase 2 — the chart runs to Joseph, and the axis strip that had been drawing into a canvas ZERO PIXELS TALL since v1.6.140.** v1.6.140 stopped at Terah because Genesis 5 and 11 state their figures in a formula and the narrative does not, and stopping there left the chart unable to answer the question it was built for. **The answer, and the finding worth the round: the two texts state the same begetting age and lifespan for 4 of 19 men in Genesis 5 and 11, and for 4 of 4 in Genesis 12-50** — the choice of text moves the genealogies and leaves the patriarchs exactly where they are, which is only visible with both halves on one axis. **24 patriarchs** (23 Masoretic, 24 Greek) and **three named epochs** where there was one unlabelled flood rule: the flood (Genesis 7:6, AM **1656 / 2242**), Abram leaving Haran (12:4, **2023 / 3389**), the descent into Egypt (47:9, **2238 / 3604**), each printed in the header with the verse that dates it and each labelled on the axis; the axis now runs to **2309 / 3675** (was 2187 / 3449). **Exactly one figure in the whole chart rests on no verse at all, and it is named as such**: Jacob's age at Joseph's birth follows from four verses that do — Joseph is 30 before Pharaoh (41:46), the seven plenty years pass (41:53), and 45:6 dates the descent by saying five of the seven famine years (41:30) are still to come, so Joseph is 39 at the descent and Jacob is 130 (47:9), leaving **91**. `refs` records no verse for it because there is none. **The self-check nearly vanished and did not**: Genesis 12-50 states a third figure exactly once and it is the strongest in the file — 47:9's 130 plus 47:28's 17 years in Egypt against the **147** that same verse gives, in both texts, checking the descent year as well as the parse. Sums checked **18 → 20**; the independent agreement with `family_tree.json` goes **14 of 14 → 21 of 21**, compared by *interval* for its BC-dated records because AM and BC do not convert. **Where it stops is a decision, not an omission**: at Genesis 47:9 and not the exodus, because Exodus 12:40 covers different ground in the two texts — the Hebrew's 430 years are in Egypt, the Greek's are in Egypt *and* Canaan — so carrying the axis further would make the chart choose between the texts instead of drawing both. That is said on the chart, in both traditions, not only in a comment. **Three parser traps, all in the last four rows, all the same family as v1.6.140's ἕν/ἐν flood bug — the first figure in the verse is not always the one you asked for**: unaccented Greek cannot separate εἷς "one" from εἰς "into", so 47:9's 「εις τας ημερας」 makes the LXX list `[130, 1]`; the KJV's "**a** coffin in Egypt" does the same to 50:26 (`[110, 1]`); and 45:6's ordinal δεύτερον is unparseable, so the elapsed famine years come from the remainder. **An accuracy defect caught in my own draft before it shipped**: the trust sentence keyed on `checked` alone, which would have printed "The text states all three figures" on **Jacob's** panel, where 91 and 56 are derived and only 147 is stated — `checked` and `stated` are different facts and must not share a sentence. It now counts figures carrying no verse, read off `refs` rather than off the chapter, across **four record shapes**: Genesis 5 (all stated, checked), Genesis 11 (lifespan derived), Terah/Abraham/Isaac (`livedAfter` derived, and it is a **difference**, not a sum — which is why the old string "the third is their sum" was simply false for Abraham), Jacob (two derived but checked), Joseph (one figure, stated). Three new strings in en / zh-Hans / zh-Hant. **Joseph's two missing figures are absent, not zero**: he ends the chain, so `begatAt` and `livedAfter` are null and their rows are not drawn — an empty row would say the text was asked and stayed silent — and his bar is solid end to end rather than carrying a pale tail that would claim a next generation at an unknown date. **The shipped defect the new test found, which no "no exception" test could**: `tester.getSize` on the axis strip returned **0.0**, because a `CustomPaint` with no child takes the smallest height its constraints allow and inside a `Row` that is zero — `_AxisPainter` had been positioning every label from `size.height` against a **zero-height canvas since v1.6.140**, drawing them above the strip, and nothing threw. Fixed with `SizedBox.expand()`, matching `_BarPainter`'s call site. The strip's height is no longer a 30px literal either: `axisStripHeight()` derives it from a **measured** CJK line, because the strip now holds three rows of un-ellipsised text and a clipped epoch name is silent (#297). **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite **2,782 green** (was 2,768; 36 asset/arithmetic + 9 widget in this module). Screenshots on a **LOCAL release build** at 1400px: three epoch names staggered over two rows, unclipped, with leader lines down to their rules; Jacob's bar splitting at Joseph's birth and Joseph's solid end to end; Joseph's panel showing only 「Lifespan · 110 years · Genesis 50:26」 with no begetting rows; Abraham's showing 100 (21:5) and 175 (25:7) with the derived 75 carrying **no** verse, and the Terah note beneath it. **NOT deployed** — undeployed iteration 1 of 3, this item is not a human-reported defect, and every visual claim above is from a release build rather than a guess. **The ambiguous decision, named**: the chart puts Abram's birth at **AM 1948**, reading Genesis 11:26 as his year. Acts 7:4 has him leaving Haran after Terah's death and 12:4 has him leaving at 75 — **60 years before** Terah dies at 205 (11:32) — so the chronologies that follow Acts make Abram the *youngest* of the three sons and put his birth at **AM 2008**, moving him and everyone after him 60 years later. The chart takes the plain reading of 11:26 and **discloses the alternative with its arithmetic twice**, in the header and on Abraham's own panel, rather than choosing silently; a reader who prefers Acts adds 60 to every year from Abraham down.
- **v1.6.143** — **the timeline printed 85 of its 98 dates as fact (#318 phase 3).** Check 32 (v1.6.120) stamped a `basis` and an `approximate` on all 277 people *and* all 98 timeline events, guarded both halves, and declared check 8 closed — but only the people's half ever reached a reader. **`TimelineEvent.fromJson` never read either field**, so the creation's 「4000 BC」 printed in exactly the same voice as the exodus's 「1446 BC」, and only one of those is countable from anything the text says.
