The asset's own `_meta.note` — *"nothing here is presented as a date the text gives unless the text gives it"* — was a promise only the file kept. **The existing guard could never have caught it, because the asset was right the whole time**: an asset test and a formatter test do not compose into a claim about the screen, so the new `test/bible_timeline_page_test.dart` mounts the page. Generalise it — when a field is added to a corpus *so a reader will see it*, ask which consumers were updated and whether `fromJson` is one of them. **No asset byte changed.** The first plan was to re-base the eight antediluvian events onto one creation epoch; a refuter killed it, and two counts are load-bearing for anyone who tries again — `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md` and `test/chronology_test.dart` establish that **this repository deliberately fixes no year for the creation** (MT and LXX differ by ~1,400 years and shipping one promotes it), and `audit_dates.py --write` would revert any hand-edit on its next run. So the seam is **disclosed, not re-dated**: Genesis 5 and 7:6 put **1,656** years between the creation and the flood where these events leave **1,652**, said once on the antediluvian era header with a door into Bible Chronology, which counts in AM from the text and so has no seam to hide. The hedge is worded as `BiblicalPerson.displayYears` already words it, to the space. **The year column is now measured, not chosen** — it was a fixed 90 px around text the Font Size slider scales 0.6×–2×, nothing in it ellipsises, and a multiple of the font size does not work either: the widest string needs **12.4 em in the test font and about 8 em in a real face**, so any constant is too tight to ship or too wide to look at. Two harness traps, both silent: a bare `TextPainter` measures the style you hand it and **not** the inherited `DefaultTextStyle`, undercounting by exactly the inherited `letterSpacing` (0.25 px a character, **3 px across a 12-character year** — enough to wrap it); and `find.text` on a year is not unique, since **six events sit on −1446 and two on −4000**. That collision became the best demonstration in the suite: at −1446 the exodus reads 「1446 BC」 while the burning bush, the plagues, the Red Sea, the manna and Sinai keep the hedge, in adjacent rows. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite **2,792 green** (was 2,782), and the shipped build driven in a browser — 98 events, every antediluvian year hedged, the seam note and its button on the era header, the derived-basis sentence on the open exodus row, and the button landing on Bible Chronology at **AM 1656**. **A regression the suite could not have found, because driving the deploy found it first**: measuring the lane from the *search results* rather than the whole corpus made it narrower whenever the filter was, so the rail slid left as the reader typed — **141 px down to 117** on a one-word query — fixed in v1.6.143 with a test confirmed to fail against the old code. **Deployed to dev** (v1.6.142 then v1.6.143); prod untouched. **Left open, deliberately**: `audit_dates.py:693-698` stamps `basis` from a hardcoded id whitelist without checking the year, so `ishmael_born` (Abraham + 86, Gen 16:16), `rebekah_marries` (Isaac 40, Gen 25:20), `joseph_sold` (Joseph 17, Gen 37:2) and `joseph_rises` (Joseph 30, Gen 41:46) are stamped `conventional` though the text's own arithmetic gives them — so the shipped **85/13 split is a floor** on how much of the timeline is derived, not a measurement of it. Also unfixed and visible: the count line reads **「1 events」** when a search matches one.

- **v1.6.144** — **The `.` operator was only ever taught on an EMPTY result, so a reader who got three verses never learned it (#299, handed over from #321).** She searched `the true God`, got 3 (2 Chr 15:3, Jer 10:10, 1 Jn 5:20) and could not find John 17:3, "the only true God" — which the order-free reading holds. Three is not zero, so the app showed her a short list and said nothing. The operator was **not** hidden: the strip above the results carries a `.` button and the `?` card explains it. What was missing is anything connecting it to *this* thin result, and any idea what it would give. So the fix is not a louder hint. **The rule now enforced is that the app never offers a query it has not run**: the count beside the offer is measured under the same edition and the same scope as the search just performed, and the offer is **withheld** when it is not larger. That directly answers the second half of the same report — the old hint named `.ὁ θεός` without running it, against a corpus set with no accents, and led to a second empty page; a dead end dressed as help is worse than silence. **One rung only, phrase → AND, never AND → OR**: dropping the phrase constraint drops word order and adjacency without changing which words were asked for, whereas `/the true God` is **23,248** verses — a different question. A phrase carrying `!` is refused outright, because in a phrase the `!` fills one sequence position and in an AND it excludes the whole verse. When the loosest honest reading still finds nothing, the pane now names the absent **word** instead of repeating "No results found". **Verified on the deployed build, not just in the suite**: `the true God` → 3 verses with `.the true God — 21 verses` under the header (the profile's edition, not the BSB's 18 — which is the invariant working), one tap landed on exactly 21 with **John 17:3** in the list, and `the true Elohim` produced "This search found no verse containing Elohim." with **no** offer, because the looser reading returns 0 too. **Stated as a judgement, not a measurement**: `kBroadenBelow = 20` — there is no way to read a reader's expectation off her query, so the signal used is that the result did not fill the pane; it also gates the cost, since the second scan (14–42 ms over 31,102 verses) is only paid on a short list. **Deliberately out of scope**: widening an active search scope as a second kind of alternative — the scope already has its own banner (#280), and offering both would double the surface for one defect. **Deployed to dev**; prod untouched. **Numbers corrected against the ticket**: #299 claimed 22 verses; measured across all 7 English editions it is bsb 18, kjv/kjvs 17, leb 21, nasb 21, nasb-ev/nsn-plus 19 — **22 appears nowhere**, which is exactly why nothing in the app carries a remembered count.

- **v1.6.145 — #318 phase 4: the chart runs to the death of Moses, and Exodus 12:40 turned out not to be a choice the app has to make.** Phase 2 stopped the axis at the descent into Egypt on the grounds that the Hebrew's 430 years are in Egypt and the Greek's are in Egypt *and* Canaan, so carrying it further "would make the chart choose between the texts". **That reasoning was wrong, and this round overturns it in the source rather than quietly**: each text states plainly where it counts the 430 from, so each tradition is carried on **its own wording**, and the divergence becomes a thing the chart shows instead of a thing it must settle. The exodus lands at **AM 2668 (MT) / 3819 (LXX)**, Moses dies at **2708 / 3859**, and the axis grows from 2309 / 3675. **26 patriarchs** (was 24), **24** internal sums checked (was 20), **23 of 23** independent agreement with `family_tree.json` (was 21 of 21), **five** named epochs (was three). **The Greek's start is shipped as a reading, not as a check**: the LXX's 215 + 215 split is subtraction from a total already in use, so it corroborates nothing, and the note says so and cites Galatians 3:17 as invoked on **both** sides rather than as settling it. **Aaron and Moses are the only figures on the chart read from outside Genesis** — Exodus 7:7 (83 and 80 before Pharaoh), Numbers 14:33 (the forty years), Numbers 33:39 (Aaron 123), Deuteronomy 34:7 (Moses 120) — and the check is the Genesis 5 shape, `a + b == c`, arriving from **three books instead of one verse**: 80 + 40 = 120 and 83 + 40 = 123, so a mis-parse of any of five figures cannot pass. **Aaron's pair is corroboration and not a second independent witness** (his death is dated from the same exodus year), which is why Moses' `checked` rests on **Deuteronomy 31:2**, where he states 120 himself in a different chapter on a different occasion. **Where it stops is now measured rather than argued.** The verse that would carry the axis to Solomon is 1 Kings 6:1, an ordinal, and the note is emitted **only while the two number-readers disagree about it** — the KJV comes back `[400]` and the Greek reads ἐξ, "out of", as the numeral 6 (`[6,1,1,1]`). A figure this chart cannot read is one it will not plot. **The one external test of the 430 is computed, both ways, and never asserted**: Exodus 6:18/6:20 with Genesis 46:11 caps the descent-to-exodus gap at what remains of Kohath plus all of Amram plus Moses' 80, which is **350 against the 430 the Hebrew needs — 80 short** — and **342 against the Greek's 215, 127 to spare**. The note branches on the measurement, so it cannot outlive the numbers. Both men die in the same AM year (Aaron first), and a conditional note pre-empts that reading as a bug. **The header was reworked because the axis growing made it a regression**: it is a layout **sibling** of the chart, not an overlay, so every line it prints is a line of chart, and six caveats would have been six paragraphs pushing the bars off screen. The first attempt — a bounded scroller — shipped a paragraph **cut mid-line with no visible thumb**, caught by screenshot and thrown away; it reads as a rendering fault. The header now prints only the notes about the **chart** and names the men who carry the rest (`More on (select to read) · …`), one tap from their own panels. **Two accuracy defects the extension exposed, both older than it and both plausible-reading**: (1) the trust sentence keyed on `checked` and `derived` but never on **how many figures exist**, so Moses and Aaron — who have a lifespan and nothing else — were told "The text states **all three** figures", flatly false, and Joseph has been told "every figure here… none was derived" (true, but plural about one number) since v1.6.141; two new strings in en / zh-Hans / zh-Hant now cover the single-figure shape. (2) With two epochs crowded at the right-hand end, both names clamp to the same edge and the upper one's leader line was drawn **straight through the lower name**; leaders now paint under the text and leave from the corner nearest their own year, which is a no-op wherever a label sits over its own year. **A generator capability worth knowing**: `Reader.figure` takes `book=` and **negative** indices, because Exodus 6:20 ends on Amram's years in both texts but the Greek reads εἰς γυναῖκα as the numeral 1 ahead of them — counting from the back names the figure the verse actually ends on in either language. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite **2,822 green** (was 2,792); screenshots on a **local release build** of both traditions, the reworked header, the plum Levi bars, and the panels for Moses, Joseph and Peleg (all three trust-sentence shapes), then the **deployed dev build** re-shot after the release. **Deployed to dev as v1.6.145**; prod untouched and still needs explicit approval. **Two things reported and deliberately NOT fixed.** `assets/family_tree.json` puts **5 years** between Aaron's and Moses' births where Exodus 7:7 gives **3** — it marks Aaron's dates `conventional`/`approximate`, so wiring a witness check there would emit noise rather than a finding, and the second-witness sweep leaves both men out on purpose. And `assets/originals/genesis.json` **15:13** appears to be missing לָהֶם; unconfirmed, and nothing in this module reads it.

- **#318 phase 5 — the timeline's "85 of 98 are reconstructions" was never a measurement, and nine of the 85 were dates the text fixes outright.** v1.6.143 shipped the basis to the reader and recorded what it could not fix: `tools/audit_dates.py` stamped `basis` from **thirteen event ids typed into the file**, and it never looked at a year. So the page printed *"the text fixes no year for this"* over **`ishmael_born`** — whose year Genesis 16:16 states outright — and over Rebekah's marriage (Gen 25:20), Joseph's rise (41:46 + 41:53 + 45:6), the tabernacle (Exod 40:17), Sinai (19:1), the manna (16:1), the Jordan crossing (Deut 1:3 / Josh 5:6) and the wilderness forty. **Every year is now COMPUTED** from a table of steps, each naming the verse that states its interval, and **a step may only stamp an event when its arithmetic reproduces the year already in the asset** — a step that disagrees abstains and is reported, because moving a published date is a decision this tool does not make. All 18 reproduce exactly. Split **75 conventional / 5 Thiele / 18 derived**, was 85/5/8. **Three findings the derivation forced open.** (1) **`scripture` was over-claiming.** Scripture states intervals, never a BC year; every year here is measured from Thiele's Solomon accession, so the basis is `scripture+thiele` — the word `family_tree.json` already used for the same shape, and the two assets no longer disagree about their own vocabulary. (2) **The narrative refs are not the dating verses**, and on nine of the 18 the two sets **do not overlap at all** — the Jordan crossing is narrated in Joshua 3–4, which gives no number. Events carry a separate `datingRefs`, the whole chain back to the anchor, rendered as outlined chips under 「定年所据」; `moses_born` had been `scripture` since v1.6.120 citing only Exodus 2:1-10, which contains no age. (3) **Eight of the 18 are Masoretic-only** — their chain runs through Exodus 12:40 — and now carry a `septuagintYear` **215 years later**, derived from stated figures and **cross-checked against `chronology.json`'s Greek-read epochs, which the tool aborts without**. **A refuter earned its round twice.** It killed `joseph_sold`, which I had already promoted: Genesis 37:2's "seventeen" attaches to tending the flock and the bad report, the sale is 37:28, and no interval is stated between them — the exact ground on which the plagues are refused. And it caught `wilderness_40`/`moses_dies` citing **Numbers 14:33**, whose forty years run from the *spying*, which Numbers 10:11 puts in year 2 — a 39-year gap between the year shown and the chapter tapped; re-based on Numbers 32:13 / Joshua 5:6, and Moses' death on Deuteronomy 34:7's 120 less Exodus 7:7's 80. It also proved two of my stated refusals **wrong**: `sodom_destroyed` is fixed by Genesis 17:21 and 18:10 ("at this time next year") with Genesis 19:1 the same evening — promoted, −2067 exactly — and `jericho` by Joshua 4:19 + 6:15. And it showed the Septuagint years contradict our own chart unless they carry its caveat: **where the Greek's Canaan portion begins is supplied, not read**, `chronology.json`'s own "one place on this axis where a year had to be supplied". That caveat now ships in the sentence. **`timelineBasisConventional` was softened, not deleted** — it claimed "the text fixes no year for this", a claim about scripture that is false for events the text bounds without stating an interval (Joseph's sale, the Red Sea); it now says the narrower true thing, that no chain of stated intervals reaches the event. **Still open, and a content call:** `abrahamic_covenant` ships at −2080 (Abram 86) while its own Genesis 17 ref states 99 (−2067), and Genesis 16:3 caps Genesis 15 at ≤85 (−2081) — it matches neither ref, the derivation abstains, and splitting the event or dropping a ref is for a human. Also fixed: the count line read 「1 events」. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite **2,828 green** (was 2,822), `family_tree.json` byte-identical. **NOT deployed** — undeployed iteration **1 of 3**, no human-reported defect in it; the code comments' `v1.6.146` is the next release whenever it is triggered.

- **#317 remainder — Acts holds four Pauline itineraries; the last two are drawn, and the voyage to Rome forced a new kind of stop.** v1.6.134 shipped journeys 1 and 2 and named what was left: the third journey, the voyage to Rome, the segment→Analysis wire, and a panel that printed stop names **without** the gazetteer ordinal. All but the wire are done here. **The voyage to Rome cannot be drawn honestly as a chain of stops.** Two of its most consequential places are ones the ship never called at: **Phoenix**, voted for and missed (27:12), and **Syrtis**, feared and avoided (27:17) — and both explain what the ship did next. Drawing them as waypoints asserts a harbour call that the following sixteen verses exist to deny; omitting them deletes the reason the ship ended up on Malta. So a stop now has a **kind**: an `aside` is a **hollow ring joined to nothing**, and it **takes no ordinal** — numbering it would make Phoenix stop 9 of the voyage. Hollow rather than merely faint, for the reason a provisional leg is dotted rather than merely pale: a solid mark reads as an assertion at a glance and no amount of lightening takes that back. The leg that passes it — **Fair Havens → Cauda** — is drawn from the waypoints on either side, and an asset test fails if **any** segment ever touches an aside. An **unresolved** aside costs a marker and nothing else, where an unresolved waypoint still breaks the line. **The third journey (Acts 18:23–21:17, 21 stops)** carries the same discipline. Acts 20:1 refuses the manner of the crossing to Macedonia, so that leg is **short-dashed, not solid**. Acts 20:2 says only "Greece", so **Corinth is not drawn** — it is an inference from the letters, not from Acts, and 2 Cor 2:12-13's Troas is left off for the same reason. **Acts 20:13 is the one leg where the text gives two modes at once** — the company sailed round to Assos, Paul went over land (πεζεύειν) — and the line is drawn **by land**, because the itinerary is Paul's. **Trogyllium is noted and not drawn**: Acts 20:15's "tarried at Trogyllium" is a Received Text reading the KJV prints and the earliest manuscripts do not carry, and the gazetteer has no such place — so the absence became a note rather than a gap. **Galatia, Phrygia, Macedonia and Greece are regions**, and their notes and the route's `basis` say so: the marker stands for the region, not for a city. **Cyprus (27:4, 21:3) and Lasea (27:8) are deliberately NOT plotted** — the text passes them and names them to locate something else; a centroid would assert a position it never gave. **Caesarea opens the voyage citing Acts 23:33, not 27:1**, because 27:1 never names the port; the note and the basis both say why. **Forum of Appius is marked attested** on the reasoning, written into its own note, that 28:15 describes the brothers' movement and Paul's presence follows from the meeting. **The ordinal defect is fixed**: the panel appends the gazetteer ordinal, so the first journey's two Antiochs — **500 km apart** — now read `Antioch 1` and `Antioch 2` instead of both reading "Antioch". A **fourth palette slot** was needed for the fourth route and is an **Okabe-Ito bluish green**, which looks like the red/green mistake the file warns against and is not: it is pulled far toward cyan and the amber far toward yellow, so they separate on the blue-yellow axis that red-green blindness leaves intact. Counts in the list and the panel are **split** rather than summed — "14 stops · 2 named, not reached" — to avoid #308's ambiguous-count defect class. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite **2,840 green** (was 2,828), and screenshotted on a **local release build** — all four routes listed, Phoenix and Syrtis as hollow rings unjoined to the green track with a `–` badge and the "Named, not reached" tag, the Fair Havens→Cauda dash running straight past Phoenix, the third journey's short-dashed Macedonia crossing, and the panel opening at `Antioch 1`. **Known limitation, not introduced by this data but first made visible by it**: at whole-Mediterranean zoom the badges for Forum of Appius, Three Taverns and Rome overlap and 12 is fully occluded by 13; there is no label-collision engine, and zooming separates them. **Still open on #317**: selecting a **segment** does not drive the Analysis pane per #313's rule — only whole routes are readable — and that crosses into #313. **NOT deployed** — undeployed iteration **2 of 3**, no human-reported defect in it.

**2026-08-18 — check 38: the Hebrew printed 1,103 verses with a word the text does not have.** `tools/build_originals.py` walked each WLC verse with `verse.iter(w)` — a **descendant** walk — and the Westminster Leningrad Codex keeps the Qere in a `<note>`, so the marginal reading was lifted into the running text with nothing to say where it came from: 2 Samuel 18:20 as `כי על על כן`, Genesis 30:11 as `בגד בא גד`, three words where the text has one written and two read. `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md` had recorded the scope as **four verses** — that was four verses whose two readings happen to be spelled the **same**, so a repeated-word scan could see them; the other ~1,100 are spelled differently, which is usually **why** the Masoretes wrote a note, and were invisible to that instrument. Measured across the corpus: **1,103 verses, 2,509 words**. **BibleWorks checked first** (mandatory): bwh17 says a wildcard matches "all forms … including Qere, Kethib, and neither" and the WTM codes end `Rk`/`Rq`/`Rx`, so **keeping both readings and marking them** is the parity-correct repair and deleting one would be the deviation — counts are unchanged on purpose. The mark carries **four** roles, not two: `kx` **Ketiv velo Qere** (written, directed not to be read, nothing offered in its place — 6 words) and `qx` **Qere velo Ketiv** (read though the text writes nothing — 8); a two-valued mark would have promised a counterpart that does not exist at **15 sites**. Fixed at the **generator**, and `tools/repair_originals_qere.py` **imports** it rather than restating it, so the two cannot drift. **The join was proved, not assumed**: replaying the importer's own filter reproduces the shipped word sequence in **23,213 of 23,213 verses** before a single mark was written, and the script refuses to guess on any misalignment. **Two independent counts agree** — the WLC marks 1,268 Ketiv, all unpointed; the shipped asset holds exactly **1,257 unpointed Hebrew words in 300,808**, the 11 difference being Ketiv whose lemma carries no Strong's number — and after the repair the **marked set and the unpointed set are the same 1,257 words**. **The word list inherited it**: where a number's only occurrences in scope are one Ketiv and its Qere both count 1, and the old `>` handed the headline to whichever came first — always the Ketiv, because the Qere follows it — so 1 Chronicles 1:11 headed H3866 with `לודיים`, the form the reader is directed **not** to read; **360** book-scope rows, now **114**, the remainder being numbers whose only occurrence in scope genuinely **is** a Ketiv (spot-checked H1939 at 1 Chr 3:24, H8053 at 1 Chr 24:24). Browse, the Analysis pane and the Word Study window each say which is which in **en / zh-Hans / zh-Hant**, phrased **about the text** rather than "beside it" because at some sites the counterpart never entered the corpus, and the `kx` sentence is frozen by test never to name a Qere. **The screenshot earned its place**: the `K`/`Q` letter was emitted first and landed **farther from its word than the Strong's number did** — every Latin span after a Hebrew one joins a single left-to-right run, so span order reverses on screen — and it now emits **last**, re-screenshotted at Genesis 30:11 to confirm `H1409 K בגד` reads word → mark → number right to left. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite **2,865 green** (was 2,863), 11 new tests freezing the counts, the six `kx` and eight `qx` references by name, and that every Ketiv is unpointed and every Qere is pointed — an independent witness that the marks landed on the right words. **Disclosed gaps**: Jeremiah 50:29's Qere velo Ketiv is absent because its lemma carries no Strong's number, and ~19 Ketiv stand with their Qere absent for the same reason (Exodus 21:8's `לא`/`לו` crux among them). **NOT deployed** — undeployed iteration **1 of 3**, audit-origin, no human-reported defect in it; dev is **1 commit / 1 iteration** behind.

**2026-08-18 — the Notes tab: the app's oldest user data finally sits beside the verse it is about.** `docs/PARITY-BACKLOG.md` §3.4 called the missing User Notes tab "the biggest single gap in the Analysis pane", and the gap was never the notes — they have existed with titles and timestamps since **v1.2.59** — it was that both ways to reach one, a modal sheet and the Library page, **take the verse off the screen in order to show you what you wrote about it**, which is exactly the pattern #313 was raised about. The tab is the **fourteenth**, appended because `workbench.analysisTab` persists **by index**, and it writes the **existing store**: no new data model, no migration, and the modal editor and the Library page still read the same keys. **bwh15 was read first** (mandatory) and specifies the feature in four sentences — start typing, saved when the verse changes or you exit, loaded when the verse changes, and the destination shown above the editor "so you always know where notes go". Three of those four are about *not asking the user to do anything*, and that is not a simplification: **a Save button is a synchronisation point**, and removing it turns every moment the buffer stops being the current verse's note into a place a keystroke can die. There are exactly **four**, and each one is a widget test — the reader pauses (**700 ms** debounce), the selection moves (**flush to the OUTGOING verse**, which is why the pane keeps its own `_loaded` list: by the time `didUpdateWidget` runs, `widget.verses` is already the new one), the pane is torn down (flush on `dispose`), and something else writes the same note behind it (adopt it, **unless** the reader is mid-edit, in which case what they are typing wins). **The trap was a crash, not a lost keystroke**: cases 2 and 3 both run *inside a frame*, and the write reaches `MainProvider.notifyListeners()`, which marks the pane's own **ancestor** dirty — Flutter asserts `'!_dirty': is not true`, so **typing a note and clicking the next verse threw instead of saving**. Both flushes now capture body/title/verses **by value** and post with `scheduleMicrotask`; the inline version passed analysis and passed every pure-function test, and only the widget half of `test/verse_notes_test.dart` caught it. **Note search is new at any depth** — the app had none — `searchNotes` over title + body, case-insensitive, canonical order, a **passage note answering once as its range** rather than once per verse, and a result tap navigates *and* hands the pane back to the editor, bwh15's "click to load". The strip's Notes icon carries a **5 px dot** when the focused verse already has a note: bwh15 appends a letter, which is unavailable here because the strip has an icon-only mode below ~420 px and #297 forbids ellipsising a CJK label — the dot is drawn in a `Stack` with `clipBehavior: Clip.none` so it adds **zero layout** and #297's "what the strip measures is what `_TabButton` draws" still holds. The **14th tab was proved free before it was added**: `analysisStripLayout` gives `perRow == 7` for both 13 and 14 (7+7 exactly, where 13 left a gap), and 笔记/筆記/Notes are narrower than the widest label the strip already carried, so `analysisStripMinLabelledWidth` does not move in any of the three locales — all four are tests. `export_service.dart`'s **private copy of the verse-id parser was deleted** in favour of the shared `parseVerseId`, which walks the id from the **right** because book names contain spaces and verse labels are not always numbers; two copies of *how a storage key is read* stay correct until one of them is fixed. **Deliberately NOT built, and this is the conservative call, stated rather than hidden**: bwh15's **chapter notes** and its verse/chapter mode switch. Every note in this store hangs off a **verse** key and a chapter note has none, so building it means inventing a second shape for data readers have already written — and then answering what that shape does on **export** and in the **Library list**. That is a product decision, not an implementation one; a chapter note can be written on the chapter's first verse today. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite **2,887 green** (was 2,885 — 22 new tests, plus the pre-existing tab-index ratchet in `morph_search_pane_test.dart` extended to 14). **NOT verified**: nothing was screenshotted, because nothing was deployed — the pane's appearance at the 256 px analysis minimum and the dot's placement on the icon are **assumed**, not seen. **NOT deployed** — undeployed iteration **2 of 3**, no human-reported defect in it. Dev is now **2 commits / 2 iterations** behind, and the queue behind it includes check 38's accuracy fix, so **the next iteration must deploy**.

**2026-08-18 — v1.6.147: Browse shows where the versions disagree, and the backlog cited the wrong help topic.** `docs/PARITY-BACKLOG.md` §3.3 filed difference highlighting as ABSENT and cited **bwh37** — which is *Compiling Version Databases* and says nothing about colour. The feature is **bwh30 "Using Colors" § Comparing Bible Versions**, and reading the right topic changed the design in three ways a guess from the feature's *name* would have missed. **(1) It is a same-language operation** — bwh30's own words, "comparing two translations *in the same language*" — so the stack is partitioned by `BibleVersionInfo.language` before anything is compared, and a language with fewer than two editions on screen is not compared at all. That one rule stops the pane diffing Greek against English (the usual stack carries a single `grc` row), and settles 简体 vs 繁體 without a special case: `zh-Hans` and `zh-Hant` are different languages, so 雅简+ is never diffed against 雅繁+ — it would be a **true** diff and a useless one, since nearly every character differs by *script*, which says nothing about the *translation*. **(2) The first version is the base** (bwh30: "the order of the versions is significant"), which here is `displayVersions.first` — the reader's own reading version, a better default than BibleWorks' "whatever was typed first into a settings box". **(3) LCS, not Word Occurrence**, because bwh30 says to; the rejected method is a set difference and is blind to reordering, and it is recorded in the source rather than shipped. **The documented departure: the base row is marked by INTERSECTION, not union.** BibleWorks unions the base's differences across every comparison, so a base word survives unmarked only if *every* other row carries it. Measured on the five-English stack this pane actually offers — `kjv nasb bsb leb kjvs`, **31,080** verses all five carry, **789,310** base tokens — the two rules are not a matter of taste: union marks **460,253** tokens (**58.3%** of the base row painted), intersection marks **489** (**0.1%**). Intersecting makes the base row answer a question worth asking — *what does my edition say that nobody else on screen says* — and bwh30 half-admits the problem itself by offering a fourth mode that re-runs the comparison with every version as base. For the two-version case the rules are identical. **Chinese is where BibleWorks cannot follow**: bwh30 states flatly that "color filters and text coloring are not currently supported for double-byte languages", and this is the comparison this app's readers most need — `cuvs-plus` against `cuvs-yhwh`, the same 和合本 base text with and without the Name restored, differs in **7,892 of 31,102 verses (25.4%)**, and in **71.7%** of those the **only** characters marked are divine-name characters (**76.1%** of all 46,569 marked characters). One toggle now shows a reader every place the restoration touched, without running a search. **A claim I had already written was false and is withdrawn in the same commit**: the library doc said 和简+ against 雅简+ was "4,857 restorations and almost nothing else" — measured, that pair is **2.2%** name characters and **zero** name-only verses, because `biblexg-v2` is a different translation, not a restoration of the same one. The probe that found it first reported **zero** shared verses for the pair, which was an **instrument limit and not a corpus defect**: `cuvs-yhwh` ids are 9-character zero-padded and `biblexg-v2`'s are 8-character and NT-only, so the join key had to be `book|chapter|verse`. **The mark had nowhere obvious to go, and the obvious place was a false negative.** All four `Container` fills on a Browse word are already taken by word-*identity* states (hover, hit, sibling, pin), and `wordMarkUnderline` already owns the **text underline** on tagged words for hover and pin — so drawing the diff there would have **erased the diff mark for as long as a differing word stayed pinned**, which is precisely when a reader is looking hardest. It is drawn instead as the Container's **bottom border**; `BoxDecoration` forbids a `borderRadius` on a non-uniform `Border`, so the marked word is square, which is #279's house rule anyway. **The search highlighter cuts the same string on its own boundaries and knows nothing about this one**, and both facts are real — a word can be a search hit *and* be worded differently from the base — so neither split owns the string: `sliceByMarks` cuts on the **union** of the two boundary sets and each piece carries a fill from one and an underline from the other. **`mergedMarkSpans` exists so Chinese is not confetti**: the house tokeniser emits one token per Han character, and four separate underlines with hairline gaps read as damage rather than as a finding. The diff and the renderer traverse **the same list in the same order** — `_diffUnitsOf` is the single place that decides the drawn units — and notes and versification markers are **emptied rather than dropped** so a token index can never mean one word to the comparison and another to the paint. The toggle is **off by default**, persisted at `workbench.browseDiff.v1`, greyed **with a reason** when Browse is too narrow, and deliberately **not** in the pane's `ValueKey`, so turning it on repaints instead of reloading. A legend names the base of each group. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite **2,912 green** (was 2,887 — 25 new tests), deployed to dev as **v1.6.147** and screenshotted on that build — the View item enabled and labelled, the legend naming both bases once 雅简+ joined the stack, KJV Genesis 1:1 marking **`heaven` and nothing else** against the NASB base's `heavens`, the WTT row unmarked, BSB 1:2's tagged runs drawing continuous rose bottom-borders with no reflow, NASB 2:4's `the heavens` merging **two words and the space between them** into one underline while `earth and` stays clean, and Genesis 2:4's 雅简+ `在雅伟` / 和简+ `在耶和华` each drawing **one continuous line**. **Verified vs assumed, stated plainly**: the per-Han-character *merge* was seen working on English adjacency, not on Chinese — both Chinese editions on screen are **tagged**, so their unit is the tagged run, not the character, and the confetti case belongs to an *untagged* Chinese edition, which this stack cannot currently display. **Deliberately not built**: bwh30's first three options — fold accents, fold a movable final nu, fold sin/shin — all describe comparisons a stack with one Greek and one Hebrew row **cannot contain**, and an untested fold for a group that never occurs is worse than none. This deploy also carried the **two undeployed iterations behind it** (check 38's Qere/Ketiv repair and the Notes tab) to dev; #278/#292/#293/#296/#309 remain blocked on the owner.

**2026-08-19 — check 40: the Biblical Evidence Archive's 225 records had never been read for what they SAY, and said the wrong thing in three ways.** `assets/bible_evidence.json` was one of the last two assets under "Not checked yet"; check 25 had verified its 225 **scripture references** resolve, which proves nothing about its prose. **(1) 152 strings in 111 records were UTF-8 read back as Latin-1** by the Vite/React/TS migration — `AndrÃ© Parrot`, `IEJ 35 (1985): 22â<80><93>27`, `MusÃ©e du Louvre` — and **93 of the 152 are inside `academicSources`**, so the part of the archive that was least readable was its own evidence for its claims. The gate matters more than the repair: decoding is attempted **only** behind a signature that cannot occur in real text — a **C1 control (U+0080–U+009F)**, unassigned in every legitimate string, which covers 131 — plus `[ÂÃ]` + a UTF-8 continuation byte for the **21** accented-Latin cases that carry no control byte, and the result is rejected if it still matches. **(2) 209 of the 225 records served the Simplified string under the `zh-Hant` key, byte for byte**, so a 繁體 reader was shown Simplified in **836 fields**. **The detector is the finding here**: a per-character test (`s2tw(c) != c`) flags 里 占 岩 征 托 干 后 准 游 岳, **every one of which is valid Traditional**, and over-fired by 6 records; an inventory built from our own shipped 繁體 Bibles is **contaminated** (约 语 词 纪 组 绝 脱 镜 all occur in `biblexg-v2-tr.json`); `tw2s(h) == h` has **43** false negatives. What works is **byte identity between the two slots** — if `zh-Hant` holds the same bytes as `zh-Hans`, no conversion ever ran — and it splits the file **209 / 16 exactly**, with no judgement in it. Regenerated with **`opencc -c s2tw`** (Taiwan standard *without* vocabulary substitution) plus **42 hand-read exception rules**, and the rules were read out of opencc rather than guessed: converting each distinct Han character **alone** gives its context-free opinion, and diffing that against the full-text conversion exposes **792 phrase-dictionary overrides in ~70 classes**. Every rule was refuted or confirmed against the app's own 和合本繁體 — 髮掘→發掘, 昇天→升天, 馬裡→馬里, 闡明瞭→闡明了, 一齣會堂→一出會堂 — and **half the classes were deliberately left alone** because opencc is right (彷彿 饑荒 細緻 鐘乳石 沖積), which is the other half of the rule and is frozen by test so a future over-eager pass cannot undo them. The **16 already-Traditional records were not touched**: their readings are better than the converter's (卷 not 捲, 讚 not 贊, 鏟除 not 剷除). **(3) Two Chinese titles did not mean what their English meant** — Tel Dan was **但以理石碑**, *the prophet Daniel*, for the city of Dan (×4), and two ossuaries were **骨灰罐/骨灰盒**, cremation urns, in records whose own body text says 藏骨罐 (×8); Second Temple Jews did not cremate. **`_meta.confidenceCounts` also summed to 209 while the file holds 225.** **A fourth candidate was withdrawn before it was written down**, per the refuter rule: 馬大拉紙莎草殘片 looked like a Magdala/Magdalen-College conflation until the archive was measured — it renders Magdala as **抹大拉 in all 18 places** and uses 馬大拉 only for the college. **`tools/repair_evidence_archive.py`** is idempotent, **converts nothing by default**, asserts a count for **every** rule, and early-returns "already repaired" — verified with `cmp` that a second `--write` leaves the file byte-identical, because on a second run every count reads 0 and that is otherwise indistinguishable from a broken rule. **The test was checked for teeth before it was trusted**: each of the 18 Simplified-only characters it forbids was counted in the `zh-Hans` side first (6–741 occurrences each), so the negative assertions are not vacuous. **Clean negatives, reported because zero is a result**: 716 image URLs, **716 distinct**, all 206 hosts return real image bytes (jpeg 652, png 55, webp 8, gif 1) and **0** Netlify SPA-fallback HTML — checked by **magic number**, not status code, because the fallback returns 200; 0 duplicate ids, 0 empty source lists, all icons a single emoji. **The screen was then pointed at the two 繁體 Bible editions (40f)**: `cuvs-yhwh-tr.json` is **clean** — all 74 仆 are 仆倒, all 51 后 are 王后/太后, which is what #323's repair predicts — and `biblexg-v2-tr.json` flags **207 occurrences across 45 characters** that are a **mixture** of certain defects (稣 9 vs 穌 1,456; 话 3 vs 話 529 — 提多書 2:3 prints 「上了年纪的婦女」), certain false positives (里 征 干 于 云), and **舊字形** (内 啓 着 没 麽 — older orthography used consistently, not Simplified). **The true count is unknown and is deliberately NOT recorded as a finding**; it is filed as a new open item, and #323's memory applies in full — a blanket `opencc -c s2t` *invents* a defect at 以賽亞書 29:17. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite **2,926 green** (was 2,912 — 15 new tests, of which 14 are new files' and one extends the archive's own), repair script idempotent by `cmp`, and a 15-URL random image re-sample after the rewrite. **NOT verified**: nothing was screenshotted and the detail page was not seen on a build — the fix is in an asset the page reads verbatim, and the assertion that it *renders* correctly is reasoned from `BibleEvidence.localizedTitle` serving the locale key straight through, not observed. **NOT closed**: the archive's 900 **body** fields (~440 KB) were never read against their English — only the 225 title pairs were — and whether an image is *of* what its record claims is unasked. **NOT deployed** — undeployed iteration **2 of 3** after v1.6.147 (check 39's Browse fix is iteration 1), audit-origin, no human-reported defect in it; dev is **3 commits / 2 iterations** behind, so **the next iteration must deploy**. #278, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.

**v1.6.148 — check 41: 梁家鏗繁體 judged by its own Simplified twin, and 4 verses missing from the other file.** Took `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md` "Next, in order" item 9, the **207 Simplified-looking characters in `biblexg-v2-tr.json`** whose true count was "unknown and deliberately not written down". It is **30 characters in 28 verses**, repaired by `tools/repair_biblexg_v2_tr.py` (idempotent, one named verse-id-plus-context rule per site, converts nothing by default) and guarded by 17 tests in `test/traditional_forms_test.dart`. **The instrument is the new part**: the edition ships in BOTH scripts, so where the conversion turned 说 into 說 1,898 times and into 説 4 times, the 4 are the defect — no external Traditional authority needed. Five classes, and the 207 screen could only ever see one of them: **A** 12 Simplified survivors (提多書 2:3 printed 「上了年纪的婦女」), **B** 1 wrong word (會堂里→會堂裡, while the other 45 里 are correct and must not be swept), **C** 4 **over**-conversions — 準許→准許 ×3 and 矇住→蒙住 — a class nobody had named and one a "Simplified survivor" screen *structurally cannot see* because the wrong output is a real Traditional character, **D** 12 舊字形 stragglers (説 爲 啓 証), a **search** defect not a meaning one: a reader who types 開啟 was finding 1 of this file's 4, and **E** 1 that the file had already settled one book earlier (顫斗→顫抖, on the precedent of 馬可福音 16:8). **"207" was never a defect count**: the whole-file screen is 694 occurrences / 32 characters, of which **14** are defects, 676 were read one word at a time and kept (499 are opencc over-reaching into 舊字形 — 吃 群 秘 床 唇 岳 熏, which this file declines to convert 499 times), and 4 are undecided. **16 of the 30 are invisible to that screen entirely** because 説 爲 啓 証 矇 準 are not Simplified characters. **The bigger finding is 41f and it is NOT fixed**: `assets/biblexg-v2.json` (the **Simplified** edition) **is missing 馬可福音 6:8-11**, and its 6:7 is truncated mid-clause — a reader sees a sentence stop dead and four verses vanish. Measured corpus-wide, it is the **only** such gap; every other missing verse in every Bible asset is a critical-text omission all editions share. Deliberately **not** repaired: the text is in the Traditional twin, but the two editions agree only **78.0%** under `t2s` (the Simplified prefers 着/什/借/它), so converting it back would **invent a translator's house-style choices in a translator's own Bible**. It is filed **first and unnumbered** in "Next, in order" so the existing item numbers stay citable. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite green, repair idempotent (second run says "already repaired", exit 0), the compact JSON round-tripped so `git diff --stat` reads 1 insertion / 1 deletion for 30 real edits, and every negative test needle counted in the *pre*-repair file first — the 20 repair needles each had ≥1 occurrence to bite on, and running the tests against the pre-repair asset fails exactly the five repair classes. **Corrected by the refuter, and worth knowing**: my first write-up said the Traditional file "was made from the Simplified one" and that every difference is a converter decision — **false**, they diverge editorially in 22% of verses; the claim now stands only for same-word glyph pairs. It also caught 里拉琴 ×4→3, 臺 28→29, 游泳 ×9→×2, "16 of 30" missing 準, and a 隻 rule I had padded with 船 to reach "0 violations" (46 of 50 follow a numeral; 船隻 ×3 and 一隻隻 are the rest). **NOT verified**: nothing screenshotted — this is an asset the reader prints verbatim, and no `lib/` code changed. **NOT closed**: 一台戲, 游手好閒 ×4 and 包紥 ×1 are left alone for want of any witness (the standard forms occur **zero** times in either 繁體 edition), and the Simplified edition still carries the upstream typo 颤斗 ×2 — all owner's calls. **Deployed to dev as v1.6.148** (the 3rd undeployed iteration, so the deploy was mandatory; checks 39 and 40 rode out with it) and the live asset was re-fetched and verified: 7,925 records, all six spot-checked repairs present, and 説/爲/啓/証/矇/準許/稣 all **0**. #278, #292-source, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.
- **Compound searches shipped, and the backlog entry that asked for them described the wrong feature.** PARITY-BACKLOG §8 item 5 read compound search as boolean *grouping* (`G25 AND G26 OR G27`); bwh16 is not that, and the difference IS the feature. Each `( )` is a whole sub-search with its own control character and its own `;N`, and the separator `.` `/` `!` carries a verse distance of its **own** — so `(.grac* work*;5).15(/jesus christ)` is a proximity **JOIN** between two result sets, not an intersection: it finds grace-and-works sitting *near* Christ, in verses that share no word. **Measured on the KJV** (pinned in `command_query_corpus_test.dart`): same-verse **24** → with `.15` **159**; and because bwh16's last group decides what is LISTED, swapping the two groups gives **82** from the identical logic, so the echo now says which side it is listing. Union arithmetic checks out to the verse (116 + 1208 − 24 = 1300), which independently proves both set ops exact. **A working search was nearly taken away**: a control-free line is plain substring text here, so `(hello)` must keep working — a `(` line is claimed only when unambiguously compound-*shaped*, and `(god OR world)`, which used to be refused as "compound not supported", is a plain text search again. **Ambiguity found late and resolved conservatively without removing capability**: there is no precedence (left to right, as in BibleWorks), and on the fixture the English AND-binds-tighter reading of `(.a)/(.b).(.c)` gives 3 verses where the engine gives 1 — so the echo appends "applied left to right" whenever the joins are mixed, and stays silent for two groups, which cannot be misread. **Refused BY NAME, not half-guessed**: nesting (flat in BibleWorks, and it makes "which group is listed" unanswerable) and a Strong's expression as a group (corpus indices vs concordance refs, one async — needs a reference→index map; still open, re-scoped in §3.1). Cap of **6 groups**: each is a separate synchronous corpus pass, two measure ~140ms. Per-group counts are kept so an empty compound names WHICH half was empty — the combined list cannot. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, full suite green at **2,974** tests (30 new unit + 5 corpus + 3 provider + 2 card), and the numbers above were re-run rather than reasoned — **two of my own predictions were wrong** (`.grace` is 170 *occurrences* in **159 verses**, the occurrence/verse trap again; and a predicted `[3]` was empty because the verse was never in the union). **NOT verified**: nothing screenshotted — no deploy this iteration. **Not deployed**: undeployed iteration **1 of 3**; no human-reported defect was fixed and the owner did not ask, so the deploy-on-demand test is not met. #278, #292-source, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.
- **The Word List Manager can now compare two books, and "nowhere else in the Bible" is exact rather than a hedge.** bwh26's headline is not the list, it is the comparison — *"find all words that occur in John that do not occur anywhere else in the New Testament"* — and we had lists and no compare. Three things a naive diff gets wrong are fixed in the pure core (`lib/utils/word_list_compare.dart`), not the widget. **A shared word's identity is its LEMMA, not its form**: ἀγαπάω is ἠγάπησεν in one book and ἀγαπῶντες in the other, so the single-list habit of heading a row with the commonest surface form would print one book's inflection as the thing the two *share*; the compare row heads with the lexicon lemma and keeps both forms beside it. **Two counts side by side invite a comparison the corpora do not support** — Jude is 458 words and 2 Peter 1,099, so `3 · 4` is not the tie it looks like — so both denominators are printed above the table, spelled out (`Jude 227 distinct / 458 words`) after I read my own output and found `227 / 458` with nothing saying which was which, and `1099words` with no space. **A Hebrew book against a Greek one shares nothing by construction**, so an empty intersection there is a property of the numbering, not a result: the page says why instead of printing the zero as a finding. **The claim is stated flatly because it was measured first**: all 66 books tallied through the app's own code path agree with `concordance.json`'s `n` on all **14,040** numbers — 0 disagreements, 0 missing either side — and `countA + countB == corpusTotal` is only sound while the two scopes are DISJOINT, which is why the compare is book-against-book and not range-against-range. Written into the code and the backlog so nobody widens it casually. **Design corrected late, against my own first build**: "Nowhere else" was a fifth *bucket*, which mixed 71 single-book words in with the 3 shared ones and could not express bwh26's own example query at all. It is now a **modifier** over whichever bucket is open — so *Only in Jude* + *nowhere else* IS bwh26's query, and every chip's count recomputes over the narrowed pool (#308) so a number always matches the list it opens: 16 + 55 + 3 = **74**. **The feature answers the question on the first screen**: the default sort is rarest-in-the-Bible, so opening Jude against 2 Peter puts G1703 ἐμπαίκτης, G4910 συνευωχέω and G5246 ὑπέρογκος — the three words the commentaries cite for the literary relationship between the two letters — as the **top three rows**, pinned by number (not headword) in the widget test, since the row is headed by the lexicon lemma rather than the form a commentary prints. The nowhere-else marker is carried in **words as well as colour** (#317). `ConcordanceService.totalsFor` reads only the `n` field because `lookup()` would push up to 6,977 refs per number through a regex and a comparison asks about ~2,000 numbers at once. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, full suite green at **2,999** tests (19 pure-core + 6 page, mounted against the real assets at 420×900 and at 320×640/40pt). Three overflows were found by that page test and fixed rather than tolerated — a `DropdownButton` sizes to the widest of 66 book names (156 px over), the pinned control block was **505 px taller than a 320×640 phone at 40 pt**, and three fixed-width numeric cells do not shrink (48 px) — so the control block is capped at 60% of the page and scrolls, and the cell width is computed once from the row's own width. **Two numbers I predicted were wrong** (`In both 141`, `Only in Jud 15`) and both were replaced by measurement, not argument. **NOT verified**: nothing screenshotted — no deploy this iteration, so layout rests on the widget test at two extremes rather than on an image. **Not deployed**: undeployed iteration **2 of 3** after v1.6.148; no human-reported defect was fixed and the owner did not ask, so the deploy-on-demand test is not met and the next iteration must deploy. #278, #292-source, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.
- **Type `agape`, reach G26 — romanised Greek/Hebrew search input, and the gate that makes it honest took two corrections.** PARITY-BACKLOG §8 item 6 / §3.7 **bwh45**: a reader who knows the word `agape` and has an English Bible open used to get "No results found" and nothing else, over a corpus carrying the word **116 times in 106 verses**. **bwh24 turns out not to describe transliteration at all** — BibleWorks binds a font *keyboard* to the search version, and its key charts are ASCII rendered in `Bwgrkl`/`Bwhebb`, fonts we do not have, so the mapping is not recoverable from the extracted help; typing the Greek script already worked here, and what did not was the common case, so this ships the goal by the other road (better on a tablet, which is the target device). Strong's romanisation is from **1890** (`shâlôwm`, `chêçêd`, `ʼĕlôhîym`), so `lib/utils/romanised_lemma.dart` is a **two-tier** index — the printed spelling with its accents folded, and that put through per-language collapse rules derived from the data — and candidates are **shown, never auto-resolved**: of **21,147 typable spellings, 2,757 (13.0%)** reach more than one entry and the worst reaches **8**; `ruach` genuinely is three words. **The gate is the whole accuracy argument, and my first two versions of it were both wrong.** Not an empty result list: a plain one-word search is a **substring match over the space-stripped key**, so in the KJV `theos` finds "these are" (4), `shalom` finds "Jehovahshalom" (3), `torah` finds "unto Rahab" and `charis` finds "Issachar is" — that gate would have silenced the offer for exactly the four words most likely to be typed. And **not the reader's scope either**, which the refuter caught: `_runCommand` applies `searchLimit`, so with a Genesis scope `wen` (a KJV word, Leviticus 22:22) read as absent and was offered **H1121 *bên* "a son"**; **152 of 1,814** words leaked that way. It now asks whether any verse of the **whole edition** uses the word, unscoped. That buys a **provable** safety argument rather than a blocklist: all **1,814** KJV words (of 12,546 distinct) that resolve to a lexicon key are words the KJV *contains*, swept one by one in `test/romanised_gate_corpus_test.dart`, so `bad`→H905, `den`→H1836, `dove`→H1679 and `gay`→H1471 cannot reach the reader **by construction**. The honest cost is stated rather than hidden: `hesed` **is** a KJV word (1 Kings 4:10, "the son of Hesed"), so typing it over the KJV gets the verse and no offer of H2617 — the alternative is an app that claims a word it can see is absent. **Also records, without fixing, a defect the work exposed**: that same substring rule matches **across word boundaries**, so `forth` lists the **2,542** verses that say "for the" with nothing markable in them (**89 of a 502-word sample** do this, distinct from the 116 that only match *within* a word, which is legitimate) — the fix is per-script because substring matching is load-bearing for Chinese, so it is its own §3.1 backlog item, and a comment in `command_pane.dart` that **denied** the behaviour now states it with the number. **My second refuter finding, also acted on**: `strictKeyCount + looseKeyCount` (27,288) **double-counts** — the two maps share thousands of strings and a spelling in both reaches the *union* — so every ambiguity figure above is now measured through `resolve()` itself. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, full suite green at **3,038** tests, and **screenshotted on the live dev deploy at 1400 px** — `agape` over an empty list shows "No verse uses the word "agape", but the original does: G26 · ἀγάπη agápē … 106 verses", tapping it puts `G26` in the field and returns **106 verses · 116 occurrences**, and `nabi` (26 substring rows, e.g. Genesis 20:9 "the**n Abi**melech") shows the *other* lead, "Those matches are inside other words — no verse uses "nabi" on its own", above H5030 נָבִיא · 288 verses. **NOT verified**: the Hebrew/Greek collapse rules are pinned by **62** reader spellings landing on the right number, not by a lexicographer; and no romanised offer has been seen in a Chinese locale on screen (the two Chinese leads are covered by widget test only). **Deployed to dev as v1.6.149** — undeployed iteration 3 of 3, so the deploy was mandatory. #278, #292-source, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.
- **Nave's Topical Bible shipped, and the sizing question found the defect.** PARITY-BACKLOG §8 item 3 / §3.5 **bwh34**: BibleWorks lists Nave's beside TSK as a cross-reference resource, and we had TSK plus the Eagle's View Modern Concordance — a **different work, New Testament only** — so an **Old Testament verse had no topical index at all**. Now it does: **5,322 topics, 29,379 lines, 77,974 references** (75,804 verse-or-range, 2,170 chapter-level) imported by `tools/import_naves.py` from CCEL's ThML edition, sharded 134 ways with a per-book reverse index so a verse pane reads one 90 KB index plus the one book in view. It enters from the verse in the Topics tab **above** the concordance, because the work that covers all 66 books leads. **Not one of those counts was true when the import first produced them**, and the screen that caught it was not a defect hunt: choosing between an eager and a lazy list meant measuring the worst-case citations for one verse, and the answer was **Jude 1:1 = 115 topics**, which is not something a topical index says about a doxology. **CCEL's tagger cannot read a one-chapter book** — Nave prints "Jude 14", the tagger reads 14 as the *chapter*, fails, emits `Jude.1.1` and leaves the real verse stranded in the prose after the closing tag — so **all 250 references into Obadiah, Philemon, 2 John, 3 John and Jude arrived as `BOOK.1.1`**, and **Jude 1:14, the Enoch prophecy, carried none**. Repaired from the printed text the tagger left behind (the machine value is the derived thing, so the human string is the original): 246 direct, 2 re-read as the smaller class, 2 emitted as **chapter citations** marked "whole chapter" rather than as a false verse claim; the 250 sites became 298 references, all in range, with **Jude 25/25, Philemon 25/25, 2 John 13/13, 3 John 14/14 and Obadiah 21/21** reaching their exact last verses, and Jude 1:1 down to 6 entries while 1:14 has 14. A smaller version of the same failure swallows the first digit in ordinary books (**40 sites, 30 confirmed against the KJV text of the target**), and **my first fix for it shipped four references to a Colossians 8 and 12 that do not exist** by stopping at the first continuation — `eat_continuations` follows the comma under a guard narrow enough (same *wrong* osisRef book **and** a bare-digit anchor) that a genuine neighbouring reference is never swallowed. **CrossWire's SWORD `Nave` module is not a witness** — its conf names the same CCEL XML — which is worth remembering: a second *distribution* is not a second *witness*. **12 apocrypha and 5 unresolvable references dropped by name, not guessed** (Exodus 3:29, Mark 18:42, Mark 18:43, 1 Chronicles 22:27, 1 Chronicles 22:30), printed every run. **One defect was ours and only a unit test could have found it**: `ancestorsOf` matched an ancestor at exactly one level up, Nave skips levels, so the walk stepped over the real parent into the **previous topic's subtree** — **63 of 22,249 cited lines** printed a path Nave never wrote, invisible on every verse anyone would spot-check. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, full suite green at **3,062** tests (18 unit + 6 mounted-pane, including one that resolves all 77,974 references against `assets/kjv.json`), the importer **re-run and its output `diff -r` IDENTICAL** to the shipped asset, and every number in this line re-derived from the asset rather than from notes — 22,249 and 63 were recomputed, not recalled. **NOT verified**: nothing screenshotted (no deploy this iteration), and **the 5,322 headwords and 29,379 line texts are checked against nothing** — check 42 proves every *reference* resolves and sits where Nave put it, not that Nave was right or that CCEL transcribed him faithfully. **NOT done**: bwh36_RWP's *browsable* half (entry list, lookup box, history under `Resources | X-Refs`) — a UI slice on `NavesService.search`/`.topic`, which exist and are tested. **Not deployed**: undeployed iteration **1 of 3**; no human-reported defect was fixed and the owner did not ask, so the deploy-on-demand test is not met. #278, #292-source, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.
- **A door into Nave's — and the screenshot found the defect the whole test file was blind to.** bwh34's *browsable* half, the piece the previous line left open: the corpus shipped in 46ed151 with exactly one way in — sit on a verse, read the Topics tab — which answers *what is THIS verse about* and nothing else, so a reader who wanted what Nave filed under REPENTANCE had to first guess a verse that might be under it. `Resources | Nave's Topical Bible` now opens the whole work: **5,322 topics** with an A–Z strip, a two-tier search, and the entry itself. **Three measurements shaped it and each is pinned by a test.** *JESUS, THE CHRIST* is **801 lines / 3,828 references** — printed flat it is a wall — but its depth-1 skeleton is **87 lines** and the MEDIAN topic's depth-1 count is **2**, so one rule serves both ends: render the top level, collapse each subtree, print how many lines are inside it. *QUOTATIONS AND ALLUSIONS* puts **642 references on a single line** and **113 lines carry more than 80**, so a line prints its first **24** and offers the rest **by number** — a cap on a sorted list is a silent WHERE clause. And the headwords are an **1896** vocabulary while the reader's is not, so a second tier searches the entry TEXT and **arms itself when the headword tier comes back empty**, because that is precisely the moment a reader would otherwise get a blank page. It is **not sold as a cure**: `worry` and `depression` both match zero headwords and `depression` matches zero lines as well, so that case gets a sentence saying Nave files nothing under it and no entry mentions it, rather than an empty list that reads as a broken app. **`assets/nave/lines.json` is a fourth artifact from the importer** — the line text alone, flat, **790 KB and one request** rather than the **134 shard fetches** the same query would otherwise cost — verified **byte-for-byte against all 134 committed shards** (5,322 topics, 29,379 lines, **zero** drift), needing no new pubspec entry because `assets/nave/` already covers it, and one test proves it is genuinely **in the bundle** rather than merely on disk: an unloaded index and an empty one both total zero, so that test needs a real hit out of the real file (`worry` → **SIDON**, *"lived in security and without a worry"*). **Two claims the previous iteration's comments carried were wrong and are corrected against the asset**: **649** topics carry no references at all, not 646; and DESPONDENCY and DIVORCE are **both headwords**, so neither is the dead end they were cited as. **Then the page was opened on the deploy, and that is what found the real defect.** The English headers substituted a count into a plural template: **"1 topics named"**, **"1 lines mention it"**, **"1 lines · 1 references"**. Not an edge — of the 5,322 topics **2,413 are exactly one line long, 1,356 cite exactly one verse, and 1,153 are both**, so *"1 line · 1 reference"* is the header an English reader meets more often than any other. **Every assertion in `naves_page_test.dart` stayed green through it and would have forever**: they are all in Chinese, which has no singular — the locale that can see this defect is the one those tests deliberately do not use — so three tests now build the page under `setLocale('en')`. `navesTopicCounts` was **deleted rather than given a singular**, since its two counts pluralise independently and one two-slot template would need four English forms; it is two strings joined by `' · '`, identical in all three locales, so the existing `801 行 · 3828 处经文` assertion is unchanged. (Trap for the next run: `setLocale` arms AppSettings' **600 ms user-prefs write debounce**, which `pumpAndSettle` does not drain — it only advances the clock while frames are scheduled — so an English test passes its assertion and then fails on a pending timer.) **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, full suite green at **3,090** tests (19 pure-core + 9 mounted-page), and **screenshotted on the live dev deploy at 1400 px** — the index (5,322 topics, letter strip, `cross-reference` tags on the reference-less entries), *JESUS, THE CHRIST* arriving collapsed at 87 rows with its true `801 lines · 3828 references` header and `+81 more` chips, and ABADDON reading **`1 line · 1 reference`** after the fix. **NOT fixed, and measured before being left**: **30 of the 29,379 line texts (0.10%)** join a heading to its first child with no space — `MISCELLANEOUS FACTS CONCERNING.Brothers (physical) of`, `INSTANCES OF.Abraham` — a defect in the corpus imported by 46ed151, not in this page, whose repair means regenerating all 134 shards *and* `lines.json`; it is recorded here rather than started at the end of a run. **NOT done**: the deep link — the Topics pane already knows the topic id behind every citation it prints and `NavesPage` takes an `initialTopicId`, but **nothing passes it yet**; the doc comment says so rather than implying it is wired. Also worth knowing: `chevron_right` now means **two** things on that screen (history-forward and outline-expander), so a test that finds it `.first` taps the wrong one. **Deployed to dev as v1.6.150** (undeployed iteration **3 of 3**, so mandatory) and again as **v1.6.151** for the plural fix — a second deploy inside one iteration, taken deliberately: the string was wrong on 45% of topics in the build that had just been announced, and leaving dev wrong for up to three more runs is the worse call. #278, #292-source, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.
- **The lexicons are readable now, not only queryable — and "alphabetical" was the hard word in the sentence.** PARITY-BACKLOG §8 / §6 **bwh35**: `strongs_entry_page.dart` has always shown ONE entry, reached by tapping a word, so a reader had to already hold the word in order to ask about it and could never ask what stands beside it. `Resources | 原文词典浏览` now opens both lexicons entire — **5,523 Greek, 8,674 Hebrew** — as `lib/pages/lexicon_page.dart` over a pure core in `lib/utils/lexicon_browse.dart`. **The naive reading of "alphabetic browse" fails in both alphabets, for opposite reasons, and one fix repairs only one of them**: Greek polytonic is **precomposed**, so ἀγάπη begins at U+1F00 and a code-point sort throws every accented word thousands of positions past plain α (measured: index **>3000** unfolded, **<200** folded); Hebrew points are **combining marks**, so the same sort looks right until two words share a first letter — under it the Hebrew lexicon opened at אֱגוֹז, *a nut*, instead of H1 אָב, the first word of every printed Hebrew lexicon. #321's `foldDiacritics` already handled both, so the collation key is two lines rather than a project. **Alphabetical is the DEFAULT and Strong's numbering is the alternative**, because Strong's order is an artefact of an 1890 index and no lexicon has ever been printed in it; both are offered as a **pair of buttons, not a toggle**, since a toggle labelled with the current state cannot say whether tapping it means "you are in alphabetical order" or "put me in alphabetical order", and the reader finds out by losing her place in 8,674 rows. **The second search is the one that matters and it is not full-text**: Strong's prints `ʼĕlôhîym`, nobody types that, and no amount of searching the definitions helps because the string the reader typed appears nowhere in the lexicon — so headword search goes through **bwh45's** `romanisedKey` + per-language collapse rather than folding the printed transliteration a second time. **That reuse was nearly missed**: `lexiconCollationKey` was already in hand and looked close enough, but it leaves `ʼ` and the superscript `ᵉ` in place, so `elohim` missed H430 while `elohiym` hit it — a search that works only for readers who can already spell the thing they are looking up. Wildcards forced a conservative call taken without a human: `romanisedKey` refuses anything outside `a-z`, so each fragment is romanised separately and the stars put back, and **a fragment that will not romanise fails the whole query** rather than being dropped, because dropping it silently widens the search (`agap*θ` would quietly become `agap*`). Full text is the **second tier**, same shape as Nave's — it arms itself when the headword tier is empty, which is exactly when a reader would otherwise be shown a blank page, and the two counts are **reported separately and never summed** ("0 entries named / 5 articles mention it"). It reads the **locale's** articles: a Chinese reader searching 圣约 gets the CBOL Chinese, not the English underneath it. Rows open the existing entry page — **deliberately no second entry renderer**. **A false claim in my own library comment, caught by a test that had been written from it**: I wrote that the fold lets an English reader typing `theos` reach an article spelling it θεός. It does not — `foldDiacritics` folds accents, it does not transliterate — so the claim was **withdrawn in place** and a test now asserts `theos` → **0** so it cannot creep back. **A 1.3 px overflow that was a `BuildContext` bug, worth generalising**: `TextPainter` measured the widest Strong's number at 55.0 px and `WbTag` rendered it at 56.3, because I measured with the **State's own `context`, which sits ABOVE its `Scaffold`** — Material `bodyMedium`'s 0.25 px `letterSpacing` was not yet in scope, × 5 characters = 1.25 px, and every Greek row clipped while Hebrew hid it by showing H1–H9 first. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, full suite green at **3,126** tests — 26 pure-core (including the real bundled corpus: exactly **24** Greek letters and **22** Hebrew with no final forms, Strong's numbering non-decreasing at >98%, every entry reachable by its own spelling, and `elohim`→H430 `shalom`→H7965 `hesed`→H2617 `agape`→G26 `logos`→G3056 `charis`→G5485) and 10 mounted-page. **The page tests assert in CHINESE by default** because `AppSettings` does, and pump English **only** for the counts — Chinese has no singular, so a zh assertion is structurally blind to "1 entries named", which is exactly the defect that shipped in Nave's a week ago; neither locale alone catches both classes. **NOT verified**: nothing screenshotted — no deploy this iteration — so the layout rests on the widget tests and the measured column, not on an image; and the collation is pinned by reader spellings landing on the right numbers, not by a lexicographer. **NOT done, deliberately**: `bdb_zh.json` / `thayer_zh.json` are bundled and served by `ChineseLexiconService` but not browsable — a lexicon picker is a second axis on the page and their coverage is not 1:1 with Strong's numbering, so it is filed as PARITY-BACKLOG §8 item **1a** rather than half-built. **Two backlog corrections found by grepping before believing it**: §8 item 4 (verse-list set operations) has been **shipped all along** as `VerseList.selectCommonWith` / `selectUniqueTo`, wired at `verse_list_pane.dart:237-239` and tested — my first grep for `intersect|union|difference` returned nothing but `EdgeInsets.symmetric` noise and nearly produced a confident "still open", the lesson being **grep for the reader's verb, not the set algebra**; and item 3's "browsable side" shipped yesterday. **§8's items 2–6 are now all struck through**, so live candidates 1a–1c were written in to replace them. **Not deployed**: undeployed iteration **1 of 3** after v1.6.151; this is backlog work, no human-reported defect was fixed and the owner did not ask, so the deploy-on-demand test is not met. #278, #292-source, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.
- **Three lexicons over one headword list — and the coverage measurement the backlog demanded found a word that had been shipped unreachable.** PARITY-BACKLOG §8 item **1a** (bwh35's second lexicon), closed the day it was picked. The browser now offers **Strong's, the English Thayer's, and the Chinese module** (BDB on the Hebrew side, Thayer on the Greek) as `LexiconSource`, and the architecture is the point: the picker changes only what is *said* about a word — `LexiconId` still decides which words exist, how they are spelled, in what order and under which alphabet strip — so the same search returns the same entries whichever lexicographer is open, and there is no 8,674-row re-sort per work (and no silently unpointed Hebrew, since `bdb_zh` prints its lemmas bare). Only `articleOf` / `summaryOf` / `foldedOf` change, and the article-text tier reads whichever book is open: `Vulgate` finds articles under Strong's and **none** under Thayer's, which is the assertion that proves it. **The defect it found on the way**: two of `assets/thayer.json`'s 5,799 keys are zero-padded (`G0190`, `G0446`), so `lookup('G190')` missed **ἀκολουθέω**, the New Testament's verb for *following* Jesus — the article was in the bundle, shipped, and unreachable from any surface. `ThayerService.canonicalKey` now strips the padding on both build and query, so it is unreachable-by-construction rather than fixed twice; English Thayer coverage is **5,523 of 5,523**. **The measurement that nearly lied**: a first pass counted *keys* and reported 100% coverage in both directions. Presence of a key is not presence of an article — joined properly there are **19 holes in 14,197** (4 in BDB 中文, 1 in Thayer 中文, and **14 in Strong's own Chinese gloss that had been blank on screen since the browser shipped the day before**). The row now says `lexiconWorkSilent` rather than going blank, because a blank cell is indistinguishable from a failed load and borrowing another work's gloss credits the wrong lexicographer. **Thayer under Hebrew is refused out loud**: the button is shown disabled rather than hidden, the lit button says what is on screen rather than what was last asked for, and the substitution is announced — a page answering out of a different book than the one named is the same defect as a count that will not say what it counted. Every count line now names its work (`8674 个词条 · Strong's`). **Filed, not fixed**: DATA-INTEGRITY check **43c** — 28 of the 14,696 Chinese entries have an `etymology` truncated mid-clause (`charcah (khar'- saw`), an importer field-split defect that renders in the word-study pane today; it belongs to **#301**, which already re-opens that import, and the fix goes in the generator, not the asset. Note a per-*sense* parenthesis count is not a detector — 30,508 sense lines are legitimately unbalanced. **Not deployed**: undeployed iteration **2 of 3** after v1.6.151; backlog work, no human-reported defect fixed, owner did not ask. Suite **3,136**, analyze exit 0. #278, #292-source, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.
- **The Chinese lexicon was cut wherever the printed page broke — and check 43c's own diagnosis of it was wrong twice over (#301, `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md` check 44).** Picked the accuracy item at the head of PARITY-BACKLOG §8: *"28 of the 14,696 Chinese entries have a truncated `etymology`, detectable by an unbalanced parenthesis; the importer split a field on a delimiter that also occurs inside the parentheses."* **Both halves of that sentence were false, and the way they were false is worth more than the repair.** The MySword module stores **one `<p>` per VISUAL LINE**, not one per field, and `import_yahweh_modules.py` read `lines[2]` as the etymology and `lines[3]` as the KJV usage — correct only for an entry whose every field happened to fit on one printed line. A field that wrapped arrived as two, three, up to **nine** elements; the importer kept the first and misfiled the rest. In H205 אָוֶן the reader saw a definition stopping mid-clause at 使尽浑身解数, **no KJV counts at all**, and the missing half of the etymology printed as **sense 1 of the word**. It rendered perfectly, threw nothing, and the suite was green throughout. **The parenthesis test was not an undercount — it was a different quantity**: it can only see a break landing between a bracket and its partner, which is 0.2% of an **11.5%** defect. The detector that works is the module's own convention — a usage block reads `钦定本 - word N, …; TOTAL`, and the trailing total makes it **self-terminating**, so a wrapped continuation is distinguishable from the next real field because the field is not finished until the total arrives; sense markers close the other end. **Measured on 14,197 lexical entries: 468 etymologies and 1,635 usage fields repaired (468 of them entirely absent before), 1,631 sense lists cleaned, entries carrying a usage field 13,721 → 14,189, unbalanced-bracket etymologies 26 → 13** (the residual is damage in the module itself — H2775 ships as `charcah (khar'- saw` — left alone rather than invented, as are the 8 entries with no 钦定本 line). **The verification that matters is conservation, not those counts**: flattened `etymology+usage+senses` per entry, old against new, **0 entries lost text and 0 gained** — every change is a redistribution between fields and nothing was written that the lexicographer did not write. **Two near-misses on the way, both silent.** A first sense-marker regex `^\d+[a-z]?\d*\)` does not match `1a1d)`, `1c2d)` or a bare `a)`, and would have glued ~1,900 real senses into the line above — *worse than the defect being repaired*; the 14 marker shapes were enumerated from the data before the pattern was settled. And the tidier branch was the wrong one: for the 8 entries with no usage line, joining the whole body into the etymology emptied H2194's five numbered senses, H2059's three and H2540's five — caught only because check 43b had **pinned the article-less headword set** and it grew from 5 to 8. **A pinned set from an earlier check is what made a silent regression loud.** **Deliberately NOT repaired**: 1,514 non-marker lines inside sense blocks mix genuine wraps with standalone annotations (`专有名词, 阳性`, `其同义词, 见 5859`) and nothing in the markup separates them; senses render one per line so a wrap is cosmetic, while a wrong join would state a sense the lexicon does not. The same reasoning fixed the failure direction for the usage block — a **truncated count claim is worse than an absent one**. **A green test that was green because of the defect**: `lexicon_page_test`'s "picking a work changes the rows" asserted Thayer 中文 differs from Strong's on G26 ἀγάπη — it passed because Thayer 中文's first "sense" was the fragment `feast of charity 1; 116`. With the real sense restored **the two works say the same thing**, and measurement explains why: **4,875 of 5,514 Greek headwords (88.4%) carry a Chinese summary identical to Strong's but for punctuation**, because both descend from CBOL. The browser's second Chinese work largely repeats the first *in the row summary*; it differs in the article body, which is what the article-tier search reads.
