Re-pinned on **G76 Ἀδάμ**, where they genuinely part — Strong's describes the person, the module gives the name's meaning (`亚当 = "红土"`). **A test that asserts two things differ is only as good as the reason they differ.** **Found on the way, measured, and NOT fixed (check 44f, now item 0 of "Next, in order")**: the identical cut in a second asset — `tools/build_originals.py:244` keeps the **first physical line** of CBOL's sense 1, so **at least 279 shipped Strong's Chinese glosses end mid-clause on a comma** (193 Hebrew, 86 Greek: H204 stops at 崇拜太阳神的中心, G4102 πίστις at 相信;). That 279 is a **floor measured by the same undercounting instrument** that reported 43c as 28; the real figure must be counted from `defZh`, which ships whole, so the repair needs no network — but it is a different generator and one repair per iteration is what keeps a conservation proof meaningful. **Also recorded in the importer's docstring**: re-running it **regresses 27 verses in 15 tagged books**, because `repair_chinese_lookalikes.py` runs afterwards and a plain re-run puts the 丶/、 lookalike back; it happened during this work and `git status` showing 15 files nobody meant to touch is what caught it. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite **3,141 green** (was 3,136). **Deployed to dev as v1.6.152**; prod untouched.
- **The Strong's Chinese gloss reached the end of its own sentence — and the naive repair invented a reading no lexicon contains.** `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md` check **44g**, closing 44f and item **0** of "Next, in order", the job the previous iteration measured and deliberately left. `tools/build_originals.py` kept the **first physical LINE** of CBOL's sense 1, and CBOL wraps a long sense at its own column width, so `glossZh` — the row summary in the Lexicon Browser and the gloss in word study — stopped wherever the printed page broke: H204 אֹן read `位于埃及低地的一个城市, 歌珊的边界处, 崇拜太阳神的中心,` and the clause naming it as **Potiphera's home town** was on line two. **The scope 44f published was 279 and the real figure is 593** — 2.1×, because 279 counts only the cuts that happen to land on a separator, which is the **same partial instrument that reported check 43c as 28 when it was 468**. Measured from `defZh` as 44f demanded. **488 entries rewritten (367 Hebrew of 8,674, 121 Greek of 5,523), +25,125 characters restored, glosses ending on a separator 558 → 0**, and **675 scripture citations became reachable from the gloss** that had only ever been in the body (46,052 → 46,727; the *unreadable* count did not move). No network: `defZh`/`defZhTw` ship whole, and `glossZhTw` is parsed out of the **Traditional body** rather than converted from the repaired Simplified, so every 繁體 character is one that already shipped — OpenCC `s2t` is not a safe blanket operation on this corpus. `tools/repair_zh_gloss_linebreaks.py` **imports** `sense_one_gloss` from the generator rather than restating it, so asset and generator cannot drift. **The difficulty is that CBOL's newline means two different things and nothing in the markup says which.** G749 ἀρχιερεύς has `祭司长, 大祭司` on a **17-column** line inside a **90-column** entry, and the next line opens a fresh article; "join the lines" produced **`大祭司在祭司中最大的一`, a reading found in no lexicon** — and a fabricated gloss is worse than a truncated one, which is the whole reason 44f was left rather than rushed. There is **no global column width to cut at** (sense-1 line widths run continuously from 5 to 99 with no gap), but **within one entry the wrap is visible**, so a break counts as a wrap only when the line it ends could not have held more: it dangles on a separator, or it is unterminated and reaches **70% of the widest line in its own entry**. Same class as G749, all left alone: G5330, G5019, G200, G2769, G3567, G4950, G205, G2884, G1537, G1722, G1519, G3684. **Three sub-rules, each paid for by a named entry.** A **plain bracket is not a terminator, a CBOL citation is** — `|` ends an item, a bare `)` does not, because H1374 closes `(今 Anata 亚拿塔)` mid-sentence and reading that as the end says the village is *at* Anathoth rather than **between the ridges of Anathoth and Nob** (also H5683 `别是巴[884]`, H6048, H6489, G1359 `与莉达(Leda)`). **A space is not a word boundary in Chinese** — join directly between two wide characters or H1841 reads `神所赐 解梦的恩赐`, H3038 `他的后 裔`, H6540 `里 海和`; join tight before punctuation too, or G5330 gets `自豪 , 相对之下`. And **CBOL nests four levels deep**: `_ZH_NUMBERED` matched `1a)` but not `1a1)`, so **~3,900 sub-sense markers were being read as ordinary prose** — H7293's sense 1 was running on into `1a1) 神话中的海怪`. That last is a **second defect in the same function, found only because the wrap rule made it visible**. Part-of-speech lines still end a sense by a **closed vocabulary, not by indentation or length**: CBOL indents three tags (H369, H4616, H8478) and leaves 107 at column zero, while genuine text runs as short as `的手上` (H2078) and `地名` is a two-character tag. The trailing separator is stripped only **after** joining, and only after checking it was safe: every gloss still ending on one is followed by a sub-sense (26), the next sense (16) or the end of the body (19), and **never** by text the rule declined to take. **Verified**: analyze exit **0**, suite **3,145 green** (was 3,141); the repair is **idempotent** (second run: 0 changes) and a **structural diff against `HEAD` confirms only `glossZh`/`glossZhTw` moved** in 488 entries, with the serialisation round-tripping **byte-identical** on 8.2 MB. The strongest new test is a corpus invariant: **all 28,368 glosses appear verbatim inside their own definition body once line breaks are removed** — that is what separates reflowing from paraphrasing, and it holds for the untouched glosses too. **NOT verified**: nothing screenshotted, no deploy this iteration, so the Lexicon Browser row was not seen with the longer text — it is `maxLines: 1` with ellipsis and the length distribution was measured instead (13,466 glosses under 40 chars, 84 over 80, longest **270** at G5330 Φαρισαῖος, a genuinely single wrapped paragraph); an ellipsised long gloss is honest where the old fragment ending in a comma looked complete. Also **not** checked by a lexicographer — the join reflows CBOL, it does not adjudicate CBOL. **Not deployed**: undeployed iteration **1 of 3** after v1.6.152; this is loop-found accuracy work, no human-reported defect was fixed and the owner did not ask, so the deploy-on-demand test is not met — stated, not omitted. #278, #292-source, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.
- **The flat Bible and its tagged layer had never been compared — and the comparison found the reading text itself wrong in about twenty Chinese verses.** `docs/DATA-INTEGRITY.md` check **45**, closing the "never chased across the other books" note check 21 left in "Not checked yet". `assets/<edition>.json` is the text on the page and `assets/tagged/<edition>/<book>.json` is what the word under the reader's finger answers; they are built by different processes, so **each witnesses the other with no outside source**. `tools/audit_tagged_layer.py` sweeps all five committed tagged editions (`nsn-plus` excluded — the licensed NASB passes locally and fails CI). **COVERAGE: 111 of 155,193 flat verses carry no tagged record, 90 of them placeholders** (`见上节`, `OMIT`) where having none is correct; the other **21 are real Greek in `lxxwh`** — 1 Chronicles 1:30, Ezra 10:35/36/40, Nehemiah 10 ×15, Nehemiah 12:2–3, every one inside a list of names. **Check 21's published 15-in-Nehemiah-10 survived re-measurement from the source**, the rarer outcome after 43c and 44; what it could not know was the six elsewhere. Left unrepaired on purpose — `browse_window.dart:1036` and `phrasing_page.dart:381` fall back to plain text, so the reader sees the Greek and merely cannot tap it, and a guessed Strong's number on a Hebrew name is worse than a blank. **AGREEMENT: `kjvs`, `lxxwh` and `cuvs-plus` are 92,892 of 92,894 BYTE-IDENTICAL** between the two layers (the 2 are a trailing space at Numbers 10:35 and Deuteronomy 23:23) — the normalisation is not carrying that result. `bsb` drops a word in **2 of 31,086**, Exodus 38:28 ("of silver") and Judges 16:14 ("it"), and **both were inside the 248-verse punctuation residue `test/bsb_tagged_layer_test.dart` already tolerates** — a coarser comparison is a *different* question, not a worse one; reducing until only whole words survive is what made two dropped words separable from 248 dropped commas. **THE FINDING IS HOW THE FIRST DRAFT FAILED.** I wrote 45d claiming `cuvs-yhwh`'s 372 disagreements were "two editions, not a corruption" — 〔…〕 marginal notes plus orthography — with exactly 2 real defects, and **a refutation pass broke it**. Re-measured myself: **372 = 161 orthographic-only + 116 note placement + 16 same-characters-reordered + 79 REAL WORD DIFFERENCES**. A plausible editorial story covered 293 of 372 and hid the rest; **covering most of a set is not explaining it**. Of the 79, **15 print a literal `#` on screen** where the flat prints `[基督]` (「算不得吃主#的晚餐」, 1 Cor 11:20 and 14 more — `_TaggedLine` renders runs verbatim). The other 64 are genuine textual defects **in both files, neither of which is the authority**: the FLAT text — what the app displays — has Judges 12:7 「作以色列的士师年」 with the 六 gone so it no longer says how long Jephthah judged, Isaiah 23:1 「因为罗变为荒场」 with 推罗 unnamed, Judges 9:57 missing 他, Judges 15:5 missing 葡萄园, spurious characters at 1 Samuel 15:12 and Jeremiah 50:32, a spurious 神 at Lamentations 3:1, 木丕子 for 木墩子, 挓抄手 for 挓挲手, 意料 for 逆料; the TAGGED layer has doubled characters (若若 Lev 5:7, 箭箭 1 Sam 20:37, 未未曾 1 Kgs 19:18) and drops 上 at Leviticus 8:14. **Not repaired — that is now the leading unnumbered item in "Next, in order"**, it can be taken unattended, and the brief for it says do NOT bulk-apply either layer over the other. Refutation also killed one more claim of mine: `kwic_pane.dart:127` skips a null-runs line while `_totalRefs` still counts it, so its "All N references" footer **overstates** on `lxxwh` — a small false statement, not under-coverage. `test/tagged_layer_coverage_test.dart`, **9 tests**, re-derives every figure from the assets and freezes the 21 and the 15 by name. **Named blind spots: a shift present in BOTH layers passes at 100.0000%** (exactly how check 40's `cuvs-plus` 1 Chronicles 22 defect survived), `<note: …>` CONTENT is stripped so ~47 further verses go unexamined, and `reuniteGlossRuns` rewrites 198 `cuvs-yhwh` verses at load so for that edition this measures the file and not the screen. **Also closed #323 this iteration** (雅偉繁體 wrong Traditional forms, owner-reported): re-verified from the asset rather than from the ticket — 賽2:16 船隻, 出14:22 走乾地, and the trap verse 賽29:17 still 只有, with 0 occurrences of 船只/其余/走幹地/凈. Marked closed in `PROJECT_STATE.md` and its body moved from `prompt.md` to `prompt-archive.md`; #323 was the only id removed. **#313 was NOT closed but its headline evidence is disproved** — the ticket says the reader has no wire to the Analysis pane, and `bible_reading_pane.dart:144/149` carries `onAnalysisRequest` and `activeAnalysisRequest`. Re-scope it before working it. #308, #314, #316, #319 and #320 were spot-checked and look shipped but are left open on purpose: a spot check is not a closing pass.

- **The third witness failed, and the failure is the finding — 22 verses repaired, 21 defects named in the text the reader reads.** Check 45d ended on a deadlock ("neither layer is the authority; each caught the other") over **79** `cuvs-yhwh` verses where the reading text and its own tagged layer disagree on a word. The obvious way out was a third file, and `assets/cuvs-plus.json` — a second 和合本 already in the repo, imported separately — **does not work**: folded for the divine name and reduced to Han it matches the reading text in **29,790 of 31,102 verses (95.78%)** and **917,572 of 920,316 characters (99.70%)**, which no two independent translations reach. It is descent, not independence. This is not theoretical — the first version of `tools/adjudicate_cuvs_yhwh.py` trusted the majority and proposed **deleting the 六 from Judges 12:7**, where both flat editions read 「作以色列的士师年」 and only the tagged layer says Jephthah judged Israel *six* years. Check 26's lesson a second time. **So the script ships as a report with a deliberately narrow repair gate, and applied only the two classes that need no vote:** 15 verses printing a literal `#` where the reading text prints the supplied `[基督]` (17 marks; `#` is not a Chinese character), and 7 doubling a character against itself (若若, 箭箭, 未未曾, 我们我们, 你要要, 以以色列, 耶稣说说) where both flats read it once. Confirmed by an instrument that knows nothing about the script: `audit_tagged_layer.py` went **372 → 350**, exactly the 22 touched. **What it found and did NOT repair is the more valuable half: 21 word-level defects in the reading text** — 12 standing alone against both other files (**Isaiah 23:1 has lost the 推 of 推罗 and no longer names Tyre**; Lamentations 3:1 carries a 神 the 和合本 lacks) and **9 where BOTH flat editions lost a word the tagged layer keeps** (Judges 9:57's 他 — the page reads 「咒诅归到们身上」 and 们 cannot stand alone; 12:7's 六; Malachi 2:3's 在). Those nine are **exactly what a majority vote destroys**, and were findable only by refusing it. **Direction is not mechanical and the script does not pretend it is**: it groups them, then each was read against the Strong's number its characters carry, and the ten verdicts are written into `ADJUDICATED` with a guard that warns if the table drifts from the data. **One of the ten went the other way** — Job 31:36's 「愿那敌我敌」 is a *single* H7379 run with a doubled character, so there the reading text is right; a nine-of-ten rule applied to all ten would have damaged it. It is the same defect as the class-B seven but **non-adjacent**, which is why the doubled gate could not see it. **Two counts were wrong before they were right, both caught by refusing a plausible story.** A raw substring test would have mis-sorted note placement from word loss in both directions (1 Samuel 1:24's note is punctuated, 「那时，孩子还小。」, so it is not found; Judges 9:57's genuinely missing 他 IS "found" three clauses away) — testing the note **bodies** specifically split the 25 into 10 real and 15 placement. And the 〔〕 side finding was drafted as "25 unbalanced verses, Matthew 18:10 dangling" and **that was false**: 和合本 opens a note in one verse and closes it in a later one, so 22 of the 25 are 11 correct cross-verse pairs. Counting over the whole edition (**12 〔 to 13 〕**) leaves exactly three real ones: 士师记 8:24 and 耶利米书 10:11 end on a 〕 nothing opened, and 路加福音 8:45 closes its 〔 with an **ASCII `)`**. Regression tests in `test/cuvs_yhwh_tagged_layer_test.dart` (2 new, 8 assertions) were each verified to **fail against the pre-repair asset** — the doubled ones need the character *before* the stutter in the fragment, since a doubled character otherwise just shifts the match one place right and the assertion passes on the damaged text. `tagged_layer_coverage_test.dart`'s ceiling ratcheted 372 → 350, as its own comment instructed. **Deployed v1.6.153 to dev from a detached worktree at c49eb05**, not the shared tree — a second writer was mid-rebrand with uncommitted files and `release_web.sh` builds from the working tree. Verified on the immutable deploy URL, not assumed: the deployed `assets/tagged/cuvs-yhwh/matthew.json` carries **0 hashes**, 太22:44 reads 「主[基督]」 and 太9:28 reads 「耶稣说」.

- **The closing pass: eight rows shut, and seven of them had been finished for weeks.** `PROJECT_STATE.md` had been carrying its own warning since 2026-08-23 — *"these statuses are NOT audited… closing the finished rows is worth an iteration of its own"* — and the previous iteration had deliberately declined to close five rows it had spot-checked, on the correct grounds that **a spot check is not a closing pass**. This iteration did the pass. **#289, #307, #313, #314, #316, #319, #320 were already shipped and nobody had written it down**, and each is now closed carrying the file and line that proves it, so the next reader does not re-derive what was derived. The cost of leaving them open was not bookkeeping: **an unclosed row keeps its body in `prompt.md`, and `prompt.md` is read whole at the top of every iteration and again by every subagent**. Moving the nine bodies (the eight plus #321, closed since v1.6.126 with its body still in place) took the brief from **1,682 to 1,322 lines, 102,547 to 81,530 characters** — about a fifth off the entrance fee of every future iteration. Verified by set arithmetic rather than by eye: the union of `#NNN` across `prompt.md` and `prompt-archive.md` is **unchanged at 50 ids**, and every residual mention of a moved ticket is a cross-reference (`"per #313's rule"`, `"do this AFTER #313"`), not a body. **Two rows were deliberately NOT closed, and that is the discipline the pass exists to demonstrate.** #312 sits beside #307 on the same screen and #307's two asks are both shipped — but `PARITY-BACKLOG.md:361` still says "track the remaining #312 items" and the export work it names is unbuilt, so #312 stays open and now says why. **The rows also confirmed the standing grep rule the hard way**: #319's fix is a doc comment reading *"whether the map also draws what the filter left out"* and #313's is the single word `hostChrome` — **neither contains its own ticket number**, which is exactly how `PARITY-BACKLOG` §8 once called two finished items outstanding. **Only #308 needed work, and only on a surface the ticket never named.** The strip and the Stats tab were fixed when it was filed; `WordDistribution` — the panel inside the word-study sheet, and the surface a narrow layout falls back to — was still unlabelled **and plots the OTHER unit**: its only caller feeds `ConcordanceResult.byBook`, the per-book OCCURRENCE map, while the strip beside it plots VERSES. **G25 in John was 37 in one place and 27 in the other, in the same session, and the second number carried no label at all.** `unit` is now a **required** parameter rather than a defaulted one, so a future caller holding a verse map has to say so instead of inheriting a wrong label silently, and it reuses `search_stats.dart`'s `HitUnit` vocabulary rather than inventing a second one that happens to agree today. The five new tests assert **real text on screen** (`find.text('Distribution (occurrences)')`, and `find.text('Distribution')` findsNothing) in all three locales — the strip's original defect survived a green suite precisely because a property assertion passes whether or not the label is drawn. **The more useful finding is #315's, and it is a finding about the detector, not the code.** A `fontSize:` literal is only ONE of the two ways to write a size the reader's slider cannot move: `main.dart:578-591` rewires exactly three Material roles from `settings.fontSize` — `bodyLarge`, `bodyMedium`, `titleLarge` — so **every other role in `textTheme` is a fixed number wearing a name**, and `theme.textTheme.bodySmall` is as deaf as `fontSize: 12` while scoring zero on the literal ratchet. That gap was not hypothetical: **`small_screen_advisory.dart` and `analysis_tabs.dart` both sat on the ratchet's `finished` list — a written promise that the setting reaches everything on them — while holding seven such sites between them**, four of them on the hard-gate screen whose only control is the language switch. Added `WbType.scaleRole()`, doc-commented to refuse the three rewired roles because **scaling those again squares the reader's setting**; fixed the seven; added a tree-wide zero check for the second mechanism. **My own first measurement of the scope was wrong in the same direction the project keeps being wrong**: 21 sites, until excluding the ones whose `copyWith` supplies its own `t.scaled(...)` brought it to 7 — all 7 `labelSmall` uses, for instance, set their own size on the very next line. *A detector reports its own reach, not the defect's size*, applied to my own instrument before publishing the number. **The new ratchet was negative-tested rather than assumed**: reverting one site made it fail naming `small_screen_advisory.dart:198 headlineSmall`, then the file was restored — a ratchet that has never been seen to fail is not known to work. **Verified**: `flutter analyze` exit **0**, suite **3,282 green** (was 3,276). **NOT verified**: nothing screenshotted — the seven fixes are a source-level change to which number a style reads, and the ratchet is a source test by design, so no pixel was inspected; the claim is that the slider now reaches these labels, not that they look right at every stop. **Deployed to dev as v1.6.156**, prod untouched. The undeployed counter was only at 1 of 3, but #308 is **owner-reported** and this iteration fixed its third surface, which is the deploy-on-demand trigger in its own right. **Verified on the immutable deploy URL, and the verification nearly published a false alarm**: the two Chinese labels came back **0 occurrences** from the bundle, which looks exactly like a shipped defect. `main.dart.js` escapes CJK as lowercase `\uXXXX`, so a grep for Han text finds nothing whatever the bundle holds — four strings known to have shipped for months also scored 0. Running that control first is what turned an absence into an instrument limit; the real search then found all three locales present, plus the shared unit vocabulary. The second writer was mid-rebrand *during* the deploy — new `android/` locale resources and a modified `build.gradle.kts` appeared while it ran — which was safe only because a web build reads neither, and nothing of theirs was staged. #278, #292-source, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.
- **The size that reads as wired, compiles as wired, reviews as wired — and never moves.** #315 has now been audited three times and each audit found a mechanism the previous detector could not see. The first was the hardcoded literal. The second was a Material `textTheme` role other than the three `main.dart` rewires. The third, found this iteration, is the largest and the most deceptive: **a clamp.** `fontSize: (settings.fontSize - 2).clamp(11.0, 16.0)` is deaf from 18 pt upward, which is **below the app's own default of 20** — so a reader who has never touched the slider is already sitting on the ceiling, and the entire upper half of the control, 21 of its 29 stops, moves nothing. Nothing about the line looks wrong. It names the setting, it does arithmetic on it, it compiles. Measured tree-wide with an instrument that flattens the file so a clamp wrapped across lines is still one expression: **80 text sizes run through such a ceiling and 73 are already saturated at the default.** `settings_page.dart` holds 28 of them, which is the page carrying the Font Size slider contradicting its own control in front of the reader who came there to fix exactly this. That is the general case behind the owner's 「还有很多界面都是」 — *and it is the second time on this ticket that the user's estimate of scope beat the detector's.* The standing rule that a detector reports its own reach rather than the defect's size earned its keep twice here: the first pass at this third mechanism used a three-line grep window, reported **73 ceilings / 59 saturated**, and was wrong on both numbers because it could not see a wrapped expression. Those figures were published in a comment before the better instrument existed and are now corrected in place, because a scope measured by a partial instrument is a different quantity, not a low estimate. **The reader itself is repaired end to end** — 41 literals, 10 saturating ceilings and one clamped line height, all routed through a private `_ReaderTypeScale` extension that is also where the text-vs-chrome decision for the file is written down. The transform is design-value-at-20 pt, so a reader who never moved a slider sees a pixel-identical screen and only the ends of the range change. The extension reads settings with `listen: false`, not `WbType.of()`, because several call sites are `showModalBottomSheet` builders and `PopupMenuButton.itemBuilder`s, where `context.watch` throws outside the build phase — a runtime crash avoided rather than hit. **The part worth arguing with:** persistent bars were deliberately put on the CHROME scale, not the reading scale. Growing furniture that cannot wrap is new overflow risk, and the conservative reading of an owner-reported legibility complaint is not to make the app wider. Chrome keeps real travel via Menu Scale, which is asserted, not assumed. **And a behaviour test, because a source ratchet fundamentally cannot prove a size moves** — the ratchet's own header says a widget test is useless here, and for a single `TextStyle` assertion that is true, since the number is real whatever its provenance; a DELTA across two slider positions is a different instrument. `reader_font_size_behaviour_test.dart` pumps the real reader (seeded with two verses, because the pane shows a spinner until `MainProvider` has data — which is why the responsive smoke test skips it) and drives both sliders. It was then **run against the previous commit in a detached worktree, where exactly one of its three cases fails, at 12.0 against an expected 19.0**: before this change, dragging Font Size to 12 pt shrank the chapter reference in the persistent bar to 12 px alongside the body text, which is precisely the condition in the two photographs that opened the ticket. That before/after also **shrank the claim honestly**: on the five labels reachable in a widget test, nothing else changed at all, so the repair's other 49 sites live in sheets and popups this instrument cannot open and are covered only by the source ratchet. Said plainly rather than left to be discovered. Deployed **v1.6.157** to dev — #315 is owner-reported, the standing trigger — and built from a **detached worktree at the pushed SHA** rather than in place, because the second writer had uncommitted edits to `radial_chronology_layout.dart` in the shared tree and `release_web.sh` builds from the working tree; in-place would have shipped a bundle matching no commit. #315 stays open: 63 saturated ceilings and 64 literals remain across 15 other files.

- **The column was straight, and only because one constant was covering another's mistake.** #322's structural work shipped in v1.6.139 — `BrowseVerseRow` (`browse_window.dart:889`) is the single row for all three render paths — and reading the source this iteration said it was done. Measuring it said otherwise. `referenceGutterWidth` sized the reference column with **no letter spacing**, while the reference `Text` inherits `bodyMedium`'s **0.25** from the ambient `DefaultTextStyle` under a `Scaffold`: the painted string ran `runes.length × 0.25` px wider than the box computed for it. For 「帖撒羅尼迦前書 1:1」 that is **2.75 px of the 8 px gap**, so nothing looked broken — the gap was bigger than the error. Shrink the gap for a design reason and Chinese book names go ragged with nothing in source to explain why. Its sibling one column to the left, `versionGutterWidthForLabels`, had charged letterSpacing all along. Fixed by naming `kBrowseReferenceLetterSpacing = 0.25` and passing the same number to the measurement and the paint, so they agree **by construction rather than by luck**. **The reason five existing tests missed it is that every one of them compared the model to itself** (`expect(width, referenceLabelEms('Psalms 119:176') * 15 + 8)`) — arithmetic identities that hold whatever the font does. `test/browse_reference_real_font_test.dart` is the first test in the repo to load the **shipped** faces, `Roboto-VariableFont` and `NotoSansSC-Sub`, via `FontLoader` + `rootBundle`, and lay the references out for real: 66 books × 3 scripts × 5 verse forms × 5 sizes = **4,950 assertions**, none overflowing. **That instrument had to be debugged before it could be trusted, and the debugging is now three permanent control tests.** With `fontFamily: null` the first probe reported 244 px against a 122 px model — a flat 1.0 em per character, Latin and Han alike — because `flutter_test` resolves to its own **fixed-width stand-in** and never consults `fontFamilyFallback`; a width test run that way passes while proving nothing. The controls assert the face is proportional (`mmmmmmmmmm` > 2× `iiiiiiiiii`), that the **variable wght axis** is applied (600 wider than 400 — the model is calibrated at the weight the reference is actually set in), and that a Han glyph really is the 1.0 em the model charges it. A second trap: the "bare" run and the 0.25 run came back byte-identical, because `TextStyle(letterSpacing: null)` **inherits** rather than disables — the defect was identified arithmetically (129.96 + 11×0.25 = 132.71 vs 132.70 measured) rather than by trusting the label on the run. **The strict assertion was negative-tested**: as first written it recomputed the model by hand and so passed regardless of production code; rewired to call `referenceGutterWidth(..., gap: 0)` — the production function with its gap spent to nothing, leaving the model's own claim about the text — it reports **1,033 references over** with the fix reverted. **The doc comment's calibration was also simply false** and is corrected in place: it claimed "+4.5% to +15% across all 66 English book names", both ends wrong and English-only. Measured: **English +4.19%…+19.57%, Simplified and Traditional +1.34%…+3.90%**. The Chinese margin is **three times thinner and structurally so** — a Han glyph is charged exactly 1.0 em with no rounding up at all, so a Chinese reference's entire safety margin comes from its ` 1:1` tail, and anyone retuning the Latin classes is tuning the only slack it has. **#322 stays open on purpose.** The owner's complaint was visual; what this iteration obtained is proof-by-measurement, and 「不要不协调」 is closed by a photograph, not by 4,950 green assertions. **Not deployed** — the visible misalignment was fixed in v1.6.139 and this is a latent fix no reader can see, so no owner-reported trigger fires; the undeployed counter stands at **1 of 3**. Verified in an isolated `git worktree` at `HEAD` plus only these three files, because CI sees only what is pushed: `flutter analyze` exit **0**, suite **3,304 green**. The second writer's #318 work (`wheel_history.json`, `radial_chronology_*`) was live in the shared tree throughout and is deliberately still uncommitted — staging was by explicit pathspec, never `git add -A`.


- **A clamp has two bounds, and the repair that deletes both fixes the complaint by inventing its opposite.** #315's third mechanism was found last iteration; this one paid the file that made it worth finding. `settings_page.dart` held **28 of the app's 80 saturating ceilings and 5 of its literals** — the largest concentration in the tree, on the one page where the reader can see the Font Size slider and its result **at the same time**. Dragging it to 40 pt left every hint, subtitle and section header exactly where it was. **The obvious repair — strip the `.clamp()` — is wrong, and that is the finding.** A clamp's ceiling was the defect; its FLOOR was doing real work. A reader who drags Font Size down to 12 is asking for dense *scripture*, and a proportional caption at 0.6× puts a 7.8 px hint under a settings row, smaller than anything the app prints anywhere. So the floor survives **once**, as `WbMetrics.smallPrintFloor` (= `WbMetrics.chrome`, 11.0), replacing the four different numbers — 10, 11, 12 and 13 — that the thirty sites carried between them; documented as a *comfort* bound explicitly unlike `originalFloor`, which is a *correctness* one (a 9 px hint is unpleasant and still says what it says; a 9 px qamats is a vowel the reader is not being shown). **The second half of the repair is that `fontSize - k` is additive and additive offsets cannot hold a type hierarchy.** Two sizes 5 pt apart are a ratio of 1.8 at the bottom of the slider and 1.15 at the top, where the design was 1.38 — so the shape was drifting even in the range it *could* reach. `WbType.scaledSmall(atDefault)` multiplies instead and keeps the design ratio at every one of the slider's 29 stops; the argument is the size the site renders **today at 20 pt**, so a reader who never moved the slider sees a pixel-identical page and only the unreachable range changes. **Which scale the page belongs to was not a judgement call, and the page had already answered it.** Card titles here are `settings.fontSize + 2` with no bound at all and a switch row's label is `settings.fontSize` outright — this is Font Size's page, not Menu Size's, and the clamped sites were ones that had fallen off the file's own convention rather than a deliberate second scale. Leaving them produced an **inverted hierarchy at the top of the range**: at 40 pt a `_SettingsSwitch` printed its title at 40 px over its own subtitle at 14, a 2.9× gap where the design was 20 and 14. **The behaviour test is the half a source ratchet cannot supply** — the ratchet next door can prove a size is not a literal, not a deaf Material role and not a clamp; it cannot prove the size MOVES, which is the entire report. `settings_font_size_behaviour_test.dart` drives the real page across the real slider and reads back the `RichText` root style the tree actually resolved, with its anchors looked up through `uiStrings` so an editor rewording a hint does not fail a type test. Run against **`b75ffc6`** before believing it: the Display section header comes back **22.0 px at 40 pt against an expected 36.0**. At the default it is 18.0 both before and after — the repair is invisible to a reader who never touched the control, which is the point. **A stale comment was also corrected rather than left**: `_SectionHeader` described "a default of 16 pt, capped at 22" when the default has been 20 for some time, so a header stopped growing barely above the default it was documented from. **One site was deliberately NOT converted** — `avatarRadius = (settings.fontSize * 0.8).clamp(20.0, 28.0)` is a box, not text, and is not saturated at the default. **A 7.3 px overflow at 320 px / 40 pt was found, investigated, and reported instead of fixed**: stashing the change and re-measuring gave an **identical 7.3 px**, so it belongs to the already-unbounded card titles, and 320 px is below the **992** `SmallScreenGate` admits (`workbench_fit.dart:82`) — a layout no reader can reach. Widening a ratchet to cover unreachable states is how a suite starts failing for reasons nobody can act on; the overflow cases pin **992 and 1280** instead. **Residue was re-counted from the ratchet's own maps rather than copied forward, and the old figure did not reconcile**: it is **35 ceilings over 14 files + 57 literals over 12**, where the previous row said 64 literals and 57 + the 5 paid is 62. Sum the map, not the prose. **Verified**: suite **3,312 green** (was 3,304), `flutter analyze` exit **0**, run last after the final edit. **Deployed v1.6.158 to dev** — #315 is **owner-reported**, which is the standing trigger on its own — built from a **detached worktree at the pushed SHA (e7268da)**, because the second writer had uncommitted `radial_chronology_page.dart`, `url_sync_service*` and `main.dart` edits live in the shared tree and `release_web.sh` builds from the *working tree*; in place would have shipped a bundle matching no commit. Their files were never staged — staging was by explicit pathspec, never `git add -A`. **And then the deploy was overwritten, which is a hazard this log has not recorded before: the shared repo is also a shared SITE.** 83 seconds after v1.6.158 went live the second writer published a **v1.6.157** build over the alias (`6a8b9178…` 00:34:00 vs `6a8b9125…` 00:32:37), so `seeksparks-dev.netlify.app` reports **1.6.157** while only the immutable deploy URL carries 1.6.158. The version in the status bar therefore read *older than the one just shipped*, which is exactly the shape of the service-worker trap in the screenshot notes and is **not** that — `version.json` on the alias and on the immutable URL disagreed, and `listSiteDeploys` named the culprit. **The fix is live regardless, and that was measured rather than reasoned**: their build came off the shared working tree *after* e7268da was committed into it, and driving the real Font Size slider to 40 pt on the live alias shows ACCOUNT/DISPLAY, the profile subtitle and the card's small print all roughly doubled, wrapping to three lines with no overflow and no clipping — which also settles the one question a number could not answer, whether the spacing reads wrong when text doubles and the `16 * s` paddings do not. It does not. **Deliberately NOT re-published over them**: it would clobber a deploy they may be verifying and buys no behaviour that is not already served. Worth knowing that their bundle was built from a dirty tree and matches no commit. #315 stays open.

- **The app has one theme, it built every text size from constants, and that made all fifteen Material roles deaf at once.** #315 has now been audited four times. The first three mechanisms are all *source patterns* — a `fontSize: 12` literal, a bare Material role, a saturating `.clamp()` — and each was caught by grepping call sites. The fourth is written nowhere near a call site, and it is the largest: **`workbenchTheme` is the app's ONLY theme.** `main.dart` wraps both `theme:` and `darkTheme:` with it; it builds from a *fresh* `ThemeData.light/dark` and reads exactly two things off the theme handed to it — `brightness` and `fontFamilyFallback`. The three roles `main.dart` carefully rewired from `settings.fontSize` were therefore **discarded one line after they were set**. Measured on `b6d9859` before touching anything: all fifteen roles, the `Scaffold`'s `DefaultTextStyle`, and a bare `Text` are **identical at 12 pt, 20 pt and 40 pt** — `displayLarge` 57, `titleLarge` 22, `labelLarge` 14 (which alone sizes every button and chip label in the app), and a `Text('…')` with no `style:` painting at **12.0 px at both ends of a 12–40 pt slider**. **This is the root of mechanism two, not a fourth peer.** The roles were fixed *because* `workbenchTheme` made them fixed, so `WbType.scaleRole()` — added in v1.6.156 to work around exactly this — is deleted along with its 7 call sites: with the root repaired it would now **square** the scale. The ratchet's mechanism-2 test goes with it, and that retirement is the honest part: it forbade `theme.textTheme.bodySmall`, which is now the *correct* thing to write. **The wrong first attempt is recorded in the code because it is the whole subtlety of the fix.** Scaling `base.textTheme` changed nothing and the probe still read 12.0 px at both ends: at the moment the function runs **every role in `base.textTheme` has a null `fontSize`**, because the numbers arrive later from `ThemeData.localize`, which merges `typography.geometryThemeFor(scriptCategory)` when the theme is applied to a subtree. The scale has to go on the **`Typography` geometry** — and on all three categories, because `dense` is the one a **Chinese** UI resolves to, so a fix applied only to `englishLike` would have reached the English app and left the Chinese one deaf. **A second trap cost more than the first and is now written into the test file: `MaterialApp` wraps its theme in `AnimatedTheme`.** A single `pump()` after changing `theme:` reads the theme **mid-lerp**, which at t=0 is the OLD one — so the probe reported everything still deaf when the fix was already working, and would just as happily have reported a broken fix as fine. Every pump settles. That also means the pre-fix baseline had to be **re-measured** with the corrected probe, in a detached worktree at `b6d9859`, before the "all deaf" claim was allowed to stand; it stood. **At the default 20 pt the scale is exactly 1.0 and every role is byte-identical to before**, so a reader who never moved the slider sees no change at all — this only opens the range the slider could not reach; a test asserts the fifteen numbers explicitly. The floor is `WbMetrics.smallPrintFloor` (11.0), the same policy `WbType.scaledSmall` applies and the same one v1.6.158 chose one iteration earlier. **The seam the fix opens is guarded, because `textScale` defaults to 1.0** — a call site that forgets it re-creates the entire defect silently and locally. All **6** `workbenchTheme(` call sites in `lib/` are pinned by a source test, and **the detector was verified to fire** by deleting one `textScale:` and watching it name `books_page.dart:50`. **`theme_font_size_behaviour_test.dart` is the instrument that could see this, and the ratchet's header — which asserted "the defect is invisible to a widget test" — is corrected in place**: the fourth mechanism is the exact converse, invisible to source and found in one pump. Neither instrument dominates; they are halves. **Verified**: suite **3,318 green** (was 3,312), `flutter analyze` exit **0**, run last after the final edit to the final file. **Deployed v1.6.159 to dev** — #315 is **owner-reported**, the standing trigger — from a **detached worktree at the pushed SHA (`01b5c80`)`**, because the second writer's 106 modified launcher icons and an untracked `output/` were live in the shared tree; staging was by explicit pathspec, never `git add -A`. This time the alias **does** serve it: `version.json` and the in-app status bar both read **1.6.159**. **Live verification found no defect but did catch a false negative before it was written down.** The File menu's items do *not* grow with Font Size at 40 pt, which reads exactly like the fix failing on the deployed build; they carry an explicit `fontSize: t.chrome` (`workbench_chrome.dart:133`) and belong to the **Menu Size** slider, correctly. The honest live checks were the small-screen advisory — the file whose 4 `scaleRole` sites this change converted to bare roles, i.e. the riskiest edit — which still scales at 40 pt with no regression, and the workbench at **both slider extremes at 1300 px**, where the three panes hold with no overflow or clipping. **The product consequence worth arguing with**: because roles now scale *down* as well as up, a heading that was frozen at 24 px is 14.4 px at the minimum setting. That is symmetric, floored at 11, and preserves hierarchy at every stop — and the reader who drags to 12 is asking for density — but it is a visible change at the bottom of the range for a ticket that opened with a complaint about text being too small, so it is named here rather than left to be found. #315 stays open: **35 ceilings over 14 files + 57 literals over 12** remain, unchanged by this iteration, which paid the root instead of a file.

- **A control the reader could only learn by losing their page, and a header that could delete the page entirely — v1.6.161, #312 item 7.** The four phrasing level chips printed Biblearc's vocabulary (`Verses` / `Clauses` / `+ Verbals` / `+ Phrases`) and nothing else, and **BibleWorks offers no help here at all**: `bwh25`'s Diagrammer is a symbol canvas with drag-and-drop connectors and *no granularity setting of any kind*, so a reader arrives with no model of these levels from any tool they have used. The only way to find out what `+ Verbals` did was to tap it — which replaces the page they were reading. That would be a modest complaint if the answer were usually interesting. It is not: swept over **9,982** three-verse windows of the bundled original-language corpus, `+ Verbals` draws the **identical page** to `Clauses` in **2,087 (20.9%)** — one tap in five moved nothing — plus `Clauses` 236 and `+ Phrases` 356. John 1:1-5 is the instance that started it. The existing guard cannot see this and never could: `availablePhrasingLevels` asks whether the **edition** carries a parse, not whether **this passage** contains a participle, and its own doc comment already warned that a reader who taps and sees nothing "learns that the tool is broken". Each chip now carries the line count it would draw, measured through the widgets' own path over the same words, source and **verse window** (#308: a count that silently measured the chapter would be a different quantity wearing the same label — two dedicated tests pin the window and the reader's own added/removed breaks). The chosen level gets one sentence naming what it cuts at, and when it draws nothing new it says so. **"Same lines" is exact, not a hedge** — the levels are monotone supersets, so an equal count *cannot* mean a different page — and rather than assert that in prose the corpus test proves it over all **2,679** equal-count cases by comparing the line STARTS, not the totals. Not a tooltip, deliberately: the app is tablet-first and a tablet has no hover, which is the same root cause #312 item 1 opened with. Equal-count chips are **not** disabled either, because the level is a persisted claim about how the reader wants the text cut and it still applies when the window moves. **The second half was not on the ticket and is the more serious defect.** The header is an unbounded `Column` above an `Expanded` diagram, so it takes its natural height and the text gets the remainder. Measured by pumping the real page: at the reader's largest font in a 480 px window it **already overflowed by 20 px and left the diagram exactly 0 px tall** — the font-size slider could push the scripture off its own page — and the new note would have taken that to 68 px. Shipping a teaching note on top of that would have been paying for #312 item 7 with #312 item 4. The header is now capped at **55%** of the pane and scrolls past it (`SingleChildScrollView` still shrink-wraps under a bounded `maxHeight`, so a short header is not padded out — asserted, because nothing else would notice). Across every font × height cell the diagram now floors at ~152 px, which is **better than before this feature existed** (200 / 60 / 0 at 24 pt). `phrasing_page_header_test.dart` was confirmed to fail — 48.5 px — with the cap removed, so it is a detector and not a decoration. **A trap worth keeping**: a second `testWidgets` in that file sits on the spinner forever, because the chapter is served by a process-wide cache that only resolves in whichever test loaded it first; the whole matrix runs in one test for that reason, not for brevity. **#312 stays open, and the row's old reason was wrong** — it blamed unbuilt export, but `PARITY-BACKLOG.md:445` is the Report Generator's own entry, not a #312 item, and rich export has been built and tested since `phrasing_test.dart:908`. All three items the backlog enumerates are now shipped. What keeps it open is only that a reporter, not the loop, decides when "redesign, don't patch" is finished.

- **The join that fails by succeeding — #317, the wilderness itinerary, `0a14966`.** This iteration began with a dirty tree: the previous run was killed by the watchdog (`Killed: 9`) mid-#317, leaving 1,164 lines across 13 files uncommitted. Per the standing rule a killed run's tree is evidence, so it was analysed and re-measured rather than discarded — and it was very nearly finished work. **Numbers 33 is the one route in scripture the text itself calls a list** (33:2: Moses wrote the stages down at the LORD's command), so unlike the Exodus reconstructions #317 explicitly warns against, its ORDER carries no scholarly claim at all. All 42 stations ship. **What the route found is a second failure mode, and a quieter one than the ticket was written against.** An unplaceable stop breaks the line and the panel has said so since v1.6.134. A stop the gazetteer places *on top of its neighbour* draws perfectly and says nothing. Measured through the app's own parser (a throwaway `test/tmp_*.dart`, never python — re-implementing the parse would measure a different quantity): **909 of the 1,228 located places share a point**, 1,228 places on 560 distinct coordinates of which 241 carry more than one, and **27 of Numbers 33's 42 stations fall into six such runs**, the largest eleven camps deep. Drawn stop-by-stop the itinerary emits **37 legs of which 21 are exactly zero kilometres**: eleven markers on one pixel, eleven badges overprinted, eleven invisible legs, and **one dot where the text names eleven camps with nothing on screen to say anything was missing**. That is worse than a confident wrong line, because it is silent. **Four shipped routes never revealed it, and the reason is worth keeping**: Paul's itineraries move between named cities the gazetteer locates individually, so **0 of their 71 stops** share a point with the next, and keying markers on the place id was indistinguishable from keying on the position until two *different* places sat on one. The `ordinalsByPlace` doc comment had warned about precisely this smudge since v1.6.134 — **the comment was right and the code did not implement it.** `markerKeyFor` (the coordinate) is a strict generalisation, which is why no Pauline expectation changed. **The wording is about the DATA, never the scholarship**: unidentified sites and genuinely adjacent places (Jerusalem's gates share points too) are indistinguishable in what we hold, so *"{n} of these stops share one map point with the stop next to them"* ships and *"their location is unknown"* does not. Doubt sits at a run's **ends and nowhere else** — a leg is provisional when the text does not place the travellers at one of *its two ends*, and a mid-run camp is not an end of anything; taking the weakest camp in a run was tried and would have drawn a false statement in the same channel, only in the other direction. Badges range-compress with an en dash **only where the run really is consecutive**, since `8,10` is Lystra visited twice and `8–10` would claim three stops standing still. **A data defect was repaired on the way**: `repairPlaceName` could not reach four names because the `eph`→`Eph` substitution landed at index 1, behind the name's own initial capital — `REphidim` (a stop on this route), `REphaim`, `NEphtoah`, `SEphar` all shipped mangled. `BEze 1`/`BEze 2` are deliberately **not** repaired: their verses name Bezek, so the entry lost a final letter too, and a rule wide enough to catch them would emit the plausible-but-still-wrong `Beze`. **What this run added to the killed one was verification, and it caught a contradiction.** Two files stated the same fact with different numbers — `place_map.dart` said "914 of 1,233 located places", `journey_route.dart` and check 48 said "909 of 1,228". Measurement settled it at **909 of 1,228**; the stale pair is corrected, along with a badge example written as `18–28` when the shipped run is `17–27`. Every other figure verified exactly: 27 of 42 in 6 runs, largest run 11, 37 legs / 21 zero-length, 0 of 4 Pauline routes, 17 raw interior capitals of which 15 are `Eph` damage and 4 unreachable. **Verified**: suite **3,363 green** (was 3,340), `flutter analyze` exit **0**, both run last after the final edit. **No deploy, and for once that is not a skipped build.** The second writer ran `release_web.sh` at ~14:22 while this work was live and *uncommitted* in the shared tree, so **v1.6.164 snapshotted it** — confirmed on the deployed bundle, which carries all five journeys, the panel's `stops share a point, in` fallback and the `(?<=[A-Za-z])E(?=ph)` repair. Dev was therefore serving a build made from a mid-edit tree **that no suite had ever run against**; the gap this iteration closed was the commit, not the deploy. Undeployed loop iterations: **2**. **The lesson for a shared tree**: a second writer's release can ship your uncommitted work without either of you deciding to, so unfinished work in this tree is not private — commit early or expect it deployed.

- **Four wrong counts, and not one of them threw — #295's second pass, the command grammar.** The first #295 pass drove the deployed build over CDP and fixed three things in the chrome *around* the search box; this one drove the grammar itself, ~60 syntaxes with the count each returned written down in `docs/SEARCH-AUDIT.md`. **Four counts were wrong and every one of them looked like an answer.** That is the ticket's whole point — it says in as many words that "'447 tests pass' is a DIFFERENT claim" — and it is why every expectation in the new `command_grammar_audit_test.dart` is an absolute number: `isNotEmpty` would have passed on all four bugs. **(A) `int.parse` on `;N`, `*N` and a compound join distance.** On the Dart VM `;99999999999999999999` throws, and the throw escapes `WorkbenchProvider.runSearch` — which has a `finally` and **no** `catch` — leaving `searchPerformed` true, the list empty, and no issue to explain it: the reader is told "no results" about a search that never ran. **Compiled to JS the same call returns 1e20 instead of throwing**, so three of the four shapes were already refusing politely on the shipped target; the overstated version of this claim was written first and **withdrawn** after running the probe under `flutter test --platform chrome`, with a refuter confirming independently via `dart compile js` + node. `tryParse` makes the two targets agree and closes the native hole. **A numeric parse that diverges by platform is invisible to a VM-only suite for a web-shipped app.** **(C) An apostrophe at a word's edge could never match.** `phraseTokens` strips edge apostrophes when it builds a corpus token, so the token behind the printed word `sons'` is `sons`; the query side kept its, and the two could never meet. `.sons'` returned **0** for a word the KJV prints in **212** verses — kjvs 214, nasb 428, nasb-ev and nsn-plus 936 each, leb 158, bsb 82. Fixed on the query side, and it is **not a widening**: `hits(".sons'")` is now element-for-element equal to `hits('.sons')` (956), because those verses were always inside the answer and were being withheld. Edges only, so `.god's` = 25 and `.lord's` = 26 are untouched. **(D) `*N` above 50 was clamped, silently**, while its sibling `;N` above 176 returns a named issue — the one operator in the grammar that answered a different question than the one asked and said nothing. **I refuted this myself and I was wrong**: eight hand-picked probes showed no difference and I wrote that the clamp never bit. The refuter produced **KJV Esther 8:9** (90 tokens, `then` at 0, `language` at 89) and `'then *50 language` = 5 verses without it vs `'then *89 language` = 7 with it settled it. **Eight probes is not a measurement of a ceiling; the extreme of the distribution is** — a throwaway running the *real* tokenizer over every edition in `assets/` gave **202, `lxxwh` 1 Kings 16:28**, with 782 KJV verses over the old 50. Before raising it I measured what the old number was buying: **nothing.** `_matchFrom` already clamps each gap to the tokens remaining in the verse, so the worst multi-gap phrase over `lxxwh`'s 30,800 verses ran **350 ms at `*400` and 355 ms at `*50`** — flat. That is what made the real number affordable and let the ceiling become a named refusal (`CommandIssue.gapTooLarge`, three locales) instead of a rewrite. **(B) The Greek final sigma: found, fixed, and withdrawn — the finding stands, the fix does not ship.** `foldDiacritics` does not map `ς` to `σ`, and every caller lower-cases afterwards where Dart's simple case mapping sends `Σ` to `σ` and never to `ς`, so `.θεός` = 1611 while `.ΘΕΌΣ` = **0**, and `.κύριος` = 3219 while `.ΚΎΡΙΟΣ` = 0; `lxxwh` holds **115,332 word-final ς across 9,411 of its 42,952 distinct forms**. The generator fix works and was reverted for two reasons, the second decisive: it adds **48 new false positives** to the reader-facing substring scan, because `Ἰησοῦς` folded to a medial sigma matches inside `ποιησουσιν` ("they shall make") — Exodus 18:20, 21:31, 28:3, 28:4, 28:6, so a reader searching for *Jesus* is shown tabernacle furniture; and a refuter found **no all-caps Greek anywhere** in `strongs/greek.json`, `lxxwh.json` or `thayer.json`, nothing in the app displays majuscule Greek, and the title-case form a reader would actually type (`.Θεός`) already worked. Narrower fixes fail too — word-final-only folding still collides because the sequence is *medial* in `ποιησουσιν`, and token-path-only folding breaks the prefilter's superset contract and loses hits. **#321's own rule decided it by flipping sign**: over-matching shows a reader a verse they can check and under-matching tells them a word is not in the Bible, but here the under-match is a form nothing displays and the over-match is a wrong verse in the reading text. **A one-way fold's direction of safety is a property of its callers, not of the fold.** **The instrument nearly lied first**: my initial differential reconstructed the "old" fold by replacing `σ`→`ς` on *both* sides, which is symmetric, and duly reported old = new = 650, extra = **0** — a clean bill of health for a change that adds 48 false hits. **A differential whose two arms are related by a symmetry measures nothing.** **Two rows of my own deliverable were wrong and turning the doc into tests caught both**: a bare `@G25` is *not* `strongsTagUnsupported` (`@` is caught in a command *body*; a bare line was never a command and goes to the substring scan), and `'a !b c` parses fine — `phraseNotMultiToken` needs a negated term of **more than one token**, e.g. `'a !神爱 b`. **A table written from memory an hour after the measurement is not a measurement.** Two further expectations of mine were wrong in the safe direction and the code was right: `.god$` = `.god` = 3877 because punctuation is transparent to the tokenizer on *both* sides, while interior punctuation still splits a term (`.g$d` = 0) and `[` `{` really are grammar (`.[]` = 0 is an empty character class, not a swallowed query) — pinned, because in a sweep that reads like a bug and is consistency. **Verified**: suite **3,387 green** (was 3,363), `flutter analyze` exit **0**, both run last after the final edit. **What #295 still owes a human at a browser** is named in `docs/SEARCH-AUDIT.md` §6 rather than left implied: the operator-strip caret sequencing, the `d -nas` / `d c` / `p a b c` / `l gen` verbs, that a narrowed scope is *visible* and not merely correct, reference navigation (`John 1:1`, `3:16`, bare `17`), `G25 NEAR5 G26` and the missing-number case, a capped list admitting its cap, and the whole sweep under a Chinese UI locale — the three locale *strings* are asserted, that they render un-truncated in the result header is not.

- **A frozen size beside a scaled one does not just fail to grow — it can change RANK.** #315's fifth pass took the four surfaces that mix a scaled body with frozen furniture, and one of them holds the ticket's sharpest case. `sermon_detail_page.dart` set the sermon's own title to the literal **22** above a body of `settings.fontSize`: the largest text on the page at the default 20 pt, and **smaller than the sermon it introduces from 23 pt on** — stop 12 of the slider's 29 — finishing at just over half its size at 40. Every earlier #315 finding was a size that stopped moving; this one **inverts the page's own hierarchy**, and it does so from just above the default, where most readers who touch the slider at all will land. It is also the **only** true inversion in the app, which was checked rather than assumed: I started framing several literals this way and refuted it before writing it down — `verse_popup_sheet`'s 18 px bold reference over a 20 px verse is correct subordination, and the rest distort ratios without reversing them. The same page's **condensed-sermon notice** is the quieter half of the finding: designed at 13 px against a 20 px body (1.54× contrast) and rendered at 13 against 40 (3.08×). That line is the disclosure that the transcript below is *abridged rather than the preaching* — the one sentence a reader who raised the slider **because they cannot see small text** most needs to read, set at the size they already said was too small. **`strongs_entry_page.dart` carried a second defect that no reach detector could ever have found**, because it is wrong at the default and not merely deaf above it: `_RelatedChip` printed a **lemma** — accented Greek, pointed Hebrew — at 13 px, **two under the app's own `WbMetrics.originalFloor`**. Per that constant's rule the design size was raised to the floor rather than clamped to it; clamping would have quietly enlarged only the bottom of the slider and left the default reading wrong. Its headword was `settings.fontSize + 8`, an **additive** offset, which cannot hold a hierarchy at all — 1.67× the body at 12 pt and 1.20× at 40 — now `scaledOriginal(28)`. Paid this pass: **16 literals over four files** (`sermon_detail_page` 6, `strongs_entry_page` 7, `verse_popup_sheet` 2, `note_reference_picker_sheet` 1, all four moved from the budget to `finished`) and **3 saturating ceilings** — `version_picker_sheet`'s `.clamp(12, 15)` answered **26 of the slider's 29 stops with the same number**, on the sheet where a reader chooses their Bible; `sidebar_panel`'s `.clamp(14, 18)` froze from 18 pt up; `loading_page` gave up one more. A new **`WbSettingsScale on AppSettings`** extension (`settings.wbType`) retires the longhand four-argument `WbType.resolve` at the roughly half of call sites that hold the settings as a field or build inside a dialog or sheet whose context sits above the provider — which is precisely how `fontFamily` came to be passed at some of those sites and omitted at others. **The claim is measured, not read out of the source.** A ratchet can prove a size is not a literal; only a pumped page can prove it *moves*, and only a pumped page can compare two sizes that are written in **different files** — which is what a rank claim is. `sermon_font_size_behaviour_test.dart` builds the real page (`_MetaChip`, `_LanguageToggle` and `_CondensedNotice` are private, so it reaches them the way a reader does) and reads each `RichText`'s painted size. Run against **`f8f927a`**, the commit before the fix, **all three of its tests fail**, and the rank test fails at exactly 24 pt: *"at 24.0 pt the title is 22.0 px, under a body of 24.0"*. The default is deliberately untouched — title still 22.0 and byline still 13.0 at 20 pt — because a repair a reader can *see* is a redesign, and this is not one. Two things went wrong on the way and both are worth keeping. A single `pump()` after `setFontSize` reads the tree **mid-lerp**, since `MaterialApp` wraps its theme in an `AnimatedTheme`; that trap reported a working fix as broken during v1.6.159 and it was waiting here too. And **my own count was the smaller instrument**: a regex I wrote found 12 saturating ceilings where the ratchet's own detector finds **35** — the repo's own lesson (*a detector reports its reach, not the defect's size*) landing on the person applying it. The published figure is the detector's. Residue, taken from the ratchet's maps rather than from any grep of mine: **32 ceilings over 12 files + 41 literals over 8**, and 9 of those 41 are `app_style_preset.dart`'s preset **fields** — the value handed to `setFontSize`, not a size anything renders. Suite green, `flutter analyze` exit 0.

- **A size can be wired correctly, move at every stop, and still paint the same pixels — #315's fifth mechanism, `37c4bb2`, v1.6.167.** The four mechanisms #315 already polices are all ways of writing a NUMBER the slider cannot move: a literal, a deaf Material role, a saturating clamp, a theme built from constants. This one is none of them. `book_chapter_picker.dart` wrote `fontSize: settings.fontSize * 1.15` — the correct shape, which passes every source ratchet and moves at all 29 stops — inside a `FittedBox` inside a grid cell whose width came from the **MENU** scale. `fontSize` then set a ceiling the fit never reached and the painted glyph was `cellWidth / labelEmWidth`, a constant. Measured against **`7874e01`**, the commit before the fix: **`Jonah` painted 44.0 px wide at 20 pt and 44.0 px at 40 pt.** Across the 156 shipped abbreviations in the 280 px reader sidebar, **62 were already at their maximum painted size at the DEFAULT 20 pt** and every one of the 156 froze somewhere inside the slider; at 800 px it is 4 at the default and 89 within the slider. **This is the app's primary navigation surface** — the grid a reader uses to reach a book — and it is a plausible part of what the owner photographed with 「这些字很难看清楚还有很多界面都是」. **The instrument is the whole point, and it is a third kind.** A source rule sees nothing wrong, because nothing is wrong with the source. A widget test that reads `RichText.text.style.fontSize` reads the **declared** size, which under a `FittedBox` is correct while the pixels are not — so `settings_font_size_behaviour_test.dart` and its siblings would have passed on this forever, and `tester.getSize` agrees with them because it returns the pre-transform render size. Only `tester.getRect` separates them: it resolves through `localToGlobal`, and so through the fit's transform. `test/fitted_label_reach_test.dart` is that third instrument, and the ratchet's header now says so. **A sixth thing turned up in the same widget while measuring, and it is a trap worth naming: `StrutStyle` does not inherit `fontSize`.** An unset strut `fontSize` is **14**, not the text's, and `forceStrutHeight: true` then pins the line box to 14 px at every setting while the glyph grows past it — measured at **12.35 px of line box for a 23 pt glyph** — so the baseline was placed by a 14 px box and a large letter sat well above the centre of its tile. There is exactly one `StrutStyle(` in `lib/`, and it now takes the same expression as the text. **My starting guess was refuted by the measurement.** Chinese looked like the crowded case; Latin is far worse. `Jonah` is **2.78 em** against `林前` at 2.00, and the shipped Chinese abbreviations are **one ideograph** (`马太福音` → `太`, 1.00 em), so the gap is nearly threefold. That asymmetry is why the widest label is **measured per grid** rather than assumed — a global constant would have charged every script for `Jonah`. **The floor was capped by the label, not simply lowered.** The old `.clamp(4, 10)` minimum of four is the defect (four columns in a 280 px sidebar leave 44 px of tile), but a flat floor of 2 would have thinned the **Chinese** sidebar from 4 columns to 3 while painting exactly the same glyph, since one ideograph fits four columns comfortably. So the floor stays at 4 and is capped by what the label can actually use, with 2 as the hard bottom — one column would satisfy the widest Latin label at 40 pt and is not offered, because 66 books in a single file is a worse way to find Habakkuk than a label 14% under size. Both no-regression cases are asserted: the Chinese grid still draws **10** columns at 800 px / 20 pt and **4** in the 280 px sidebar. **A visible change is stated rather than hidden**: at 800 px and the default 20 pt an **English** grid now draws **9 columns instead of 10**, because `2.78 × 23 + 14 = 77.9 px` is what the label wants and a tenth column cannot give it. That is intentional — 4 of the 156 labels were frozen at that default — but the #315 precedent has been to hold the default, so it is the one place this iteration spends appearance to buy correctness. **The measurement is wired to the paint, per #322's lesson.** `widestLabelEmWidth` is handed `settings.fontFamily` and `kCjkFontFallback`, the exact strings the `Text` paints with, so the model cannot disagree with the string on screen the way #322's gutter model disagreed by omitting a `letterSpacing`; and an em width is scale-invariant, so one `TextPainter` layout per (label, family, weight) answers all 29 stops from a cache. **A false comment was corrected in place rather than deleted.** The 2026-05-10 note claiming `BoxFit.contain` makes single-character labels scale UP has never once happened: `Container(alignment:)` inserts an `Align`, which passes **loose** constraints, so the `FittedBox` shrink-wraps its child and the fit is computed between the child and a box the same size — scale 1.0, `contain` and `scaleDown` identical here. Measured: at 12 pt the label painted 41.4 px inside a 104.4 px tile. What the fit really does is the second half, shrink, which is what made it a silent ceiling. **`stats_page.dart`'s `_NumberGrid` was the second real instance** and got the same treatment: its chip box was a literal `width: 56` under a label that HAS scaled since v1.6.159, so the `FittedBox` stopped being a safety net for Psalm 119 and became a ceiling; it is now `56 * WbType.of(context).textScale`, which is 56 at the default. Two other `FittedBox` sites were checked and are **not** instances — `version_picker_sheet._languagePill`'s `minimumSize` is a minimum under loose constraints, and `word_distribution_table` scales font and cell together from the table's own `_zoom`, which is consistent and answers a different control. **A test-harness trap that would have made the suite lie, and it nearly did.** The tile asks for `settings.fontFamily`, whose shipped value is the CSS token `-apple-system`; `flutter test` resolves any unregistered family to its fixed-width stand-in where **every glyph is 1.0 em**, and never walks `fontFamilyFallback` at all because the stand-in has every glyph. Loading Roboto under the name `Roboto` therefore never reached the widget — it had to be registered **under `-apple-system`**. Without that the widget group measured 1.0 em per glyph, the Latin/Han distinction collapsed entirely, and the Chinese-versus-Latin test returned **zh=10, en=10** — which is also exactly what the *unfixed* code returns, so a green tick there would have meant nothing. **Run against `7874e01`, 3 of the 4 widget tests fail** and the fourth is the no-regression guard, which passes before and after because that is what it is for. **Verified**: suite **3,403 green** (was 3,391), `flutter analyze` exit **0**, both run last after the final edit. Deployed as **v1.6.167** from a detached worktree at the pushed SHA, the shared tree carrying the second writer's untracked `output/`; the alias serves it (`version.json` reads 1.6.167, cache-busted). Deployed rather than banked because the rule's second clause applies — a defect a real person reported. #315 stays **open**: residue is unchanged at **32 ceilings over 12 files + 41 literals over 8**, since this pass paid a mechanism rather than a file.

- **A region's coordinate is a city's, and that is a different failure from an unplaceable stop — #317, `fffae59`, not deployed.** The owner named three routes on 2026-08-16; the first two shipped and this is the third, 主耶稣路线. It is **Mark's own sequence, never a harmony of the four Gospels**, because a harmony is a reconstruction and a reconstruction drawn as a line is the thing the feature exists not to do — and the asset test's one-book rule now names the book rather than merely counting to one. **The substance of the iteration is what the route refuses to draw.** The wilderness itinerary found "a join that fails by succeeding" — a stop placed on top of its neighbour draws perfectly and says nothing. This is its sibling and it is worse, because it is not silent, it is *wrong*: a gazetteer entry for a **region** still carries a point, and that point is usually some city's. Measured through the shipped asset: `Judea` is **[31.77, 35.23], Jerusalem's own**; `Decapolis` is **[33.51, 36.31], Damascus**; `Galilee` is **[32.7, 35.3], byte-identical to Nazareth**; `Dalmanutha` is **[32.84, 35.52], byte-identical to `Magadan`** — which is *Matthew's* identification and not Mark's word, so drawing it would smuggle in the harmony the route's own framing forbids; `Gethsemane` and `Golgotha` are **Jerusalem exactly**; and `Jordan` is one point for a 250 km river, mid-valley near Beth-shean, far north of where Mark 1:5 puts John. Mark names every one of them. Drawing Mark 10:1 would have put Jesus in Jerusalem a chapter before he enters it. So **eight named places are omitted**, the `basis` says which and why, and `journey_asset_test.dart` fails the build if one is ever added back. The two `Sea of Galilee` stops went the same way and for the same reason, late and reluctantly: the point is the **lake's centre**, some 7 km off any shore, and 1:16 has him *walking beside* it. Keeping them would have bought 27 km and made the route's own disclosure false. **The refuter earned its place this run.** It was launched on the 19-stop draft and came back with five corrections that all held up against our own shipped editions, and two of them were mine to be embarrassed by. Bethsaida 6:45 was drafted as an **aside** — the schema's word for a place *named but never reached*, like Phoenix in Acts 27:12 — on my inference that the crossing missed it. Mark never says that, and 8:22 has them arrive; the map would have shown one hollow and one solid Bethsaida two chapters apart. Dropped. And `Dalmanutha`/`Magadan` I had not checked at all. **Sidon is the one provisional stop, and it is provisional because the app ships both sides of the split**: BSB, LEB and NASB read διὰ Σιδῶνος, "through Sidon", while the **KJV we ship** reads "departing from the coasts of Tyre and Sidon", which places him at neither. Drawn as dots rather than dropped, per the standing rule that a reader comparing a printed atlas needs to see where its stop came from. **Mark is full of boats and the route has no sea leg at all** — every crossing departs from or arrives at a place with no usable coordinate (the solitary place of 6:32, the country of the Gerasenes at 5:1 which is absent from the gazetteer entirely and is itself a textual variant our KJV prints as "Gadarenes") — so a test fails the build if one ever appears. **The sixth route forced the palette question the file had been carrying since v1.6.134.** `journey_style.dart` stops at five hues honestly, because five cannot be held apart under deuteranopia and protanopia, and its own note ended *"a sixth route should add a channel, not a sixth hue"*. That channel is now `JourneyMark` — round is Acts, square is the Torah, diamond is the Gospels, **chosen by body of narrative rather than by slot arithmetic**, so it is something a reader learns once and keeps. Identity is a PAIR, so Mark's route safely reuses slot 0's amber, and the asset test's old "distinct hues" row is replaced by "no two routes share an **appearance**". The shapes are scaled to **equal AREA, not equal radius** — a square across the circle's diameter is 27% heavier and reads as a more important stop, an inscribed diamond 36% lighter — and the test **rasterises them and counts opaque pixels** rather than re-asserting the two constants. That measurement has its own instrument limit worth recording: thresholding an antialiased alpha channel treats a diamond's straight diagonals differently from a circle's curve, and the residual is proportional to the **perimeter**, so at r=40 the two disagreed by 2% and at r=100 by well under 1%. The legend swatch draws the silhouette now, because a hue that may repeat needs the legend to teach the pairing. **The short dash was widened, deliberately and in writing.** It meant "the text refuses the manner" (Acts 20:1). Mark needs "the text routes them through a place we cannot locate" — the manner is plain, they walked. Both make the reader the same promise, *this line is not underwritten as a direct journey*, which is why they share a dash instead of getting a fifth pattern; `journeysKey` and `journeyLegUnknown` are reworded to the claim rather than to either cause. A solid Nazareth→Capernaum would have erased the baptism and the forty days Mark puts between them. **Two defects found on the way and fixed here.** The journeys block was sized by the **data** and was not the index column's flexible child, so it grew at the place list's expense until route six pushed a 320×640 pane over by **34 px** — a bug about the seventh route as much as the sixth. Capped against the pane's own height and scrolling inside that cap. My first regression test for it pumped 320×400 and I checked it against the pre-change tree per the repo's own rule: it overflowed there by **162 px**, i.e. it was a pre-existing limit at a size below any supported configuration and **not what I had fixed**, so the test was rewritten to assert the block's height against the pane at the suite's established 320×640. That version fails on the old layout with the new asset and passes after. Second, `the straight-line totals` asserted a **500..20000 km** band whose real content was "every route in this file is a Mediterranean voyage"; Mark's never leaves Galilee and Judea and totals **478.6 km**, so the floor would have rejected it *for being the right size*. Replaced by a **pinned total per route**, which is the coordinate-drift detector the comment always claimed to be — and pinning immediately caught that my own probe's figure for the wilderness route (1,841 km) was wrong, because `straightLineKm` sums the **drawn** segments and a collapsed run contributes once; the real number is 1,638.4. **Verified**: suite **3,412 green** (was 3,403), `flutter analyze` exit **0**, both run last after the final edit. **Not deployed, as a stated decision**: this is undeployed loop iteration **1 of 3**, and a new route is a feature rather than a defect a real person reported, so it is banked under the deploy-on-demand rule. #317 stays **open** — Luke, Matthew and John are natural further slices, and they now compose as overlapping toggles without a palette collision.
- **The frozen sizes inside the two files the ratchet called finished — #315, `3f42bff`.** Six passes of this ticket have built three detectors: a literal, a saturating clamp, and a correct size inside a `FittedBox`. This pass found two more mechanisms that all three are blind to, and the finding is not that two more sites existed — it is **where** they were. `originals_sheet.dart` and `word_distribution_table.dart` are on the ratchet's `finished` list, which the file itself describes as *"a promise, not a debt"*; both were carrying frozen sizes the whole time, and the test was actively certifying them clean. **The sixth mechanism is a design constant wearing a principled name.** `fontSize: WbMetrics.text` names the app's own type scale instead of inventing a number, so it reads as the fix and reviews as the fix; it is exactly as deaf as `fontSize: 12`, because `WbMetrics` is where the sizes AT THE DEFAULT are written down and `WbType` is the only thing that multiplies them by what the reader set. **Fourteen sites tree-wide. Five were the workbench's own empty states**, and one of those five is `_analysisHint` — the placeholder for **eleven** analysis tabs, called from **thirteen** places. It is the first text in the pane and the sentence telling the reader what to tap, so a reader who dragged the slider to 40 pt *because they cannot see small text* was answered at 12 px, in every tab, before there was anything else there to read. **The seventh is a literal on the far side of a question mark.** The ratchet's regex anchored the number immediately after `fontSize:`, so `_st.dense ? _st.body : 13` hid one token further along — four such sites in `originals_sheet.dart`, all in the MODAL presentation, one of them a **lemma** at exactly `WbMetrics.originalFloor`, in a file whose whole purpose is `WordStudyStyle`, the class built to hold precisely those numbers.
