The same blindness has a clamp-shaped variant: the ceiling detector required a NUMERIC offset, so `(settings.fontSize - (compact ? 4 : 2)).clamp(11, 15)` in `contact_line.dart` and `(… - (prominent ? 1 : 3)).clamp(10, 16)` in `confidence_badge.dart` were both **saturated at the DEFAULT on BOTH branches** — no setting and no call site ever moved either, and the 2 px each ternary asked for has never been drawn. **A third hole surfaced while widening the second, and it is the one worth remembering:** the clamp test's "is this text?" guard looked for the word `fontSize` in the 60 characters *before* the match, which requires the clamp to sit at a `fontSize:` itself. `confidence_badge.dart` wrote `final fs = (settings.fontSize - …).clamp(10, 16);` on one line and `fontSize: fs` fifteen lines later, and was invisible for that reason alone. **Every new detector was confirmed to FIRE** by reverting the site it was written for — 1+4+1 literals, 5 named constants, 1 and then 1 ceiling — and the three new behaviour tests confirmed to **fail against the pre-fix sources**: the contact line painted **15 px at 40 pt where it should paint 30**, the badge **16 where it should paint 32**, the analysis hint **19 where it should paint 12**. **The distribution table needed its geometry moved too, not just its font.** Its numeric cells sit in a `FittedBox` — this ticket's fifth mechanism — so raising the font inside an unchanged column width would have been scaled straight back down and the repair would have painted nothing; `_cellScale` is now `_zoom × textScale` and drives the widths and the padding, while the zoom bar still reports `_zoom` alone, because that is the control the reader is holding. **A duplicate found by the measurement, not by reading:** `analysisEmptyHint` is rendered TWICE in `workbench_page.dart`, once inline at `settings.fontSize - 1` and once through `_analysisHint`, so the same sentence was **19 px or 12 px depending on which empty state the reader arrived in**; the inline copy is gone. **And the instrument itself had to be repaired before it could be trusted.** `Text.rich(span)` does not paint `span` — it builds `RichText(text: TextSpan(style: <the DefaultTextStyle>, children: [span]))`, so the root style is whatever the theme inherited and the size the widget actually asked for is one level down. Reading `richText.text.style.fontSize`, which is what the existing behaviour tests do, reported the **already-repaired** `ContactLine` as frozen at **14.0 px at 12, 20 AND 40 pt** — a false negative in the precise shape of the defect under test. The new test walks the span tree. **Honest about reach:** the distribution table's change is source-verified only — its `StrongsService`/`ConcordanceService` futures never resolve under `flutter test`, so the table pumps empty and its growth is reasoned, not measured. `bible_trivia_page.dart`'s nine `WbMetrics` sites are budgeted with a stated reason (3,213 lines, no `WbType` anywhere, a game rather than a study surface). Residue from the ratchet's own maps: **32 ceilings over 12 files + 41 literals over 8 + 9 named constants over 1**.
- **The ceiling that put the artefact's name under its own description — #315, `1ffa816`, v1.6.169.** Seven passes of this ticket built four detectors, and on 2026-08-24 the repo wrote down that `sermon_detail_page.dart` carried **"the only true inversion in the app"**. That claim is **withdrawn**: it was measured with the LITERAL detector only, and the ceiling detector finds a second one. `evidence_detail_page.dart` set the artefact's own name to `(fs + 6).clamp(20.0, 32.0)` above a summary, a description and a scriptural correlation of bare `fs` — 1.30× the body at the default 20 pt, **1.00× at 32 and 0.80× at 40**, so on **9 of the slider's 29 stops the name of the thing is at or below the paragraph explaining it**. The lesson is not that one more site existed; it is that **a ceiling and a literal are not two severities of one bug**. Both freeze a size, either can reverse a rank, and a claim about inversions measured with one instrument says nothing about the other. Expressing it as `t.scaled(26)` also *holds* the 1.3× the design asked for, which `fs + 6` never did — that additive offset was 1.5× at the bottom of the slider and 1.15× at the top. **The second inversion on this resource was caused by an earlier pass of this same ticket.** `ConfidenceBadge` was repaired days ago and now runs to 32 px at 40 pt; the archive card's title stayed frozen at 18, so `Definitive` printed **double** the name of the artefact it qualifies. No per-file ratchet count could ever see this — the badge's own number went **down** while the screen got worse — which is the argument for a pumped-page **rank** test per surface rather than only a reach test per file. **And the card's layout headroom was fictional.** `mainAxisExtent: 240` carried a comment promising "headroom for larger font scales" and had never once been exercised, because all three text sizes inside it were clamped and stopped growing at or below the default; the moment they were unfrozen the tile's own 240 became the frozen number. It is now `100 + math.max(140, t.scaled(140))`, and the `max` is **load-bearing rather than defensive**: below the default those sizes stop shrinking at `WbMetrics.smallPrintFloor`, so the text block has a hard minimum height that a purely proportional extent undercuts — **measured, ten columns overflowed at 12 pt** before the floor was put back. The card title is `scaledSmall(18)` for the same reason: a plain 18 × 0.6 is 10.8, which puts the card's own heading **under** the floor its summary sits at, the same inversion arriving from the other end of the slider. Paid: 6 literals + 11 ceilings, both files moved to `finished`. `evidence_font_size_behaviour_test.dart` measures rank, ratio, the untouched default and the absence of overflow; **5 of its assertions were confirmed to fail against the pre-fix code** by temporary revert. **Two harness traps, both recorded in the test file.** A `ListView` builds lazily, so at a 900 px viewport the correlation panel and the academic sources looked like **missing widgets** rather than off-screen ones — and the first failing `expect` threw before the lookup, which hid it for a while; the test now runs at 1280×6000. And an `Icon` is a `RichText` in the `MaterialIcons` family whose `fontSize` is the glyph size, so an unfiltered "largest painted text" heuristic reports a 32 px placeholder icon at every setting and never looks at text at all. Photographed on the deployed alias at 40 pt and 12 pt: the hero title leads the detail page, the card title leads the badge, and the 12 pt grid has no overflow. **Checked and NOT a defect:** `WbPanel`'s section titles (`Description`, `Scriptural correlation`) look frozen beside a 40 px body but are workbench chrome and belong to **Menu Size** — read from `_Panel`, not inferred from the screenshot. Residue: **21 ceilings / 10 files + 35 literals / 6 + 9 named constants / 1**; `bible_trivia_page.dart` alone holds 23 of the 65 and is the largest single item left.
- **The reader's own `Aa` control could not reach the app's maximum — #315's eighth mechanism, `585e7ee`, v1.6.172.** Nine passes of this ticket hunted for a NUMBER the slider cannot move: a literal, a deaf Material role, a saturating clamp, a correct size inside a `FittedBox`, a named design constant, a literal past a `?`, a theme built from constants. This is none of those. Nothing is frozen — every size downstream of `settings.fontSize` moves at all 29 stops. What was frozen is the reader's **reach** over them, because `bible_reading_pane.dart` shipped a **second control over the same setting that disagreed with the first about its range**: `_showFontSizeSheet` carried `.clamp(12, 32)` from the **initial commit**, inherited from the YsWords phone reader and therefore predating `kFontSizeMin`/`kFontSizeMax` entirely. `5a258b6` (#311) unified the Settings slider and the theme clamp at 40 and walked straight past the stepper, because a **bound** is not a `fontSize:` and every instrument this ticket has built looks for sizes. Two consequences, both **measured against `f62cf73`**: **8 of the slider's 29 stops (33–40) were unreachable from the reader**, the one surface where the size is the product; and the sheet was a **one-way trapdoor** — decrease clamped *into* 32 while increase was disabled *at* 32, so from 40 pt a single tap cost **8 pt** and no sequence of taps inside the reader could undo it. The readout froze with it, so the number reporting the size stopped agreeing with the size from 26.7 pt up. **The 40-vs-32 question the ninth pass left open is answered 40, and conservatively:** three sources already say 40 (`kFontSizeMax`, the slider, `WbType.scaleFor`'s guard) against one that says 32, the 32 predates SeekSparks' own constants, choosing 32 would *remove* travel #311 deliberately bought, and #315 opened with 「这些字很难看清楚」 — capping lower is the wrong direction. **The repair is structural, not local.** Anything that MOVES the size now goes through `fontSizeAfterStep()`; anything that STORES it goes through `setFontSize()`, which bounds it — so a third control cannot reintroduce a range of its own. `WbType.scaleFor`'s comment had already written down that *"neither `setFontSize`, nor `restoreState`, nor the settings-import path bounds `fontSize`"*; all three now bound all three scales, which matters because an unbounded value reaches a `Slider` directly (**whose assert is stripped in release JS, so the thumb simply paints off the track**) and because, since #286 removed sync, the **import file is the only way settings cross devices** — a genuine system boundary, and the only one here. Line spacing had **no constants at all**; it has three now, and its slider reads them. `test/reader_font_stepper_range_test.dart` is 12 tests and, run in a detached worktree at `f62cf73`, **7 of its 8 runnable cases fail**. The 8th — *both ends still refuse to leave the range* — **passes on both commits**, and that is the finding behind the finding: the **bottom** of the range was always correct, which is a large part of why a defect about text being too small survived nine passes of a ticket about text being too small.
- **A size that scales perfectly can still be too small to read — #315's ninth shape, `efac8ee`, v1.6.173.** Every detector this ticket has built asks *is there a number the slider cannot move?* Here the answer is **no**: these sites scale at all 29 stops, and the number is simply below the app's own declared minimum. `WbMetrics.smallPrintFloor` is 11 and `WbType.scaledSmall()` **asserts** on exactly that, so **37 sites across 8 files** — not the 18 the queue row claimed, the **third** time on this ticket that a count turned out to be the instrument's reach — routed around an assert the codebase had already written, painting 4.5–6.3 px at the 12 pt minimum. The fix **raises the design number and keeps each site's helper**, because a floor applied to some sites and not others **inverts rank at the bottom of the slider** (the radial hub's title is `scaled(12)` = 7.2 px at 12 pt, under a floored 11 px subordinate; 99 further sizes sit in the at-risk band). One of the sites is a **bound name** — `double _axisFont(WbType t) => t.scaledChrome(10)` — which no `fontSize:`-anchored regex can see, so the ratchet's new fourth detector follows bindings too. **The transferable finding is a test of mine that passed for the wrong reason:** the chronology axis labels are drawn by a `TextPainter` inside a private `CustomPainter`, so they are not `RichText`, and the new behaviour test's span sweep was **blind** to them — "the axis strip clears the floor" passed against the pre-fix commit while the axis still drew at 10 px. **A span sweep is not a screen sweep.** It now reads the size the painter was handed; pre-fix, 3 of its 4 tests fail. **Withheld on purpose and written at the foot of that test:** this proves nothing is *designed* below the floor, not that nothing ever paints below 11 px — **183 of 188 `scaled(n>=11)` sites paint under 11 px somewhere below the default**, and the deployed 12 pt photograph names the real shape: **the two sliders' outputs cross over**, so the chart's row labels are 7.2 px against axis furniture fixed at 11. That is a question about `kFontSizeMin`, i.e. a product call, not a refactor.
- **The chronology past Moses is counted, not drawn — #318 phase 6, `ebec932`, not deployed.** The documented blocker was real: `scripts/build_chronology.py` could read cardinals in English and Greek and not ordinals, and 1 Kings 6:1 — the verse that would carry the axis from Moses to Solomon's temple — states its year as one. The shipped `chart_end` note said exactly that, and it was **true**. It is now false, and **what it was hiding is the whole iteration**. A separate ordinal parser reads the verse: `EN_ORDINALS`, `GK_ORDINALS`, `_ordinal_runs`, and a rule that **a run counts only if it contains an ordinal**, so *four hundred and eightieth* is one run and the cardinal noise around it is not. It is a **second** parser on purpose rather than an extension of `_runs`, because **`εκτος` is a homograph** — sixth, and also "outside" — and folding ordinals into the cardinal word list would let it fire on prose; `οκτωκαιδεκα` went in at the same time, because Judges 10:8 writes eighteen as one word where 3:14 writes `δεκα οκτω`, and until then that verse silently stated **no number at all**. **Read out of our own two shipped assets and verified by grepping them directly rather than through the generator**: `kjv.json` gives *"in the four hundred and eightieth year"* → **480**, `lxxwh.json` gives *τεσσαρακοστω και τετρακοσιοστω ετει* → 40 + 400 = **440**. The OT's single most-cited chronological anchor **is not the same number in the two texts we ship**, and the generator now raises `SystemExit` if they ever read alike, because an equal read there means the ordinal parser has failed in one of them rather than that the texts agree. **And then the arithmetic refused the chart.** Reading the 21 periods the text states *inside* that span — the wilderness, eighteen Judges spans, Eli, David — gives **MT 530 against a stated 479, over by 51**, and **LXX 520 against 439, over by 81**, and that is before **five further stretches** (Joshua's leadership, the elders, Samuel, Saul, Solomon's years before the temple) the ledger does not count. Two of the 21 differ between the texts: `othniel` (Judges 3:11, **40 vs 50**) and `eli` (1 Samuel 4:18, **40 vs 20**). **So the axis is NOT extended, and that is the substance of the decision.** Genesis hands this chart an unbroken chain — *A lived x years and begat B* — and after Moses the text stops doing that: it goes on stating durations but **never says one period begins where the last ends**, and at Judges 10:7 two oppressions run at once. Laying 21 bars end to end would be a **reconstruction**, the one thing this module has refused since its first line, and the 51-year overflow is the proof that a reconstruction is what it would be. The era ships as a **ledger below the last bar**: every period with its verse, the two totals, the difference between them, and the uncounted stretches named. **The transferable finding: a true note can go false without anything editing it.** `chart_end` was accurate the day it was written and became a lie the moment the parser learned ordinals — *while still passing its own test*, which only asserted that the note mentions `6:1`. It now carries the counted total, the residue and the ordinal, every one interpolated from the measured values, so it cannot outlive the arithmetic behind it. **A note's test should assert the numbers it makes a claim about, not the verse it cites.** **One overclaim found in my own draft and fixed before shipping.** The uncounted list was headed *"Stretches the text gives no number to"* and listed five — but the fifth, **Solomon's years before the temple, HAS a number**: 1 Kings 6:1 dates the founding in his fourth year. It is left out on purpose so the overflow stays the smallest the text allows. Reworded to what the list *is* (uncounted), with each entry saying for itself why; a reader who checks the verse must not find the ledger silent about a number plainly there. The Chinese prose also cites the book in Chinese now (列王纪上 6:1), matching every other note in this asset, with only the book name typed — chapter and verse come off the same constant the parser read at. **Verified**: `flutter analyze` exit **0**, suite **3,461 green** (was 3,446), both run last after the final edit; `assets/chronology.json`'s `patriarchs`, `epochs`, `traditions` and `notes` re-checked **byte-identical** apart from `chart_end`, so the only new top-level key is `era`. **The four new page tests were confirmed to fail with the ledger unwired** — the widget existed for a while before it was reachable, which is exactly the failure an asset test cannot see. **Two instrument notes.** `kjv.json` stores `chapter`/`verse` as **strings**, so an int-keyed probe raises `KeyError` and reads as a missing verse. And this suite does **not** use a full-em stand-in font: the app's bundled font loads, and `'530'` measures **0.606 em per glyph**, so a numeric column that would overflow under Ahem does not overflow here — the new column test was run against a deliberately narrow **1.5 em** column to confirm it can fail at all (14.9 needed, 10.8 granted), and it is documented in the file as **a floor, not a caught defect**. **Not deployed, as a stated decision**: undeployed loop iteration **1 of 3**, and #318 is an owner **feature request**, not a human-reported defect, so the standing exemption does not apply. **#318 stays open** — the kings of Israel and Judah are the natural next slice and already have a Thiele-based asset of their own, though #292's source question gates it.
- **The flood is dated twice and the parser could not read the second verse — #318 phase 7, `cf914ff`, not deployed.** The chart placed the flood from **Genesis 7:6** ("Noah was six hundred years old") and never mentioned **Genesis 7:11**, which dates the same day to "the six hundredth year of his life" — an ordinal, 599 complete. The two are **one year apart**, and because Shem's birth is counted off the flood (`flood + 2 - shem.begatAt`) the choice moves **every year on the axis after AM 1656 / 2242**. The chart already makes that exact subtraction at 1 Kings 6:1 (480th year → 479 elapsed) and states it; at the flood it made the opposite choice silently. **The reason it was silent is that the parser returned 106.** Phase 6's `EN_ORDINALS` listed `"hundredth": 100` as a plain word, so `_value` **added** it: `en_ordinal_runs("six hundredth year")` = 6 + 100. It was **unreachable** — the ordinal reader's only address was 1 Kings 6:1, which spells the multiplier the ordinary way ("four hundred **and** eightieth") — so nothing was wrong on screen and no test could see it. `"hundredth"` is now a multiplier alongside `"hundred"` (and still 100 standing alone); 1 Kings 6:1 still reads 480 at index 0, verified. The Greek never had it: `εξακοσιοστω` is one token. **No figure on the chart moved** — the asset diff is 21 insertions and 1 deletion. What ships is a *measured* disclosure: the generator now reads both verses, `SystemExit`s if they stop naming the same year of Noah's life, and emits a per-tradition note carrying **both** AM years (1656/1655 and 2242/2241) in all three locales. Verified the guard fires by monkeypatching the additive `hundredth` back in — it reports "states 600 and ... the 106th year" and refuses to write the asset, which is why the parser fix needs no Dart test: **the shipped asset is the evidence.** **The disclosure was deliberately routed to a path the page actually calls.** `ChronologyEpoch.noteFor` (`lib/models/chronology.dart:161`) is **never called anywhere in `lib/`** — all five epochs carry a paragraph of sourcing no reader has ever seen, and the flood's was **wrong** ("puts the flood in Noah's 600th year", 7:11's wording on 7:6's verse; the Chinese beside it was right). English corrected; **rendering the five is a feature and is left open**, because the header is an unconstrained `Column` above an `Expanded` chart. So the note is a `ChronologyNote(personId: 'noah')`, pinned by a **widget** test that taps Noah, not an asset test. All three new tests were run against the pre-change asset and **all three fail there**. Also fixed `chronologyEraNoNumber`'s Dart fallback, which still carried the overclaimed wording `ui_strings.dart` had already replaced — and measuring that found **12 more `_s(key, fallback)` fallbacks across 7 files that disagree with the shipped English**, two of which would print "Traditional"/"Simplified" where the shipped strings say 繁體中文/简体中文. Dead while every key has every locale; worth a ratchet ticket. **3,464 tests green** (3,461 + 3), analyze exit 0. **Not deployed on purpose:** #318 is a feature request rather than a reported defect, and the one false sentence corrected was never rendered, so no reader has ever seen it — undeployed loop iteration **2 of 3**.

- **The five notes nothing rendered, and the English underneath them — #318 phase 8, `cf23818`, dev v1.6.174.** `ChronologyEpoch.noteFor` had **no call site in `lib/`**: five paragraphs saying how each verse yields each date, written, translated into three scripts, shipped in the asset, unreachable. Each dated event in the header is now a link opening a sheet — a sheet at every width, not the side panel, because that panel is keyed on `_selectedId` to draw one man's band across the rows and an epoch has no band. **Then the reason the notes were worth reaching turned out to be the defect.** Every citation this page printed was the **stored** English — the form `parseReference` needs on the way back, not the form a reader reads — so a Chinese reader got 创世纪 in the prose of a note and `Genesis 5:25` in the citation directly under it. **Grepping for `Genesis` would have found one member of a class.** Sweeping the rendered text for any Latin run also found `unitNote`: the Anno Mundi paragraph carrying the Ussher caveat — the sentence that stops the axis being read as a BC dating — printed in English on the header and on every epoch sheet. Sweeping the **asset** instead of the screen found three more, in prose where no localiser can reach: `Deuteronomy 31:2` in the moses_death note and `Exodus 6:18`/`6:20` in the levi_ceiling note, on a panel behind a tap on Moses, which the rendered sweep does not visit unless told to. Those are built by `cite_zh` now, which **raises `SystemExit`** on a book it has no Chinese name for rather than falling back to English silently — the fallback is the defect, and it fails quiet. **And the asset disagreed with the app about the book's name**: 创世记 in 30 places of prose while the shipped Chinese corpora file all **1,533** of Genesis's verses under 创世纪, which is what every chip, header and search result says. The generator copies `book_name_mapping.dart` now. Three guards, each blind to what the others see, and each counts what it saw first because a sweep over nothing passes. **Seven assertions fail against `428f7d0`** — checked in a worktree, not assumed. Verified on the deployed build in zh-Hans, not inferred: 洪水's sheet and 摩西去世's sheet both photographed. **Two findings measured and left**: `wheel_history.json`'s **111** references print raw English in the World History Wheel (`_refRow`, the note rows, and the canvas ring label), and `traditionsNote`/`secondWitness` are parsed off the chronology asset with no render site — the same shape as the epoch notes.

- **The trust story shipped in the asset and nothing rendered a word of it — #318 phase 9, `0c82173`, not deployed.** The queue named two unrendered keys; there were **six, plus two counts**. `traditionsNote`, `secondWitness`, `traditionAgreement`, `checks.note`, `sumsChecked`, `disagreements` and both `derivedFrom` sentences had **no call site in `lib/`** — which editions the figures were read out of, what "checked" means, how far the two texts diverge, that a separately built artefact agrees. A chart of dates is the most persuasive thing this app draws and the answer to *how do you know* was in a file the reader cannot open. It is now one **"How this chart was made"** modal sheet off the header — a sheet at every width, deliberately not the side `_DetailPanel`, which is keyed on `_selectedId` to draw one man's band across the rows and would silently drop whoever the reader was comparing. **And writing them out for a reader is what caught three untrue sentences, because nothing had ever read them at all.** (1) **The second witness skipped five of its own rows in silence.** This chart spells men as the Authorised Version does — Enos, Cainan, Mahalaleel, Salah, Nahor — and `family_tree.json` as modern versions do — Enosh, Kenan, Mahalalel, Shelah, `nahor_elder` — so five lookups missed and were dropped *before any comparison happened*. All five agree, so no number on the chart was ever wrong; what was wrong is the sentence's closing promise, **"a disagreement stops this chart being built", false for a fifth of the men it covered**. The aliases are written out one by one and never guessed from the spelling, because the tree holds **both** Nahors and a prefix match would have compared Terah's father with Abram's brother and reported an agreement either way; an unjoined id now raises `SystemExit`. **23 of 23 → 28 of 28.** (2) **The 23 were never one comparison.** The sentence called them "Anno Mundi birth years"; the tree dates the patriarchs BC, so **9 of them are intervals** — a lifespan, or a father-to-son gap — which test the arithmetic and *not the anchor*. The kinds are counted separately and named: **19 birth years, 6 lifespans, 3 gaps**. (3) **"The same begetting age and lifespan" claimed a figure three men do not have.** Joseph, Aaron and Moses state no begetting age in either text, so the sentence reported an agreement of **two absences** as two statements; it says "the same figures" now. The group tally also covered **23 of 25** rows, having been written before Moses and Aaron joined the chart — *Exodus to Deuteronomy* is a third group and a guard fails the build unless every comparable row is in exactly one. **Two conservative calls, stated rather than assumed:** the reader is given the **edition** each figure was read out of and never our asset path (a path cannot be translated and names a file they cannot open), and the disagreement **list** is never rendered, only its count — a non-empty one can no longer ship, and printing it would put latent English on a Chinese page. `"24 figures"` now says which unit it counts: twelve men read twice. **The refuter earned its keep**: it confirmed all four numbers I was about to ship and then found the join hole underneath them, which is the second time on this ticket that a claim was arithmetically true and structurally weaker than it read. **Verified**: analyze exit 0, suite **3,476 green** (was 3,469), both after the final edit; asset diff is `_meta` only — 36 insertions, 6 deletions, no patriarch, epoch or era record moved. **Photographed on a local release build at 1400 px, English**, the whole sheet scrolled end to end: *28 of 28 … 19 Anno Mundi birth years, 6 lifespans … 3 father-to-son gaps*, *Disagreements: 0*, *the same figures for 4 of 19 men … Exodus to Deuteronomy: 2 of 2*, and the Ussher caveat in muted chrome at the foot. **Not deployed, as a stated decision:** undeployed loop iteration **1 of 3**, and although #318 is owner-reported, every sentence corrected here was **unreachable in the shipped build** — no reader has ever seen one of them, so shipping today buys nothing a batch deploy will not. **The measured next slice, and it is the same defect one module over:** `lib/pages/radial_chronology_page.dart` has **zero** calls to `localizedReferenceLabel` and prints `n.ref`/`e.refs` raw, so all **111** distinct references in `wheel_history.json` reach a Chinese reader in English. Also worth recording: every accessor on `lib/models/chronology.dart` was swept for a call site outside the model and **all of them now have one** — a first draft of the state row guessed the era's gaps and divergence were still unrendered and was wrong, which is the phase's own lesson applied to itself.

- **The wheel's labels were a constant box, so almost nothing was drawn whole — #318 phase 11, `13dc802`, not deployed.** Every rim label on the chronology wheel was allocated a **fixed** radial box — `span*0.36` for scripture-dated events, `span*0.40` for conventional ones, about 40 px on a 700 px canvas — regardless of what it said, then cut down **two characters at a time** until it fitted. Measured in the shipped faces over the real decluttered corpus at 900 px and rest: **not one of the 55 English labels was drawn whole, and 46 of the 55 Chinese ones were ellipsised**. The Chinese case breaks standing rule **#297** outright — a reader got `莫斯…` for *Moscow Council Restores the Patriarchate* and `奧斯…` for 奧斯曼境內基督徒遭驅逐與殺害, and 莫斯 is not an abbreviation of 莫斯科, it is a different word. The two-character step could also overshoot the best fit by one, which is fatal where every character is a morpheme. **Nothing in the suite could see any of it**, and that is the structural point: these labels are painted with `TextPainter` on a canvas, so there is no `RichText` and no semantics node, and `find.text` cannot reach them — the page's own widget test says so in its header and was right. So the **decision** moved out of the painter into **`planRadialSpokes`**, which returns the resolved strings a test can read; the painter now only draws what it is handed. A label is **legible or absent**: Chinese whole or nothing, Latin falling back to whole **words** with an ellipsis and never mid-word, and the **tick is always drawn on the band**, so no event disappears from the wheel or becomes untappable. Before → after, same decluttered set, real faces: **900 px rest en 0 of 55 whole → 31 whole + 24 word-cut**; **900 px rest zh 9 whole / 46 cut → 55 of 55 whole, 0 ellipsised**; 700 px rest en 0 of 48 → 6 + 42; 700 px rest zh 3 of 48 → 38 whole, 10 tick-only; 900 px 400% en 18 of 92 → **92 of 92**; and **the verse on the label 0 of 4 → 3 of 4**. That last row is the parity gain — `_radialLabel`'s own comment promised *“the reference IS the evidence… carried right on the label”* and it essentially never fired, while BibleWorks' Timeline (`bwh39`, the only chronology feature in the help) puts the reference **on** the event and lets you jump from it. **The one genuinely debatable call, stated rather than buried:** the two-zone “scripture baseline” was costing roughly two thirds of the wheel's words, and it is explained **nowhere on screen** — no legend entry — while the basis is disclosed properly in words on every detail sheet (`radial_chronology_page.dart:969`). Counting it settled the question: only **5 of the asset's 491 events** are scripture-dated at all (`scripture+thiele`, all between -980 and -609), and the declutter keeps **exactly one** of them visible at rest *and* at 400% — a whole layout zone serving one label. The distinction is **kept** but re-encoded by **anchoring** instead of zoning: scripture flush against the bands growing outward, conventional flush against the rim growing inward, both using the whole annulus. They cannot collide, and the proof is in the doc comment — the declutter already holds any two spokes `minGap` apart and `minGap = lineHeight / rBands`, so arc separation at any `r >= rBands` is at least a line. The 0.5 px hairline that marked the old boundary went with the zone it marked. **Also measured and recorded: `stackRadialLabels` is provably unreachable.** It is presented in three comments as this wheel's headline mechanism, but the declutter keeps consecutive events `>= minGap` apart while the stacker only stacks within `minGap/2`. It is retained, still called, and a test now pins that it never fires. **Verified**: analyze exit 0 and **3,500 tests green** (3,484 + 16), both run after the final edit. `test/wheel_label_legibility_test.dart` loads the real `Roboto-VariableFont` and `NotoSansSC-Sub.otf` faces — `flutter test` renders a fixed-width stand-in otherwise, and a width test without `FontLoader` compares a model only to itself — and sweeps 3 locales × 2 canvas sizes × 3 zooms. **The guard could not be run against the parent commit**, because the API it tests is new; the before column was instead measured by simulating the old constant-box algorithm over the **same** decluttered set, which is why the numbers above are exact rather than indicative. **What is NOT verified: how the glyphs actually look.** An offscreen render of the pumped page confirmed the *geometry* — labels flush at the rim, evenly spaced, no collisions, and the chips crossing the rim are `_paintCenturies` at `rRim+11/22`, pre-existing chrome, not spoke labels — but `flutter test` substitutes a stand-in face, so the type in that picture is tofu and the aesthetic judgement waits for a deploy. **Not deployed on purpose:** undeployed loop iteration **1 of 3**; dev still serves 1.6.175 from `2c4c1fc`, which predates this work, so photographing it would have shown the *old* wheel. **The next slice on this page is the band names**, still `TextPainter` and still unread by any test.
- **A clamp's floor held every name on the wheel at one size, and printed it across the next stream — #318 phase 12, `cba8f36`, not deployed.** Phase 11 signed off predicting the band names were next. **They were not, and saying so is most of this iteration's value.** The arc labels — the power names set along their own bands — were sized by `.clamp(6.0, 10.0)`, and it was the **floor** that bound: the geometric cap at a 700 px canvas is 3.12, and the clamp raised it back to 6. Measured over the shipped asset in the shipped faces, **every arc label on the chart was set at exactly 6.00 canvas units, at every canvas size, every locale and every zoom.** Three consequences follow and all three were measured before a line changed. The size is in **canvas** units and `InteractiveViewer` multiplies them, so at 800% zoom an arc label was drawn at **48 px** beside rim labels at 10.5. The drawn set **never grew with zoom** — `[22, 22, 22, 22]` from 100% to 800% — because a constant size cannot admit a name that did not already fit, which quietly contradicts the page's own stated rule that type holds its on-screen size *while more of it fits*. And at 700 px the ring pitch is 5.41 units while **all 22 English and all 34 Chinese drawn labels carried 5.88–5.94 units of ink**, so every single one printed over the neighbouring stream. `fitArcLabel` replaces the clamp: the floor is read in **screen** units so zoom cannot inflate it, the em box is capped at 0.9 of the ring pitch, and the size is found by **re-measuring** rather than back-solving. The back-solve is the interesting failure — `size * room / width` assumes width is proportional to size, glyph advances are hinted and quantised, and it overran *Old Assyrian Period*'s arc at 700 px. The new test caught that, not inspection. After: 900 px at rest draws the same 26 en / 39 zh names a shade larger; zoomed, the set grows (26→44→55→60 en, 39→52→59→61 zh) and on-screen size is bounded at 25.67/27.51 px at 800% instead of 48; 700 px at rest draws **none**, with labels returning from ~123% — which is exactly the case where the overprint was real, so the honest outcome of fixing an overprint is fewer labels, not more. **A second, independent defect** surfaced on the way: `_handleTap` selects a **stream** id when the reader taps a band, but both painters compared only power and event ids, so tapping a band greyed out the whole wheel *including the band tapped*. `selectionCovers` fixes both call sites and is swept over every stream's powers and events. **The band names were the suspect and came back sound** — 0 ink collisions over 21 adjacent pairs at three canvas sides in both locales, worst clearance 0.91 units — so they are left byte-for-byte as they were, and `planBandNames`, `bandNameSize`, `bandNameRoom` and `BandNamePlan` were **deleted after being written**. That reversal is the lesson worth keeping: **the line box is not the ink.** A `TextPainter`'s `.height` carries leading and quantises to whole pixels — 1.17 em Latin, 1.37 em Han, against real ink of 0.98 and 0.99 — and a line-box instrument therefore reported collisions that do not exist. The real answer needed rendering to a `Picture`, reading it back as raw RGBA at 16× supersampling, and scanning the alpha channel for the first and last row with any coverage. I had the overlap claim **wrong twice** before that (once from an inverted predicate, once from the wrong instrument), which is why the shipped fix is one ring smaller than the one I set out to write. `test/wheel_arc_label_behaviour_test.dart` is 18 tests over the real `Roboto` and `NotoSansSC-Sub`; the page's constants are mirrored as literals on purpose, since a test that imported them would follow a typo into agreement. All four load-bearing assertions were run against `819d13a` and **fail there**: `48.0` against a `<31` bound, `[22, 22, 22, 22]`, `22 of 22 drawn labels cross a 5.41 pitch`, and `selecting band israel left israel-united-monarchy dimmed`. **Stated rather than buried:** the floor stays at **6 px**, not #315's written chrome floor of **11**. At 11 nothing on this chart is named until roughly 200% zoom. The phase-11 precedent (`planRadialSpokes`) applies no px floor at all, so there is no rule to follow here, and no honest answer is available from the code — this pass keeps the number that already ships and the constant's doc comment says why the question is open. **Not deployed on purpose:** undeployed loop iteration **1 of 3**. The counter is 1 and not 2 because the owner's own **1.6.176** was cut from a tree that already contained phase 11, so phase 11 is live after all — verified by reading `1.6.177` out of the bundle at `seeksparks-dev.netlify.app`, not inferred from the `chore:` commit. Two further reasons: `release_web.sh` builds from the **working tree**, and the owner committed and deployed from this same tree three times in the half hour before this commit, so a loop deploy would land inside his cadence and on top of anything he has uncommitted. His `ed1208c` and `b5e4ad8` arrived mid-iteration; `main` was **rebased** onto them and `flutter analyze` and the full suite were re-run **after** the rebase rather than only before it — 3,538 green, exit 0. **Still unphotographed:** the wheel's type has never been seen on a deployed build, and `flutter test` substitutes a fixed-width stand-in face in which every glyph is a square, so how the glyphs *look* remains unverified even though their sizes and ink extents are now measured in the shipped faces.
- **The ledger cited one verse for a claim the Greek makes in two — #318 phase 13, `1f0b385`, not deployed (undeployed iteration 2 of 3).** The iteration began as a plan to extend the AM axis to the temple's founding; the mandated refuter killed it and the refutation exposed an accuracy defect in what already ships. The exodus→temple ledger cites **1 Kings 6:1** for the total each text states. Our verse keys are the **Hebrew's** numbering, so our single record at that address holds **five** of the Septuagint's own verses: its `6:1` gives the 440th year, dates it to Solomon's fourth year and the second month, and then **says nothing about what was done in it**; the founding is in its `6:1c` (`εθεμελιωσεν τον οικον κυριου`). A reader who followed our own citation into the Greek met a date and no temple, with no way to tell whether the missing half was absent from the text or supplied by us — the number was right and the reference was half of one. The generator now *reads* which unit states the year and which states the founding by splitting on the `<vs:>` markers the asset already carries and the reading text already prints as `(6:1c)`; the search key is the **finite verb**, because `6:1a` speaks of stones hewn `εις τον θεμελιον του οικου` and states no founding, so the noun would have named the wrong unit. Build stops if either key stops matching. The Greek now carries an `era_join` note and the row printing the figure names the second unit; the Hebrew carries neither, because `units: 1` and the app must not report a split that text does not have. **Do not re-derive the dead end:** extending the axis to the temple as one span would assert that the 530 (MT) / 520 (LXX) counted interior years fit inside 479 / 439 — the overrun *is* the finding, which is why the era is a ledger and not a bar. Suite 3,544 green (+6), `flutter analyze` exit 0. **The before/after is weaker than usual and the weakness is stated rather than papered over:** the change is additive to the model, so the new tests do not *compile* against `e9e2b86` and cannot be run there. What was measured instead is that `git show e9e2b86:assets/chronology.json` carries none of `yearAt` / `foundingAt` / `units` and no `era_join` note, and `git show e9e2b86:lib/pages/chronology_page.dart` has zero occurrences of `chronologyEraFoundingAt` — so every new assertion is about something that did not exist one commit ago.

- **#322 is closed, and it closed on the one kind of evidence the ticket had never been given: somebody looked at it.** The complaint was that Browse's three render paths printed the reference three different ways; `72a618f` unified them and `440084e` fixed the width they were measured with, and after both the row still said *"proof-by-measurement is not visual sign-off"*. **The obstacle to the sign-off was reaching the case the measurements say is worst.** `version_gutter.dart` documents the CJK margin as `+1.34%…+3.90%` against English's `+4.19%…+19.57%` — three times thinner, and structurally so, since a Han glyph is charged exactly 1.0 em and a Chinese reference's entire slack is its ` 1:1` tail. Reaching it looked like it needed the UI in Chinese, and seeding `flutter.locale` does not switch it (a ProfileService-scoped `userPrefs` blob is applied *over* the per-key reads at `app_settings.dart:776`). **It does not need the UI in Chinese at all**: `browse_window.dart:576` builds the reference through `localeAwareBookName(book, locale, versionCodes.first)`, and `bookScriptFor` prefers the VERSION over the locale — so a `雅繁+`-first stack prints a Traditional reference under an English browser. Photographed on the deployed build at 1400×900 and the default 20 pt: **帖撒羅尼迦後書 1:1**, seven Han characters and the longest book name in the canon, across 雅繁+ · BSB · NASB · KJV · BGT — five rows, every reference ending on the same x with no ellipsis, every verse text AND every wrapped line starting on the same x. Then **耶利米哀歌 1:1** on Lamentations 1, which puts the thinnest margin and the RTL case on one screen: 雅繁+ · BSB · NASB · KJV · **WTT**, the Hebrew right-aligned inside its own cell with its reference still at the left, outside the `Directionality` scope. All four of the ticket's requirements, on screen. **Neither fix had a test, and a widget test cannot give them one** — `FetchVerses.loadVerseList` does not resolve under `flutter test`, so `browse_window_tagged_test.dart` sits on its spinner and no verse text is ever laid out, which is why two fixes to a layout defect shipped with only a smoke test behind them. What made the defect possible is a property of the SOURCE — three call sites where there should be one — so `browse_render_paths_ratchet_test.dart` (7 tests) reads the source and says so in its header. It pins: exactly ONE `BrowseVerseRow` construction for all three paths; the reference never interpolated or spanned into the verse line (the pre-`72a618f` shape, where it was the first span of a `Text.rich` and so took part in line-breaking); each fixed column measured exactly once; `kBrowseReferenceLetterSpacing` named on BOTH the measure and the paint side with **no numeric `letterSpacing:` literal anywhere in code**; the superscription indented by the same `referenceWidth` variable; and the single row built by `_RowView` above every `TextDirection.rtl`. **Sized against the commits before each fix rather than asserted, per the standing rule** — at `72a618f^` **5 of 7 fail**, at `440084e^` **exactly 2**, and the 2 are the identity guard and the measure/paint pair, so each assertion catches the fix that names it. **A false alarm from the previous session was chased down and withdrawn rather than filed.** A "fresh" Chrome profile reporting `navigator.language = en-GB` had booted into a Simplified Chinese UI with `flutter.locale = "zh-Hans"`, and the working hypothesis was a race: `AppSettings`'s field default is `_locale = 'zh-Hans'`, `notifyListeners()` schedules a 600 ms debounced `_writeUserPrefsBlob()`, and a blob of uninitialised defaults written before `loadSettings()` reaches its blob read would be applied straight back over the detected locale. **Reading the code refutes it**: no `notifyListeners()` can fire on `AppSettings` before line 776 — `main.dart:228` awaits `loadSettings()` before anything touches a setter, and the only earlier call is the `SharedPreferences` failure path, which returns before any blob could be written. Only `AppSettings` writes that key (grepped tree-wide). The observation's own evidence contradicts itself, too: the blob's `userPrefsTimestamp` was **5.2 hours older than the profile directory it was found in**, and `DateTime.now()` cannot produce that — so the profile was not pristine and the reading is contaminated, not a defect. **The deploy is the other thing that changed here.** The counter was at 3 of 3 and #322 is owner-reported, so two triggers were met; but this row has warned for three iterations that `release_web.sh` builds from the **working tree** and a second writer shares it. The build was cut from `git worktree add --detach /tmp/ss-deploy f242200`. **This entry then claimed two things about that deploy which are false, and the correction is the more useful half of it.** It said this was the first deploy here to close the working-tree hazard: it was not — check 45g was deployed from a detached worktree at `c49eb05`, and `PROJECT_STATE.md` has recorded that since 2026-08-24. And it said a worktree ships a bundle with **no NASB**, so the licensed data must be copied in by hand. **A worktree ships exactly what the working tree ships.** The NASB a reader sees is `assets/nasb.json`, which is **tracked** and listed at `pubspec.yaml:85`, so it is in any checkout of the pushed SHA; the three `.gitignore`d paths (`assets/nasb-ev.json`, `assets/nsn-plus.json`, `assets/tagged/nsn-plus/`) appear in **no** `pubspec.yaml` asset list and have no reader anywhere in `lib/` — they are inputs to `scripts/`, and `DELETION-REVIEW.md:93` records that their being unbundled is deliberate and must stay that way. So copying them in was not a safeguard; it pushed licensed data toward a deploy tree, against the standing rule. Probed rather than assumed: on the deployed site `assets/assets/nasb.json` is 7,215,432 bytes while both licensed paths return the SPA fallback, byte-identical (20,286) to what a nonsense path returns — nothing licensed shipped. **Where the error came from is the part worth keeping: a loop memory asserting that four gitignored paths are "paths the app loads". A memory that names an asset is a claim about `pubspec.yaml`, and one grep of `pubspec.yaml` would have killed it before it reached three documents and a pushed commit message.** Since the belief argued for moving licensed data **toward** a build, the invariant it misread is now a test instead of a paragraph: `data_integrity_test.dart` pins that the three licensed paths appear in no `pubspec.yaml` asset list and stay gitignored, and that `assets/nasb.json` stays listed and on disk — so a future "cleanup" cannot take the reader's NASB away while the guard still passes. Verified on the immutable deploy URL rather than the dev alias: `main.dart.js` carries `1.6.178` ten times and `1.6.177` zero times, and Lamentations 1 renders NASB · BSB · KJV with the column straight. **Verified**: `flutter analyze` exit 0, suite **3,551 green** (+7), both after the final edit; the differential against both pre-fix commits; the deployed bundle's version and its reading pane. **NOT verified**: nothing was screenshotted at the ends of the Font Size slider — the sign-off is at the default 20 pt, and the width model is a *minimum*, so a reader on a font wider than the model gets a ragged column rather than a clipped reference by design. #278, #292, #293, #296 and #309 remain blocked on the owner.

- **A clamp that travels can still be dead, and a number that fits can still be cut — #315 CLOSED, `6d9ced0`, deployed dev v1.6.179.** Two mechanisms, taking the ticket's documented count from nine to ten. The **tenth** is a clamp the detector passed *because it moves*: `(fontSize - 2).clamp(12, 20)` renders 18 at 20 pt and 20 at 40 pt, so the ceiling rule — which asked whether the whole range is constant — cleared it, while the reader gets an identical size at **19 of the slider's 29 stops**. The rule is now strictly stronger (dead if the top two stops agree), and measuring the tree rather than guessing found **exactly four** sites: three text, one `vertPadding` that was a false positive of the heuristic. That one was **excluded rather than budgeted** — the heuristic now asks what the expression *binds to* before asking whether it is a size — because a real defect filed next to a non-defect stops both being read. The **second mechanism is not a size at all, and no source ratchet can ever see it, because the source is correct.** Both chapter grids grow the label with the font and leave the box fixed, so past a threshold the label does not shrink — it **CLIPS**, and a clipped number is a *plausible wrong number*: Psalm 119's `176` reads `17`, chapter `150` reads `15`. Catching it needed a fourth instrument beside the three this ticket already had: lay the label out unconstrained with a `TextPainter` **in the style read back off the pumped `RichText`**, and compare against the width the tile actually granted. INTRINSIC > GRANTED is a clip whatever the style says — and it is the one instrument that can tell clipping from shrinking, since a `FittedBox` lies to the style-reading probe. The two views needed different repairs because they have different freedoms: the grid can **trade a column** (`columnsThatFit`), the `Wrap` of fixed squares cannot, so its **box has to grow** (`math.max` against the breakpoint). **One conversion broke this ticket's own rule and it is worth knowing:** every other repair here was invisible at the 20 pt default by construction. This one is not. Run against `300dcfd`, `150` wanted **54.8 px in a 52.0 px tile at the shipped default** — it has been clipping for every reader since it shipped, and the repair is a visible change at rest. The 2.8 px magnitude is instrument-dependent (the test registers Roboto; the app ships `-apple-system`); the **sign** is not. Also cleared three strings that stated something untrue, with the blast radius measured instead of fixing the one I noticed: the offline note still promised a Google-Fonts download that has been bundled since v1.6.73 **and** a cloud sign-in that no longer exists, and the profile notice described a Google photo overriding the local one — 2 rendered strings, 3 claims, 1 dead key, 2 `??` fallbacks repeating them, 1 stale source comment. `cloudSignIn` was **deleted, not corrected**, because there is nothing to correct it *to*. #315 closes on **10 documented non-defects** rather than on zero. 6 of 8 new assertions fail against `300dcfd`. **Two things for whoever is next:** the owner's `17d6a6d` landed mid-iteration and says `main` had been red on CI for 6 runs across 4 loop iterations that all reported themselves green — the loop's macOS `flutter test` cannot see a Linux text-metrics gap, so read `gh run list`, not your own terminal. And the list and grid chapter tiles now size the same label differently; that is a design question, left for the owner.

- **The app gave a reader two years for the division of the kingdom — #318 phase 14, `576b3be`, not deployed (undeployed iteration 2 of 3).** The iteration began as a plan to draw `bible_timeline.json`'s 89 events on the wheel; the mandated refuter killed it and the refutation indicted what already ships, which is now the **second consecutive** time on this ticket. **The dead end first, so nobody re-derives it:** the wheel's declutter is greedy and first-past-the-post in **year order** (`radial_chronology_page.dart:685-691`), so injecting the timeline would have drawn **0 of its 23 NT events at any zoom** — six of them share the year 33 and the entire NT spans roughly one `minGap` — while **evicting 17 events the wheel already draws**. The feature would have shipped the exact absence it existed to remove. More data is not the fix; a different declutter is. **What the refutation exposed underneath: the division of the kingdom is stated eight times across three assets, five times as 931 BC and three times as 930.** `hebrew_kings.json` says 931 at `epochs/division`, `solomon/reignEnd`, `jeroboam_i/reignStart` and `rehoboam/reignStart`; `bible_timeline.json` says 931 at `kingdom_divided`; `wheel_history.json` says **930** at `israel-united-monarchy/end`, `kingdom-of-israel/start` and `kingdom-of-judah/start`. A reader who opened Resources → Bible Chronology and then Resources → World History Wheel was handed two different years for one event by one app. **This needed no chronologist, and that is the whole reason it could be fixed unattended.** All three wheel records carry `basis: "scripture+thiele"`, which `wheel_history.json`'s **own** `_meta.basisValues` defines as *"scripture states the interval; the absolute year follows Thiele's reconstruction"*, and the page prints that to the reader in words as *"interval from scripture, year from Thiele"*. `hebrew_kings.json` **is** this app's Thiele chart — `"system": "thiele"` at its root — and it says 931. So the label was false **by the file's own definition**, not by any outside authority's, which is why the fix does not wait on **#292** (a citable Thiele source, still open). **Nothing here decides between 931 and 930**; that is #292's question, and a disagreement is a defect whichever side is right. **It reads as a slip and not an editorial choice, and that was checked rather than assumed:** the same three records take Thiele's number every other time — 722 for Samaria, 586 for Jerusalem — `kingdom-of-israel`'s own note discloses *"931 or 930"* and `kingdom-of-judah`'s discloses *"586 … some reckon 587"* while two of the three disclose nothing at all about the division, and `git log -S'"end": -930'` returns exactly one commit, the bulk authoring one. **Corpus measured before the report, per the standing rule:** **31** Thiele-claiming records live outside `hebrew_kings.json` (23 in `bible_timeline.json`, 8 in `wheel_history.json`); every one but these three either agrees or states nothing the chart can contradict, `family_tree.json` agrees on all **14** shared reigns, `chronology.json` is Anno-Mundi-only, and no wheel *event* states the division — so **8 is the complete set and 3 is the whole defect**. **The structural finding is why it survived: none of the three assets shares an id with another, so there was no key to join on and no guard could have been written by accident.** `test/cross_asset_year_agreement_test.dart` writes the join out **by hand** — six facts, 23 statements, all three assets — and that hand-written join is the thing most likely to rot, so its **first** test resolves every path and fails on any that no longer lands, with a floor on the statement count; a renamed record must break the file loudly rather than quietly shrink it to nothing. It carries the Roman destruction of the temple as well as the five monarchy facts, so the instrument is not silently a one-era one. Run against the unfixed assets it fails on **exactly** this fact, naming both years and all eight paths, and clears the other five. **A guard already existed and it swept the wrong carrier.** `wheel_history_asset_test.dart`'s Thiele check opened with the promise that these datasets *"cannot be allowed to drift apart in silence"* and then iterated `raw['events']` only — the `powers` list holding all three offending records was never read, and its ±60 years of slack are sixty times the size of an error that was one year. It now sweeps both carriers and floors on each, so an emptied one cannot pass. **Recorded honestly in check 49: that extension would NOT have caught this defect** — 930 sits well inside ±60 — it closes a carrier gap, not this gap, and the two tests are kept side by side because a range check and an agreement check see different things. **Reported and deliberately not fixed, the conservative call stated rather than taken:** `israel-united-monarchy`'s **−1050** start also claims `scripture+thiele` and is defensible as −1010 (Thiele's David) minus the forty years Acts 13:21 gives Saul — I had it queued as an over-claim, withdrew it before shipping when the arithmetic turned out to be exactly what the label means, and the second refuter independently agreed the downgrade would be a regression, since it would print *"conventional date · not stated in scripture"* two lines under 1 Kings 12:19-20, verbatim the falsehood v1.6.175 shipped to remove. `bible_timeline.json` nonetheless calls that same year `conventional`, so the two files label one year two ways even though they agree on it — a smaller version of this same defect, left for a human. Also noted: `hiram_tyre` at −980 falls inside David's reign while its description is about Solomon's temple. **Verified**: `flutter analyze` exit 0 and the suite **3,562 green** (+2), both run after the final edit; the new guard run **red against the unfixed assets** before the data moved; `wheel_history_disclosure_test.dart` still pins the non-conventional power id set and `conventional == 59` unchanged, because **no `basis` value was touched** — only three years. **Not deployed, as a stated decision:** the counter stood at **1 of 3**, the owner has not asked, the window is not closing, and this defect was found by the loop rather than reported by a human, so no trigger was met; it is three integers with no code path, no new string and no visual surface, so there is nothing a deploy would let anyone see. dev still serves **v1.6.179**. **For whoever is next:** the wheel's declutter is the real blocker on this page — it is the reason the NT cannot be drawn, and it is a pure function, so it can be replaced and measured without touching the painter.
- **The wheel drew 55 of 491 events and printed 491 two inches away — #318 phase 15, `d23f1e9`, not deployed (this is undeployed iteration **2** of 3; the previous line's "2 of 3" was written for the iteration that was actually the 1st, and `PROJECT_STATE.md`'s prose — "the counter stood at 1 of 3" — is the one that matches git, since only phase 14 sits between the v1.6.179 deploy and now).** Phase 14 predicted the fix would be "a different declutter, not more data", and phase 15 measured how bad the existing one was before changing it: on a 900 px canvas the greedy year-ordered declutter draws **55 of 491 at rest and 136 at the `InteractiveViewer`'s ceiling of 14x**, so ~72% of the corpus was unreachable at any magnification the app permits, and `_handleTap` iterates the **drawn** spokes, so a dropped event had **no hit target at all** — not merely unlabelled, unreachable. One spoke stood for the **66 events of 1900-1957** and said only *Boxer Uprising Martyrdoms*. **Zoom is not a remedy on a linear axis** and this is the generalisable part: angle is a linear function of the year, so the **125 events sharing a year with another** (55 such years) are at identical angles at every zoom, forever. **The fix is the same rule read the other way:** `clusterByAngle` opens a cluster on exactly the old keep-test and records the losers instead of discarding them, so the representatives are the **identical events at the identical angles** — nothing on screen moves — and each survivor carries a muted `+n` badge and a tap listing every member in year order, each row opening its own sheet. Selection now *represents* its cluster rather than being drawn beside it, which **shrinks** the collision exception the layout proof rests on. **The badge's cost is recorded in both columns, not the flattering one:** it is reserved before the title and given up last (verse → title → badge), which costs English **nothing at all** (48/48 at 700 px, 55/55 at 900 px, because Latin already cuts at whole words) and costs Chinese, which #297 makes whole-or-nothing, **55→53 titles at 900 px and 38→22 at 700 px** — against which spokes saying *nothing at all* fell from **10 to 2**. The badge takes the **verse's** size for hierarchy, not for room: measured, shrinking it buys nothing, because Chinese titles are quantised in whole ideographs; a variant that recovered 7 titles did so only in combination with a one-space gap, which is a knife-edge on one corpus at one canvas size and was not taken. **The comment defending the old design was false three ways** — "half the corpus at 1x, all of it by 3x, nothing is lost" (really 11%/17%, ceiling 27.7%), "189 labels round 320°" (the corpus size **when the comment was written**; it is 491), and "tappable-adjacent". **Same defect found on a second surface and fixed:** a stream's sheet named every event on the stream and offered no way to open one; those rows are now tappable, while the **powers** above them are deliberately left alone because a power occupies a band and tapping the band already opens it. **Two instrument traps, each of which produced a wrong number first.** (1) *A lazy list does not put its children in the widget tree* — counting `InkWell` elements returned **22 for a list of 47**, and would have returned the same 22 for a genuinely truncated list, so the assertion could not tell the defect from the fix; a completeness claim about a scrollable must travel the list. (2) *And you cannot travel it by dragging* — `tester.drag` on a modal sheet moves nothing, because the modal's drag-to-dismiss recogniser takes the gesture and `position.pixels` stays at **0.0**, which is indistinguishable from a list that has bottomed out; `jumpTo` asks the question the test is actually asking. **A data finding, left alone on purpose:** keying harvested rows on displayed text also loses one row for a real reason — `nero_persecution` and `great_fire_rome` are **two records for one fire**, same year 64, identical title in all three locales, near-identical descriptions, differing only in `era`/`stream` (world/rome vs church/church). It is the **only** title+year collision in the 491 in either language. Which record should exist is an editorial call, the rows are already distinguishable on screen by their stream swatch, and no human was available — so the data was not touched and the test keys rows by **position**. Guards: `clusterByAngle` is now the single implementation of the keep-rule (the legibility test carried its own copy and could only ever agree with itself), no event is lost across 4 sizes × 5 zooms, the representative set is identical to the old declutter's kept set, a pinned event always represents its own cluster, no spoke standing for others is ever mute, and the page test taps its way onto clustered spokes and checks the header's number equals the rows a reader can reach. **Still unphotographed, and now more so:** every visible part of this change is canvas text on a page whose type has never been seen on a deployed build. Suite 3,574 green, `analyze` clean, both by exit code.

**2026-08-25 — phase 15, read back and corrected.** CI came back **green** on the pushed HEAD `366f2273` (run `32851128165`), read from `gh run view`, never inferred from the local macOS suite. Two corrections then followed, both to claims phase 15 itself had shipped. **(1) "English pays nothing at all" was false.** It counted labels DRAWN (48 of 48 at 700 px, 55 of 55 at 900 px, badge or no badge) and read that as labels drawn WHOLE. Re-measured badge-on against badge-off on the same canvas in the same faces, English uncut titles fall **31 → 22 at 900 px and 6 → 3 at 700 px**; English pays in words where Chinese pays in whole names, because Latin may cut at a word and Han may not (#297). At 1400 px the badge is free in both (79 of 79 either way), so the whole cost sits on the two smallest canvases the wheel is reachable on. The `fitRadialLabel` doc comment and the legibility test's baseline both said only the Chinese half and now say both. **(2) The green CI was not evidence that the margins were safe.** A sensitivity sweep — every measured width scaled by (1+e) to stand in for a rasterizer that disagrees — shows the English uncut count falling **22 → 21 → 19 at e = 0, 1%, 2%**, i.e. two counts of margin was about 1.5% of metric divergence, on the exact assertion class that kept `main` red for six runs (owner's `17d6a6d`, CoreText vs FreeType). That floor is now **15** (survives ≈10%); the Chinese floor holds 53 of 55 out to e=5% and was left alone. A regression back to constant boxes undercuts to 0, so the wider margin still fails loudly on the bug being guarded. Suite 3,574 green, `analyze` clean, both by exit code, run after the last edit. **Still undeployed and still unphotographed** — dev serves v1.6.179; the counter stands at 2 of 3, and the next iteration must deploy and must screenshot the wheel.

**2026-08-25 — #318 phase 16: the wheel had 657 records and no way to ask for one. Shipped, deployed, photographed, and the photograph found three more defects.** Phase 15 made every record reachable by tap; nothing made one findable. BibleWorks' Timeline (`bwh39`) has a **command line taking either a term or a date** — `-46` is 46 BC — and the date half is what a naive reading of "add search" drops, because half the questions a chronology gets are years. Both wildcards, not one (`bwh18`): `*` and **`?`**, the latter being how a reader asks for Nebuchadnezzar without knowing whether we spell it -nezzar or -rezzar. Designed against **false absence**, since a search that says "not found" about a record the app holds is the app stating something untrue: all four kinds (491 events, 62 powers, 82 nations, 22 streams = **657**), all three locales (an English query opens the Chinese record), descriptions, verse references, and a hidden band **marks** its results rather than swallowing them. A bare `586` is not guessable on an axis running 4000 BC – AD 2026, so both eras are searched and every row prints its own year. One deliberate departure from `bwh39`, recorded at `_panTo`: **a date does not scroll the wheel at rest**, because unlike that strip this is a disc with all of it already on screen — selection does the finding, by forcing the record through the declutter that draws 55 of 491 labels. **THE FEATURE FELL OVER AN ACCURACY DEFECT IN THREE FILES.** Round-tripping printed years through the parser meant reading `yearLabel`, which chose `主后` for every `zh` locale: 后 is a queen, 後 is "after", Simplified merged them and Traditional did not, so `startsWith('zh')` can never choose — **382 events, 27 powers and the hub** were showing Traditional readers a Simplified era word, and the refuter found the same slip in `timeline_event.dart` (75 of 98 events, **all 98 carrying Traditional titles**) and `biblical_person.dart` (plus `创世后` for Anno Mundi). All three now share `lib/utils/date_hedge.dart`. **The sweep that guards the shape had to be built three times**: v1 required flag and character on one line, which is not what any of the three defects looked like, so it ran clean over every defect it was written for; v2 followed the flag but read the line as one string, so the flag named `zh` matched inside the literal `'zh-Hant'` and it accused the one line in the repo that gets this right; v3 reads **the flag in code and the character in string literals** and feeds itself verbatim pre-fix source as a fixture. Two shipped tests had **enshrined** the defect (`person_dating_test` passed `zh-Hant` and expected 约). **DEPLOYED — counter 3 of 3 — as v1.6.180 (`4d39385`), and then the screenshot earned its place.** Driving the shipped build found three things 3,624 green tests could not: the result list took **526 px of a 630 px allowance with zero rows**, so one hit sat atop an empty half-page (a suite that reads only text cannot see a height — `shrinkWrap`, guarded by a size assertion run against the pre-fix source, where it fails 526 vs 315); **"1 results"**, invisible *by construction* because every widget test renders zh-Hans and Chinese does not inflect (fixed with a second key and a plain string-table `test`); and an English hint advertising `主前586`, a keyboard that reader does not have. Re-deployed as **v1.6.181 (`bfd1ccf`)** and re-verified on the immutable URL: `1 result` singular, the sheet tight around its content, and tapping a row closed the find sheet, opened the right detail sheet **and drew a "Magna Carta…" label at AD 1215 that was not on the wheel a moment before** — the declutter mechanism confirmed in the only place it is visible. Suite **3,626** green, `analyze` clean, both by exit code after the last edit.

**2026-08-25 — #318 phase 17: the wheel drew 491 events of world history and, on the two bands named for God's people, 18 records holding neither the Exodus nor David nor a verse of the New Testament. Six events sharing AD 33 is arithmetic (0.000927 rad/year against a 0.0553 rad label ≈ one name per 60 years), not a defect. **Rule B declined on measurement**: preferring the scripture-citing cluster member gains 3 biblical representatives and *renames 7 of 55 existing clusters*, so the declutter was left alone. Exactly **one** fact is told by both assets — timeline `temple_destroyed` vs wheel `jerusalem_destroyed`, AD 70, both `judah` — excluded by name. Pre-existing on the timeline, evidence and family-tree surfaces; my change would have put it on the wheel rim as well. Fixed at the formatter with `_extentKeptVerbatim` (localise the book, keep the reader's own tail, detect narrowing **on the digits alone** so `Jude 11`→`Jude 1:11` still goes through the ordinary path), 79 → 3, the 3 being correct one-chapter-book expansions. **The new tests were run against the pre-fix function via `git stash`**: exactly the 5 narrowing cases went red, Jude and `1 Cor 13` stayed green. Deployed and confirmed in both locales: `Genesis 6-9` and `创世纪 6-9`. `wheel_label_legibility_test` was **guarding the wrong corpus** — it read `assets/wheel_history.json` directly and was blind to 97 of the labels it protects; it now reads through the service. Suite **3,649** green, `analyze` clean, both by exit code after the last edit; CI green on `4006bde` and `9c930fd` read from `gh run list`. **Deployed at counter 1 of 3, deliberately**: the counter exists to avoid churn for changes nobody can see (phases 14 and 15 were integers and a pure function), and this alters what six shipped surfaces print and adds 97 spokes to the featured page. **The photograph paid again, and its finding is the natural phase 18**: `_paintCenturies` draws its 500-year labels `center: true` at `rRim + 11/22`, knowing nothing of `planRadialSpokes`, so a label whose year is near a tick **mashes glyph-for-glyph into it** — photographed as `主前的轮式车辆500` (「最早的轮式车辆」over「主前3500」). It is **pre-existing, not a phase-17 regression**, and the proof is that the photographed instance is a *wheel-native* event: **25 wheel-native** records sit within 8 years of a 500-tick against **10 injected**. **2026-08-26 — #318 phase 18: the wheel printed its own scale over its own data, and printed a year that never existed. Shipped as `a108cad`; deploy deliberately skipped at 1 of 3.** The wheel has four families of words and only three were guarded — rim labels since phase 11, arc labels since phase 12, band names measured sound in the same pass. The fourth is the **SCALE** (twelve 500-year ticks, two axis ends) and it had no guard, because every audit of this page had been an audit of what the **data** says and the scale is not data. It was placed by constants that knew nothing of the strings they positioned: `rRim + 11` alternating `rRim + 22`, and `rRim + 17`. **A constant cannot be right there**: a horizontal label is not rotated with its ray, so its reach back toward the centre is the support function of its box, `w/2·|cos a| + h/2·|sin a|` — `h/2` at twelve o'clock, the whole width at nine. **Measured before anything changed**, 3 locales × 3 sizes × 4 zooms = **504 labels** in the shipped faces: **210 reached inside the rim** (396 came closer than the clearance now guaranteed; worst 主前2500 at 900 px, 12.3 units in), **113 pairs** of scale-and-event ink actually overlapped by separating-axis test, **7 pairs** of scale labels overlapped *each other*, and **AD 1000 / 主后1000 was clipped off the left edge of the canvas at 700 px in all three locales** — it sits at 175°, so its whole width points outward along the horizontal. **The stagger's own comment was false.** It claimed the two radii existed "so adjacent labels clear each other"; adjacent 500-year labels are 26.5° and ~190 canvas units apart and were never in danger. What the stagger achieved was to push half the scale further **in**. **Zoom 0.8 is the worst case, not 14** — every font here is divided by `sqrt(zoom)`, so zooming *out* grows the type in canvas units while the rim stays put. **No constant could have fixed it**, and the arithmetic says so before any code: painting is clipped at 0.5 of the side, leaving 49.5 units outside the rim at 900 px, and a horizontal 主后1000 needs 53.6. Shrinking the type is #315's defect class; dropping the era word loses information. So the century labels now **lie along the ring** (`ringLabelRadius`), where only their height points inward, and the two ends keep their horizontal reading but are placed by the **support function itself** (`axialLabelRadius`), not a guess. `kAxisEndSwing` widens 0.055 → 0.10 because AD 2000 is 26 years — **1.4°** — from the AD 2026 end. A straight tangential run, not `_charsOnArc`: bending buys ~0.6 units of fidelity over 6° and costs every kerning pair. **Underneath the layout defect was an accuracy one.** The origin tick printed **`AD 0` / `主后0` / `主後0`**, a year the Dionysian reckoning does not have. The mandated refuter failed to refute it and strengthened it: astronomical numbering *has* a year 0 but never prefixes it "AD", and the corpus proves this axis is historical-BC-with-a-sign, not astronomical (Actium −31, Herod −4, Augustus −27, Han −202; astronomical would be −30/−3/−26/−201). The tick marks a **boundary**, so it now reads `BC | AD` / `主前｜主后` / `主前｜主後`; worst-case misplacement against a true zero-width epoch is 0.053°. The refuter's one obligation is discharged rather than deferred: `wheel_search.dart` promises the parser accepts every year the wheel prints, so **the one exemption is written into `parseWheelYears`'s doc comment and asserted in the test**, and the suite now also asserts **no record anywhere carries year 0** — so if one ever does, what it *means* gets asked before the label does. **The decision moved out of the painter**, as phase 11 did for the rim: canvas text leaves no widget and no semantics node, so `planAxisLabels` now returns the plan and `test/wheel_axis_label_clearance_test.dart` (14 tests, real faces, SAT between convex quads) can read it. A radius comparison was rejected as the instrument because it cannot tell "inside the rim over an event" from "inside the rim over nothing". **The guard was run against the pre-fix placement before being kept** — the finished file copied, only the two constants and the label function swapped back — and **6 of its 14 assertions went red**. That is how the clipped AD 1000 and the AD 2000/2026 collision were found: by running the new guard against the old code, not by reading the old code. **bwh39 checked** (mandatory, before code): BibleWorks' Timeline axis dialog exposes **"Label axis every"** and **"Space between labels"** as *user* settings — the same problem, solved by handing it to the reader. Our chart is a disc the reader cannot reconfigure, so the spacing must be computed from the measured string instead. Not a parity gap; a different answer forced by a different surface. Also confirmed already-done and not re-opened: bwh39's "search on `-46` to find 46 B.C." is implemented at `wheel_search.dart:294`, citing BibleWorks as the source of the convention. **DEPLOY SKIPPED, and it is a stated decision, not an omission.** Counter stands at **1 of 3**; no human reported this, the owner did not ask, and — the fact that settles it — **prod is 1.6.136 and the entire wheel is dev-only, so no real user has ever seen `AD 0` or the collision.** The measurable half of this change is proven to 1e-9 (the support function agrees with brute-force projection over 400 random cases) rather than needing a photograph. **The unmeasurable half is not waived, only deferred**: whether a tangential run *reads* well is judgement, it is owed a photograph, and that falls due at 3 of 3 — at most two iterations away. `flutter analyze` exit 0; full suite exit 0 at **3,663** tests (3,649 + 14).

**2026-08-26 — #318 phase 20: the chronology and the family tree name the same people and had never compared notes.
