Nothing rendered it, so nothing checked it: **a field with no call site is a field with no audit**, and this one had shipped 88 claims on no screen. **They disagreed.** `family_tree` dated Moses −1525..−1405; the timeline dated `moses_born` **−1526** and `moses_dies` **−1406** — both files stating the year EXACTLY (`approximate: false` / `dating.kind: birth`), on the **same** `scripture+thiele` basis, citing the **same two verses**, 1 Kings 6:1 and Exodus 7:7. That is not two reconstructions; it is **one derivation written down twice with one copy contradicting its own arithmetic**. `tools/audit_dates.py` check 32b validates the timeline's chain (exodus −1446, Exodus 7:7's 80 years), and **Isaac (−2066) and Jacob (−2006) agree exactly across the two files** — the controls that prove the offset was isolated rather than a convention. `family_tree` was corrected and `corrections` records both fields with the reasoning. **THE DECISION RULE IS THE DURABLE PART, because the next disagreement will not be this one:** where both sides state a year exactly, a disagreement is a defect and is repaired; where **either** side hedges, it is two published reconstructions and this repository does not pick one. So **Jesus is named and left alone** — the tree says c. 4 BC – c. AD 30, the timeline −5 and AD 33, and *both* sides are `conventional` and approximate, each already hedged to the reader with a "c."; picking a side would be one test file deciding a chronology (see #292). **And Ishmael is the third shape, which the rule also covers:** −2080 in *both* files, but the timeline states it exactly on `scripture+thiele` (Genesis 16:16 gives Abram's age outright, which is why `audit_dates.py` promoted it in v1.6.146) while the tree still hedges the same year as `conventional`/`approximate` — so the family tree prints "c. 2080 BC" for a date the chronology prints as 2080 BC. **One fact at two confidences, not at two years.** Not repaired, because the repair **STRENGTHENS a published claim** and the repository's standing instinct is that hedging is the conservative direction; named in the guard's library doc so the next reader does not have to re-find it. **THE GUARD WALKS THE JOIN RATHER THAN ENUMERATING IT.** `cross_asset_year_agreement_test.dart` had to hand-write its paths because its three assets share no key; here `personIds` **IS** the key, so `test/timeline_person_join_test.dart` (3 tests) walks all 88 links, and floors on 88/61/37 exist to catch the opposite failure — **a field quietly emptied would otherwise make the whole file pass by checking nothing**. Two detectors share one allowlist: an **id-derived exactness** check (`<pid>_born`/`<pid>_dies` whose own `personIds` names `<pid>`, restricted to records both sides state exactly) and a **lifespan containment** check. **Run against the pre-fix asset the sharp one went red on BOTH Moses fields and the broad one on only the birth**, which is the whole argument for having it: a death dated a year EARLY still falls inside the lifespan, so containment was structurally blind to `moses_dies`. Containment is also deliberately a **list with written reasons rather than a bare assertion** — the Transfiguration puts Moses in an AD 30 scene *on purpose*, and `wilderness_40` is a forty-year span keyed to its END so Aaron is correctly linked to a year one after his death (Numbers 33:38); a bare assertion would call both defects and be wrong twice. A dead-entry check fails any exception that stops matching, so a repair cannot leave blanket permission behind. **A third test guards the NAMESPACE, because a link that resolves is not a link that resolves CORRECTLY.** The tree holds two men called Manasseh (Joseph's son and the king) and two Eleazars; neither pair is linked today, but `personIds: ["manasseh"]` added to `josiah_reform` meaning the king would show a chip reading "Manasseh" that opens Joseph's son, and **no id check can see that**. All three scripts are tested because the chip renders one. **The 35 Anno Mundi people are out of both checks' reach and that is an instrument limit, not an omission** — AM and BC are not convertible here because the repo fixes no creation year, and `family_tree.json`'s own `yearLegend` says so; comparing them is a unit error, not a finding. **THE FIELD IS NOW RENDERED, AND THE SEARCH DEFECT IT EXPOSED WAS THE WORSE ONE.** An open row lists its people as chips under a **People / 相关人物** label, each opening `PersonDetailSheet` with the same lineage-hopping handler the family tree uses. Linked names in **all three scripts** joined the search haystack, which fixes a **false absence**: 耶哥尼雅 appears in **no event text anywhere in the asset**, so the page said "no results" about a record it holds — the one failure mode that is the app stating something untrue. The hint now says so ("Search events and people…"). Chips are deliberately **not** `scheme.primary` (on this page that colour means "jumps to the reader") and carry **no years**, because the row already shows one and a chip reading "Moses (1526–1406 BC)" two lines under a year column asks the reader to hold three dates to read one. **A COPIED WIDGET CARRIED A RULE VIOLATION WITH IT.** `_PersonChip` was copied from `_RefChip` and arrived holding two `Radius.circular(4)`, which took `bible_timeline_page.dart` from its declared **5** #279 offences to **7** and failed `page_chrome_pass_test.dart` — the only red in the run, and in neither file this phase wrote. **Both** chip species were squared rather than the growth declared, so the budget goes **5 → 3**. Phase 18's deferred judgement: the century labels now **lie tangentially outside the rim**, clear of the radial event titles, legible, and the improvement over printing them through the data is not close. Phase 19's: the antediluvian sheet's paragraph runs **three lines with "Open Bible Chronology" beneath it**, sizes to content well inside the 70% cap, and `Genesis 6-9` prints its full extent. **One thing that photograph shows and does not fix:** that sheet still labels Noah's Flood **"Elsewhere"**, the English name of the band `antediluvian` was mapped to in phase 15. Phase 15 declined to rename a band 54 correct events already sit on and that call still holds, but it reads oddly enough on a real screen to be named again rather than left to be rediscovered. #318 stays open.

**2026-08-26 — #317: the Atlas estimated travel time where it knew least and refused where it knew most. Shipped as `b188a0d`, dev NOT redeployed (counter 2 of 3, stated), two photographs now owed.** The ruler footer printed one confident figure, `ceil(km / 32)` captioned "about", between two arbitrary gazetteer points; the journey overlays, where **every leg carries the mode of travel the text itself uses**, printed no time at all. The refuter killed my first framing: 32 is **not unsourced folklore**, it is ISBE's "day's journey" (32–40 km, derived from a pack mule at 3 mph × 8 h), so this was a choice between two published conventions and needed a reason. **Deuteronomy 1:2 supplied one** — eleven days from Horeb to Kadesh-barnea, **244.3 km** measured through our own `haversineKm` over our own gazetteer: ISBE gives **7–8 days and contradicts the verse**, ORBIS's 20–30 km/day gives **9–13 and contains it**. Both arithmetics are pinned in `test/travel_time_test.dart`. New `lib/utils/travel_time.dart` holds the band (both ends quoted from one sentence of ORBIS v1.0, Scheidel/Meeks/Weiland, Stanford 2012 — a band of **load**, not of pace) and `travelOf`, which splits a route's drawn segments by leg. Land legs get a band plus the three hedges that must never be edited away: the source, the straight-line floor, and the unit (days on the road, not elapsed — Acts 18:11, 20:31). **Sea legs get no number and say why**, and the reason had to be re-based too: not "no sailing speed is known" (false — Casson gives 4–6 knots downwind) but **"none can be applied to a chord"**, which does not carry the wind's bearing; Acts 27 runs under the lee of Cyprus and Crete rather than the short way, so our own data is the proof. My draft's "1–2 knots against" and "more than an order of magnitude" were both simply wrong and are gone. Every non-zero bucket prints with **no threshold**, because `paul-rome` is 93.6% sea and `jesus-mark` 58% unstated — a band shown for paul-rome's three land legs with nothing beside it would say Paul walked to Rome in a week and a half. Numbers 33 is a nation moving with flocks (Gen 33:13–14), which ORBIS's marching figure understates; **that caution went into that route's own `basis` paragraph in the asset, not into code** — a claim about one journey belongs in its provenance. `daysOnFootFor` and `placesMapDays` are deleted outright. 3,717 tests (+23), analyze clean by exit code.

**2026-08-31 — #317: the ticket's own knowingly-unpaid item closed — the reader can now choose the pace, from ORBIS's own categories. BibleWorks' Travel Speed Window (bwh33) lets a reader type any speed and hours-per-day; copying that as a free-form box would have turned a cited ORBIS figure into an uncited one, since `travelDaysBasis` prints ORBIS beside every estimate. So `TravelBand` in `travel_time.dart` offers three adjacent pairs straight out of ORBIS's one sentence (12–20 / 20–30 / 30–36 km/day, "carts" / "onFoot" / "vehicle") rather than a number the reader invents. `walkingDaysFor` and `JourneyTravel.walk` (now a method, not a getter) take an optional `band:` defaulting to the shipped 20–30 km/day band, so all six existing pinned test numbers kept meaning exactly what they meant after a mechanical `.walk` → `.walk()`. A new `_bandPicker` column in `atlas_page.dart`'s `_JourneyPanel` lets the reader pick; the choice lives on `_AtlasPageState._band` (session-only, per #280) and reaches both the journey panel's estimate and `PlaceMapView`'s ruler footer through one shared value, so the two never print different bands for the same route. Choosing a non-default band shows `travelBandNotOurs`, which states its own arithmetic rather than merely hedging: over the gazetteer's own Horeb→Kadesh-barnea distance (244.3 km, haversine), only the 20–30 km/day band's 9–13 days contains Deuteronomy 1:2's eleven; carts give 13–21, vehicular 7–9. +7 tests (`travel_time_test.dart` 22→29), full suite 3,742 green (up from the prior run's 7 failures, which were the other session's in-flight yahwehdehua import and are now gone — that import landed as `49af9be`/`2f777d5`, which also separately deployed dev+prod to v1.6.185). `flutter analyze` exit 0. Shipped as `841c0d4`, then `PROJECT_STATE.md`/`HANDOFF.md` docs. NOT deployed this run — counter 2 of 3.**

No new `ui_strings` key, no new band — every string reused verbatim. **2026-08-31 — #315 residue: landed the killed run's small-print-floor repair, found already-correct on disk and uncommitted. The prior iteration had raised all 52 `fontSize: t.chrome - N` sites (15 files) to `t.chrome`, matching `WbMetrics.smallPrintFloor`, plus a new ratchet forbidding the `.chrome - N` notation with an 18-entry allowlist for above-floor additive offsets — but was killed before it could commit. This run audited the diff mechanically (52 removed lines, 52 added, each pair exactly `<recv>.chrome - N` → `<recv>.chrome`), reproduced the one red test the prior run left behind (`places_pane_test.dart`'s "the added line does not eat the map", 267.0 vs the old `>268.0` threshold), and re-baselined that assertion on all three measured canvas heights at the 320×400 pane floor: 293.0 before #317's band footer existed, 276.0 with the footer at `t.chrome - 1`, 267.0 now that those rows sit at the floor — a 9px cost, not the ~1px a single line would suggest, because the footer stacks ~7 text rows. No wrap, no overflow; only the old arbitrary tolerance was exceeded, by 1px. New threshold is `>255.0`, chosen to still catch a genuinely added footer line while not firing on this repair. **2026-08-31 — #315 residue, second pass: closed the last deferred field, `word_study_style.dart`'s dense-column `translit`/`micro`. They resolved to 9.0px at the default Menu Size — under `WbMetrics.smallPrintFloor` (11.0) — because `WordStudyStyle.resolve` wrote them as `type.chrome - 2`, a *named-argument initialiser on a data class*, read six files away in `originals_sheet.dart` as `fontSize: _st.translit`. Neither existing detector in `font_size_reach_ratchet_test.dart` can see that shape: one matches a literal or bound name at a `fontSize:` colon, the other matches `.chrome - N` arithmetic at that same colon; this is a fourth shape, a size assigned once and consumed through a field read elsewhere, invisible to both regexes. The file had in fact sat first on the ratchet's own `finished` list ("zero literals, and it stays zero") the whole time — true of the notation, false of the pixels it produced. The fix changed two lines to `type.chrome` (matching the column's other two small fields, `ref`/`gloss`) and amended the `finished` list's doc comment to say so rather than leave the false impression standing. The deferral's original stated reason — raising these collapses four ranks of the pane to one size — was checked at all six call sites in `originals_sheet.dart` (980, 1004, 1048, 1531, 2234, 2408) and does not hold: every one already carries rank on a non-size channel (muted colour, italics, `w700`, letter-spacing), so equalising the size does not equalise the reader's sense of hierarchy. One of the six, line 1531, is the CBOL CC-BY-NC-SA 4.0 attribution the licence obliges the app to print — it was the smallest, least-opaque text on the page. The phone-modal column (`translit: type.scaled(10)`, `micro: type.scaled(9)`) is left deliberately under the floor: `word_study_style_test.dart:142-143` pins those exact values as #284's explicit decision about a surface nobody asked to change, and `smallPrintFloor`'s own comment calls itself a comfort bound, not a correctness one, which does not license overriding a different surface's settled decision — a new test asserts that breach out loud (`m.translit, lessThan(smallPrintFloor)`) as a recorded owner question rather than silently passing over it. Added a 4-test group to `word_study_style_test.dart` that reads resolved `WordStudyStyle` values (the only instrument that can see this class of bug) rather than source text; ran it before the production edit to confirm 3 of the 4 reproduce the defect (`translit resolved to 9.0px`, `micro resolved to 9.0px`, and the rank-collapse assertion), confirming the guard is not a green light on a broken tree. Full suite 3,751 green (was 3,747), analyze exit 0. Shipped as `fce19c7`. NOT deployed — counter 2 of 3. No screenshot was taken; the visual question inherited from the first pass (does a caption read as subordinate at equal size to its heading?) is now *broader*, since this run makes four ranks in the Word Study pane equal, and remains unverified — say so plainly rather than imply it was checked.**

**2026-08-31 — #318 residue: the featured chronology wheel's canvas text had no CJK face, so Chinese drew blank on the web build in the app's default locale. #316 gave every style in `workbenchTheme` the `kCjkFontFallback` chain, but a theme only reaches `Text` widgets — a `TextPainter` inherits nothing, and `radial_chronology_page.dart` drew every one of its nine painters (ribbon names, spoke titles, verse refs, count badges, arc text, the shared measure/paint helpers) through a bare `TextStyle`, zero fallbacks in the file against 32 of 36 on the comparable atlas page. On CanvasKit that is not cosmetic: `NotoSansSC-Sub` is registered as its own family and a style naming no fallback has no face that can render Chinese at all — per `pubspec.yaml`'s own note, the label goes absent, not tofu, which reads as a layout bug and is why it survived. It also corrupted the fit arithmetic: `_measureLabel`/`_measureChars` feed `fitRadialLabel`'s fits-in-band decision while measuring with a font that cannot render the string. Added `canvasTextStyle()` to `font_catalog.dart` (color/fontSize/fontWeight, always carrying `kCjkFontFallback`) and converted all 8 inline `TextStyle(` construction sites on the wheel's canvas path to it — the 51 `Text`-widget styles on the same page were left untouched, already covered by #316. New source-ratchet `test/canvas_cjk_fallback_ratchet_test.dart`: no `TextPainter(` may have a bare `TextStyle(` within its next 15 lines (confirmed failing at lines 369/382/2262/2510 on the unmodified tree before the fix), plus a count pin at exactly 8 `canvasTextStyle(` sites. One bug found in my own test, not the page: `'canvasTextStyle('.contains('TextStyle(')` as a literal substring, so the naive "window doesn't contain TextStyle(" check fired on the very sites it was meant to pass — fixed by stripping converted sites out of the window before checking for a bare one. `chronology_page.dart` (3 painters, 0 fallbacks), `bible_timeline_page.dart` (1, 0) and `place_map.dart` (2, 3) are the same defect class, measured and deliberately deferred — the ratchet names only the wheel, on purpose. Full suite 3,764 green, +4 from the new ratchet file (no full-suite baseline was measured before this run's edits, so no "was N" claim is made). analyze exit 0. Shipped as `f7778d2`. NOT deployed — counter 1 of 3, dev last released at `a077c47`.**
